## Hardware Install Runbooks

Step-by-step procedures for bringing a piece of hardware up on a **new machine** (as
opposed to the design history, which lives in `coding plans/Update plans/`). Follow the
runbook rather than re-deriving the procedure — each one encodes traps that cost a bench
session to find.

| Hardware | Runbook |
|---|---|
| **Tucsen camera** (Libra / Dhyana / Aries / FL) | `docs/TUCSEN_CAMERA_INSTALL.md` — driver install (needs the operator; UAC cannot be answered non-interactively), then **verify the SDK property map on that camera by consequence**: the IDs are per-model, the `GetAttr` gate cannot catch an ID that exists but means something else, and on ME3B the initial guess had exposure pointed at the temperature property. |
| **Nikon Ti microscope body** | `tools_install_nikon_ti_sdk.ps1` (elevated) + the `MEBP_v75x_NIKON_TI_MICROSCOPE_CONTROL.md` plan. Note the HVCI trap: an old driver failing to load looks like a signature problem and the signature fix does not work. |

## ⚠️ Critical Safety Rules

**These invariants protect the hardware (a dragged/crashed needle bends the
needle, scratches the plate, or de-syncs the stage). Check them whenever you
touch stage motion, print execution, calibration navigation, or workflow travel.**

### 1. Retract Z to the "move" / safe Z before ANY cross-position XY move

Any time the stage moves XY **to a different location**, the needle MUST first
retract to the safe / "move" / travel Z **and the code must WAIT for Z to arrive
before the XY move starts**.

- **EXEMPT:** moving *within the same well* during a print/workflow (the print
  pattern itself — discrete `PRINT_PATH` segments, in-well trajectory waypoints).
  Z is intentionally at print height there.
- **EXEMPT:** manual incremental jog (arrow keys, jog buttons, Xbox, click-nudge,
  `move_xy_relative` / `move_xy_relative_um`) — user-controlled motion; the
  operator owns Z. Auto-retracting every nudge would make jogging unusable.
- **MUST retract:** click-to-travel, "Go To" well/A1/corner, return-home / `HOME_XY`,
  move-to-print-start (`MOVE_XY`), inter-well travel, plate-location scan hops.

**How to comply (use the existing primitives — do NOT bake a new ad-hoc gate):**
- Point-to-point travel → `StageController.safe_travel_to(...)` (raise → M400 +
  `wait_for_z_arrival` → XY → `wait_for_xy_arrival` → optional lower). It ABORTS
  the XY move if Z does not confirm at safe height.
- "Retract first, don't move XY yet" → `StageController.ensure_retracted_to(safe_z)`
  (raises to a height + waits; **never descends**).
- Print discrete travel → `PrintManager._retract_for_travel(...)` is invoked at the
  top of the `MOVE_XY` / `HOME_XY` handlers.
- Calibration navigation → `CalibrationPage._safe_navigate_to(...)`.
- Pick & place → `PickAndPlaceManager._safe_move_to` (inter-well = `safe_travel_to`;
  intra-well = small retract → wait → XY → lower).

**Never** write `if current_z >= safe_z: <move XY without retracting>`. That raw
comparison is **polarity-wrong** on ME3B V1 (`StageController.ZDIR = -1`, needle
descends as raw Z increases). Use the polarity-safe helpers
`StageController.z_height_of()` / `needle_at_or_above()`, or just always call
`safe_travel_to` (it is a near-no-op when the needle is already retracted).

A "retract for travel" must **never lower the needle** — only ever raise it in the
HEIGHT frame. A misconfigured/too-low travel-Z must degrade to a no-op, not a
crash-down. Never fall back to a raw literal travel height (e.g. `5.0`) — on
`ZDIR=-1` that is a *descent*; use `StageController.default_travel_z(ref, margin)`.

**Known gap:** `PrintTrajectoryPlanner` (pure-trajectory print path) still assumes
`ZDIR=+1` in its Z geometry, so its raise-before-travel is NOT safe on `ZDIR=-1`.
It logs a warning; prefer the discrete / hybrid-direct print path on ME3B V1 until
that planner gets a polarity pass. See
`coding plans/Update plans/MEBP_v75x_Z_RETRACT_BEFORE_XY_TRAVEL.md`.

### 2. Z polarity (ME3B V1)

The needle **descends as raw Marlin Z increases** (`ZDIR = -1`). Never assume
"up = larger Z". Do height comparisons in the `ZDIR`-scaled height frame.

### 3. Well-plate orientation — ONE convention everywhere

The convention is **well A1 displays TOP-LEFT; the stage physical 0,0 is
BOTTOM-RIGHT** (ME3B V1 Prior origin bottom-right, +X/+Y toward top-left). It is
driven by a single per-machine setting — `device_profile.plate_flip_180`,
surfaced on `StageController.plate_flip_180()` / `plate_axis_sign()` (restored at
startup via `apply_z_convention`). `plate_flip_180=True` means the plate is
mounted 180° to the stage, so `plate_axis_sign() == (-1, -1)`.

- The `WellPlate` data model is **plate-local** (A1 at relative `(0,0)`, +col→+X,
  +row→+Y) and is **never flipped** — don't touch it; `get_well_position` is an
  invariant many tests assert.
- The sign lives **only at the plate-local→stage mapping boundary**. Any code
  that turns a well/plate-centre into a STAGE coordinate via *geometry* must
  multiply the A1-relative offset by `controller.plate_axis_sign()`
  (`WellPlate.get_all_positions_from_a1(..., plate_axis_sign=)` and siblings take
  it; default `(1,1)`). Positions that are **taught/warped** (calibrated dict,
  mosaic-fit, `PlateWarpCalibrator`) are already absolute stage µm — **never
  re-sign them** (double-flip → crash).
- **Any new plate view** that plots a stage/zero-ref point (needle, target) over
  plate-local wells, OR renders the stage envelope, MUST apply the flip. Stage-
  frame views (`JogWorkspaceView`, `PrintTrajectoryMonitorView`,
  `print_monitor.PlateOverviewWidget` needle) read it via `set_plate_flip_180`;
  plate-local schematics (navigator, calibration plate view, designer, sketch)
  already show A1 top-left and need no flip.
- A saved calibration is stamped with the orientation; on load, a mismatch
  invalidates the taught XY calibration and forces a re-teach (the warp's
  predicted-leg would otherwise drive to a mirrored/garbage position).

See `coding plans/Update plans/MEBP_v75x_PLATE_ORIENTATION_CONVENTION.md`.

## Project Overview

MEBP is a Python/PySide6 desktop application for controlling laboratory bioprinting equipment. It orchestrates Prior ProScan XY stages, Marlin Z/pump controllers, and Hamilton syringe systems for multi-material biological printing.

### Key Directories

| Path | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `main.py` | Entry point — CLI args, mode selection |
| `SupportClasses/` | Backend modules — zero GUI dependencies |
| `gui/` | PySide6 frontend — pages, widgets, dialogs, styles |
| `config/` | Hardware configs, controller protocols, print files |
| `coding plans/` | Architecture docs, update plans, readmes |
| `tests/` | Test suite |
| `patches/` | Legacy version upgrade patches (deprecated — content migrated to update plans) |

### Core Design Principles

1. **Hardware abstraction** — GUI pages never touch serial ports. All hardware access through `StageController` or `PrintManager`.
2. **JSON protocol abstraction** — XY commands defined in `config/controllers/` JSON files.
3. **Hardware-first** — Page 0 (Hardware Setup) must complete before other pages unlock.
4. **µL-native pumps** — All user-facing pump values in microliters. Conversion to mm happens only inside `StageController.move_pump_uL()`.
   - **Plunger vocabulary (use everywhere):** **ASPIRATE** = draw fluid IN (plunger toward MAX/full; raises fill); **DISPENSE** = push fluid OUT (plunger toward ZERO/empty; lowers fill). Volume-delta sign: `+` = dispense, `−` = aspirate. Fill/position: 0 = empty (fully dispensed), capacity = full (fully aspirated). The canonical block lives atop `StageController.move_pump_uL`. Do **not** use "inject". Reserved terms NOT folded in: `prime` (start-of-print pre-flow lead-in) and `retract` (Z safe-travel raise / pump pressure-relief). Print-physics "extrusion" (`FlowPhysics`/`GeometryEngine`) and Marlin firmware terms (`M302` cold extrusion, `M83` relative) are domain terms, left as-is.
   - **Per-pump plunger calibration owns direction.** Each pump's dispense/aspirate raw direction is DERIVED by `StageController.apply_pump_setup` (the Hardware Setup → Pump "Pump Plunger Setup": Set Dispensed=empty/0, Set Aspirated=full), the same way `z_up_sign` owns Z. Once a pump is calibrated, `pump_dir_sign` (= `−aspirate_sign`) drives motion, retiring the per-pump `_flip_sign`/`axis_flip` hack for that pump. Uncalibrated pumps keep the legacy `_flip_sign` path. **Re-run the plunger calibration on each machine after this change** — a wrong derived sign reverses fluid flow.
5. **Safety-layered** — Software endstops, flow-rate clamping, crash recovery throughout.
6. **DPI-aware GUI** — All pixel dimensions go through `gui/scaling.py` so the UI scales correctly on any screen (1080p → 4K/Retina). QSS theme generated by `build_theme(scale)`.

---

## Per-Machine Config (multi-rig setups)

This repo is shared, via git, across several physical rigs (`ME3B_01`, `ME3B_02`, ...). `config/hardware/` is split structurally so per-machine calibration can never collide between rigs:

- `config/hardware/<machine-id>/` — **per-machine** state: camera/objective calibration, taught plate positions, mosaics, needle-bore/focus calibration, calibration status, LabLink node config, Tucsen per-camera SDK files, etc. Gitignored (`config/hardware/*/` except the two folders below), so it never syncs.
- `config/hardware/ME3B_general/` — **shared/portable** catalogs meant to travel between rigs: needle/plate/well/target-type libraries, plate/rosette designs, and the swappable `*Setup*.json` hardware configs. Tracked.
- `config/hardware/devices/` — **device profiles**. Tracked. See below.

**The machine identity IS the active device profile's name.** The profile already names the rig, is chosen by the operator on Hardware Setup → Device (the mandatory Page 0), and is recorded in the per-machine, gitignored `settings.json` under `device_profile.active` — so naming a rig `ME3B_01` gives it `config/hardware/ME3B_01/`, and there is exactly one home for "which machine is this". Resolution order: `MEBP_MACHINE_ID` env var (test/CI) → `device_profile.active` → the literal `"unassigned"` fallback (a real, functioning bucket so a fresh checkout never hard-crashes; `MachineConfig.machine_id_is_configured()` detects it).

⚠ **Device profiles live in the machine-INDEPENDENT `config/hardware/devices/`** — a profile *names* the per-machine folder, so it cannot live inside one. They are **tracked in git** deliberately: each rig's file has a distinct name so they can never conflict on pull, and it backs up every rig's safety envelope / steps-per-mm / axis map for a rebuild. Loading one is always an explicit operator action, so no rig adopts another's settings. A machine with **no** device profile has no identity yet, which triggers the onboarding wizard and lands the operator on the Device sub-page. `python tools_set_machine_id.py <ID>` sets/shows the identity headlessly.

⚠ **Renaming a profile (Save As) asks whether to carry the calibration folder** — a rename is "same rig, new label" (calibration must follow) while a new name is "different rig" (it must not); only the operator knows which. Switching identity mid-session needs a **restart**, because every store resolves its path once at import time; the panel says so.

**Every store must resolve its path through `SupportClasses/MachineConfig.py`'s `resolve_machine_path(relative)` / `resolve_shared_path(relative)`** — never hardcode `config/hardware/<file>`. Both auto-migrate a file/folder still sitting at the legacy flat `config/hardware/<relative>` location the first time it's asked for, so existing installs self-heal with no manual step beyond setting the machine id once. `python tools_migrate_machine_config.py` runs that migration for every known store in one deterministic pass (recommended after upgrading, so `git status` shows the old tracked per-machine paths as deletions in one commit instead of trickling in).

This is why any **new** per-machine store never needs a `.gitignore` edit: as long as it goes through `resolve_machine_path`, it lives under a folder that's already ignored structurally.

### ⚠ Four invariants that must not be relaxed (each is mutation-pinned)

1. **Nothing is MOVED while the machine id is unconfigured.** These paths resolve at *module-import* time, and a store can be imported before a device profile has been chosen — importing `gui.app` alone pulls in five of them. Migrating then would file real calibration under `unassigned/`, and once the operator names the rig the app would look in `<their-id>/` and find an empty folder: the calibration is still on disk but invisible. While unconfigured, `resolve_machine_path` therefore **reads the legacy location in place** and moves nothing; the real migration happens on the next run. (This failure has occurred once on the bench.)
2. **`main.py` imports `gui.app` only AFTER `_ensure_machine_id`.** Ordering is load-bearing and pinned by an AST test (`tests/test_v717_machine_config.py`) — do not tidy those imports back to the top of `run_gui`.
3. **The leftover sweep never files a per-machine store as shared.** "Shared" means committed to git and pushed to every other rig, so `sweep_remaining_flat_files` is denied by name via `PER_MACHINE_FILENAMES` rather than trusting that every store module imported successfully. **Add new per-machine filenames to that set.**
4. **Reserved machine names are refused.** A rig named `ME3B_general` or `devices` would write its private calibration into a shared, tracked folder and publish it to every other rig. `MachineConfig` refuses those names (and path traversal like `..`); it never many-to-one *sanitizes* a name, which would silently merge two rigs' calibration into one folder.

## Update Plans

For each update task in a version, a plan document **must** exist in:

`coding plans/Update plans/`

### Naming Convention

`MEBP_v{from}_to_v{to}_UPDATE.md` — e.g., `MEBP_v728_to_v729_UPDATE.md`

### Plan Document Requirements

Each update plan is a **living document** that must include:

1. **Objective** — What this update accomplishes
2. **Files Modified** — List of all files touched with brief rationale
3. **Implementation Steps** — Ordered checklist of changes
4. **Status Tracking** — Mark each step as: `[ ]` pending, `[x]` done, `[~]` in-progress
5. **Testing Notes** — How to verify the changes work
6. **Issues & Decisions** — Log of problems encountered and decisions made during implementation

### Workflow

- Before starting work on a new update task, **create or update** the plan file
- As work progresses, **update the status** of each step in the plan
- When complete, ensure all steps are marked `[x]` and add a summary of what was done

---

## Version Completion Checklist

**When the user says an update is complete**, perform these three steps before pushing:

### 1. Architecture Document

Create a new architecture file in `coding plans/Architectures/`:

- **Naming**: `ARCHITECTURE_V{version}.md` (e.g., `ARCHITECTURE_V736.md`)
- **Base**: Copy from the previous version's architecture and update with all changes
- **Update**: New files, modified modules, new design principles, updated folder structure

### 2. Update Plan

Ensure the update plan in `coding plans/Update plans/` is finalized:

- All implementation steps marked `[x]`
- Files modified list is complete
- Testing notes reflect what was actually tested
- Add the new plan to the **Existing Update Plans** table in this file

### 3. README

- **Archive**: Copy the current `README.md` to `coding plans/Readmes/README_V{previous}.md`
- **Update**: Update `README.md` with:
  - New version number
  - Any new features, installation steps, or usage changes
  - Updated architecture doc reference
  - New project structure entries if applicable

### 4. Push

- Commit all changes
- Push to the version branch (e.g., `Version-7.3.7`)

A test pins that the size default stays NON-ZERO — *"no time limit"* must not become *"nothing stops it"*. Deliberately not "raise 600 to 3600": that moves the surprise rather than removing it. **(2) 🔴 REMOVING THE TIME CAP IS EXACTLY WHAT MAKES A MULTI-GB FILE REACHABLE, turning a latent container problem into a live one:** AVI is RIFF, whose chunk offsets are 32-bit (4 GB absolute, and the OpenCV AVI muxer is widely reported to stop producing seekable files nearer 2 GB) — at a 10-minute cap nothing got close, without one an 8 GB AVI is ordinary. NEW `CaptureVideoWriter.container_byte_limit()` + `CONTAINER_MAX_BYTES`: mp4 gets exactly the operator's value, AVI gets theirs **or 2 GB if theirs is larger or unlimited** — a **floor on trust, not a preference** (it only ever LOWERS the configured cap, and only for AVI), so a long AVI stops CLEANLY at a size the container can still describe instead of running on into a file that looks finished and will not play. A raw time-lapse is a DIRECTORY of TIFFs, not one container, so it is exempt **even when `video_container` still says avi from a previous session** (mutation-pinned both ways). The 2 GB figure is conservative on purpose and cannot regress anyone: an AVI could never previously exceed 10 minutes. **(3) THE DIALOG ANSWERS THE QUESTION THE OPERATOR NOW HAS** — with no time cap, *"how long can I record?"* is a division they would otherwise do in their head. | `MEBP_v7217_HOLD_IN_LIQUID_AFTER_ASPIRATE.md` | v7.21.7 **the needle is held in the liquid after an aspirate, so the tail of the aspirate is not air** (operator: *"whenever the needle aspirates a liquid (e.g. oil or ink), the Z is moving up before the pump is done aspirating … the needle being outside of the liquid and into the air … aspirates air -- this is bad"*). **🔴 THE OBVIOUS DIAGNOSIS IS WRONG AND ACTING ON IT CHANGES NOTHING:** the plunger side is ALREADY synchronous — every pick/place actuation goes `_settled_pump_move` → `move_pump_uL(settle=True)` → `_finish_pump_submove(block=True)` → `_wait_pump_move_complete`, which **drains the move from Marlin's planner with M400** (timeout scaled to the move's own duration, poller suspended), so when `aspirate_ink`/`run_prep` returns the PLUNGER is provably finished, and `pump_settle_time_s` dwells on top of that. What is NOT finished is the **FLUID**: the column between plunger and tip is compliant (fine bore, viscous ink, long tube) so liquid keeps being drawn in after the plunger stops — and the very next step is a `safe_travel_to` whose FIRST action is the Z retract, lifting the tip out of the well inside exactly that window. **`pump_settle_time_s` is the wrong instrument** (it brackets EVERY discrete move, before AND after, dispenses and the print prime included — buying seconds of in-liquid hold with it would slow every unrelated actuation by the same amount), hence a separate purpose-named value. **ONE global, resolved in ONE place:** `HardwareConfig.pump_post_aspirate_dwell_s` → `StageController.pump_post_aspirate_dwell_s()` → the executor's `post_aspirate_dwell_s = **None** ⇒ INHERIT`, which is what makes this one line per surface — **no workflow page wires the field**, so Quick Print, spheroid, cell targeting and cell labeling all pick it up by construction and cannot drift (the load-bearing test; break inheritance and EVERY workflow silently loses the hold). Fires at the six places the needle is in a reagent well, has just ASPIRATED, and a travel follows: `aspirate_ink` · `run_prep` oil + buffer · `run_post_clean` buffer · `run_print_cleanup`'s oil reset · `prepare_starting_oil`'s top-up; NOT on a dispense, the streamed print path, the prime, or manual jog. **Default 2.0 s and an ABSENT key takes it too** — a knob defaulted to 0 leaves the reported defect in place until the operator finds the setting, and a setup saved before this existed was written by code carrying the defect, so inheriting the fix IS the intended migration (~2 s × a few pickups against a failure mode that loses a whole run while reporting SUCCESS). **Three placement decisions:** `aspirate_ink` holds ONCE at the END of the in-well sequence, because with tip-priming the order is `aspirate(vol+prime)` → `dispense(prime)` back into the SAME well with no Z move between, so one hold immediately before the needle actually leaves is what was asked for and does not double the wait (inside the `try`, so a granular orbit keeps stirring through it); the cleanup oil reset is **gated on DIRECTION not on the step** (`if oil_uL < 0`) because that reset is SIGNED and can go either way depending on where the plunger ended up; and 0 s is byte-identical to legacy **including** the sub-step/dwell-tick chatter. Abort-aware (routes through the existing `_dwell`, so an Abort mid-hold raises instead of sitting it out). Surfaced on Hardware Setup → Pump, the Common Print Settings page and all four workflow ⚙ popouts (registered in `CommonPrintSettings.GLOBAL_KEYS`), and **both Quick Print confirm dialogs name it** — an operator told "pick up 3 µL" who then watches the needle sit still for seconds needs the dialog to have said so, or the fix reads as a hang. Tests: NEW `test_v7217_hold_in_liquid_after_aspirate.py` (**28**) whose fixture records travel/pump/wash/hold onto **ONE ORDERED EVENT LIST** — the defect is purely an ORDERING defect, so a fixture that can only COUNT holds passes with the hold on the wrong side of the retract, i.e. with the bug still shipping; the controller fake implements `move_pump_uL` rather than the test overriding `_pump_move`, because the prep's `compensate=None` aspirates go through the module-level `_settled_pump_move` instead and an override would leave exactly the reagent aspirates this change is about un-recorded. **11/11 mutations CAUGHT** (incl. *the hold moved BEFORE the pump move* and *the controller reader aliased onto the settle dwell*), sources restored byte-identical, harness asserts the baseline is GREEN first and restores in a `finally`. ⚠ **My own first cut broke an abort opportunity and a test caught it:** routing the hold through `actuate()` used `else: return` on the nothing-actuated branch, which also skipped the `_check_abort()` that ran there before — replaced with a `moved` flag so the abort check stays UNCONDITIONAL. ⚠ `_post_aspirate_dwell_s` reads its OWN field with `getattr`: several suites drive `PickPlaceExecutor` as a `__new__` partial whose `__init__` never ran, and 12 pre-existing tests went red until that was fixed. + `captured_at`, `list_history`/`history_count`/`add_history_entry`/`restore_history` (a **swap**, so reversible). **(3) `attach_processed` now derives its name from THIS capture's raw stem** — with a fixed `_proc.png` a fresh raw inherits the previous run's processed file, and the overlays PREFER the processed one, so the screen would show a stale image while the store claimed a new one. **(4) `clear_channel(include_history=True)`/`clear_well` unlink archived images** (else a delete leaves invisible disk usage); `False` promotes the next-newest. **🔴 (5) THE OTHER OVERWRITE — A LOST UPDATE, CAUGHT LIVE MID-CHANGE:** the app holds ONE store all session and `_save_meta` dumps the ENTIRE `_data`, so any external edit is reverted by its next capture — the import completed at **17:16** and the running app's genuine `plt_7863c425dc27 B2 DAPI` capture at **17:28** wrote the file back from startup-era memory, dropping all 33 imported records (diagnosed from the record itself: untimestamped name + no `captured_at` = old code, `frames=121`/`exposure=150 ms` = a real scan, not a test). | `MEBP_v7215_SKETCH_EXTRUSION_TO_QUICK_PRINT.md` | v7.21.5 **the sketch's exact output — including a calculation of the extrusion modifier ALONG the printing path — is what Quick Print prints** (operator: *"the exact output of the print sketch including a calculation of the extrusion modifier along the printing path should be sent to the quick print"*). **THE TOOLPATH ALWAYS TRANSFERRED; THE EXTRUSION NEVER DID:** the bake writes the full Nx7 CSV (pump columns and all), but Quick Print kept only the XY of each sub-path — the pump column was used *solely* as a travel mask — and re-derived ONE flow for the whole print from its own Flow@100% / Extrusion × / bore area. So after v7.21.4 made an outline a single pass whose width comes from FLOW, a declared line width had **no effect on the machine at all**, a *no extrude* section still extruded once it went through Quick Print, and the v7.21.4 plan had to record the limitation *"the multiplier is sketch-wide, so shapes with different line widths cannot all match at once"* — exactly what this closes. **(1) THE COMPILER** splits deposition into a BASE (orifice cross-section = modifier 1.0) × a PER-SHAPE modifier `extrusion_multiplier × line_width_mm / ref_width`, where ref_width is the orifice **INNER** Ø — the same reference the page's shaded band and its *"this width needs N×"* readout already use, so what the page REPORTS is what the compiler APPLIES. `move_to` stamps every emitted segment **in lockstep with `waypoints`** (the profile cannot drift out of alignment with the toolpath), travel/lift/hold-pressure come out **0.0**, and `total_volume_uL` is now SUMMED per segment — with a per-shape modifier that is no longer length × one scalar. ⚠ **A MIGRATION THAT HAD TO BE EXPLICIT:** before this a width was never an extrusion instruction AND the page seeded new shapes from the orifice **OUTER** Ø while 1× is the INNER Ø, so honouring stored widths multiplies an old sketch's deposition by od/id ≈ **1.7× on a hypodermic needle** — hence `Sketch.width_drives_extrusion` defaults True for a NEW sketch and **False for anything loaded from a dict without the key**, with a checkbox to opt in and a tooltip naming the consequence; and the page now seeds a new shape's width at the **1× reference** so a freshly drawn shape is exactly 1×. **(2) NEW pure `SupportClasses/ExtrusionProfile.py`** (stdlib only — produced by the compiler and Quick Print, consumed on the print thread): ONE description of "how much per mm, WHERE" and ONE lookup. `build()` **REFUSES** an absent/empty/wrong-length/non-finite profile (an off-by-one applies a shape's flow to the wrong stretch of path — a thick bead where a thin one was designed — while falling back to the scalar is merely the old behaviour); `at_index` serves the discrete executor and `at_arclen` the velocity followers off **one** cumulative-length table so they cannot disagree, a position exactly on a waypoint taking the segment it is ENTERING; built against the **RAW** points so the closed-loop follower's coincident-point de-dup is harmless (dropping zero-length segments cannot change an arc length). **µL/mm is the transferable quantity** (a modifier is bore-relative and only means something next to the needle it was measured against), and `rate_for` exists because **the pump rate has to track the volume** — the discrete executor paces each segment by `max(xy_move_time, pump_move_time)`, so a fixed rate would just make a thicker segment take LONGER, spreading the extra volume over a longer bead and quietly cancelling the width it was meant to produce. **(3) QUICK PRINT** reads the baked profile, refuses a length that does not match the object's own trajectory, and slices it **in lockstep** with the existing travel-move sub-path split (published on a side channel, because `_path_segments_for_selection` has a dozen callers that want plain XY); it **recomputes µL/mm against the needle fitted NOW** rather than replaying the stored µL/mm, so the DECLARED WIDTHS print instead of a volume that was only right for the needle present at bake time, with Quick Print's own Extrusion × as a **trim** (×1 = exactly the sketch) — stated in the readiness card, because an operator whose × *looks ignored* needs to see what governs the bead. 🔴 **THE FLOW CEILING IS SIZED BY THE THICKEST SEGMENT** (`_flow_modifier_peak` = the trim alone when there is no profile, so an ordinary print is unchanged): a mean would let the widest segment over-pressure the needle and **a pulled glass tip shatters**; the peak is cached from the SAME geometry pass that produces the segments so neither re-reads the print file. Multi-ink gets the same fidelity (its sub-sketches are already recompiled per group). `PrintReadiness` gains `extrusion_span`/`extrusion_trim` + an Extrusion check, so it flows through the ONE evaluation that drives the card, the status line, the button state and the tooltip. **(4) THE JOB + ALL FOUR EXECUTOR SITES:** `build_well_plate_job(path_extrusion_profiles=)` in µL/mm, validated at BUILD time (a mismatch reported once, not silently per print) and **param-for-param identical to before when omitted**; one `_extrusion_profile_for(cmd)` feeds the discrete stream (`at_index(i-1)`), open-loop velocity, the closed-loop follower and the **feed-plan section runner** (`at_arclen(s_global - ds)` — the plan splits into sections but keeps a GLOBAL arc length, so deposition follows the shape across every section boundary), with every residual flush and the exactness top-up made profile-aware so a path depositing only through its profile cannot drop its tail volume. **`no_print` is enforced in exactly ONE place** — `move_to`'s `printing` flag; a first cut also checked it in `_shape_modifier` and a mutation proved that second check DEAD, so it was deleted. Tests: NEW `test_v7215_sketch_extrusion_to_quick_print.py` (**36**, through the production compiler, the REAL SketchPage bake, a REAL QuickPrintWorkflowPage, the real job builder and the **real discrete executor**) incl. the plunger column agreeing with the profile, geometry untouched by an 8× width change, *no profiles at all is param-for-param identical*, and an end-to-end bake→job carrying two µL/mm values in a 2:1 ratio summing to the sketch's own planned volume; **15/15 mutations CAUGHT**. ⚠⚠ **THE MUTATION HARNESS ITSELF MANUFACTURED TWO FALSE "CAUGHT"s AND THAT IS THE LESSON:** a 2-minute tool timeout killed the first matrix run **while a mutation was applied**, leaving it in the tree, so every later mutation was scored against an already-RED suite. The stranded mutation was restored and the harness now **asserts the baseline is GREEN before it starts** and restores in a `finally`; re-run honestly it reported **13/15** and exposed two real weaknesses — `no_print` having two enforcement points (mutating either alone was a no-op) and the ceiling test asserting on `_flow_modifier_peak()` (the MODEL) instead of on `_flow_limited_xy_max_mm_s()` (the BEHAVIOUR), the recurring weakness this file keeps recording; the latter now pins a binding ceiling and asserts the resolved speed HALVES. Four contract-changed tests updated (the width→volume relationship is now the point; fixtures declare the width they mean). Regression **405** (every sketch suite) + **242** (executors/extrusion model/readiness/log reader) + **421** (Quick Print consumers + the v7.19 queue, v7.20 calibrator, v7.21 section promotion, v7.7 zones, print library) + **82** (new suites + hygiene) + a `gui.app` import smoke. **One pre-existing failure PROVED not ours:** `test_v77_print_log_reader::TestRealHardwareLog` globs `logs/prints/*.jsonl` and takes the lexicographically last file, now one of the operator's own **multi-stroke** prints (`send_it`, 2026-08-19 17:36) holding **6 separate PRINT_PATHs** that each restart `plan_section.index` at 0 with `base_s_mm = 0.0`, so `section_bases` — which keys by index alone — collides them and the running-sum assertion cannot hold; `PrintLogReader.py` is **not in this diff** and the failure reproduces with the suite run alone (a real pre-existing reader limitation for multi-stroke runs, worth its own fix). **Needs HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER:** an ordinary non-sketch print shows **no** Extrusion row and the same flow/speed as before (go/no-go) → an **existing** saved sketch opens with *Line widths set extrusion* **OFF** and an unchanged volume → a new two-line sketch at 1× and 2× welded end to end reads *"Extrusion — from the print itself, ×1.00–2.00 along the path"* → **print it and look at the bead: the second half visibly wider, no pen-up between them**, dispensed ≈1.5× a uniform print → Extrusion × 0.5 thins the whole bead and the ratio holds → a **No extrude** shape deposits nothing while still being traversed → a 6× width **auto-limits the top speed** with the warning naming the needle's flow ceiling (the PEAK binds, not the average) → repeat through a multi-ink sketch → the run's JSONL carries the `extrusion_profile` event and its planned µL matches the Sketch page. |
| `MEBP_v7214_SKETCH_SINGLE_PASS_JOIN_NOEXTRUDE.md` | v7.21.4 **three Sketch-page changes: one pass per line, join a line to any object's print start/stop, and no-extrude on any section** (operator: *"do not auto calculate the need for multiple lines to fill in the width of any line. We will set that line width and just increase the extrusion modifier to match."* + *"we want to be able to join a line to the start or stop position of any existing print object"* + *"we want to have the ability to do a no extrude on any section we want"*). **(1) 🔴 THE WIDTH WAS A GEOMETRY INSTRUCTION AND IT MOVED THE PRINT OFF THE SKETCH:** `_pass_offsets` expanded any outline wider than the fill pitch into `round(line_width/bead)` **adjacent parallel passes** centred on the drawn geometry — a `radius=5, width=1.2 mm` circle printed rings at **r = 4.6 / 5.0 / 5.4**, i.e. the outer pass sat half the declared width OUTSIDE what was drawn, and length/volume/time jumped 5× at 2 mm (an existing test asserted exactly that `>3×`). Now it returns `[0.0]` unconditionally: ONE bead on the drawn geometry, width reached by **flow**. `_pass_offsets` is **kept, not deleted** — five shape branches iterate `offsets`, so one uniform per-pass shape means the whole change is one function and an explicit opt-in multi-pass mode would be a change in ONE place. **Fills are deliberately untouched** (the rule is about outline WIDTH, not rasters) and that is guard-the-guarded, else this could ship with fills broken and the suite still green. **`line_width_mm` would have become a dead setting**, so the shape card now PRICES it — *"1.00× deposits ≈ 0.400 mm; this width needs 3.00×"* + a one-click **Set extrusion to N× to match** that moves the shaded band with it; ⚠ the band still shows the PREDICTED width, never the declared one (painting the declared width would assert a bead the flow may not back — the exact confusion being removed), and the tooltip states the real limit: the multiplier is sketch-wide, so shapes with different widths cannot all match at once. **(2) 🔴 COINCIDING POINTS ARE NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT** — the compiler welds only ADJACENT shapes and a drawn line is APPENDED, so snapping alone would have looked joined and printed as two beads. Two halves: NEW `_print_point_targets` offers every unfilled shape's `_effective_start_world`/`_effective_end_world` — the SAME points the green ▸ / red ■ markers mark, i.e. after a custom start point, a rolled seam or a closure overlap, NOT a mere vertex (pinning a start to the far endpoint swaps entry and exit and the targets follow) — consulted FIRST in `_snap` and winning ties (the vertex/edge loops replace only on a *strictly* smaller distance) because landing there is what welds; then `_join_to_print_point` re-orders, with **four cases** and only ONE needing help: press-on-START pins `start_point` to the other endpoint so the line runs p1→p0 and ENDS at the target's entry — the compiler's own pass-0 flip looks *backwards* (at the previous shape's exit) and cannot fix that one. `new_idx` is always the last index, so `pop` cannot shift `j`. Constraint capture is anchor-aware: a print point that IS a solver DOF becomes a **coincident** constraint, a DERIVED one (circle seam, trimmed end) captures **nothing** rather than writing a ref the solver cannot resolve. `_snap_vertices` refactored onto a shared `_shape_anchor_points` (one definition of a shape's snap points). NEW `join_result` signal → *"Joined to the stop of circle — they print as one bead"*. **(3) `no_print` HAD NO UI AT ALL** — in the model, serialized, honoured by both the compiler and `plan_print_sections`, with `backtrace_shape` its only production writer. NEW undoable `set_no_print` (skips travel markers, returns a count) driven from the shape card, the multi-selection card, AND a per-**section** `extruding`/`no extrude` toggle in the Print-sequence header (the literal ask; a section's shapes are already selectable there); body rows read `· no extrude`. The preview needed no code — runs are categorised from `pump_states`, so a move-only shape already draws as a dashed travel run. Tests: NEW `test_v7214_sketch_single_pass_join_noextrude.py` (**36**, driving the PRODUCTION canvas/page) incl. `test_without_the_reorder_it_would_NOT_have_welded` (the same geometry appended at the end stays 3 sections, so the join tests measure the reorder and not a coincidence) and an end-to-end test that asks the REAL `_snap` for the hit and hands it to the REAL commit path; **10/10 mutations CAUGHT**, sources restored byte-identically. ⚠ **One of my own tests was too weak and a mutation caught it first:** the neighbour-index swap (`shapes[dest+1]`↔`shapes[dest-1]`) survived as an ASSERTION — caught only by an incidental `IndexError` elsewhere — because the announce test checked `"stop" in msg` with two LINES in the fixture, so naming the wrong neighbour read identically; it now uses a **circle** in both directions. Four contract-changed tests were **replaced by their inverse, not deleted** (both multi-pass tests + the snap-provenance test, which now also asserts the coincident capture still happens + the section-header toolbutton count). Regression **369** (every sketch suite together) + **75** (print-library/multi-ink/seam/extrusion-volume/print-setup-routine/travel-split) + a `gui.app` import smoke + a real `SketchPage` built offscreen + a **real-mouse** smoke (press/drag/release 0.02 mm off a print stop → p0 exactly `(10.0,0.0)`, inserted at index 1, welded into section `[0,1]`, coincident captured). **Perf measured** because `_snap` runs per mouse-move and a print STOP on an overlap-closed loop compiles that loop: **0.07 ms** with no overlaps → 0.56 ms at 20 overlap circles → 5.36 ms at a pathological 200, inside a 16 ms frame (and `paintEvent` already pays it for the markers). ⚠ **v7.21.3 was already claimed** by concurrent work in this tree (the pulled-capillary input freeze), hence v7.21.4. **Needs GUI verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER:** set a 0.4 mm line to **2 mm** and confirm ONE toolpath line on the drawn geometry, not five offset rings, with length/volume unchanged (go/no-go) → **Set extrusion to N×** grows the band to that width → drag a second line's end onto the first's **red ■ stop** and the sequence collapses to ONE section with the status naming the join → repeat onto a **green ▸ start** and it lands BEFORE that shape → join a circle's seam (no constraint there is correct) → snap a plain vertex and confirm the order does NOT change → tick **No extrude** and the run turns dashed with the volume dropping → the section toggle switches a whole section and back → Ctrl+A + the selection checkbox does all of it → **undo** covers each step → bake and confirm Quick Print shows the same single-pass geometry. |
| `MEBP_v7213_NEEDLE_CAPILLARY_INPUT_FREEZE.md` | v7.21.3 **the pulled-glass-capillary needle fields froze the app on every keystroke, and clamped what you typed** (operator: *"under pulled glass capillary it freezes every time i input a number"* + *"it wont allow me to input the pulled tip diameter it puts a range on the value, i should be able to input anything i want"*). **🔴 ROOT-CAUSED FROM THE OPERATOR'S OWN `logs/freeze.log`, not inferred** — the v7.16 `GuiWatchdog` had already caught it: **six GUI-thread stalls on 2026-08-19 16:21–16:22, EVERY one rooted at `hardware_setup._on_needle_changed`** and each stuck frames deep in a *different* page. `config_changed` is **not a local notification**: the receiver writes `settings.json` AND calls `set_hardware_config` on every page, which cascades into the sketch page's `_refresh_sequence`, the calibration page's own synchronous re-emit (→ workflows → print calibrator) and Quick Print's `_render_readiness` — and a `QDoubleSpinBox` emits `valueChanged` on **every keystroke**, so a three-digit tip diameter ran that whole cascade three times. Measured: the page's OWN per-edit work is **~1 ms** (profiled) against **>5 s** for the real cascade on the rig. Fixed at the **SOURCE, one choke point** (the page has ~25 `_on_config_changed` call sites; a per-call-site fix leaves the next one unprotected) — a 250 ms `CONFIG_EMIT_DEBOUNCE_MS` timer coalesces the emit while everything the page owns stays **synchronous**, because debouncing that too would trade a freeze for a stale readout (mutation-pinned, M5). Because the choke point is shared this also fixes the identical per-keystroke fan-out on the setup-name/notes fields, the pump settle/prime spins and the camera µm/px override. **A pending emit is never DROPPED** — the receiver persists to `settings.json`, so a swallowed edit is a silently lost setting, worse than the freeze: `hideEvent` + `aboutToQuit` both flush (the pair the v7.16 crop-persist debounce already uses), and a whole-config **LOAD flushes at once** rather than waiting out a keystroke debounce (one deliberate decision, not a burst — the crop store's `persist_now` reasoning). Typed text now commits on Enter/focus-out (`setKeyboardTracking(False)`) so the LIVE needle no longer walks through 3 µm and 30 µm on its way to 300, each of those a real config change fanned out and persisted; arrow steps stay immediate. **🔴 THE CLAMP WAS ENFORCING NOTHING** — a spin box does not refuse an out-of-range number, it **REWRITES it as you type**, so `tip_id`'s 0.5–500 µm band silently substituted a value the operator never entered; the bundled `capillary-500-µm-tip` preset sits *exactly* on the old maximum, so no wider tip was expressible at all. Every constraint the range encoded is already checked by `HardwareConfig._needle_bore_issues` and reported BY NAME (*"tip inner Ø (900.0 µm) cannot exceed the barrel inner Ø (100 µm)"*), and `NeedleSpec` itself already logs *"a pulled tip should be narrower. **Keeping the values as entered.**"* — the model was always designed to accept and warn; only the widget clamped. All six capillary ranges are now open (0→100 mm Ø, 0→10 m length) at finer decimals so a sub-micron pull is typeable, `0` reads as "not set", and the shared `_CAP_SPIN_SPECS` table means every per-bore row of a backpack inherits it by construction. Verified all **6 bundled pull recipes still round-trip with 0 flipping to "(custom)"**.
