Worse than a refusal: `XYStageModel.command_velocity` clamps only `if top > 0`, so simulating against an empty store models an **UNCLAMPED, infinitely fast stage**, and `stamp_print_settings` stamped `xy_max_speed_um_s = 0.0` onto every print job. Plus the one-click **never measured** the top speed (that lived only inside the GUI page, and never called `check_fits` — a clamped sweep silently reports a LOW top speed, which makes every commanded mm/s run proportionally FAST), and the auto-tune drove **every** candidate on hardware (23 runs ≈ 8–22 min) minimising `path_error()["rms_um"]`, the one-sided untimed metric its own docstring forbids as an autotune objective — the stored `lookahead_mm: 0.2` / `corner_speed_factor: 0.2` were both that descent's **grid minimum**, its fingerprint. Decisions (AskUserQuestion ×6): grid scored in **simulation**, winner verified on the stage · home = the **Timing Calibration page**, Challenge dialog demoted to a bench diagnostic · measurement **overwrites everywhere automatically** (an r²/3× sanity gate replaces the retired explicit Apply) · axes `lookahead_mm` × `corner_speed_factor` · **speed is an OUTPUT** · the store is **re-armed**. **🔴 THE FINDING THAT DECIDES THE GRID:** a design review concluded *"no pair holds a 2 mm star to 30 µm — geometry, not tuning"* — **wrong**, and the reason is the design. It bracketed the lookahead at `[la0/4, 2·la0]`; measured head to head on this rig at 2 mm/s that axis gives **p95 40.2 µm FAIL** while `[corner_budget, 2·la0]` gives **15.3 µm PASS**. `derive_settings` sizes the lookahead so the target SPEED is *permitted*; the **corner budget** sizes it for *accuracy* — on a 2 mm star the 142° tips tolerate 0.040 mm against a closed-form 0.838 mm, 21× apart. **🔴 THREE SILENTLY-INERT-AXIS TRAPS**, each of which makes every grid cell score identically while looking like a result: (1) the simulator's tuning keys are `lookahead`/`corner_factor`, NOT the store's `lookahead_mm`/`corner_speed_factor` — `resolve_for` does `p.update(tuning)` then reads `p["lookahead"]`, so a store-named key is **silently ignored** (my first sweep hit exactly this); (2) `min_lookahead_frac` from the derivation does `la = max(la, mlf·speed·L)` and **re-raises every candidate back to `la0`**, while `hold_speed` pins the cap to the request — both neutralised per candidate and **re-derived from the winner** so what persists reproduces what was simulated; (3) the requested speed is not what runs (a requested 3.0 mm/s runs at **0.43 mm/s**, `cap_reason="dead_time"`), so everything reported quotes the RESOLVED cap. The sweep runs **largest-lookahead-first**: the cap is monotonic in lookahead, so the first passing row holds the fastest passing candidate (the optimum, not a heuristic) and those are also the ~20 ms sims, not the ~160 ms ones. NEW GUI-free `SupportClasses/XYTopSpeed.py` (sweep + `EncoderMotion` extracted from the page; adds the `check_fits` bound it never had, absolute stage µm, alternating direction so the return leg IS the next measurement) + NEW `SupportClasses/XYCalibrationRun.py` (`commit_top_speed` — ONE writer, **store first** because it alone unblocks the derivation and the simulator — plus `build_grid`/`score_grid`/`verify_on_hardware`/`persist`/`run_calibration`). `PositionPoller` suspend is now **REFCOUNTED** (`_suspended` a property, so every existing reader is unchanged): the calibration nests suspends, and with the old bool the FIRST sub-probe's `finally` resumed polling for everything after it, so every later step had been measured against a contended serial read path. **Quick Print inherits with no restart** — `stamp_print_settings` is the ONE stamper both print paths call at build time, so it reads the freshly persisted tuning; NEW `refresh_speed_limits()` on Quick Print + a `workflows_mode` forwarder + `_stage_panel` added to `gui/app.py`'s fan-out (before this the calibration wrote the value everywhere EXCEPT the field that displays it). Persistence is per-setup: the tuning in the machine-scoped `print_timing_calibration.json`, the top speed in both `settings.json` keys and therefore the device profile. `control_panel._write_axis_max("xy")` — a second, weaker writer that moved the anchor while leaving the SMS denominator stale — now delegates. Measured budget **≈53 s typical / ≈98 s conservative**; grid 1.2 s (very coarse) → 10.2 s (very fine), every level passing 30 µm on a 2 mm star. Tests: NEW `test_v7212_one_click_xy_calibration.py` (**28**, incl. the end-to-end run and the Quick-Print-inherits proof); **6/6 mutations CAUGHT**, sources restored byte-identical. **🐞 A TEST-ISOLATION DEFECT I INTRODUCED AND CAUGHT:** routing `_write_axis_max` through `commit_top_speed` means a GUI edit writes the per-machine store — a process-wide singleton pointing at the REAL rig's file — so `test_v75x_axis_max_speed_inputs` wrote `xy_max_speed_um_s: 40000.0` into ME3B_01's calibration and three `test_v7211` tests then failed; the file was restored (the real dead-time/loop measurements untouched) and the class now takes `use_temp_store` in `setUp`. ⚠ **Shell heredocs collapse backslash escapes here** — `\n` inside Qt tooltips became real newlines and broke the file; edit escape-heavy source with the file tools. Regression **134 + 193 + 162 green** + a `gui.app` import smoke + offscreen builds of the REAL timing page and REAL challenge dialog; **10 pre-existing failures PROVED not ours in a `git worktree` at HEAD** (9 `_note_move_estimate_xy` in `xy_envelope_absolute` + 1 in `two_param_and_overlays`). Contracts deleted not renamed: `TestWorkerSweep` (the settle sweep is retired — measured dead time supersedes the legacy `by_phase`, whose ME3B_01 entries included two physically impossible negatives) and `test_apply_button_is_what_makes_it_authoritative`. **Needs HW verification on ME3B_01, IN ORDER:** set Max speed ≈6000 µm/s → **Run XY Calibration completes with NO second button, under 3 min** → the store gains `xy_max_speed_um_s` and Hardware Setup shows it **live, no restart** → Quick Print's *"Stage motion not characterised"* is **gone** → print the 2 mm star against the reported p95 and achievable speed → Very fine gives the same winner within a grid step → abort at each step ends at Safe Z with nothing persisted. |
| `MEBP_v721_CUSTOMISABLE_WORKFLOW_SECTIONS.md` | v7.21 **any ⚙ Settings section can be MOVED onto its workflow page, and every card in that column reorders ▲▼** (operator: *"on the quickprint workflow setup page I want to be able to take any section in the settings and move it into the setup workflow section area where the queue, readiness etc is, all of these sections should be able to be moved up and down to make a customizable workflow. in the settings page make a checkbox on all sections to move to main workflow page."*). Decisions (AskUserQuestion ×3): the arrangement is a **machine preference** (`settings.json`, NOT the profile — loading a colleague's print settings must change VALUES, never rearrange the page) · **everything in the column** reorders, built-ins included · **all seven** popout workflows get the checkbox. **🔴 THE FACT THE DESIGN RESTS ON: `WorkflowSettingsDialog` registers fields by WIDGET IDENTITY** (`_fields[key] = (widget, default)`), so promotion is a *reparent of one `Card`* and every field keeps saving/loading/resetting/inheriting with **zero re-registration** — which is also why the host must PLACE widgets and never BUILD them, the opposite of `CustomContextPanel`: rebuilding a settings section would mint new widgets and orphan every registered field, so `SectionStack` entries are **externally owned** and it never deletes one (AST-pinned that no `deleteLater` targets a caller's widget). NEW Qt-free `SupportClasses/WorkflowLayoutStore.py` (duck-typed on `Settings`; ⚠ never constructs one — that wipes the file) + NEW `gui/widgets/section_stack.py` (`SectionStack`, `PromotedSectionsPanel`, and ONE `wire_section_promotion` all seven pages call) + `Card.has_header()`/`add_header_widget()` (the header slot a titled card lacked; a headerless card **REFUSES** rather than dropping a control into the body where it reads as content). **🔴 A STORED ORDER IS A HINT, NEVER AN AUTHORITY** — `merge_order` always returns a PERMUTATION of what exists, so a dead id is dropped and a card added by a later version is appended instead of omitted; that one property makes it impossible for a stale/hand-edited order to **hide a card**, the failure hardest to diagnose from a screenshot. Section ids are **title slugs, not ordinals** (an ordinal shifts every stored promotion when a section is inserted above it; a renamed section instead quietly reverts to the popout — self-healing), and because slugging is many-to-one `add_section` **dedupes** rather than letting two sections fight over one checkbox. An un-promoted card returns to its original **NEIGHBOURS, not its original index** (siblings above it may themselves be promoted, so a stored index drifts) and always before `finalize()`'s trailing stretch. **🐞 THE BUG THAT WOULD HAVE READ AS "reordering doesn't persist":** `restore_promotions` fired the host's `on_change` per restored section, persisting the CURRENT (default) order over the stored one *before* it could be read — promotions survived a restart while the arrangement silently reset to default every launch; found by the end-to-end smoke, not a unit test, fixed with `notify=False` (mirroring the `persist=False` already there) and mutation-pinned. **Quick Print hosts DIRECTLY** (its column already scrolls; the five built-in cards are ordinary entries — a fixed block plus a reorderable tail would be two placement rules for one column and "move Readiness above Queue", the operator's own example, would be impossible), while **the other six get a bounded self-hiding drawer**: their columns do NOT scroll, so three tall promoted sections would push the run row — and therefore **Abort** — off screen with no scrollbar; the drawer is hidden with zero footprint while empty (pre-v7.21 geometry exactly), height-bounded with internal scrolling when not, and added **after** the run row so Start/Abort never move. An **embedded** Quick Print (the v7.20 calibrator) gets **no host** — moving a card onto someone else's page invisibly — the same reasoning that hides the v7.19 plate queue there. **⚠ THREE REDUNDANCIES DELETED BECAUSE MUTATIONS PROVED THEM DEAD** (each a second enforcement point for one fact, so mutating either alone was a NO-OP): a construction-time `setVisible(False)` beside `_sync_promote_checks` · an explicit `removeWidget` before handing the card over (Qt drops a reparented widget from its old layout itself — proved by the mutation changing nothing) · a `_promo_applying` re-entry guard that never fired because `set_section_promoted` is already idempotent. Tests: NEW `test_v721_workflow_layout_store.py` (**21**, no Qt, 1 ms) + `test_v721_section_promotion.py` (**49**, offscreen); **30/30 mutations CAUGHT**. ⚠ **One of my own tests was passing on a CORPSE and a mutation caught it:** `deleteLater` is DEFERRED, so a widget already scheduled for destruction still answers `objectName()` — the "take() doesn't delete the caller's widget" test survived the mutation that deletes it; both `take()` tests now flush the deferred-delete queue first, which is what gives them teeth. ⚠ The harness also needed a fix: a mutated run emitted non-cp1252 bytes and the subprocess decode raised, killing the matrix mid-run. Regression **411** (new suites + settings-popout + v7.19 queue + zones + v7.20 calibrator + hygiene) · **372** (all six integrated pages) · **178** (`Card`/components consumers) + a `gui.app` import smoke; **3 pre-existing failures PROVED not ours** — the cell-targeting golden file (its `push_depth` is **0.1 in the working tree vs 0.400052 committed**, an operator edit present in this session's FIRST `git status`, and the failing assertion is exactly a push_depth-derived volume) and `plate_builder_ui::TestLearnLoopSavesToADesign` ×2 (literally `{'max': None}`), both already named in this file. Plus end-to-end smokes through the **REAL** dialog/stack, the **REAL** Quick Print page, and **all seven pages built for real** (a stand-in that accepts a push proves nothing about the widget). **Needs GUI verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER:** nothing looks different on launch (go/no-go) → every settings section has an **"On page"** checkbox; tick one and it moves to the Setup column **with its values still round-tripping through Save/load** → ▲▼ reorder Queue/Readiness/the promoted card, edges greyed → **restart and BOTH the promotion and the order come back** → ↩ returns it **between its original neighbours** → **loading a different profile does NOT rearrange the page** → on **Stress Test** promote three sections and confirm the drawer sits below the run row, scrolls internally, and **Start/Abort never move**, then un-promote and it vanishes → the Print Calibrator's embedded Quick Print has **no** checkboxes and no drawer → spot-check the other five. |
| `MEBP_v720_PRINT_CALIBRATOR_PRIME_TIME.md` | v7.20 **a Print Calibrator workflow that MEASURES print settings on the machine instead of guessing them — starting with the prime time, and with a second line that prices the mid-print reposition** (operator: *"a workflow page that help the user tune the print settings. One important calibration is the prime time … the print should be a single line, and the printer should move at a set velocity from point A to B … the user selects point C which is the location where the print actually started. the distance from A to C divided by the velocity is the prime time needed"*, then *"add a second line just below to calibrate the settings for mid print move up and reposition settings"*). **Why:** `build_well_plate_job` already dispenses `flow × pump_prime_time_s` in the `MOVE_Z → PRINT_PATH` gap, but that global was a hand-typed 0.25 s that nothing measured, while the quantity depends on syringe compliance, needle bore/length and ink viscosity — too small and the first millimetres of every print are missing, too large and there is a blob. **The measurement:** the pump and XY commands start together but ink emerges only once the compliance is taken up, so the bead starts at C, not A, and `τ = prime_used_in_run + (A→C along the line) ÷ velocity`. **`prime_used` is IN the sum on purpose** — it makes one formula serve a *baseline* run (prime forced to 0 ⇒ τ is absolute) and a *verify* run (the residual is what is still MISSING, so `used + residual` CONVERGES instead of restarting), and it makes over-priming expressible (ink before A ⇒ negative `along` ⇒ the prime correctly REDUCES, clamped at 0; a negative `along` with no prime is physically impossible, so *there* it is a refusal — the click is wrong). **THE SECOND LINE — TWO DIFFERENT PRIMES, ONE KNOB** (operator: *"the first line for prime time is measuring the time needed to start the flow after the wash and clean steps, the seond line is measureing the time for the restart time after a pause in the pump"*). It prints just below the first as a SEPARATE object, so `build_well_plate_job` inserts its lift → reposition → lower → re-prime between them — and **the pump is STOPPED** for that, with `_print_pump_suckback("quick_move")` relieving the column. So **τ₁ is the COLD START** (after wash & clean: a relaxed column that may hold buffer or air ⇒ the LONG prime) and **τ₂ is the RESTART** (after a mere pause: still largely pressurised ⇒ the SHORT prime), i.e. **τ₁ ≥ τ₂ is the expected direction**. `build_well_plate_job` primes EVERY segment from the one value, so there is a real trade and the panel STATES it instead of leaving it to be discovered: at τ₁ every mid-print restart **over-primes** by τ₁−τ₂ (a small blob where each line resumes), at τ₂ the **first line under-primes** (a missing start) — so `required = max` is applied, because nothing-missing is the safe error, and the cost is named. ⚠ **τ₂ > τ₁ is the UNEXPECTED case and is flagged as such**: a restart needing more than a clean start means the PAUSE is losing more pressure than the wash left behind, which points at the quick-move pressure relief or at how long the pump is paused (the hop height/speeds), not at the prime — the button says *(the RESTART binds)* and the dialog says where to look. And because line 1 is DEFINED as "after wash & clean", **whether prep actually ran is part of what the number means**, so the provenance line states it either way and warns when prep was off. ⚠ **My first cut had this backwards** — it framed line 2 as "the hop costs EXTRA" and treated τ₂>τ₁ as normal, so the reporting would have read as a surprise in the ordinary case and as ordinary in the case worth investigating; renamed `HopCost`→`PrimeComparison` / `hop_cost`→`compare_primes` with `initial_s`/`restart_s`/`difference_s`/`restart_binds` and re-worded throughout. **DESIGN — the page HOSTS A LIVE `QuickPrintWorkflowPage`** (`embedded=True, owns_camera=False`), the repo's embedded-instance pattern (`full_print_workflow` wraps `PrintingModePage`; Spheroid hosts a live Fluorescence Mosaic), so needle prep, ink pickup, syringe-budget preflight, readiness, the print-floor confirm, the preposition→`PrintManager` launch, abort and post-print cleanup are **the same code** — no second print-launch implementation exists. NEW pure `SupportClasses/PrimeTimeCalibration.py` (stdlib + a duck-typed `PrintLog`; no Qt/numpy/cv2) owns the projection, the τ formula, five refusals (`DEGENERATE_LINE`/`NO_VELOCITY`/`OFF_LINE`/`BEFORE_START`/`BEYOND_END`, each because the alternative is a plausible-but-wrong number), `hop_cost`, and the log readers. **A and B are read back out of the run's own exec log** (`path_start.points` IS what the executor was handed), so a desync between "what printed" and "what is measured" is structurally impossible; the page's own arithmetic is only a pre-v7.7 fallback and SAYS so. **🔴 VELOCITY IS RESOLVED PER SEGMENT, and that is a hard requirement of the second line, not a refinement** — each `PRINT_PATH` restarts `s_mm` at 0, so a whole-log first-to-last span crosses the reset AND swallows the hop: on the suite's two-line fixture that reads **0.5 mm/s against a true 3.0** (6×, multiplying straight into every prime time), so `segments_from_log` windows each segment's samples to its own `[path_start.t, path_end.t]` and a test pins BOTH the right answer and that the whole-log helper is the one that gets it wrong. Priority is actual-position-first: `vel_sample` (measured) → `openvel_sample` (a commanded *schedule*, labelled as such) → the commanded speed; ⚠ `path_end.t − path_start.t` is deliberately NEVER used because `path_start` is logged BEFORE the move-to-`points[0]` and its 5 s settle, which would under-state the velocity and over-state the prime — the very blob this calibration removes. **🔴 THE FIX THAT MADE IT WORK AT ALL, found by design review and confirmed in source: applying the measurement would have changed NOTHING.** Quick Print's pre-flow spin was `sec.add(...)`, seeded from `_prime_default_s()` **once** at dialog-build time — when `_hw_config` is still None, so it took the 0.25 s fallback — then overwritten by the saved profile. So `CommonPrintSettings.set("pump_prime_time_s", τ)` reached every workflow that LINKS that key and silently missed Quick Print, the primary consumer; the calibration would have appeared to succeed and done nothing. Now `add_common(common_key="pump_prime_time_s", overridable=True)`, the convention the sibling `g_settle` row already uses (inherit force-sets the widget, so `_preflow_s()` already returns the effective value; `_migrate_common_links` promotes an existing customised profile value to an explicit override so saved profiles keep their prime). **Embedding hooks on Quick Print, each with a specific failure it prevents:** `owns_camera` is a SEPARATE flag from `embedded` because a `QTabWidget` hides the inactive tab and Qt delivers `showEvent` to a **child before its parent**, so an embedded instance would win the start race, claim the stop, and blank the host's live view mid-measurement — ownership is *declared, not won* (⚠ my first cut early-returned BEFORE `set_camera`, so the embedded feed would never have re-bound to the right slot; the bind is now unconditional and only start/stop is ceded, mutation-pinned); `settings_id` because `WorkflowSettingsDialog.save_last()` fires on the popout's `hideEvent` and Quick Print hides its popout on every `hideEvent`, so a shared `"quick_print"` id would silently overwrite the operator's real last-used values (including the v7.19 plate queue); `set_external_objects` takes a **LIST** so each line becomes its own segment (one merged path would extrude straight across the gap the second line exists to measure) with the entry re-ADDED and re-SELECTED by `_refresh_objects`, which runs on every `showEvent`; `set_prime_override_s` at the single choke point `_preflow_s`; `print_state_changed` emitted LAST in `_on_state` (so `_pm` is cleared and `_last_log_path` set) and documented **re-entrant**; and the v7.19 plate queue is **hidden** when embedded because `QueuedPrint.object_data` stores the combo userData as a *reference* (a host object would persist dangling), the per-well snapshot re-applies widget values on every plate click (overwriting the calibration line), and "Run all queued" would stamp a one-off calibration across the plate. **Safety:** all motion is existing primitives — the print is the embedded page's own preposition → `PrintManager`; the calibrator's only motion is "Go to line N" via `SafeTravelWorker(target_z_mm=None)` (retract, travel, **never lower**), gated on `cleanup_running()` because on a clean completion Quick Print immediately drives waste → wash → oil, so the live view is NOT still showing the line, and on `is_position_poller_suspended()` (⚠ a PROXY, not a lease). Markers use `set_bore_markers` (camera-centre-relative µm through `stage_offset_to_pixel`, the exact inverse of the click path) and NOT `set_reference_markers`, whose naive divide mis-places every marker on a rotated camera — mutation-pinned. **⭐ ROUND 2 — the line is DRAWN on the live view, never placed FROM it** (operator: *"we should not be setting the locations of the line start and end, those should just be overlays on the live view that live at some xy location, and the print path line between the calibation line should overlay on the live view as well, as we move the live view around the overlay matches to what should be there"*). The first cut had a **⌖ Set line start from view** button whose `_place_line_start` back-solved a click into the Offset X/Y spins; it is GONE, because a location may flow **spins → view** (computed, then drawn) and never **view → spins** (which makes the printed line and the on-screen line two objects with no authority). A click can now do exactly ONE thing — record where the ink started — and the retired arm string is itself mutation-pinned so re-adding the affordance fails a test. **And the line itself is now drawn, not just its endpoints:** NEW `CameraFeedView.set_stage_paths(paths)` strokes polylines in the **SAME camera-centre-relative µm frame as `set_bore_markers`** (deliberately one frame — the path, the A/B dots at its ends and a click on it must be registered with each other; a second convention is a sign error waiting to happen), pushed against the **live** stage position and re-pushed on the ~300 ms tick, so jogging slides the overlay across the frame and it stays on the glass. Two overlays answering different questions: **DASHED** (`overlay1`) = where the NEXT run will print — this is what REPLACES clicking to place the line, and it is also how the operator confirms `_step_offset_for_next_run` stepped onto clean glass; **SOLID** (`teal`) = the stroke the last run COMMANDED, read back out of its own exec log. That is not cosmetic: **the drawn stroke landing on the real bead IS the check that the camera↔stage frame is right**, and a stroke parallel-but-offset or rotated away from the bead is a calibration fault (the same projection converts the C click), not an overlay bug. ⚠ **No bounding-box cull, deliberately** — the bore dots skip an off-frame marker, which is right for a POINT and wrong for a LINE, whose most useful case is both endpoints outside the view and the middle crossing it; a clip rect does the work (and bounds a long line at a small µm/px), pinned by a render test with both ends 5000 px outside a 640×480 frame plus a guard-the-guard that a fully off-frame path paints nothing. ⚠ Paths draw **under** the dots (they label the ends) — mutation-pinned, because draw order is what a later tidy-up breaks. ⚠ **One of my own mutations was a NO-OP and proved nothing until the production code was fixed:** dropping `pen.setStyle(DashLine)` left the line dashed anyway because `setDashPattern` *implicitly* sets `CustomDashLine` — two enforcement points for one fact is what made it harmless, so the redundant `setStyle` was deleted and the mutation re-aimed. ⚠ Render tests go through `_render_frame` (the real paint chain), NOT `_draw_stage_paths` directly, or "the overlay is never wired into the paint chain" would be invisible — a mutation proved exactly that; and the naive-divide mutation needs a **rotated+mirrored** fake manager sampled at a **quarter** point (these endpoints are symmetric about the frame centre, so both maps agree at the midpoint), with an up-front assertion that the two predictions differ by >30 px so it cannot pass vacuously. `COLORS` carries neither `overlay1` nor `teal` (both Catppuccin Mocha; two existing sites already use this same literal fallback), so the shared palette was left untouched. Round-2 tests: **17 added (145 total)**; **13/13 further mutations CAUGHT** (no path pushed · planned line not drawn · **stage sign flipped so the overlay rides the camera** · planned drawn solid · only line 1 drawn · **a click can move the line again** · change-gate removed · 1-point path kept · **the per-point cull reinstated** · unwired from the paint chain · the naive divide · the dash pattern · paths over the dots). Regression **616 green** across bore-dot-overlay/live-view-vs-measured/square-crop/mount-square-up/camera-rotation-cal/bore-wizard/gui-watchdog + the v7.19 queue/zones/hygiene, plus an offscreen end-to-end smoke through the **REAL** page and **REAL** `CameraFeedView` (a `MagicMock` feed proves nothing about the widget): both planned lines dashed, both printed strokes solid with `A1/B1/C1/A2/B2`, a +1 mm X jog moving **every** overlay point exactly −1 mm in frame, and 320 teal pixels reaching the rendered pixmap. The line is **clamped** to `well_radius − needle_radius − 0.25 mm` and the clamp is always STATED (shortening can make the line fail to contain the ink start, so a silent clamp is a silently different measurement), while an offset that puts the line outside the well is **refused** and pushes no object, letting the embedded page's own readiness block Print and name the reason. **Disclosed, not solved:** prime is not the only pump take-up (the pickup's compliance compensation, `pump_relief_uL`, the dead-volume reserve), so a τ measured with one preamble is not transferable — the provenance line and Apply dialog name the run's pump, needle, ink, velocity+source, flow, prime-used and hop settings (read from the LOG, not the current widgets) and say to re-measure. Tests: NEW `test_v720_print_calibrator.py` (**128**, all green); **33/33 mutations CAUGHT** (incl. the prime override ignored, `preflow` back to a plain field = the Apply-does-nothing bug, the per-segment velocity window removed, `required` taking one line instead of the max, the unexpected direction unflagged, the second line not offset so it prints on line 1, the step no longer clearing the pair, and the prep state unreported); **🐞 one of my own tests HUNG instead of failing and a mutation caught it** — `test_apply_does_nothing_on_a_refusal` called `_on_apply()` without patching `QMessageBox`, so with the guard mutated away it opened a REAL modal and blocked forever offscreen (this repo's documented trap); it now patches the modal and asserts it was NEVER called. ⚠ **The mutation harness itself needed two fixes before it proved anything:** stale hardcoded anchors (it now VERIFIES every anchor against the live source and refuses to run otherwise) and **CRLF vs LF** — `quick_print_workflow.py` is CRLF while the new files are LF, so multi-line anchors written with `\n` matched nothing against raw decoded bytes and three mutations silently SKIPPED. **4 pre-existing failures PROVED not ours in a `git worktree` at committed HEAD** (never `git stash` here): `_speed_pct_spin` (retired by v7.6) · stale `_readiness` · a `_Page` that overrides `__init__` without super · "Stage motion not characterised" — all four reproduce with neither this change nor the v7.19 queue present. ⚠⚠ **PROCESS HAZARD, DISCLOSED: something on the machine was concurrently SAVING OVER `quick_print_workflow.py` during this work** — the file changed under me repeatedly and a `hop_settings()` accessor was reverted **three times**. Every other edit survived and is verified present; the accessor was dropped in favour of `hop_settings_from_log`, which needs no change to the contested file and is the better source anyway (it reports what the run USED, not what a spin says now). **Anyone continuing here should re-verify the v7.20 markers in that file before editing it.** **Needs GUI/HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER:** the embedded surface usable with no duplicate Back button / no queue card and the object locked to **⟂ Calibration lines ×2** → two parallel lines drawn in the well, the clamp message on a small well, the second line dropped WITH a warning at a large gap → baseline run with the prime `DISPENSE` absent and **two `path_start` events with a `MOVE_XY {hop_z}` between them** → line 1 (cold start) starts late and **line 2 (restart) starts SOONER**; mark both; **the two reported velocities should be nearly equal** (a wildly different line-2 velocity means the per-segment windowing is wrong), and the provenance must say prep **RAN** → off-line refuses that line while the other keeps its result → Apply names `max(τ₁,τ₂)` — normally τ₁ — with the dialog stating the restart over-prime, and **Hardware Setup → Pump AND the calibrator's own Pre-flow both show it** (if not, the link is broken and the calibration is inert) → **if the button says *the RESTART binds*, shorten the pause (halve the hop height / raise the hop speeds) or check the quick-move relief, then confirm τ₂ drops below τ₁** → verify run starts both lines at A and converges → over-prime deliberately and confirm *over-primed; reduce* → **the round-2 overlay, which doubles as the rotated-camera check: BEFORE printing, a DASHED line shows where the next run will go — nudge Offset X/Y and watch it slide over the glass, and confirm there is NO way to click the view and move it; after a run the SOLID stroke must lie ON the actual bead with A1/B1/A2/B2/C at its ends; then jog well away and back and the overlay must stay stuck to the glass rather than riding with the camera** (parallel-but-offset or rotated = re-run the objective µm/px + orientation calibration before trusting any prime time measured through it) → go-to disabled until cleanup finishes, then travels **without lowering the needle**. |
| `MEBP_v719_QUICK_PRINT_PLATE_QUEUE.md` | v7.19 **Quick Print gains a plate-wide queue of INDEPENDENT prints, per-well toolpath glyphs, and a Setup-zone print preview** (operator: *"in the setup page I want the section with the well plate selector to have the print preview above the well … I want to be able to select in multiple prints in queue. An overlay of the print should be drawn into the well selected … clicking on any well populates that print into the active view for editing … a button for run all prints or run active print … each print is still treated as an independent activity."*). Decisions (AskUserQuestion ×7): **both** a large ACTIVE-print preview above the plate AND small glyphs in every queued well · a **full snapshot** per well · **prep once / ink per print / cleanup once**, swap only on an ink change · select wells (**drag-click**) then **"Apply print to wells"** bottom-right of the preview · **plate order** default with a **Group by ink** toggle · an unrunnable entry is **skipped and named** · **hold between wells** so the operator can go run a fluorescence mosaic. NEW Qt-free `SupportClasses/PrintQueue.py` (`QueuedPrint`, `order_queue`, `count_ink_swaps`, tolerant (de)serialization). **🔴 THREE DECISIONS THAT CARRY THE WEIGHT. (1) Store INPUTS, never derived outputs** — no `segments`, no `PrintSettings`, and above all no ZERO-REF Z: between queueing and running, Plate Location / a needle swap / a plate-bottom re-teach all move the frame, so a stored zref Z would print at a **stale absolute height with nothing on screen saying so**; heights are kept *above plate bottom* and re-resolved every run (which also keeps a snapshot JSON-serializable and makes a deleted print file detectable). **(2) `ink_map_by_name`, not `dict[int, str]`** — JSON round-trips int keys as STRINGS, *and* `_rebuild_ink_mapping_ui` hard-resets `self._ink_map = {}` then re-derives it from the name-keyed `_ink_map_last`, so a snapshot assigning `_ink_map` would be **wiped by the very refresh that follows applying it**. **(3) NO modal dialog while a transient snapshot is applied** — resolving an off-screen snapshot means transiently driving the widgets, which is safe only while nothing re-enters the Qt event loop; `_confirm_print_floor` calls `QMessageBox.warning`, and the ~300 ms status tick would then repaint the preview from the TRANSIENT values, so the floor is evaluated **numerically** per unit and ONE dialog is shown after everything is restored (better UX *and* it removes the hazard). Two more traps in that block: with signals blocked the derived caches do **not** refresh (the risk is the side effect NOT happening — `_loaded_sketch` would still hold the previous object's sketch), and **`setValue` clamps silently** (`_update_top_speed_cap` sets a dynamic maximum, so a print queued at 6 mm/s on a machine since measured at 4.2 would quietly run slower) — every numeric set is compared back and **disclosed**. Restore IS an apply of the snapshot captured on the way in, so the save- and restore-lists cannot drift. **🔴 The sortable atom is the queued print, NEVER the unit** — `_ink_groups()` returns sketch-shape order (support before structure), so globally regrouping units by ink would silently reorder a multi-ink print and print it wrong; a multi-ink entry's run is ATOMIC. **🔴 ONE shared abort predicate** — `_run_group_job_blocking` reads the sticky flag directly to close the documented `pm.start()` race, so a queue with its own flag would have left that guard **dead for the queue** and a unit would print after the operator aborted; both engines now use `_sequence_abort_requested()`. **HOLD holds no hardware** (verified: `safe_travel_to` resumes the poller in its own `finally`, the poller/watchdog suspends are scoped to PRINT_PATH, no `PrintManager` is live between units) so the mosaic detour is real; the reverse hazard — Resume while another workflow drives the stage — is guarded by NEW `StageController.is_position_poller_suspended()`, ⚠ **a PROXY, not a lease** (catches a scan or print, not a manual jog; `_stage_busy()` is per-PAGE and there is no cross-page registry — a real stage lease is out of scope). **Refusals stated, not guessed:** `ink_name == ""` is refused by Run all (after a swap the needle is washed out, so "print with whatever is loaded" is a continuity claim the engine already invalidated) unless the whole queue is `""` with prep+cleanup off; same-ink adjacency correctly skips the swap but drifts the plunger monotonically, so NEW `_check_queue_syringe_budget` sums the WHOLE queue through the existing `simulate_pump_budget` and always states one of three verdicts — **fits** (naming that wash/prep volumes are NOT modelled) · **won't fit** (blocks, remedies named) · **NOT CHECKED** (uncalibrated plunger; proceeds, operator informed) — rather than skipping it silently the way the multi-ink path does; and an uncalibrated well is refused rather than run against `_well_center_zero_ref_mm`'s geometric fallback (two deliberate enforcement points, the skip pre-check owning the plate FLAG and expand's guard owning the geometry, **each independently pinned** so neither becomes dead code). **PERFORMANCE:** 384 wells × `generate_object_trajectory` on a 120 ms debounce is not viable, so glyph geometry is cached by **CONFIG identity** (object+size+resolution), not by well — a real queue is "the same print in forty wells", so it holds ONE entry (measured 1 miss + 7 hits for 8 wells) — and polylines are decimated to ≤64 points for **display only**. **UI:** a SECOND `PrintTrajectoryMonitorView` (not `WellPreviewWidget`, which would add a second coordinate convention — well-relative mm, **Y up** — and force a second copy of the well-centre→zero-ref conversion that owns `plate_axis_sign()`); the **live trace stays on `_traj_view` only** (mid-queue the Setup preview shows the ACTIVE print, which need not be the well printing, so a needle dot there is FALSE); Apply is a footer ROW not a floating overlay (the monitor paints its prediction caption along the bottom — the very text you need before stamping 40 wells); **Apply is NOT gated on readiness** (queueing offline is the point) while **Run all IS**, on `blocking()` minus `{object, well}` (those describe the ACTIVE print, which a batch supplies per entry); glyphs use **NO Y flip** (plate-local is Y-down, matching `PlateThumbnail` not `PrintThumbnail`), are **clipped not scaled** (an oversized print is a real design problem the operator should SEE), and degrade to a dot below `_GLYPH_MIN_R_PX` — ⚠ the red spill ring is a RENDERING fact only, since `_well_radius` is floored and can exceed the true radius; multi-select is **opt-in, default OFF** (it changes what a click MEANS, and the fluorescence page selects exactly one well) using brush-drag not a rubber band (a 10 px overshoot on a 384 plate grabs a whole row); the plate is the **single selection surface** with the queue list a `NoSelection` readout keyed by well NAME; and write-back is **ONE debounced write-only sink** (four existing seams would have become five) **flushed** on well switch so a pending 120 ms debounce cannot land in the wrong entry. **🐞 TWO PRE-EXISTING BUGS FIXED (both verified): `_run_group_job_blocking` discarded `pm.exec_logger.path`**, so after a multi-ink run "Open log" opened the **previous single print's** file; and **`_ideal_path_mm` reads the live widgets + `_selected_well`**, so a batch report would have scored the last unit against whatever object was on screen in whatever well was selected — a silently wrong accuracy number in a report whose whole purpose is credible accuracy (`_load_report` gained optional `ideal_pts=`/`context=`, defaults byte-identical). Also closed: `_on_print` had no `_cleanup_thread` guard. Tests: NEW `test_v719_print_queue_model.py` (**35**, no Qt, 1 ms) + `test_v719_quick_print_queue.py` (**91**, offscreen); **25/25 mutations CAUGHT**, zero residue. ⚠ **FIVE of my own tests were too weak and the mutation run caught them first:** the clip test used a DIAGONAL line that misses the neighbour's sampled row by a whole well pitch (clip removal SURVIVED); `_render` returned a `QImage` sharing a freed local `QPixmap` buffer, so two "renders" compared freed memory; every write-back test called `_flush_write_back()` **directly** so they all passed with the debounce timer unwired (the exact v7.18 setpoint-keeper weakness — fixed with an AST pin **plus** a timer-only behavioural test); the `selection_changed` gate was unpinned because the drag path has its own already-touched guard; and both hold tests passed **vacuously** because the fakes complete instantly, so the batch finished before the hold landed (replaced with a `_GatedPM` that blocks inside unit 1, now asserting the worker parks BEFORE unit 2, retracts, stays alive, and that Resume finishes all three). Two of my **mutations** were also no-ops and proved nothing until fixed. Regression **315 green** (new suites + zones + jog-navigation + custom-plate-rendering + fluorescence-mosaic) + 182 + 355 + a `gui.app` smoke; **6 pre-existing failures PROVED not ours in a `git worktree` at HEAD** (never `git stash` here): `_speed_pct_spin` (retired by v7.6) · stale `_readiness` · "Stage motion not characterised" · the operator's re-saved cell-targeting golden file · `plate_builder_ui::TestLearnLoopSavesToADesign` ×2. ⚠ **Disclosed:** the tree also carries substantial concurrent v7.19 fluorescence work from another session; nothing here touches those files, and the harness's byte-identity check reported a mismatch only because that session edited them mid-run (verified by grepping every mutation string — zero residue). **Needs GUI/HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER:** an EMPTY queue prints exactly as today (go/no-go) → two wells with different objects, glyphs matching and each well reloading its snapshot → drag-select + Apply → **Run all one ink: one prep, NO swap between wells, one cleanup, safe Z between** → two inks, swap count matching the dialog and Group-by-ink reducing it → **Hold → go run a mosaic → Resume finishes** → Resume refused while a scan runs → abort-while-held ends at safe Z → a deleted print file is flagged and skipped BY NAME while the rest run → an oversized print rings red → restart restores the queue → **Open log opens THIS run's log and the report's numbers match the last unit's own geometry** (the two bug fixes). |
| `MEBP_v7181_XY_FAST_CONNECT.md` | v7.18.1 **every XY connect re-scanned every protocol × baud × port — 6.8 s on the GUI thread, every time** (operator: *"every connection of the xy stage does this … we used to have it so it was super fast at connecting"*, with a `GuiWatchdog` 7 s stall + `Failed to parse XY position: Expected 3 values, got 1: D` on each connect). **Root-caused from the operator's own `logs/app.log`, not inferred:** `controller_json` is `"auto"`, so `_auto_detect_controller` walks every protocol JSON × every candidate baud × every port on EVERY connect — and only ONE candidate port exists (COM4 is correctly excluded as the ZP board), so the whole 6.76 s re-proves five combinations already ruled out on the previous run: Ludl@9600 → ProScan II@38400 → ProScan III@38400 → H117@115200 → H117@38400 → **H117@9600 MATCH**. Per probe the cost is structural (0.35 s DTR-toggle + settle, 0.3–0.6 s wake, up to two 0.3 s reads). **The ZP stage has had the cure since v7.4.2 (`preferred_port`); XY never got the equivalent** — `XYStageManager` had no port parameter at all, and `controller.auto_detect_result` exists in settings but is cosmetic (read only by the Settings page). Fixed with the XY twin: a persisted `xy_stage.last_good` = **`{protocol, port, baud}`** (port alone would still leave the sweep — 5 of the 6 wasted probes were the SAME port) probed FIRST via new `_try_preferred` → one open, ~0.8 s, no watchdog trip. **A miss ALWAYS falls back to the full scan** (moved cable, swapped controller, Prior's documented baud reversion, deleted protocol JSON — each pinned): the hint can make a connect faster, never fail one; and even on a full scan the hinted port is **ordered first**, so a stale BAUD still saves every other port's probe time. **Nothing is persisted unless something actually answered** (`detection_hint` returns None without a `_connected_port`) — the case that matters is an EXPLICIT `controller_json`, where the protocol is loaded *before* detection so a protocol-is-None check alone would write `{port: None}` and send the next launch at nothing. The hint deliberately **survives `disconnect_xy`** (unlike ZP's, cleared because Marlin re-enumerates after a power cycle; the Prior is a stable FTDI assignment and a miss is cheap), and `StageController` adopts the winning triple immediately after connect so an in-session reconnect is fast before the GUI persists anything. 🐞 **Second defect, the `got 1: D` warning:** detection matches the FIRST line of the H117's multi-line `STAGE` reply and returns at once, so the rest is still arriving at 9600 baud — `get_current_position`'s `reset_input_buffer()` flushes, THEN the trailing bytes land, and the first position read parses a fragment; new `_drain_quiet` (read until quiet, then flush) runs from new `_claim_port`, the ONE place a detection match becomes the session. Tests: NEW `test_v7181_xy_fast_connect.py` (**21**, driving the production manager against a fake serial injected at the pyserial boundary so the real detection loop runs); the load-bearing one asserts **exactly one port open** with a good hint, guarded by a companion test asserting the un-hinted sweep opens ≥5 so it cannot pass because nothing probes at all; **6/6 mutations CAUGHT** (fast path removed = the original bug · drain removed = the `D` warning · a miss made fatal · a hint published with nothing found · `preferred=` not passed (AST pin) · the excluded-port guard defeated). ⚠ **TWO of my own tests were too weak and the mutation run caught them first:** the excluded-port mutation SURVIVED because I had added a *redundant* second guard in `_try_preferred` while the real one in `_find_with_protocol` still blocked the open — a partial mutation proves nothing, so the redundant copy was deleted (opening a port DTR-resets the board behind it: ONE enforcement point, not a copy per caller); and the `detection_hint` mutation SURVIVED because my "nothing found" test used auto-detect, where `_protocol` is None anyway, so the `_connected_port` check was never exercised. Regression **108 green** (jog-navigation/context-panel/pump-plunger/axis-max-speed/common-axis-speed/prior-multi-baud) + a `gui.app` import smoke; pre-existing failures PROVED not ours in a **`git worktree` at HEAD** (never `git stash` in this repo): 10 `_note_move_estimate_xy_rel` errors in the ludl/envelope/speed suites and the documented ZP trio. **Deliberately NOT done:** backgrounding the connect (it IS still on the GUI thread — a real second defect, but at ~0.8 s it is below the 5 s watchdog, and it touches the badge/state machine on both connect surfaces, so it is its own change), and pinning `controller.controller_json` to the detected protocol (it would also skip the sweep, but it silently changes what the Settings page shows selected and stops auto-detect ever reconsidering — the hint is advisory and self-healing, a pinned protocol is not). **Needs HW verification on ME3B_01:** first connect is still slow (nothing cached) → `settings.json` gains `xy_stage.last_good` → **Disconnect/Connect logs `XY fast-connect: … in 0.8s (skipped the scan)` with no GuiWatchdog stall** → restart, still fast → no more `got 1: D` → move the Prior to another USB port and confirm it self-heals via `XY fast-connect miss … falling back to the full scan` → COM4 is never opened by the XY scan. |

Honesty details: a failed AUTO attempt never raises a banner (a banner per 3 s retry is spam; the hint label names the real connect — the ZP one), a stale SIMULATOR session is auto-retired on a transport switch (the one transport with no real heaters), and a shared session is never torn down automatically (disconnecting would not stop the firmware's hold, only blind us to it); the Connect-card tests now PIN the row's absence and that the card's tick never even touches the incubator service. **THE TAB** (`gui/pages/hardware/incubator_panel.py`, registered after Microscope → the `test_v712_plate_builder_ui` tab count bumped 10→11): transport + dedicated-port/baud/sim-scale, per-zone enable/display-name/preset, **setpoint ceiling that can only LOWER the code-level 50 °C** (clamped in the store on load AND set — a config file must never raise it), watchdog-safe ramp-step cap, dither period, log-on-connect, close-prompt default; the microscope-panel contract (`load()`/`commit()`, ONE `store.save()`, nothing written before Save, injectable store/controller) with commit also pushing the ceiling onto a LIVE controller (safety-relevant — must not wait for a reconnect). **app.py closeEvent**: when a zone is actually heating, ask before closing (the firmware holds its setpoint regardless — "leave running for a long soak" is legitimate, so the ask is honest), then `shutdown_incubator()` BEFORE `controller.shutdown()` (in shared mode the heater-off commands ride the ZP link the controller is about to close). ⚠ **Post-review fix:** `showEvent` re-applied store prefs including the preset seed, silently overwriting the operator's typed setpoint on every navigation back to the page — presets now seed only while idle (pinned). The vendored backend (`zones/marlin_gcode/sensors/safety/stability/ramp/probe/marlin_link/fake_marlin/telemetry/calibration/device_config`) stays essentially verbatim — it carries the tool's hardware-verified reasoning (the 0-127 duty scale, the watchdog-arm threshold maths, the probe-below-current-temp trick); only store paths (→ `config/hardware/incubator_calibration.json` + `logs/incubator/`, env overrides resolved at CONSTRUCTION for test isolation) and the exclusion/transport plumbing changed. **⭐⭐ ROUND 3 — BENCH FAULT: *"the heater is not getting hot, ensure you are using the right port"* + *"should be the heat bed as the heater"*, and a *"section to the custom jog panel for an incubator readout"*.** The port was right; the software was silent about the real problem, and its OWN LOG had the answer all along: `logs/zp_serial.log` shows **`M104 S37 → error`, repeated every ~minute from 16:59 to 17:47** while `M105` returned `ok` throughout — the link was healthy, the firmware ANSWERED, and the answer was *no*. **`M104` is the HOTEND command (Zone B, output HE0); the heater on this rig is the heat BED (HB, `M140`, Zone A)** — so the operator was driving the zone with no heater behind it, and would have been told in one second if the software had looked at the reply. **THREE DEFECTS, all fixed. (1) THE ANSWER WAS NEVER READ:** every heater command went through the bare `_send`, whose `Transaction` — carrying `ok`, `rejected` and the board's own `Error:` text — was DISCARDED at all four call sites (`set_target`, `_ramp_command`, `_service_dither`, `heater_off`), so a refused command produced no status, no log, no card state. NEW `_send_zone_cmd(zone_id, cmd)` checks it: an answered "no" latches `ZoneRuntime.refused` (command → the board's reply), logs WARNING, writes a `command_refused` telemetry event and banners ONCE not per retry. **Only `txn.rejected` latches** — a timeout/reset is a LINK problem the stale-data warning and reset detection already own, so latching there would file the diagnosis under the wrong heading. **(2) THE HOST KEPT RE-ASSERTING IT** — the dither and ramp re-command every period, so the refusal repeated ~48× with nothing accumulating anywhere; a latched refusal now STOPS the dither and the ramp (pinned by a test that also forces the next flip and asserts no further send), because *retrying a verdict every minute forever is exactly how it stayed invisible*. **(3) A REFUSED `heater_off` MATTERS MOST** and goes through the same path — an operator who presses OFF and is refused believes a live heater is cold; any fresh action (set/ramp/fine/off) clears the latch so the verdict always describes the current attempt. **The card now names the port it drives** (HB/THB · HE0/THO header tooltip + `heater_connector` inside the refusal text) because *"is my heater on the right port"* was the actual question, so the answer belongs in the failure message, not a doc; a REFUSED pill + a persistent red card line replace the transient status text that let this hide. **This rig's `config/hardware/incubator.json` disables Zone B** (no heater on HE0, open-circuit thermistor) so no phantom zone is offered, and that file + `incubator_calibration.json` are now **gitignored** — which zone has a heater is wiring, i.e. per-rig (the CAMERA_CAL_PERSIST_STORE rule). ⚠ **Deliberately NOT done:** guessing that a refused `M104` means "use `M140`" — only the operator knows which connector the heater is in; the software's job is to say *the board refused this, this zone is not heating, and this is the port it drives*. **THE JOG-PANEL READOUT** (`gui/widgets/context_sections.py`, 🌡️ registered section — the illumination/microscope drop-in pattern): per zone `24.6 → 37 °C · 42%` plus a state word (heating/cooling/at target/off/stale/SENSOR/**REFUSED**). A **READOUT, not a control** — setpoints keep ONE writer (the Incubator page), the `set_filter` lesson — and **peek-only**: `peek_incubator()`, never `get_incubator()`/`connect_from_store()`, so session policy stays in one place and a jog panel can never start a heater session as a side effect of being visible (mutation-pinned); with no session it points at Workflows → Incubator instead of rendering a dead card, hides a store-disabled zone's row, throttles its render to 1 Hz (the controller samples at 1 Hz; the ~300 ms tick would be repaint noise) and swallows its own exceptions so a readout can never take the status tick down. **⭐⭐⭐ ROUND 4 — BENCH SESSION 2026-08-13: THE HARDWARE WAS NEVER THE PROBLEM, AND THE HOLD HAD NO KEEPER** (operator: *"the heater is still not heating up, i have plugged the heater film directly into the heat bed connection and it doesnt work"* + *"the thermistor is attached directly to the heater"* + *"i want you to run this, see the output in the char and make decisions"*). NEW **`tools_incubator_heater_diagnostic.py`** settles what the GUI cannot: Marlin reports its OWN bed PWM in every M105 (`B@:`, 0-127), which **splits the fault in half** — duty > 0 with a flat temperature is electrical (VIN/element/crimp/MOSFET); duty stuck at 0 is upstream (target not applied, bed disabled, latched fault). Plus every port with its VID:PID, M115, M503, and **M122 as an indirect VIN check** (the TMC2209s run off VIN, not USB, so a board with no supply answers M105 cheerfully while its drivers report nothing). Never moves an axis, caps the target, aborts above 45 °C, `M140 S0` in a `finally` incl. Ctrl-C. **MEASURED ON ME3B V1 (COM4): `M304 P41.78 I7.32 D158.93` present, M122 answered with full per-driver registers (VIN present), then `M140 S31` → duty 104/127 at t=3 s and 21.9 → 33.9 °C in TEN SECONDS (~72 °C/min); then `M140 S37` for three minutes → 37 °C in ~7 s, held 36.7-38.0 °C (±0.65) at 8-24 % duty, no thermal halt, no USB drop, every poll answered.** So film, wiring, MOSFET, VIN and even the stock Ender-3 bed PID are all FINE — and **my own round-3 theory that the runaway watchdog was killing the board was WRONG**, disproved by the very soak meant to reproduce it. **THE ACTUAL DEFECT, found by tracing `ramp.py` against this fast heater:** the ramp is sound (24 → 28 → 31 → 34 → 37, done in ~10 s) but it **finishes the moment it commands the final target** — its own comment says *"from this point the firmware owns the hold"*, true right up until the firmware **FORGETS**. A Marlin reset clears every heater target to 0, and **this board resets routinely: opening its serial port pulses DTR, so every ZP auto-reconnect reboots it** (the operator's app.log shows three reconnects in four minutes and literally says *"ZP auto-reconnect succeeded — re-declare the Z position (Marlin reset on reopen)"*). **The Z position is re-declared after that reset; the heater target was not, by anyone** — so the page showed a 37 °C hold while the board sat at target 0 and nothing heated. NEW `_service_setpoint_keeper()` on the 1 Hz sample loop compares the board's OWN reported target (M105 carries it) against `commanded_c` and puts it back. Four properties: **two disagreeing samples, never one** (a sample taken between issuing a command and the board applying it legitimately lags, so acting on one would put a command on the wire at every setpoint change); **a stale reading can never trigger it** (judging from a reading the board never sent would re-command every poll while the link is down); **a REFUSED zone is never re-asserted** (the round-3 rule outranks the keeper — a refusal is a verdict, not a target to retry); and the **count is surfaced on the zone card** (*"board forgot the setpoint x3, restored"*) with the status spoken only the first three times, because a reset-looping board is a fault in its own right yet must not bury every other message. ⚠ **MY OWN TESTS WERE TOO WEAK AND A MUTATION CAUGHT IT:** every keeper test called `_service_setpoint_keeper()` DIRECTLY, so all of them passed with the call REMOVED from `_publish_sample` — i.e. they would have passed with the exact fault still shipping; fixed with an AST pin on the call site **plus** a behavioural test that lets the live sampler do it (both catch the mutation). ⚠ **The diagnostic's first draft died with a `UnicodeEncodeError`** on a decorative emoji because this console is cp1252 — a bench tool that crashes when it is needed is worthless, so its output is pure ASCII (the trap already recorded for `tools_install_tucsen_sdk.ps1`). **Also corrected: `FIRMWARE_NOTES.md` says the board is COM6; it is COM4** — COM6 is the FTDI Prior XY (`0403:6001`), the board is the STM32 native-USB CDC (`0483:5740`); the app probes, so this misled only humans. `log_on_connect` now **true** in this rig's config so the next failure leaves a temperature record — its absence is why round 3 had to reason from serial traces alone. Tests: **107 green across 3 new files** (`test_v718_incubator_backend` 44 · `test_v718_incubator_shared_link` 31 — incl. the load-bearing `test_detect_never_opens_an_excluded_port` (a fake serial records every open; the excluded port must have ZERO, because the open IS the DTR reset) and `test_m105_data_would_be_LOST_without_the_terminal_line` · `test_v718_incubator_gui` 32, offscreen, modals patched — they block forever offscreen — incl. **the status tick itself starting the session**, the wiring that replaces the connect button); **15/15 mutations CAUGHT** (ZP scan exclusion removed · ok-line data dropped · identity-narrowing removed = the M115-reset bug · autotune refusal removed · poll gate ignored · timeout clamp removed · M190 degrade removed · excluded-port connect allowed · tick no longer starts the session · simulator auto-retire removed · **the dither back on the unchecked sender = the 48-minute refusal bug** · **the jog readout constructing the service instead of peeking** · **the keeper unwired from the sample loop = the forgotten-setpoint bug, caught by BOTH the AST pin and the behavioural test** · **the keeper's two-sample debounce removed** · **a REFUSED zone re-asserted**). Regression **~516 green run per batch** (zp-serial/close-crash/watchdog/jog-clamp/position-override/hygiene 58 · hard-abort/ludl/dtr/reconnect 76 · plate-builder-ui/phase0 135 · nikon-ti 101 · full-print+stress workflows 41 · timing+fluorescence 57 · illumination/per-pump 48) + a `gui.app` import smoke and an offscreen HardwareSetupPage / page-connect walkthrough; **5 pre-existing failures PROVED not ours by path-scoped stash** (`zp_dtr_no_reset::TestOpenStillConnects` ×2, `zp_auto_reconnect::test_no_z_max_keeps_defaults` — all three already documented in this file — and `plate_builder_ui::TestLearnLoopSavesToADesign` ×2, the plate-type `max` z-offset from other working-tree WIP). ⚠ **Disclosed:** the adversarial-review WORKFLOW could not run (all six reviewer agents died on a session usage limit — an empty findings list from errored agents is NOT a clean bill); the review was done inline instead, and the mutation matrix + tests back the quality claim. Shared-mode fault-text limitation disclosed in the plan (after a Marlin `kill()` the error line is usually eaten by the poller's M114, so the incubator may report only timeouts + the halted-board stale warning — the firmware's own protection is unaffected). **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER — shared transport first, nothing else is trusted until it passes:** connect ZP → open Workflows → Incubator and it **joins the board BY ITSELF within ~3 s (no connect click anywhere — the round-2 design; also open the page BEFORE ZP and confirm the hint points at the ZP connect, then the page joins once ZP comes up)** → the Firmware tab fills with **no "ZP board RESET detected" in app.log** (the M115 fix; a false reset would also poison the ZP position-restore prompt) → temps at ~2 s while jogging Z + a pump (lock interleaving) → Zone A to 37 °C **via Ramp**, watching for a stock-watchdog false halt (FIRMWARE_NOTES — still the open risk; Zone B stays correctly blocked on its open-circuit thermistor) → run a small print mid-hold: the sensor table may read stale during PRINT_PATH (polling yields BY DESIGN, no board-gone-quiet warning) and recovers, print pacing unchanged → heaters off / ZP-disconnect messaging + rejoin on ZP reconnect → a ceiling lowered on the tab clamps the page immediately → close-while-heating prompt: "off" shows `M140 S0`/`M104 S0` acked in zp_serial.log before the port closes, "leave running" survives exit → the autotune refusal names the remedies. **Round 4 is now the go/no-go: set Zone A to 37 °C in the app and confirm it REACHES AND HOLDS** (the bench proved the hardware does 21.5 → 37 °C in ~7 s and holds ±0.65 °C for minutes, so anything slower or flatter in the app is a software difference, not the heater) → **then provoke the fixed fault: while holding, reconnect the ZP board** (Marlin resets on port open and forgets the target) and confirm the card reads *"board forgot the setpoint x1, restored"*, app.log carries `re-asserting (board reset #1)`, and **the temperature does not sag** — before this fix the hold silently died there. **Round 3 adds, and step 3 changes: drive ZONE A (the heat bed, `M140`) — watch the DUTY BAR move, which is the proof the command was ACCEPTED rather than merely sent** → then deliberately provoke the old fault: re-enable Zone B on the tab, set it, and confirm the card turns **REFUSED** with the board's own `Error:` text naming HE0, a `firmware REFUSED 'M104 S37'` WARNING lands in app.log, and **the retry STOPS** (the old behaviour was a healthy-looking card re-sending a refused command every minute for 48 minutes), then disable Zone B again → Custom jog panel → ＋ Add section → 🌡️ Incubator: with no session it points at Workflows → Incubator **and does not start one**, with Zone A holding it tracks temp → target · duty, shows only Zone A's row, and survives a restart via the saved panel layout. |
| `MEBP_v717_MICROSCOPE_ILLUMINATION_AND_SHUTTER_INTERLOCK.md` | v7.17 **the three Nikon Ti devices nothing was driving — `EpiShutter`, `DiaLamp`, `LightPathDrive` — plus a shutter↔cassette interlock** (operator, from the Nikon ask list: *"EpiShutter / DiaLamp / LightPathDrive API, and whether shutter can be interlocked with the cassette move."*). v7.5.x wired 3 of the SDK's 61 `Nikon.TiScope.*` classes; these three are fitted on this body and had no code at all. **The interlock is the substantive half:** rotating the cassette with the excitation shutter open sweeps the beam across every cube that passes, flashing the sample with out-of-band excitation (photobleaching, phototoxicity on a live prep) and handing any integrating camera a bright artefact frame — and our fluorescence mosaic will need it the moment cube changes stop being manual. **THE INTERLOCK LIVES IN `MicroscopeController.set_filter`, the ONE chokepoint** every cassette move reaches (the jog combo, Hardware Setup's per-slot `Go`, anything later) — a separate `set_filter_interlocked()` would have left `Go` unprotected, the "one writer" lesson this file keeps re-recording; pinned by an AST-ish source test that `_go_filter` calls plain `set_filter`. **It is ONE submitted op**, because the controller is a process-wide singleton whose jog card polls it ~1 Hz, so three ops would let a `refresh` land between the close and the move (pinned by counting `_submit` calls). Three properties, each a distinct way to get it wrong: **restore the PREVIOUS state, never "open"** (reopening a deliberately-darkened path illuminates a sample the operator had gone dark on — the v7.16 "restore CLEARS as well as sets" hazard); **restore in a `finally`** (a move that fails mid-rotation must still reopen, or the next acquisition is black with nothing explaining why — and a restore that fails *after a good move* raises its own error naming the closed light path, while after a *failed* move it only logs so the move's own error survives); and **do nothing at all when the prior state is unreadable** (a state we cannot read is one we cannot restore, so closing on the way in would leave the body in a state we invented rather than found — it logs *"interlock skipped"* instead). **DEFAULT OFF, and not out of timidity: the shutter's open/closed ENCODING is not verified on hardware, so if it is inverted on this body the interlock would OPEN the shutter for the rotation** — precisely the exposure it prevents; same shape as `parfocal_auto_apply` in this very store. **The encoding is DERIVED, then REFINED, then overridable:** the SDK's declared range gives two codes (lower=closed per the Ti convention) → **refined by the live `DisplayString`**, since a shutter reports its state in words and the raw value in force therefore tells us which code that word belongs to with the other following by elimination (a *measurement*, not a convention, whenever the SDK populates it) → `epi_shutter_invert` as the operator override, the treatment `focus_up_is_positive`/`plate_flip_180` already get, applied in the CONTROLLER so it flips the READ and the WRITE and the interlock speaks the same operator-facing language as the manual toggle. **The discrete/continuous split is principled, not incidental:** the light path routes through the same `_set_turret` as the turrets and **REFUSES** an out-of-range index (straight from the v7.5.x hardware-verified finding that this SDK *silently clamped filter slot 999 to 6 and reported success*), while the lamp level **CLAMPS** exactly as `set_focus_um` does, because hitting the end of a continuous range is ordinary. **Lamp level is reported in the SDK's OWN declared units, never a fake percentage** — the range may be volts or an arbitrary index, and a percentage of an unknown quantity is a fabricated number that reads as measured. Own `_value_param`/`_write_value`/`_value_range` helpers because **the value-carrying property is not always `Position`** on these devices (a lamp level and a shutter state are both commonly `Value`); the hardware-verified turret/focus paths are left byte-identical. All three are **ACCESSORIES**: `_TI_CORE_DEVICES` now judges the connection, a missing accessory logs at INFO not WARNING (warning every connect trains the operator to ignore the log), every getter degrades to "not fitted", and **each jog-card row HIDES outright** when absent (a visible permanently-dead control reads as broken software, not as hardware never fitted). Nothing about illumination is persisted — restoring a saved lamp level on connect would switch a lamp on unexpectedly — and all three are **POLLED**, because the Ti-E has physical buttons and a lamp knob on the body so the operator changes them without us. `_apply_invert` is pure and total on purpose: it runs inside `_read_all`, whose reads are exception-swallowed, so anything that could raise would turn a policy flag into a silently missing readout. **⚠ Disclosed, deliberately NOT guarded:** switching the light path off the camera port blacks out every frame and reads downstream as an exposure fault — no new guard because the controller's existing exclusivity lease already makes a scan/calibration fail that op with *"microscope is reserved by …"*; the tooltip says so. **Deliberately NOT done:** store-backed light-path labels (a store key with no production writer is the dead-code trap this project keeps recording — v7.9's `prep_bores`, the longest-bore descend; the combo shows the SDK's own name else `port N`), and extending the interlock to the nosepiece (`_with_shutter_closed` is generic so it is one line, but the operator asked about the cassette). **⭐ HARDWARE SESSION ON THE Ti-E 2026-08-12 — all three devices resolved and the probe OVERTURNED THREE SHIPPED ASSUMPTIONS.** Preconditions confirmed first (device **OK/CM_PROB_NONE**, Memory Integrity **off**, `NikonTi.dll` v4.4.1.714, `Nikon.TiScope.NikonTi` registered). **🔴 F1 — "the attribute resolved" is NOT presence, and it silently disarmed the interlock.** The SDK publishes a COM object for **every device it knows about, fitted or not**: on this body `EpiShutter` AND `DiaShutter` both resolve and then report `IsMounted=0 'Device not available'` with `IsOpened=-1 'Status Unknown'`, while `DiaLamp`/`LightPathDrive` report `IsMounted=1 'Device mounted'`. **There is no epi shutter on this microscope, yet `has_epi_shutter()` returned True** — and the consequence was not cosmetic: `epi_shutter_open()` fell through to the generic `Value` (1, in a declared 1–2 range), decoded it as *closed*, so the interlock concluded *"already closed, nothing to protect"* and **silently did nothing while the UI showed a shutter row** — the operator would enable it, see no error, and believe the sample was protected. Fixed with `_accessory_present()` consulting `IsMounted` (absent = present; a device that doesn't report its mount state is still usable) and `-1` reading as **unknown, never as a state**. ⚠ **My simulator always had every accessory mounted, which is exactly why no test could see it** — the recurring "a stub that agrees with the code proves nothing" lesson; the fake now takes `mounted=False`. **🔴 F2 — `Value` is not the shutter state; there are NAMED members.** `IEpiShutter` publishes `IsOpened`, `Open()`, `Close()` **and `IsInterlockEnabled` (the SDK has its own interlock notion)**; `IDiaLamp` publishes `IsOn`, `On()`, `Off()`, `Increase()`, `MeasuredVoltage`, `IsControlled`. So the riskiest thing in this change — the derived encoding, and the `epi_shutter_invert` flag that existed to rescue it — is **unnecessary wherever the named members exist** (a named action cannot be got backwards); they are now preferred with the derived codes kept only as the fallback, and `IsInterlockEnabled` is surfaced read-only in Diagnostics. ⚠ **`dir()` on a comtypes pointer lies BOTH ways** — `ControlVoltage` appears in it and raises `AttributeError`, while `Position` is absent from `DiaLamp`'s listing — so probing must be by `getattr`, which is what the code does. **🔴 F3 — the dia lamp has a control-mode gate that refuses everything.** `IsControlled = 0 'MainMode'` (the body's front-panel knob owns the lamp) ⇒ the SDK refuses every write — `0xE01004BB` level, `0xE01004BE` switch; `1 'RemoteMode'` accepts them. That is the state the rig sits in, so it is the FIRST thing an operator hits, and a bare HRESULT says nothing about the knob in front of them: now checked **before** writing with a message naming MainMode and the remedy, plus `dia_lamp_remote()`/`set_dia_lamp_remote()`, a state field, and a panel that disables the lamp controls and explains why. **Taking Remote control is a separate explicit action, never a side effect of a slider** — it takes the lamp away from the person standing at the microscope. **⚠ F4 — the lamp LEVEL does not stick; disclosed, not fixed.** In RemoteMode the write is accepted and `Value` reads back the number written, then **reverts to the knob's setting within ~1 s**, with `MeasuredVoltage` never moving off 0.5 — so brightness does not change; identical via the production method and a direct write, and `Increase()` does nothing either. **On/off DOES work.** ⚠ **I got this wrong first and a re-measure caught it:** one probe sampling at 0.4 s saw `1→6→12` hold and I recorded "level control works"; a focused re-run with reads either side of the revert showed it latching for under a second — the claim was withdrawn rather than shipped. Left writing (correct, harmless, may hold on another body, and the ~1 s poll makes a non-sticking level visibly bounce back rather than be silently swallowed); what governs it is a question for Nikon. **Exercised through the real `MicroscopeController`: 20/22** — temp store seeded from the operator's real settings so `microscope.json` was never written (verified untouched), every original recorded and restored in a `finally` (cube 3 / lamp on at 1 / light path 3 all back), no focus moves: connect+read-all ✓ · light path 3→1 and read back ✓ · **light path 99 REFUSED and the drive did not move** ✓ · **a lamp write in MainMode refused naming MainMode** ✓ · take Remote and read back ✓ · lamp on/off toggled and read back ✓ · level accepted-but-reverts (F4) · **cassette 3→1 with the interlock ENABLED and no shutter fitted → plain move, no error** ✓ · originals restored ✓. **🔴 The interlock's PROTECTIVE path could NOT be verified — there is no epi shutter on this body to close.** What was verified is its *degradation*; the close/reopen ordering remains covered only by the simulator tests. **🐞 F5 — the first `Position` read after connect is STALE, found while checking my own restore.** Chasing an apparent inconsistency (the raw dump read the light path as `1` while `get_light_path()` read `3` in the SAME run) turned up a separate defect, and **it is the ACCESS that primes it, not elapsed time**: reading `Position` as the *first* COM access on a device returns a default — the drive physically at 3 answered **1** and kept answering 1 for as long as nothing else on that device was touched (6 reads over 0.9 s) — while one access to any other property (`IsMounted`) makes the very next `Position` read return the true 3, reproduced across two fresh connections. Every position getter is primed **today only by ACCIDENT** (they all call `_require_mounted` first), as is `dia_lamp_intensity` in `_read_all` (because `has_dia_lamp` happens to run ahead of it) — reorder those two lines and the first state published after connect silently carries a wrong value. NEW `_prime_devices()` called from `connect()` makes it deliberate (six cheap reads, no sleep), **verified on hardware: first-access `Position` now reads 3 on three consecutive fresh connections**, pinned by an AST test plus a stale-modelling fake with a guard-the-guard test. ⚠ This is also why I briefly believed my own test had MOVED the operator's light path — it had not, the drive was at 3 throughout; the false alarm came from trusting a single read. **Rig left exactly as found (light path 3, cube 3, objective 1); `config/hardware/microscope.json` never written** (the harness used a temp store seeded from their real settings). Tests: NEW `test_v717_microscope_illumination_and_interlock.py` (**96**, no skips); **10/10 mutations CAUGHT** (interlock skips the close · restore forces "open" · restore out of the `finally` · interlock ignores its setting · light path clamps instead of refusing · shutter codes hardcoded past the range + `DisplayString` · **presence back to "attribute resolved" = the F1 bug** · **lamp writes un-gated from RemoteMode = the F3 bug** · **`IsOpened` ignored, back to decoding `Value` = the F2 bug** · **devices not primed at connect = the F5 bug**), sources restored byte-identical. ⚠ **One of my own tests was too weak and I caught it pre-ship:** it `skipTest`ed when offscreen Qt would not focus a spin box, leaving the "don't overwrite a level being typed" guard unverified on every headless run — rewritten to force `hasFocus` in both directions. Every interlock test observes the shutter **AT THE MOMENT** the cassette moves (a `_Spy` subclass records it), because the final state cannot distinguish *"closed for the rotation"* from *"never touched"* — both end as they started. Regression **706 green** per-batch (nikon-ti+new+hygiene 185 · context-panel/illumination/responsive/jog-nav/workflow-settings 130 · focus-state-bug/objective-ladder/lablink×2 129 · v7.11 focus+plate-level 262) + a `gui.app` import smoke + a real `HardwareSetupPage` built and switched to its Microscope tab. **Remaining HW work (the 2026-08-12 session did the rest):** **fit an epi shutter and verify the interlock's protective path** — Diagnostics… must show `epi_shutter FITTED -> True` with an `IsOpened` that is not *Status Unknown*, then toggle *Excitation* **looking at the body, not the screen** (with `IsOpened`/`Open()`/`Close()` present the encoding is no longer derived, so *Shutter reads inverted* should never be needed — if it is, something else is wrong), **then** enable the interlock and confirm close-for-the-rotation + reopen, an already-closed shutter staying closed, Hardware Setup's `Go` behaving identically, and a forced mid-rotation failure still reopening with the error naming it → confirm **which** light-path position feeds the camera (all four switch; only an operator can say which is the camera port) → settle F4 with Nikon, or confirm at the body that the knob overrides the level, and if it is genuinely knob-only make the level read-only rather than offering a control that bounces back → **turn the lamp knob and press the front-panel buttons and confirm the panel tracks them** (what the ~1 s poll is for; needs someone at the rig) → restart and confirm both flags persisted while the lamp level did **not**. |

What contradicted it was the **gate**: ONE shared `gate()` served step 3 (a zero-motion measurement) and step 4 (a real descent toward the glass), so step 3 refused until **Plate Bottom Z** and **Fast Move (Safe) Z** were taught — two heights that exist only to compute and reach the OPTIONAL park. Tests: **73** in `test_v710_needle_bore_wizard.py` (was 71; +15, two coupled gate assertions replaced), the load-bearing one measuring a 320.0 µm two-bore offset with no plate bottom / no safe Z / no needle location and `nav == []`, `z_moves == []`; **4/4 mutations CAUGHT** (the checks back in the shared gate = the original bug · `_on_park` on the relaxed gate · advisory silenced · start button gated on the park).
