One pre-existing failure PROVED not ours (`test_v79_needle_form_ui::test_every_on_disk_setup_round_trips_through_the_page` wants ≥6 `config/hardware/*.json`, this tree has 1 — identical with the change stashed). **Needs GUI verification on ME3B V1:** step 3 usable with no reference heights taught → jog anywhere, click every bore, stage never moves → the park's disabled tooltip names both what to teach and that it is not needed → the ⚠ inactive-clamp advisory appears and then clears → after teaching both heights the park works as before → **drive each bore to one target and check DIRECTION as well as distance** (the measurement itself is unchanged) → step 4 still refuses without both heights. |
| `MEBP_v716_LIVE_VIEW_VS_MEASURED_ORIENTATION.md` | v7.16 **the live-view orientation is SEPARATE from the measured camera→stage one** (operator: *"1) I setup the camera live view so that the image is showing a correctly rotated and mirrored view 2) I then go into the objective calibration for the mosaic builder, and once it is done it always flips the camera back to the wrong orientation for the live view … if we need to, we can separate the mosaic settings from the camera live view settings"*). Their diagnosis was right and their proposed remedy is the one implemented. **ONE `(rotation_deg, mirrored, flip_y)` triple per camera identity served THREE consumers** — the live display, `MosaicBuilder._orient_tile` and `CameraManager.pixel_to_stage_offset` — **with TWO writers**: the per-slot Flip X / Flip Y / Rotation controls (a *viewing preference*) and `derive_camera_stage_orientation` inside the objective calibration (the *measured* matrix). The measurement won, so the operator's exact sequence always ended with the view flipped back; reproduced against the real `HardwareSetupPage`. **No precedence rule could have fixed it, because the two are different quantities:** the measured triple makes the display STAGE-ALIGNED (image +X → stage +X), and on a rig with `plate_flip_180` the plate then reads 180° from the A1-top-left convention every other plate view uses — so the orientation the operator wants to LOOK at is legitimately not the one the mosaic NEEDS. **What made the split small rather than a rework is that the display was ALREADY decoupled underneath:** `CameraFeedView._widget_to_image` inverts its own transform via `ViewGeometry`, so `clicked` emits RAW frame coords and `pixel_to_stage_offset` then applies the MEASURED orientation — moving the display cannot move where a click lands (pinned byte-identical across 4 flip combos × 5 rotations of the view, with a companion assertion that the *measured* mirror still DOES change it, so the test cannot pass because nothing maps clicks at all). NEW display-only `view_orientation` per camera identity (`CameraCalibrationStore` schema **1.2→1.3**) + `CameraManager.display_orientation`/`has_`/`set_`/`clear_`; read ONLY by `CameraFeedView` (`auto_orient`), the slot previews and the plate-location feed. **`objective_calibration_card` needed NO change** — once the view is its own field, that card's existing writes ARE geometry writes and are correct. **Unset ⇒ falls back to the measurement** (byte-identical pre-v7.16 for any camera never customised), while a **neutral view is stored EXPLICITLY** (popping the key would let the measurement retake the view — the same bug, harder to see). Migration seeds the view from each camera's existing geometry so **every feed looks exactly as it did before the upgrade**, never re-seeds, and leaves a fully-neutral camera byte-identical. ⚠ **Restore CLEARS as well as sets** (a slot's `CameraWidget` outlives its source, so a camera with no preference must hand the view back to the measurement or the slot inherits the previous camera's rotation — same hazard as the v7.16 crop restore). The readout names both, because *"Rotation vs stage: 180°"* would otherwise read as a claim about the feed. ⚠ **My own smoke assertion was WRONG and it is what surfaced the seeding semantic** — I expected the first edit to commit only the field it names; the real page correctly seeds from the orientation in force, so ticking Flip X means *"what I'm looking at, plus flip X"* instead of silently dropping the measured flip Y and zeroing the rotation; now pinned by `TestTheFirstEditSeedsFromWhatIsOnScreen` rather than left undocumented. 🐞 **An unrelated defect found while tracing consumers:** `capture_controller._orientation` unpacked FOUR values from a 3-tuple (`_resolve_orientation` is the 4-tuple one), the `ValueError` landing in a bare `except` — so with no `_orientation_fn` **every capture was stamped with a NEUTRAL orientation**, silently claiming `pixels_stage_aligned` for a rotated camera; verified `(0.0, False, False)` → `(-90.0, True, False)`. Tests: NEW `test_v716_live_view_vs_measured_orientation.py` (**28**, driving the production store/manager/feed-view/page) + 6 updated × 5 added in `test_v75x_camera_rotation_cal_and_monitor` (**48**); **6/6 mutations CAUGHT** (feed reads `full_orientation` again = the original bug · controls write geometry again · the view setter pops a neutral view · migration dropped · restore never clears · the mosaic resolver reads the VIEW). Regression **538 green** across live-view/orientation-audit/square-up/square-crop 214 · camera-store/scale-FOV/unified-mosaic/image-correction/hw-controls 162 · mosaic-orientation-adjust+remap/mapping-orient/reanchor/rotation-cal 115 · plate-location-click-rim/tucsen-mosaic-FOV/objective-cal/suite-hygiene 115 · capture-core 47, plus a `gui.app` import smoke and an offscreen `HardwareSetupPage` walkthrough of all three steps. One pre-existing failure PROVED not ours (`TestOrientTile::test_center_pixel_is_invariant_under_rotation` in `MosaicBuilder._orient_tile`, a file this diff does not touch — fails identically with the change stashed). **Needs GUI/HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER:** nothing looks different on launch (the migration — if a feed changed orientation, stop) → dial the live view in with Flip X/Y + Rotation → **run the objective calibration and confirm the view does NOT move while `Rotation vs stage:` DOES change** → a small mosaic still stitches with no wrong-side tiles → **click a feature and drive to it at several live-view rotations — the needle must land on it** (the safety claim) → restart → reassign the slot to a camera with no preference and confirm it does NOT inherit the old rotation. |
| `MEBP_v716_CAMERA_SQUARE_CROP.md` | v7.16 **a square sensor crop, applied at the FRAME SOURCE so every surface inherits it** (operator: *"it turns out that the image sensor is too wide, and we see some of the dark circles of the field of view. can we crop the image into a square in the camera calibration part of the camera detection settings page. this cropping should be assigned to all surfaces including mosaic, live view, image, recording etc."*). A microscope's illuminated field is a CIRCLE; a sensor wider than it images the unlit tube wall at the left/right edges — on this rig's 1.27:1 Tucsen a 276-px band each side that carries no specimen, drags the round-1 flat-field estimate toward black, and is still stepped over by the raster. **THREE frame egress points, all cropped by one `CameraWidget._apply_crop`:** `_grab_frame` (the raw cache → detection / mosaic / calibration, the on-screen pixmap, AND `frame_captured` → video **recording**), `capture_fresh_frame` (mosaic tiles, calibration grabs), and `capture_raw_average` — the **raw 16-bit still** path, which reads the sensor buffer straight from the backend and would otherwise have been the one image in the app still showing the vignette. Deliberately UNLIKE the mirror/rotation, which v7.10 moved OUT of the source so the mosaic could re-blend against a *changing* orientation: re-blending under a changed crop is meaningless (the pixels are gone), while the cost of the alternative is four consumers each deciding independently how big a frame is. **🔴 THE DANGEROUS HALF — µm/px MUST NOT MOVE.** Cropping removes pixels; it does not change what a pixel spans. `µm/px ∝ 1/width` describes the sensor being SAMPLED differently (binning, a resolution switch), so feeding a DELIVERED width into it rescales by the crop fraction: `1.2710 × 2600/2048 = 1.6136` — **+27 %, and too LARGE means the mosaic believes its field is wider than it is and steps past its own tiles = SEAMS**, the same failure as the 400-minute scan reached by another route. **FOUR sites had their own copy of that ratio** (`CameraManager.effective_um_per_px` · `MosaicCalibration._resolve_scale` · `CalibrationPage._ploc_microscope_um_per_px` · `PixelCalibrationDialog._expected_um_per_px`); all now resolve the denominator through ONE new `MosaicCalibration.capture_width_px`, and with no crop configured the capture width IS the delivered width so every one is byte-identical. **Corollary — the stamp names a SENSOR MODE, not a frame size:** `_true_capture_resolution` (whose docstring already promised "the real pixels off the sensor") and both dialogs' `result_resolution` now record the PRE-crop size, which means (a) turning the crop on or off never invalidates an existing calibration — ratio 1.0 either way, pinned — and (b) round 1's `sensor_width_um` plausibility guard keeps working, since stamping two objectives at different crops would make a camera's own objectives 'disagree'. **A FRACTION, NOT PIXELS** — `scale` is a fraction of the short side, so the crop is a physical region of the field and survives a 2600→5200 binning switch; pixels would silently mean a different area, the same stale-number class as an unstamped resolution. 10–100 % spin because the operator's symptom means the illuminated circle may be smaller than the short side, which no plain square crop fixes. **THE CENTRE IS PRESERVED EXACTLY** — `pixel_to_stage_offset` maps a click by its offset from the frame CENTRE, so an off-centre crop puts a constant error into every click→stage conversion and into the mosaic back-projection every well centre comes from; `rect_for` matches the crop's parity to the frame's (exact, not half-a-pixel), pinned across odd/even sizes × six scales. Malformed stored crop ⇒ **NO crop** (an unexpected crop is a silently wrong FOV; no crop is merely the old behaviour). Persisted per DEVICE IDENTITY in `CameraCalibrationStore` beside µm/px and the flips (it is a property of the physical camera + optics, not of the swappable setup), disabled POPS the key so an un-cropped camera's entry stays byte-identical and nothing migrates. ⚠ **Restore CLEARS as well as sets** — a slot's `CameraWidget` outlives the source assigned to it, so the crop restore runs BEFORE the no-entry early return and pushes even with nothing stored; otherwise reassigning a slot from a cropped camera to an uncropped one leaves the new one cropped. **⭐ ROUND 2 — *"i need to choose where the crop occurs in the frame"*.** The lit circle is centred on the OPTICAL AXIS, which need not pass through the middle of the sensor (a C-mount adapter, a slightly off-axis tube lens), so a centred square still clips one side — `offset_x`/`offset_y` added, as fractions of the frame, clamped to stay inside it. **That is NOT free and is the substance of this round:** `pixel_to_stage_offset` measures from the frame CENTRE, so moving the crop moves the pixel that means *"the stage is here"* and every click, overlay and mosaic tile would follow — **~1 mm at a small crop on this rig, i.e. a silently relocated plate calibration**. Two honest options: redefine the reference point and warn that it invalidates a taught calibration (every nudge forces a re-teach, and nothing here measures the true principal point so the 'definition' was always arbitrary), or **CANCEL it**. Cancelled, in the three places that convert pixel↔stage: `pixel_to_stage_offset` (`+d` in RAW px, before the flips/rotation), `stage_offset_to_pixel` (`−d` at the mirroring point of the inverse chain), and the mosaic's tile placement. `d` comes from NEW `center_offset_px`, derived from the **ACHIEVED rect, never the requested fraction** — at the clamp the request is 1300 px and the applied shift 276, and it is the applied one that must be cancelled (mutation M15). ⚠ **The mosaic and the click path MUST share ONE convention** — two conventions consistently applied would each be fine, one of each is the bug — so NEW `MosaicCalibration.crop_offset_um` puts `d` through the SAME `µm/px·R(θ)·diag(mx,my)` chain a click does, pinned by a test at every rotation × flip. The mosaic needed **zero caller churn**: one `MosaicBuilder.tile_center_offset_um` applied inside `add_frame`/`add_raster_frame`, wired from `build_mosaic_builder` (already "THE single place builder arguments are chosen"). ⚠ **The canvas also has to GROW** — the scan bounds are the operator's region, not the tiles' footprint, and at a small crop the displacement EXCEEDS half a FOV, so the tile would be clipped at the edge; `_init_composite` pads by `|d|` (origin untouched, so back-projection is unaffected). **The crosshair follows the reference pixel** — it marks where the stage points, which is the frame middle only while the crop is centred; left at the geometric middle it would aim the operator at a point the stage is not on. Verified **0.000e+00 µm** disagreement between the cropped and uncropped mapping of the same physical point across 5 rotations × 4 flip combos × 3 offsets × 2 scales, forward/inverse round-trip to 2e-13 px, and a marked feature back-projecting to its TRUE stage position in a synthetic mosaic. NEW pure `SupportClasses/CameraCrop.py` (no Qt/cv2 — numpy slicing, so 2-D uint16 raw and 3-D BGR crop identically) + a per-slot **⬛ Square crop** checkbox + size-% spin + **Offset X/Y spins + a ⌖ re-centre button** + a readout naming the delivered box, its position and the ACHIEVED offset on Hardware Setup → Cameras; the Microscope Camera Setup **FOV line now reports the DELIVERED field** (`— cropped to 2048×2048 of 2600×2048 px`) because that is what the operator prices tiles from. **The tile count goes UP ~27 % and that is the honest cost** of not stitching dark bands (FOV 3305×2603 → 2603×2603 µm at 4×); a square tile also makes round 1's rotated-bounding-box geometry EXACT at this camera's ≈−90° mount. **⭐ ROUND 3 — *"now python freezes when I apply the crop"*.** Root-caused from the operator's OWN `logs/app.log` rather than guessed: the offset spins **auto-repeat**, and one held arrow walked the crop 1 %→23 % in seven seconds writing the calibration file **40 times** — a JSON serialise + file write on the GUI thread at Qt's key-repeat rate, the shape the image-correction sliders already avoid (`sliderReleased`). Debounced: the **live push stays immediate** (aiming a crop is a visual task, so the preview must follow the spin — mutation M2) while the WRITE waits 400 ms; a checkbox or ⌖ persists at once (one discrete decision, and turning the crop OFF is what an operator does when something looks wrong); `hideEvent` + `aboutToQuit` flush, so a pending write cannot be lost. ⚠ **Stated as HYGIENE, not a proven cause** — every other suspicion was MEASURED and cleared: the cropped display path is **FASTER** (40.6→35.7→17.4 ms at 2600×2048 / square / 55 %), `cvtColor` on the non-contiguous view costs 1.6 vs 1.4 ms, `set_crop` on the real store is **0.6 ms**, and a real `CameraFeedView` in a real `QScrollArea` swept across the scrollbar threshold at five heights showed **0 resize events** either side of the 1.27:1→1:1 aspect change (the `heightForWidth` oscillation theory, dead). The freeze did not reproduce offscreen. **So the real deliverable is NEW `SupportClasses/GuiWatchdog.py`:** `faulthandler` has been armed since v7.5.x but catches a CRASH and is blind to a HANG — the process is alive, the event loop just stops turning, and nothing is written anywhere, which is why a rig-only freeze left no trail at all. A 250 ms QTimer heartbeat + a daemon watcher dump **every thread's** stack to `logs/freeze.log` after 5 s of silence, ONCE per stall (a 60-second freeze must not produce sixty tracebacks), appending so earlier freezes survive; armed in `main.py` right after the QApplication. **Observation ONLY** — a watchdog that broke a thread out of a blocking driver call would be far more dangerous than the hang, since this process holds serial ports open to a stage carrying a needle over glass; a test asserts no interrupt/kill primitive appears in the class. Verified end-to-end against a deliberately wedged GUI thread: the dump NAMED the exact blocking frame. ⚠ **Reported, NOT fixed on a guess:** their Tucsen did not come back — **two** `TUCam released` for ONE stop then `Api_Init ok — 0 camera(s)`; `release()` is idempotent per object (`_cleanup_api` is `_opened_api`-guarded), so two releases mean two backend OBJECTS each holding an API ref, i.e. the TUCam lifecycle, not the crop (this diff edits no part of camera start/stop/release). Their `[Tick]` lines date the bad window exactly: 313 ms/tick before it, **608 ms/tick** across the camera stop + failed reopen. Tests: NEW `test_v716_gui_watchdog.py` (**9**) + 8 added here (**75**); **9/9 further mutations CAUGHT** (persist per step = the 40-writes bug · the live push debounced too · hideEvent flush dropped · checkbox debounced · pending writes cleared unwritten · watchdog dumping per SECOND · only its own thread · truncating not appending · firing on a healthy loop). Confirmed against the rig: `Get-PnpDevice` reports the Libra 25 **`Status: OK, Problem: CM_PROB_NONE`** (healthy driver + USB) while a fresh out-of-app `TUCamBackend.open("0")` still gets 0 cameras — the device is wedged BELOW the SDK, consistent with the unclean teardown above (two `TUCam released` for one stop); recovery is a USB re-enumeration (unplug/replug, or elevated `Disable-PnpDevice`/`Enable-PnpDevice`). Fixed with NEW `CameraManager._probe_tucam_bounded`: the enumeration runs on a **daemon** thread (it may still be inside a 30 s C call at shutdown) waited on for `TUCAM_PROBE_TIMEOUT_S`=4 s, and — the detail that makes it safe rather than merely fast — **a late answer is KEPT in `_tucam_cache`, not discarded**, so a merely SLOW SDK costs one detection round, not the camera; the warning names the state AND the remedy instead of leaving a silent freeze. **Measured on the wedged rig: `detect_cameras()` returns in 6.8 s instead of ~30 s** and still finds both Teslongs + the simulator. ⚠ **NOT caused by the crop and not fixed by removing it** — `_api_init`/`enumerate`/`detect_cameras` are untouched by this work; what the crop work added was the watchdog that FOUND it. Also cleared: two `python -m unittest` processes hung since the previous evening (this repo's documented cross-suite camera-probe hang) were still resident and were killed — not the blocker (the fresh probe still saw 0), but exactly the kind of stale claim to check first. Tests: NEW `test_v716_tucam_probe_timeout.py` (**9**: a 30 s SDK abandoned in <3 s · the late answer cached and used next round · the cache used while still wedged · daemon thread · an AST check that `detect_cameras` no longer calls it inline). **Bench:** unplug/replug the Tucsen to re-enumerate it; if anything freezes again, send `logs/freeze.log`. | `MEBP_v716_TUCSEN_MOSAIC_FOV_AND_INTENSITY.md` | v7.16 **the Tucsen mosaic: a 400-minute scan root-caused to a THREE-LINK chain, the rectangular sensor finally used, and mosaic intensity regularization** (operator: *"i tried to do a full plate mosaic, and it said it would take 400 minutes ... I think its because the microscope camera setup section is not working for the tucsen camera. Also the mosaic tiles are using the microscope view as a square. it is not a square it is rectangular ... After we have done the full mosaic, lets reguralize the intensity across the mosaic"*). Their hunch was right and the mechanism was worse than "not working". **THE CHAIN, every number from their own log/stores: (1) the objective store was keyed by camera MODEL NAME** (`camera_spec.name`) — and there is **no Tucsen entry in `cameras.json` at all**, so the spec still read `Bestscope BUC3D-1000C (ToupTek…)` and both cameras shared one block: `Objective calibration saved: Bestscope…/4x = 0.3891 @ (2600,2048)` logged one line after `Camera orientation saved: Tucsen Camera [tucam:0…]`, **destroying the ToupTek's genuine 4x**. **(2) the identity store never got a resolution stamp** — the card reaches it via `um_per_px_committed = Signal(int, float)`, which carries no resolution, so every start logged *"µm/px restored WITHOUT a measurement resolution"* and `effective_um_per_px` degraded to a **passthrough**. **(3) the Scale/FOV dialog's Verify step re-synced `result_um_per_px` from the live manager UNCONDITIONALLY** — the manager holds whatever was last pushed into the slot, so opening Verify and changing NOTHING replaced the fresh reading with the ToupTek's stale one. That is why the saved value is **bit-identical to the ToupTek's stored 4x**, which a fresh stage-motion measurement cannot produce by chance; the dialog had measured `1.8683 µm/px` 90 s earlier. Net: FOV **1012x797 µm instead of ~3305** → **18,972 tiles instead of ~1,800** (count scales with the SQUARE of the scale error). Fixed with NEW `MosaicCalibration.objective_camera_key()` — ONE key for every caller, the DEVICE IDENTITY, with **no fallback to the spec name once an identity exists** (falling back IS how one camera reads another's block; absent is recoverable, wrong is not), routed through all 8 read/write sites; the resolution stamp now persisted directly; and Verify adopting the manager's value **only when it actually changed** (snapshot before the dialog). ⚠ Two v7.11 wizards were ALREADY identity-keyed, so they had been reading values the card never wrote — independent corroboration that the card was the outlier. 🐞 Also found: `live_target_picker` keyed on `spec.model`, a field `CameraSpec` does not define, so its lookup ALWAYS missed. **THE "SQUARE" — this camera sits at −89.97°, and `_orient_tile` rendered each rotated tile back into its input W×H**, so on a 2600×2048 frame the long axis was **clipped to 2048 (23.1 % of every frame discarded)**, black bars were blended in on the other axis, and the raster stepped by the UNROTATED FOV — real coverage collapsed to `min(w,h)²`, literally a square, with near-zero overlap on one axis. (CLAUDE.md already carried this as a known hazard; it is now fixed rather than recorded.) NEW `_fov_um`/`_oriented_fov_um`/`_oriented_size_px`: the tile is resized into the CAMERA-axis box (aspect preserved ⇒ px→µm stays **isotropic**, which is what makes rotating inside it geometrically correct), oriented into the **rotated bounding box**, and placed on the ORIENTED footprint; `_place_from_box` re-centres on the stored box so `reblend_reoriented` survives an orientation CHANGE. Verified: **100 % of pixels retained at ±90°** (was 76.9 %), footprint transposes exactly, and a marked feature back-projects to `R(θ)`'s prediction within 25 µm at 0/±90/180°. **INTENSITY REGULARIZATION** (`estimate_flat_field`/`regularize_intensity`, wired into the scan + a default-on **Even out illumination** setting): measured FROM THE SCAN — no reference slide, no extra frames — because specimen detail moves between tiles and averages out under a per-pixel MEDIAN of self-normalised tiles while the illumination pattern is fixed in the frame and survives. Heavily blurred on purpose (real vignetting is smooth; anything sharp is residual specimen structure that would otherwise be **burned into every tile**), refuses below 6 tiles, and per-tile level matching is bounded 0.5–2.0× so a tile dominated by a bright object is not dragged to the plate average. Measured on a 49-tile synthetic scan: **seam/vignette structure −78.8 %**, recovered bright:dark ratio **4.02 vs the true 4.00**. **NEW plausibility guard** `ObjectiveCalibration.implausible_reason` — spec-free and physically exact: for ONE camera `µm/px × magnification × capture_width` is a sensor property and must agree across objectives (the operator's ToupTek: 2x→5707, 4x→5669, 10x→5724, **1.0 % spread**). Compares ONLY within one camera, and returns None when there is nothing to compare against — unverifiable is reported as unverified, never as passed; the message is direction-aware (too small ⇒ *more tiles*, too large ⇒ *GAPS*). ⭐ **Operator request mid-session:** Save/Load Setup on the Identity tab defaulted to the process CWD while the browser only ever scans `config/hardware` — so a saved setup landed where the page could not see it (and is why loose setup .json files litter the repo root); NEW `_setup_dir()` used by both. **DATA MIGRATED** (`objectives.json`, backed up `.bak-v716identity`): Andor → `andor:VSC-07863` untouched (still 3.2271, FOV 3305); ToupTek → its `toupcam:…` identity taking the internally-consistent legacy block + the newer 2x (**1.0 % spread**); **Tucsen → deliberately EMPTY**, so the mosaic now REFUSES with an actionable message instead of scanning 19,000 tiles at a stolen scale. ⚠ **A separate pre-existing problem exposed and NOT repaired:** the Andor's **10x** entry has a **2.045× invariant spread** against its own 4x (13218 vs 27036) while the 4x matches the Zyla datasheet (6.5 µm × 2048 = 13312 µm) to 0.7 % — so the 10x RESOLUTION STAMP is wrong (measured at 1024, stamped 2048). Left alone and reported rather than inferred: it is a 30-second re-measure, and silently rewriting a calibration stamp is the class of thing this whole update is about. **⭐ ROUND 2 — three follow-ups.** **(a) The Tucsen now appears in the Microscope Camera Setup list** (`TUCSEN-LIBRA-25` with the v7.9 hardware-verified 5200×4096 / 2600×2048 — the latter 2×2 binned, so it does NOT change the FOV — plus a `TUCSEN-GENERIC` fallback). Its `sensor_pixel_size_um` is **`null` ON PURPOSE** and `CameraSpec` types it `Optional[float]`: no verifiable pitch exists for this model here, and a guessed one renders as a plausible "theoretical µm/px" that a real calibration is then judged against — so all four consumers report *unknown* instead (a known pitch computes byte-identically). **(b) 🐞 THE BOGUS "move less than 199 microns" — the operator's diagnosis was exactly right** (*"for it to know how far it can move it needs to know the objective its on and the measured magnification of the objective only this way can we get then native microns per pixel for the camera at a 1x frame"*). The bound is `0.25 × min(w,h) × µm/px` and it was sized from `CameraManager.effective_um_per_px`, which held **another camera's** 0.389135 µm/px — unstamped, so it passed straight through un-rescaled: `0.25 × 2048 × 0.389135 = ` **199.2 µm exactly** (reproduced in a test). At the real scale the same bound is ~651 µm. Implemented their derivation: NEW `sensor_width_um` (µm/px × magnification × width = the imaged sensor width, a CAMERA property identical for every objective), `native_um_per_px` (literally the 1×-frame native scale) and `predicted_um_per_px` (native ÷ this objective's magnification); `PixelCalibrationDialog` gains `cam_key`/`objective` and resolves **measurement-first** — this objective's stored value → **predicted from the camera's other objectives** → the manager **only when calibrated AND stamped** → silent, and the refusal now NAMES its source. The prediction leg is load-bearing: the move happens BEFORE the measurement exists, so a never-calibrated 20× has no scale of its own and would otherwise be unbounded. ⚠ **`sensor_width_um` REFUSES when the objectives disagree (>25 %)** — with two entries a plain median just PICKS one, so a single bad stamp would silently become the camera's native scale; this machine's Andor is exactly that case (13218 vs 27036) and now logs *"its objectives disagree … re-measure the odd one out"* instead of a confident wrong answer. **(c)** the identity-tab Save folder was already fixed earlier in this same update (`_setup_dir()`); re-verified that the browser, the Setup-Name list and both dialogs all use `CONFIG_HARDWARE_DIR` — **needs an app restart to take effect**. Tests: NEW `test_v716_tucsen_mosaic_fov_and_intensity.py` (**49** = 34 round 1 + 15 round 2); **14/14 mutations CAUGHT** (key falls back to the name · identity ignored · orient back into W×H · raster on the unrotated FOV · flat-field skipped · both min-tiles guards defeated · plausibility never warns · **Verify adopts unconditionally = the 400-min bug** · setup save back to the CWD · **manager passthrough restored = the 199 µm bound** · objective-prediction leg dropped · inconsistent objectives averaged instead of refused · a sensor pitch fabricated when unknown · the Tucsen catalogue entries removed). ⚠ **My first M1 and M6 were PARTIAL mutations** (a no-op rewrite; a second guard left intact) and appeared to survive — a mutation that does not change behaviour proves nothing. Regression green across unified-mosaic-calibration/plate-frame/objective-cal/camera-orientation-audit (167) · orientation-adjust/memory-perf/orientation-remap/fluor-shift/camera-rotation (60) · camera-store/hw-controls/cal-liveview/image-correction/tucsen-libra/objective-ladder (177) · nd3-export/picker-scaling/spheroid-survey/suite-hygiene/plate-well-detection (144) · plate-mosaic (110, class-by-class). Two legitimate test updates (the unified fixture keys by identity — the contract that changed; the settings round-trip covers the new key). Pre-existing failure PROVED not ours: `test_real_24_well_mosaic` (23 vs 24), the legacy blob detector in `VisionDetector.py`, a file this diff does not touch; `TestManualAlignPage` still hangs (excluded per precedent). **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER:** re-run Mosaic & Camera Calibration on the Tucsen at 4x and **watch the reported FOV — the Andor sees 3305 µm at 4x on the same port, so a Tucsen FOV near ~1000 µm means the measurement is still wrong** → no more "restored WITHOUT a measurement resolution" on start → calibrate a SECOND Tucsen objective and confirm the plausibility guard stays quiet (or fires, which settles it) → full-plate tile count in the low thousands → **look at the stitch**: no gaps, no dark seams, no black wedges, whole rectangular frame used → toggle Even out illumination OFF and re-scan a small region, the tile grid should visibly return → map wells and drive to two (proves oriented placement did not disturb back-projection) → Save Setup opens in `config/hardware` and the file appears in the list immediately (**restart first**) → the Tucsen is selectable in Microscope Camera Setup and its µm/px line reads *unknown for this model* until measured → the rotation dialog's move limit is no longer 199 µm and its message names where the scale came from. |
| `MEBP_v791_JOG_PANEL_PER_PUMP_RATE_AND_POSITION_ROW.md` | v7.9.1 **two jog-panel reports, both about features `MEBP_v79x_PER_PUMP_JOG_FLOW_AND_POSITION_READOUT.md` already claimed were done — both claims PARTLY true, which is why they read as fixed and behaved as broken.** **(1) Per-pump jog rate** (operator: *"on all the pump jog pannels we need independent control of rate for each pump"*). Audited every pump-jog surface rather than trusting the doc (this repo has form: v7.9's `prep_bores` had no production writer, the longest-bore descend no consumer) — and this one is **genuinely implemented**: `HardwareControlPanel` builds one `spin_p_pct_pumps[pid]` row per pump in % mode and `spin_p_max_pumps` in max mode, anchored per-pump via `get_max_pump_feedrate_for`, with the state held on the CONTROLLER (`set/get_pump_jog_pct`, persisted `jog.pump_jog_pct`) so the nine tiles cannot diverge; traced end-to-end, clicking P2 uses **P2's own spin × P2's own ceiling**. That covers Jog, Calibration, Hardware Setup, the Custom panel and all six workflow pages — unchanged. **TWO surfaces bypassed it. Gap A — the Settings-page context panel passed NO rate at all** (`_ctx_jog_pump` left `rate_uL_s=None`), so all three pumps ran at the controller's internal default while the same pump elsewhere ran at its configured flow; that panel has no speed widget, and the v7.9.x step-slider rework edited this very method to fix a mm/µL units mismatch and never added the rate. Fixed by reading the SHARED controller state (`get_pump_jog_pcts` × `get_max_pump_feedrate_for`) — the same resolution `_on_jog_pump` performs, so a pump jogs identically wherever it is jogged from; an unset % or any failure returns None and keeps the legacy default (a wrong rate is worse than the old behaviour). **Gap B — the Xbox anchored all three pumps to one `p_speed_max`**: `_pump_vel_mm_s` applied per-pump SYRINGE GEOMETRY but a shared ceiling, and bore ceilings differ by orders of magnitude (v7.9 measured ~2000× between a 22G bore and a 30 µm pulled tip), so a full deflection drove a fine bore at a coarse bore's rate; the ceiling now comes per-pump from `SafetyLimits.get_max_flow_rate`, the SAME source `get_max_pump_feedrate_for` uses, so Xbox and tiles resolve from one authority (measured 8.0/0.5/2.0 µL/s → three different speeds holding the ceilings' ratio). **The Xbox %-ladder stays SHARED deliberately** — it is one physical control for the whole 'p' group and per-pump percentages would leave its buttons with no defined meaning; independence comes from the ceiling, the safety-relevant half. No limits configured ⇒ legacy scalars, jogs exactly as before. **Deliberately NOT done:** per-pump STEP SIZE — `jog_button_array` builds ONE `_p_step` group for P1/P2/P3, so the step is shared even where the rate is per-pump; the operator asked for RATE, and per-pump step is a separate genuinely-missing feature to scope on its own. Also left: `stage_panel._on_jog_array_pump`, dead code (no `JogButtonArray` is built there any more) calling `move_pump_relative` with no feedrate — zero connections today, recorded not removed. **(2) The Live Position number drew ON TOP of the units** (operator's words). `PositionValueLabel` set **both** `QSizePolicy.Ignored` horizontally AND a `minimumWidth` tracking its text — and those FIGHT: `Ignored` tells the LAYOUT to size the grid column without regard to the widget, while `setMinimumWidth` forces the WIDGET to stay that wide. Measured on the real panel: the label was **108 px wide inside a ~5 px cell** (`val x=435 w=108` vs `unit x=440`), so the right-aligned number painted ~100 px over the unit **at every width from 540 down to 200 px**, not just narrow ones. Policy → `Preferred` so the layout actually reserves the width; the BAR keeps `Ignored`+10 px min and stays the element that yields, which is what lets a narrow panel scrunch without clipping the number (clean +5 px gap at 540/380/300/260/200/160; `PositionReadoutCard` shares the class, +8 px down to 100 px). ⚠ The original docstring's *"the policy stays Ignored so the layout reads the minimum, never the raw text sizeHint"* IS the bug stated as intent — the comment now says so, because `Ignored` looks deliberate. ⚠ **My first measurement was wrong and nearly hid this:** comparing text width against the label's own BOX reported OK at every width, because the box was fine — it was the box versus its CELL that was broken; only comparing the two widgets' actual geometries exposed it. Tests: NEW `test_v791_per_pump_rate_and_position_row.py` (**17**); **5/5 mutations CAUGHT** (settings rate removed · settings shared ceiling · xbox shared ceiling · policy back to Ignored · min-width stops tracking). Regression **470 green** across per-pump-jog-flow/jog-step-sliders/axis-max-speed/common-axis-speed/xbox-speed-percent/xbox-input-pipeline/jog-pump-fill/context-panel/responsive-context/jog-navigation/pump-plunger/jog-direction-z-up-sign/stage-jog-off-thread/illumination/nikon-ti/xz-custom-z/suite-hygiene + a `gui.app` import smoke. **Needs GUI/HW verification on ME3B V1:** the number sits clear of its unit on Jog and the Custom panel at full width and dragged narrow → two pumps set to different flows visibly jog at different speeds → the Settings context panel now jogs at the pump's configured flow, not the old fixed default → on Xbox, the finer bore runs slower at full deflection while the %-ladder still scales both → the % set on one page still shows everywhere and survives a restart. |
| `MEBP_v791_CALIBRATION_LAYOUT_AND_SAFE_TRAVEL_FIXES.md` | v7.9.1 **five operator-reported defects, four on ME3B V1 — one of them a genuine crash-down.** 🔴 **P3 — the bore wizard's "go to needle at survey height" drove the needle DOWN onto the plate** (operator: *"it doesnt actually move the focus"*, with a clamp + 16.5 s timeout log). Reproduced EXACTLY from this machine's real `settings.json`: `zero_position.Z = -76.35` is both the datum and `z_max`, i.e. the mechanical hard bottom, and `z_up_sign = -1`, so the envelope raw `[-136.35, -76.35]` is height `[60, 0]`. Three of the five Z references sit **24–49 mm BELOW the hard bottom** (`fast_move_z` +24.79 zref = height **−24.79**, `max_z` +25.79, `replace_z` +48.7) while `plate_top_z`/`plate_bottom_z` are correct — the exact fingerprint of an `apply_z_setup` re-datum (which rewrites `zero_position["Z"]`, `z_up_sign` AND the envelope) with **no invalidation of the references captured against the old one**. So `safe_travel_to`'s step-1 *retract* asked for height −24.79, the soft limit clamped it to the hard bottom and the print floor pulled it back to the plate bottom: **the retract was a full-depth plunge, arrested only by a clamp that exists for a different purpose**, then `wait_for_z_arrival` polled for `24.79` while the stage sat at `−25.63` and burned 16.5 s before aborting. **Five defects, all fixed:** (1) `safe_travel_to` step 1 bypassed the never-descend guard — `ensure_retracted_to` early-returns via `needle_at_or_above` so *"a misconfigured/too-low target can never cause a crash-down"*, but `safe_travel_to` called the shared `_retract_z_slow_then_fast` **directly**; the guard now lives in that helper, where both retract callers meet. ⚠ **The at-or-above check IS the whole raise-only guard** — my first cut ALSO added a "refuse a descent" branch, which is wrong and was caught pre-ship: every descent case is already an at-or-above case, so that branch could only fire on ordinary travel starting above the safe height, and would have refused it (a comment says so, because it reads like a missing check). (2) **a clamped move was structurally unconfirmable** — `move_z_absolute` mutates its destination twice and told no one, so the helper waited on the *requested* value; new pure `effective_z_target_zref()` predicts the landing point and both the retract and the step-3 descent command AND confirm against it (`move_z_absolute` now returns its effective destination; it used to return `None` unconditionally, so no caller changes). (3) new `z_reference_reachable()` + `_zoff_drop_unreachable_z_references()` — a reference mapping to a raw position the machine's OWN envelope rejects is provably unusable, so it is **dropped and flagged `⚠ stale — re-teach`, never clamped** (absent is recoverable; a silently corrected travel height is a wrong move that looks right). (4) `_safe_navigate_to`'s `_safe_z or 0.0` fallback — zero-ref 0 **IS** the hard bottom here, so the fallback was itself a descent; it now derives via `default_travel_z(ref, 10 mm)` and **refuses** when there is nothing to derive from, returns a bool, and no longer logs "Safe navigate to …" after an abort. (5) `_on_park` swallowed the failure and armed a measurement session whose validity rests on the stage being where it thinks it is. **⚠ The operator must re-teach Fast Move / Max / Replace Z** — they come up flagged on next launch. **P5 — the CONTACT touch-off recorded no plate-bottom anchor XY** (operator: *"the preflight says the taught plate bottom has no recorded xy. I did the touch-off based method because the optical method didnt work"*). `set_plate_bottom_z(at_xy_um=…)` had **one** production caller — the *optical* path; the contact touch-off routed through `_zoff_set_plate_bottom_z`, which never pushed to the controller at all, and app.py's untagged re-push passes `at_xy_um=None`, a deliberate **no-op not a clear**. So the documented FALLBACK path could never satisfy a gate that is correct and must not be relaxed (the survey measures TILT only, so the taught scalar is the plane's single absolute point and `PlateLeveling.solve` would raise on a None anchor). Fixed with one page-level writer `_zoff_push_plate_bottom_to_controller` fronting every push (so a new capture path cannot forget again), **persistence** of `plate_bottom_anchor_xy_um` + `plate_bottom_z_source` (the anchor lived only in memory, so even the optical path lost it on restart), and new `clear_plate_bottom_anchor()` called by the needle-cam **estimate** — a guess is measured NOWHERE on the plate, and leaving a prior touch-off's XY would anchor the plane at the right place and the WRONG height. ⚠ A first cut called `_read_xy_um`, which belongs to `_MosaicScanWorker`, **not** the page — the existing v7.13 suite caught it immediately. **P4 — Map-wells labelled the plate 180° out** (operator: *"the map wells does a good job finding the wells now, but the map is flipped"*). `PlateWellDetector.fit_lattice` canonicalises to the near-0° branch so its `row`/`col` anchor at the **min-pixel** corner, and its own comment says *which corner is really A1 stays the caller's decision, made from the plate-orientation convention, not from pixels* — a 180°-rotated lattice fits equally well and nothing in the image breaks the tie. `_auto_detect` made no such decision and equated detector indices to plate indices; on `plate_flip_180=True` (⇒ sign `(-1,-1)`, A1 = max-stage corner) every name landed on the diagonally opposite well. Fixed with ONE shared rule beside the existing authority — `MosaicWellRemap.orient_lattice_index()`, **per-axis** (each axis flips on its own sign; only both-negative reduces to a rotation) — pinned by a test that walks a real 24-well plate and asserts it agrees with `label_positions` on all 24, because two answers to one question is what caused this. **🔴 P4b — THE P4 FIX CORRUPTED THE STORED MOSAIC, found on the bench and repaired** (operator: *"I am not able to set the mosaic scan in the right position now. the bottom right part of the mosaic scan picture should be at the xy-stage 0,0 as a default"* — right on both counts, and the second half explains the first). **The convention was never broken:** the default whole-plate scan spans the entire reachable XY envelope, so the mosaic's world-min corner IS stage (0,0) less half a FOV and the 180° display flip renders it bottom-right. What broke is the stored EXTENT, via one line that leaks a NAME into a POSITION — `_ploc_feed_affine`'s `if name == "A1": self._taught_a1 = (sx, sy)`. `_taught_a1` is the mosaic's plate-frame anchor and `MosaicStore.reanchor` rigid-translates every stored extent by any change in it, assuming a changed anchor always means the plate MOVED. Here it meant the LABELLING was corrected while `plate_frame.extent_mm` was still recorded against the old corner — two incompatible definitions of "A1" straddling a saved artifact. Verified against the last committed `plate_mosaics.json`: correct extent `[-1652.3,-1652.3,117979.3,75887.3]` → stored `[93291.3,55369.5,212922.9,132909.0]`, a translation of **(+94943.6, +57021.8) µm — the plate diagonal**, putting X max at 212923 µm on a 116340 µm machine; the manual nudge sliders are ±10 mm so the operator genuinely COULD NOT put it back. **Fixed two ways: (code)** `reanchor` refuses a shift beyond `MAX_REANCHOR_SHIFT_UM` (25 mm) — a re-seat is millimetres, and at ~95 mm this is a frame mismatch, so leave the extent alone and say so (re-scanning is recoverable; a silently relocated mosaic is not, since every well centre mapped off it inherits the error). ⚠ **My first cut made that limit a FRACTION of the mosaic's own size and the v7.13 suite caught it immediately** — a SINGLE-WELL scan is ~2 mm across, so any fraction of it is smaller than a real remount and a legitimate 3.5 mm re-teach was refused; a re-seat is a physical quantity bounded by the plate holder, not by how much of the plate was imaged, so the limit is ABSOLUTE (pinned by a single-well test). **(data)** the operator's `nest-plastic-24` scan was restored to the provable extent (round-trips exactly to envelope + half FOV, matches the committed copy to 0.1 µm) with `plate_frame.extent_mm` re-derived against the NEW anchor so the record is self-consistent and the next re-anchor is a no-op; backed up as `plate_mosaics.json.bak-v791anchor`. ⚠ **Separately, three single-well scans are ALSO off-envelope and are NOT ours** — `#A2/#A3/#A4` moved by **(+95487.0, +55767.7) µm**, a DIFFERENT vector, and carry no `plate_frame` so `reanchor` never touched them (pre-existing damage from an earlier `_ploc_apply_global_translation`); left alone and reported rather than reverted on a guess, with the committed values recorded in the plan doc. **P1 — the rosette Layout tab could not load a bundled plate** (operator: *"i cant add a rosette to a plate because the plate i have selected doesnt load"*). `RosettePlacementPage` renders only a plate carried by `plate_doc_id`, but the library legitimately offers **bundled** cards (bare standard formats and `PlateType` products) and `_on_active_plate_changed` *clears* `plate_doc_id` for them by design — they have no document. Every saved hardware config in this repo is in that state and a fresh install's library contains nothing else, so the tab was dead. **AND a second defect the operator hit the moment the tab became reachable — stamping still did nothing, with a clean terminal.** Their saved fork proves it: the grid carried an override under member key `""`. A member key names ONE generated well (`g0_0`); `""` is the PATTERN. Three compounding pre-existing v7.12 behaviours: (a) `_press_select`'s *"a pattern member selects its FEATURE unless Alt is held"* — right in the BUILDER where you edit pattern params, but a standard plate is **one `GridPattern`**, so every Layout click yielded the pattern (new `set_member_pick_default(True)` inverts it there; Alt still reaches the pattern; the builder is unchanged); (b) `place_rosette` **accepted** a `PatternFeature` with an empty member and wrote `override("")` — a rosette saved to disk, rendered nowhere, no error — now refused; (c) `_stamp` swallowed every `KeyError` silently, which is why the terminal was clean — it now logs and tells the operator what to click. Plus a load-time filter dropping empty-member overrides so already-written files heal themselves. **⭐ AND a THIRD round the operator asked for once placement worked — *"when i place a rosette into the layout tab, it should look like the rosette chosen"*:** `_draw_rosette_badge` painted a **hard-coded ring of six dots for every placement**, so a 3-channel insert and a 6-channel one were indistinguishable, the bore sizes were fictional, and the rotation the operator had set was invisible. It now draws the rosette's OWN sub-wells (real count, diameters, positions, rotated by `rotation_deg`) from NEW `PlateDocument.rosette_subwell_offsets()` — which **`compile()` now also uses**, so the picture cannot drift from what prints (two copies of that arithmetic is exactly what produced the flipped well map). Seating stays rotation-only at the AUTHORED size, never scaled to fit, so a rosette bigger than its well is a real design problem the operator can now SEE rather than one the renderer normalises away; the six-dot mark survives solely for an unresolvable rosette, which `compile()` deliberately keeps and `validate()` reports by name. ⚠ **THREE of my own tests here were too weak and mutations caught them**, both traps this file already records: the override test asserted on `placements()`, which walks `evaluate()` and so never sees a `""` member (0 either way), and the refusal test asserted `"well" in hint` which the IDLE hint already satisfies ("select wells…") — rewritten against the raw `overrides` dict and against the pre-stamp text plus a distinctive phrase; and the "drawing matches what compiles" test compared the drawing against the shared HELPER, which says nothing about whether `compile()` still uses it, so reverting `compile()` to its own arithmetic survived — rewritten against a real `compile()` result (centroid-relative, since the two frames differ by a translation) plus an AST test naming the single authority. Fixed with the builder's own convention — *standards auto-fork on first edit*: `_active_plate_document(materialize=True)` stands up an unsaved `PlateDocument.from_standard_format(...)` (carrying `plate_type_id` so the product's Z offsets and per-plate stores still resolve), cached per `(format, type_id)`; `_on_placements_changed` saves it on the first placement and re-points `plate_doc_id`. ⚠ **`materialize` defaults to False and that is load-bearing** — my first cut made it unconditional and the v7.12 suite caught a real regression: `_rebuild_config` copies `doc.meta.name` into `plate_name`, which sits in `active_plate_key`'s precedence chain, so a bundled plate would have been rekeyed to `"Copy of 24-well"`, repointing the mosaic, taught calibration and well-training stores for a plate the operator never forked (pinned by an AST test that `_rebuild_config` passes no keyword). **P2 — choose which Z references get a quick-move badge** (operator request): a "Quick move" tick column beside the capture buttons in Advanced Z references, persisted (`calibration.z_ref_visible`) and fanned out to every `XZSideView` host (Jog, both calibration views, the three pick-and-place workflow pages) via new `set_visible_z_references`. **Presentation only, deliberately** — hiding by writing `None` into `get_z_references()` would have been simpler and would have disarmed the controller's print-floor datum and blanked the Hardware Info card, so visibility is a SEPARATE dict and the values are untouched (pinned by test). Two existing tests updated to assert the STRONGER P5 contract (`at_xy_um` **and** `source`), and the v7.11 AST provenance guard extended to follow the new shared writer rather than be defeated by the delegation, plus a new test that the writer itself forwards both. Tests: NEW `test_v791_calibration_and_layout_fixes.py` (**63**) + NEW `test_v791_mosaic_reanchor_guard.py` (**11**, incl. a check against the operator's real repaired store), built on this machine's REAL saved numbers so a synthetic envelope cannot let a wrong fix look right); **22/22 mutations confirmed CAUGHT**, sources verified restored. Regression **~1,280 green across 5 per-batch runs** (z-safety + plate-bottom 298 · orientation + plate-documents 447 · XZ/jog/context 110 · bore-safety + travel 271 · bed-level math 154) plus a `gui.app` import smoke and offscreen builds of the real `CalibrationPage` and `HardwareSetupPage`. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER — P3 first, nothing else is trusted until it passes:** the three stale refs must read `⚠ stale — re-teach` on launch → confirm the park now REFUSES in-panel instead of moving → re-teach Fast Move Z → the survey park retracts, travels, then descends to 0.5 mm above the glass (**watch the needle, not the screen**) → contact touch-off then Plate Bed Level preflight passes, and still passes after a restart → the needle-cam estimate correctly goes back to refusing → Map wells puts **A1 top-left** and two named wells drive true → the Layout tab renders a bundled plate and stamping forks it → unticked badges vanish on the Jog page and survive a restart. |
| `MEBP_v716_ND3_CONTAINER_FORMAT.md` | v7.16 **the `.nd3` container format — an ND2-like multidimensional image container WE control, holding everything needed to reconstruct images elsewhere** (operator: *"lets make the best file system that stores all that we need to reconstruct images elsewhere. we can call the file system .nd3 we will use this in other software like nd2 studios blender, and lab link"*). Writing real ND2 was investigated and REJECTED (proprietary; every open library is read-only; NIS-Elements is the only writer and the only oracle) — `.nd3` keeps ND2's semantics and adds what ND2 never carried here: **dual-frame georeferencing (absolute stage µm AND A1-relative plate mm per image)** plus **explicit 3×3 pixel→world affines, stored and authoritative** — this repo's frame/sign-bug history is exactly why derived transforms are written down rather than re-derived by each consumer. AskUserQuestion (3): container = **HDF5 (h5py)** (over ZIP+npy and an ND2-style chunk binary; buys chunked partial reads of 12k-px mosaics, per-dataset gzip + Fletcher32 checksums, and extendable datasets for time-lapse — traded: consumers need `pip install h5py`, incl. into Blender's Python) · exporters v1 = **all four** (fluorescence wells / plate mosaics / captures / time-lapse) · **backend + spec only**, no GUI this pass. NEW **`docs/ND3_SPEC.md`** — the normative spec, a first-class deliverable third parties implement readers from (worked numeric example, conformance checklists, §14 LabLink annex). NEW **`SupportClasses/ND3.py`** — writer/reader/verify/sniff; **stdlib + numpy + h5py ONLY, no repo imports, vendorable as a single file** (pinned by an AST-walk test AND a subprocess test that copies it alone to a tmp dir and round-trips). Tree: root attrs (`format="nd3"`, `schema_version` major.minor — same-major opens, higher-major refused) · `/images/<id>/data` (chunked ≤1024-px tiles, gzip-4, Fletcher32) with `axes`/`pixel_format` attrs · `meta_json`/`preview_png`/`dataset_json`/attachments as 1-D uint8 bytes datasets (**fixed-length HDF5 strings are UNSAFE for binary** — a payload ending 0x00, possible in a PNG CRC, is indistinguishable from NUL padding). Axes = subsequence of `TCZYXS` (Y,X required; S trailing, size 2–4); `pixel_format` REQUIRED (the field is the contract — readers must check, never assume); ids `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+`, case-insensitively unique, **REFUSED not sanitized** (the v7.12 many-to-one-sanitizer lesson); planes = one record per non-YXS index in C-order, count validated; atomic tmp+`os.replace` with abort-on-exception leaving NOTHING; `begin_stack` returns a frame appender (maxshape None on T — the future live-recording hook); `verify(deep=True)` chunk-reads everything (drives checksum validation) and names the offending entry. NEW **`SupportClasses/ND3Export.py`** — where MEBP vocabularies translate to the spec and **cv2's BGR dies** (color = RGB at export, byte-swap pinned at the pixel level). `export_fluorescence_well` = **one image per channel, deliberately NOT a CYX stack** (each channel owns extent/shift/scale/frozen display levels; grids can legitimately mismatch and ship VERBATIM — a stack would force the display path's lossy resize and imply radiometric comparability across different display levels); `"Bright Field"` fails the id charset → fixed bijective table to `Bright_Field`, TRUE name kept in the channel entry, unknown-invalid names refused. `export_plate_mosaic` = ACTIVE scan + v7.13 `plate_frame` + `wells_um` in dataset meta. `export_capture` = CaptureMeta split into acquisition/plane-geometry/orientation; a view rotation/flip ⇒ `pixels_stage_aligned: false` and **NO transforms**. `export_time_lapse` = `RawFrameSequenceWriter` dir (or in-memory frames) → one extendable TYX stack with per-frame `t_iso`/`t_s`. **THE THREE FRAME RULES, pinned by tests:** (1) trusted pixel origin = `extent_um[:2] − shift_um`, and `shift_known: false` = legacy/UNKNOWN not zero (mirrors `has_shift()`); (2) **⚠ the pitch trap — a mosaic composite's pixel pitch is `1/mosaic_scale`, NOT `um_per_px`** (the camera pitch; the canvas is downscaled — verified in `MosaicBuilder.tile_rects_px`: row 0 = min_y, +row = +stage-Y, no Y flip); tests seed the two DIFFERENTLY so a mix-up fails; (3) never fabricate — no anchor ⇒ `needs_plate_frame: true` and no plate matrix; `pixel_to_plate_mm` tied to `MosaicStore.stage_to_plate_mm` by a mapped-well round-trip test. **⭐ LabLink bridge (operator request mid-implementation):** LabLink's contract (`lablink/docs/IMAGE-JOB-FORMAT.md`) is TIFF/ND2 + a `.job.json` sidecar (`lablink.imagejob/1`) where **the sidecar OVERRIDES the image** — a TIFF *invents* container bit depth and a placeholder channel name; NEW `export_lablink_job(nd3, image_id, out_dir)` writes the pair, refusing rather than guessing: sensor bit depth parsed from `acquisition.bit_depth` or passed explicitly, **array dtype NEVER used as fallback** (it IS the invented container value); emission/excitation wavelengths are recorded NOWHERE in MEBP so they are caller-supplied or absent — LabLink's `missing_metadata` refusal names the fields (its designed remediation loop), which beats fabricating nominal fluorophore values; stems violating LabLink's upload-name rule refused with a `stem=` escape (true name survives in `source.original_name`); T/C/Z stacks refused (one image per job). 🐞 Found while testing: an `ND3Image` used after its reader closed hit h5py's cryptic *"invalid identifier type to function"* — pixel accessors now raise a clear "reader is closed" error. `requirements.txt` += `h5py>=3.12` (wheels only; the numpy 2.4.6 numba pin verified untouched). Tests: NEW `test_v716_nd3_container.py` (**53**: every whitelisted dtype incl. uint16>255 — the exact reason CaptureImageWriter refuses 16-bit PNG; the refusal matrix one-guard-one-test; corrupted-payload deep verify vs shallow; laziness; atomicity; purity; a raw-h5py consumer simulation with NO ND3.py on the read path) + NEW `test_v716_nd3_export.py` (**37** incl. 9 LabLink-bridge). **6/6 mutations confirmed CAUGHT** (BGR swap removed · pitch→um_per_px · shift_known forced true · needs_plate_frame dropped · id sanitized · version gate loosened), sources restored hash-identical. Regression per-suite: fluorescence-mosaic 35 · capture-core 47 · plate-mosaic 110 with only the documented pre-existing `test_real_24_well_mosaic` failure (hang class `TestManualAlignPage` excluded per precedent) · `gui.app` import smoke. **E2E on this machine's REAL stores** (`logs/nd3/`): `nest-plastic-24\|C5` FITC+Cy5 → 13.3 MB, deep verify OK, extents EXACT vs the store, and the honesty flags fired on real data (`shift_known=False` — genuine pre-v7.8 records; `needs_plate_frame=True`); plate `24` → 2.8 MB, extent EXACT, plate frame honestly ABSENT (pre-v7.13 scan); both re-read with raw h5py+json — the Blender/LabLink path. **Deferred (hook points recorded):** `.nd3` as a still-capture format option (`capture_controller.py::_do_still`, the single choke point) + an "Export .nd3" button on the Fluorescence Mosaic page · per-`scan_id` mosaic export (v1 = ACTIVE scan) · live recording straight to .nd3 via the `begin_stack` appender · a LabLink-side doc/worker update to accept `.nd3` natively (they'd vendor `ND3.py` or implement from the spec — needs a decision in the lablink repo, not this one). |
| `MEBP_v79x_JOG_STEP_SLIDERS.md` | v7.9.x **jog step SLIDERS with setpoint detents + µL-native pump steps + a collapsible step-settings section** (operator: *"we also need lower total volume for the pumps, lets make a slider bar for each axis … with setpoints close to 0.001, 0.005, 0.01, 0.05, 0.1, 0.5, 1, 5, 10 uL … a check box for snap to nearest, and custom range … via a collapsible settings section which is collapsed by default"*). **ROOT CAUSE of "we need lower total volume": the pump step was a % OF THE SYRINGE VOLUME** (`0.1/0.5/1/5/10 %` → `pump_pct_to_uL`), so on a 250 µL syringe the SMALLEST selectable step was 0.1 % = **0.25 µL — 250× the 0.001 µL asked for** — and the ladder's meaning drifted with the syringe. A % ladder structurally cannot express a sub-µL dose, so the step became a **µL VOLUME**: `jog_pump_requested` now carries signed µL on the %/µL pages (unchanged mm on Hardware Setup) and the array declares which via **`pump_step_is_uL`** (was `pump_step_is_percent`), which the panel READS rather than assuming so a future embedder cannot silently dose 100× wrong. The five preset magnitude buttons per axis (`_StepGroup`) are replaced by NEW `_StepSlider` — a **log-scale slider** with two models: snap ON (one position per setpoint, ticks under each; walking it end-to-end yields the ladder verbatim) and snap OFF (1000 log-interpolated positions, 3 s.f.); the value box doubles as the live readout and still accepts any typed value **including outside the range**, where only the slider position clamps. NEW collapsible **"Step slider settings"** (COLLAPSED by default) with the snap checkbox + a per-axis min/max range. **Snap is a display/selection model only — the step VALUE stays authoritative across a toggle**, so flipping the checkbox never re-rounds a typed value; an invalid range (min ≥ max, ≤ 0, junk) is **REFUSED, not clamped** (a clamped range leaves the slider disagreeing with the boxes describing it), and the edits snap back. **`isHidden()` NOT `isVisible()` for the disclosure state** — `isVisible()` is False while any ancestor is unshown, so a toggle keyed on it refuses to open on a page not yet displayed and reads as "collapsed" to anything inspecting it (CLAUDE.md records the `isVisible() or not isHidden()` tautology; exactly one is chosen, with the reason in the code). Settings (snap + per-axis range + the step VALUE) persist via a NEW `StageController.set/get_jog_step_settings` store + `jog.step_settings`, re-seeded on the same hooks as the speeds — the nine jog tiles cannot diverge, and a chosen low volume stays chosen instead of resetting to 1 µL on the next page. **🐞 Fixed en route: `settings_page._ctx_jog_pump` fed the array's distance to `move_pump_relative`, which expects PLUNGER MM** — with the old % array a "1 %" step commanded a **1 mm plunger move**; the units never matched. The write-only `_pump_step_is_uL` ctor field + its zero-caller `set_pump_step_mode` are deleted (a same-named-but-different-meaning flag beside the new property is the "two owners of one fact" trap). Tests: NEW `test_v79x_jog_step_sliders.py` (**16**, incl. the ladder walked verbatim, the geometric-mean midpoint proving snap is really off, the refusal set, and an **AST** check on the settings-page handler — not `in getsource()`, the recorded trap where a substring check passed on a comment); **4 mutations confirmed CAUGHT** (% conversion restored · expanded by default · clamp-instead-of-refuse · seed removed). Regression **~280 green per-suite** across jog-step-sliders/per-pump-jog-flow/responsive-context-panel/common-axis-speed/axis-max-speed/jog-direction-z-up-sign/context-panel/jog-navigation/stage-jog-off-thread/jog-pump-fill/pump-plunger/jog-motion/xbox-speed/per-pump-max-rate/illumination/suite-hygiene, plus a `gui.app` import smoke and offscreen builds of the real jog panel, `HardwareSetupPage`, and the no-pump calibration Z-teach variant at 120↔520 px. **Needs GUI verification on ME3B V1** (P slider far-left reads 0.001 µL and each detent steps the ladder; a 0.001 µL click actually moves the plunger — below one microstep is a firmware floor, not a UI bug; snap off sweeps continuously; a custom range narrows the ladder; an inverted range is refused; the step survives a page switch and a restart; Hardware Setup still jogs in mm). |
| `MEBP_v79x_PER_PUMP_JOG_FLOW_AND_POSITION_READOUT.md` | v7.9.x **per-pump jog flow rate on every jog tile + Live-Position readout no longer clipped by the bar** (operator: *"on all jog tiles, each pump should get its own flow rate"* + *"the scroll bar being too long so we cant see the text of the position"*). **(1)** The shared jog panel had ONE pump % spin anchored to `get_max_pump_feedrate()` = the **fastest** configured pump's flow ceiling, so P1/P2/P3 all jogged at the same absolute µL/s; percent mode now builds **one flow row per pump** (`spin_p_pct_pumps`, sub-1% capable, unconfigured pumps hidden) with NEW `StageController.get_max_pump_feedrate_for(pump)` (that pump's OWN `SafetyLimits.get_max_flow_rate`; 10 µL/s default; legacy delegate) so `_on_jog_pump` runs the CLICKED pump's own % × its own ceiling on both the µL and legacy-mm branches. **Per-pump % state lives on the CONTROLLER** (`set_pump_jog_pct`/`get_pump_jog_pcts`) not the panels — nine pages each build their own `StandardJogContextPanel` and per-panel state silently diverges (the illumination-LED lesson) — persisted `jog.pump_jog_pct`, re-seeded on show (controller → settings-pushed-back → shared 'p' % fallback); the shared 'p' group stays the Xbox ladder value, untouched. **(2)** The position row's value label sat behind a fixed `s(20)` floor with an Ignored policy while the `PositionBar` owned the row's only stretch column, so the bar absorbed all width and the number clipped into the unit label; NEW `PositionValueLabel` — min width **tracks its text** (re-synced on `setText` + `FontChange` so the responsive font scaler keeps narrow panels fitting; cap `s(160)`) — and the BAR is now the yielding element (`Ignored`, `s(10)` min) in BOTH copies (control panel + `PositionReadoutCard`, whose old `Expanding`/`s(60)` bar + `s(70)` label overflowed the 100 px pane instead of scrunching). Tests: NEW `test_v75x_per_pump_jog_flow.py` (**14**, incl. a two-pumps-at-different-rates jog test + cross-panel/restart sync); **2 mutations confirmed CAUGHT** (shared-anchor revert · fixed-floor revert). Regression green per-suite: axis-max-speed-inputs/common-axis-speed-source/per-pump-max-rate/stage-jog-off-gui-thread/responsive-context-panel/context-panel/jog-pump-fill-readout/xbox-axis-speed-percent/jog-motion-interpolation/pump-plunger-setup/jog-navigation/nikon-ti/suite-hygiene (~300) + a `gui.app` import smoke. **Needs GUI verification on ME3B V1** (three per-pump rows on every jog tile; two pumps at different % jog at visibly different rates; the % set on one page shows on every other; restart restores; position numbers fully readable at normal width, bar shrinks first when narrow). |
| `MEBP_v713_MOSAIC_PLATE_FRAME_AND_SCAN_LIBRARY.md` | v7.13 **a mosaic is referenced to the PLATE as well as the stage, a plate may hold SEVERAL scans, and the plate selector shows them** (operator: *"in the plate selection tool, we have the ability to select the mosaic that will be used for that plate if there are multiple. make sure that the mosaic is referenced to the plate coordinate system and the xy stage coordinate system … we should be able to see the mosaic scan that we are selecting"*). **ASSESSED FIRST — most of the plate-frame half already existed** and is untouched: the plate-local frame is `get_well_position` (A1-relative mm), the default offset is `default_plate_center_um()` = the XY-envelope midpoint (v7.5.x `PLATE_CENTERING`), and the true offset is `taught_a1`+`plate_warp` archived per plate. **🐞 BUT THAT CENTRING HAD A BUG:** `get_a1_from_plate_center` used **hardcoded ANSI constants**, so any carrier that is not 127.76×85.48 defaulted off by half the footprint difference — **33.9×22.7 mm for a 60×40 carrier**, far outside any well. NEW cached `WellPlate.footprint_mm` resolves the AUTHORED footprint from the `PlateDocument` boundary (falling back to the ANSI value it used to hardcode, so standard plates are unaffected); every format now centres its outline EXACTLY. **MosaicStore v1.0→v2.0.** A plate key holds `{active, scans:{id: …}}`; single-well `plate#well` entries stay bare leaves. **⭐ THE PUBLIC API DID NOT CHANGE** — `get_meta` resolves the container to the ACTIVE scan and returns the pre-v7.13 shape, and `image_path`/`load_image`/`get_extent_um`/`get_shift_um`/`get_wells`/`has` are all built on it — which is what let this land **without touching the nine consumer modules and eleven other test files** that read this store (the same invariant that made v7.12 shippable). v1 files are normalised **in memory** on load, so opening the app does not churn the operator's data. **BOTH FRAMES:** each scan stores `plate_frame{extent_mm, anchor_um, axis_sign}` beside its stage extent; `stage_to_plate_mm`/`plate_mm_to_stage_um` are exact inverses for every axis sign and **re-normalise the rectangle** (a negative sign swaps corners, and an un-normalised extent silently breaks every `min_x` consumer). **THE CORRECTION IS APPLIED ONCE, AT RE-TEACH** — `reanchor()` called from `_save_calibration`, the one commit funnel every XY teach path reaches — rather than inside `get_extent_um`, so the **eight reader sites** keep calling the store unchanged and still see a corrected extent; `get_extent_um(key, anchor_um=…)` exists for a preview and defaults to the stored value (byte-identical for every existing caller). The manual nudge `_ploc_shift_mosaic_by` now RE-DERIVES the plate frame, or the next re-teach would undo the nudge. **MIGRATION = REQUIRE A RE-SCAN (operator's call):** existing scans carry no plate frame, are reported by `needs_rescan()` and flagged in the UI, and keep working EXACTLY as before (stage frame, manual re-anchor, full display) — they simply cannot follow a re-teach. A plate frame is deliberately **not** back-filled: it would have to assume the calibration live when the scan was taken, and a wrong assumption puts the mosaic — and every well centre mapped off it — silently in the wrong place (the v7.8 `has_shift` precedent). Verified against the operator's real store: all three scans report `needs_rescan` and still load. **THE SELECTOR:** `PlateThumbnail.set_mosaic` draws the scan behind the wells **at its own extent**, so a mosaic that does not cover the whole plate visibly does not; a scan with no known extent is **not drawn** (showing a registration that does not exist is worse than showing none). **⚠ A THIRD FRAME MIX-UP CAUGHT HERE:** a scan's plate frame is A1-**WELL**-relative while the thumbnail draws in the document's **storage** frame (origin = the boundary A1 datum) — identical on a standard plate, 7.39/9.89 mm apart on the operator's — so NEW `PlateDocumentStore.a1_well_offset_mm` is the one place that offset is computed. NEW **`MosaicPickerDialog`** replaces the `QInputDialog` list of plate KEYS: scan list + the actual image + tiles/date/reference status + Use / Rename / Import-from-another-plate / Delete, and **previewing a scan restores the active slot** so merely LOOKING at one never re-points the plate at it. Origin leaking into the compiled frame was the top-ranked risk: every well ~0.9 well-pitch off while *Go-To-A1 still looks right* (A1 is the anchor). **IDENTITY:** an opaque hex id generated once and never re-derived; **the filename IS the id**, so renaming touches only `meta.name` — no file move, no store key change. The id is `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]`-clean because the six filesystem stores share a **many-to-one** `re.sub` sanitiser (two names differing only in punctuation collide on one mosaic PNG). `meta.legacy_keys` + `plate_store_keys()` let a plate adopt its old name as a read-fallback, so adopting ids costs no taught calibration.
