| `MEBP_v710_CAMERA_ORIENTATION_AUDIT.md` | v7.10 **Full camera-orientation audit: ONE frame of reference across live view / hardware calibration / mosaic, plus a needle-camera calibration that actually uses the needle** (operator: *“All of these surfaces should agree on the same camera orientation and frame of reference for the stage … the live view should always be non mirrored looking to the user … everything should be referencing the smallest number of variables possible”* + *“the needle must move on the needle cameras axis to be able to judge rotation … we should check z motion, and a 45 degree stage motion to ensure it stays within the camera frame”*). **The CONVENTION was already right and singular** — every consumer applies `A = R(θ)·diag(mx,my)`, and `det A = −1` for a mirrored camera, so the display transform genuinely un-mirrors. What had diverged was everything AROUND it. **F1 — THREE implementations of the same store→manager precedence** (`MosaicCalibration._resolve_orientation`, a hand-rolled copy in `CalibrationPage._ploc_microscope_frame_orientation`, and `CameraFeedView._sync_auto_orientation` which read the **manager only, no store**); three copies agreeing the day they are written says nothing about the next edit — consolidated into one public `resolve_camera_orientation`. **🐞 F2 — AN OBJECTIVE SWITCH SILENTLY RE-ORIENTED THE LIVE VIEW.** `MosaicCalibration`'s docstring is explicit that the per-objective `rotation_deg` must never be read (*“keeping a second home for it is what let a stale per-objective value overwrite a freshly measured one”*) and it indeed never reads it — but **two other call sites did** (`objective_calibration_card._push_stored_um_per_px_to_manager`, `fluorescence_mosaic_workflow._on_objective_changed`), pushing it into the live `CameraManager` on every objective change. The mosaic reads the STORE and stayed right; the live view and `pixel_to_stage_offset` read the MANAGER and went wrong — same camera, two orientations, clicks mapping to the wrong XY, nothing on screen saying which angle was in force. The existing mitigation (`adopt_camera_rotation` syncing every objective entry) only covers objectives that exist at the time. The READ is now gone; rotation has exactly one live source. **🐞 F3 — A MEASURED ROTATION COULD BE THROWN AWAY BY A RESTART:** the per-objective µm/px calibration also measures a rotation and pushed it to the manager + the per-objective copy but **never the per-identity mount store** — so the mosaic kept the old angle and `restore_calibration_from_store` reverted the measurement on the next launch. One `commit_camera_rotation` now writes all three homes. **F4 — SIX live views showed RAW pixels** because `auto_orient` defaults False and is opt-in per call site. Worst: the **`hardware_setup` per-slot preview**, i.e. the very surface where the operator SETS mirror/flip-Y/rotation — ticking “Mirrored view” changed the mosaic and every other page while the preview six inches away did not move. Also both needle-cam feeds, the Custom-tab grid, `_ploc_live_view` (oriented, but by a **fourth** mechanism that only fired on bind/tab-entry), and `TargetOverlayCameraView`, where the parameter **could not be passed at all**, so every pick/place feed (spheroid, cell targeting, cell labeling) rendered raw. Two exemptions kept and stated in the test: the square-up dialog (corrects the flips, deliberately SHOWS the rotation being changed) and the hardware-settings preview (raw device output by design). **🐞 F5 — ORIENTING THE PICKER EXPOSED A LATENT INVERSE BUG:** `_PickerCameraView._image_to_widget` was a plain scale-and-offset and its docstring justified it (*“this branch bypasses `_orient_qimage`”*) — true only while the feed was raw. Oriented, every target ring/handle/hit-test would land elsewhere on a rotated camera: the v7.8 `to_px` identity-inverse bug returning at a different layer. Now maps through `_view_true_xform`. **🐞 F6 — A NEEDLE FEED MUST NOT ROTATE BY THE RESIDUAL (my first cut got this backwards; operator caught it on the bench: *“it kept shifting the live view to the rotation orientation. the entire point of this is that the live views do not rotate. they should be at fixed rotations on our screen corresponding to 0,90,180,270 … then the rotation needed is set and we try to match it”*).** The v7.5.x split left the roll measured, stored and applied nowhere, so I applied it — but correcting the residual in software is **self-defeating for a mount squared by hand**: the tilt being removed disappears from the view, and as the operator turns the camera the correction FOLLOWS them, so the picture never appears to change and there is no feedback at all (it also resamples every frame for a few degrees, where a cardinal turn is a lossless fast path). Split by what the value is FOR: **flips always applied** (the view is always un-mirrored) · **needle-cam rotation snaps to the nearest 0/90/180/270 on the DISPLAY, residual left visible** · **microscope keeps the exact angle** (unchanged — the operator reports that path working well) · and the **exact θ still drives all GEOMETRY** (`pixel_to_stage_offset`, `_orient_tile`), which must be right to a fraction of a degree. `CameraFeedView(snap_rotation_to_cardinal=)`, opt-in and OFF by default; a test builds a view WITHOUT the parameter because a mutation flipping the default survived the first version of that test. Side benefit: ±45 snaps to a cardinal, so the three orphaned Teslong entries still holding the pre-v7.5.x conflated value no longer tilt either. **PART 2 — THE NEEDLE CAMERAS. The hardware fact:** the side cams are bolted to the **XY stage** (`plus_column_direction_deg`'s own docstring says *“Moving the **cameras** by +m…”*), so an XY move translates needle AND background together while a **Z move moves the NEEDLE and nothing else**. **🐞 ONE LEG IS RANK-ONE AND OVER-ESTIMATED µm/px UNDETECTABLY:** a side camera sees only the component perpendicular to its optical axis, so a preset off-lateral by β makes `u = D/|p|` read **`1/cos β` too HIGH** — 6 % at 20°, **41 % at 45°** — with nothing able to detect it. The operator has been compensating by hand: clicking presets and keeping *“the longest arrow”* IS a manual search for `cos β → 1`. Lab-vertical is perpendicular to a horizontal optical axis whatever the azimuth, so **`u_z` is trustworthy and `u_lat/u_z = 1/cos β` recovers the very error that was invisible**. The Z leg also settles two assumptions: the **roll** was inferred from the lateral leg, which requires knowing where lateral IS (circular) — lab-vertical is KNOWN, so where it lands on the sensor IS the roll; and **“image rows map to stage Z”**, asserted in `TwoCameraNeedleAligner`'s docstring with the whole Z-centring depending on it, was never checked and its SIGN was a manual **Invert Z checkbox** — a guess that, got wrong, drove the needle the wrong way vertically. Now measured per camera as `z_row_sign`; **absent = never measured**, so the checkbox still governs an un-remeasured camera. **THE Z LEG STANDS ALONE** (operator: *“we should not have to do the xy version of the calibration for the needle cam rotation correction, the z is independent and if we want to trust that more than the xy we should be able to use that exclusively”* — my first cut gated the Z button on the XY leg, making the trustworthy measurement depend on the one it supersedes). The Z leg fully determines µm/px, roll and Z direction; the XY leg adds ONLY the aligner's `column_dir_deg` plus the two cross-checks that need a second vector. So the lateral leg is optional (`has_lateral`), `refusal()` judges only what was measured — the Z leg's own background-lock gate is NOT relaxed — and a Z-only commit leaves any previously measured mount direction **untouched** rather than discarding the result. Pinned by a test showing the same Z data REFUSED with a hopeless XY leg attached and ACCEPTED on its own. NEW pure `SupportClasses/NeedleCameraCalibration.py` (no Qt/cv2 at import): `solve_needle_camera_axes` → `NeedleCameraAxes`. **Verified by forward simulation over roll × optical azimuth × handedness × preset (200 combos): of the 130 geometrically usable ones, ALL recover µm/px to 4.4e-16, roll to 0.0° and orthogonality to 0.0°**; the other 70 are correctly REFUSED (optical-axis moves, or β > 46° where the scale is meaningless). It never raises — a degenerate input returns an object whose `refusal()` names the problem. Four refusals: XY leg barely moved · **Z leg barely moved = *“a Z move moves the NEEDLE and nothing else, so a near-zero reading means the tracker locked onto the stationary background”*** (which is why the Z leg uses `select_trackable_patch`+`find_template`, NOT the XY leg's whole-frame phase correlation — that would faithfully report the background's zero and call it a measurement) · legs not 90° apart (square pixels on a rigid scene must be perpendicular ⇒ different objects) · scale ratio outside [0.85, 1.45]. **🐞 IN-FRAME PRE-CHECK, because the two failure modes are INDISTINGUISHABLE:** “move went along the optical axis” and “feature left the sensor” both arrive as a tiny low-confidence displacement, so the move is bounded BEFORE it is commanded (~25 % of the shorter frame dimension), making a small reading mean the first — the one the operator can fix. Silent when µm/px is unknown (the first calibration must be allowed to run). **Safety:** the Z leg goes **UP first** via `move_z_user_relative` (height frame ⇒ polarity-safe on either `z_up_sign`) and returns in a `finally`; up is away from the plate, and a blind 200 µm descent could put a needle through glass. Also fixed: the needle-cam µm/px commit **dropped its resolution stamp**, so `effective_um_per_px` had nothing to rescale from and the aligner used a value correct at only one capture resolution. Tests: NEW `test_v710_camera_orientation_audit.py` (**50**), several pinned by SOURCE STRUCTURE rather than behaviour on purpose — three copies of a precedence rule agree until they don't, and a “does the mosaic still work” test **cannot see F2 at all** since the mosaic reads the store, which stayed correct throughout; `TestEveryFeedIsOriented` walks the `CameraFeedView(...)` call sites so a NEW un-oriented feed is caught, and self-guards by asserting it found ≥ 8 (a matcher that finds nothing passes). **23/23 mutations CAUGHT across two rounds**, each a real source edit reverted in a `finally`. ⚠ **One of my own tests was too weak and a mutation caught it:** the in-frame guard test asserted the string `in_frame_refusal` appears in the function source, which passes on the **import line alone**, so deleting the call and hard-coding “no refusal” SURVIVED — rewritten to find the `Call` node by AST plus a second test that the refusal gates an actual `return`. Regression **1,000+ green across 33 suites, run per-suite**, plus a `gui.app` import smoke and an offscreen build of the real `HardwareSetupPage` (4 previews, all `auto_orient`, a mirror+180° on the manager picked up by the preview; `needle_mode` shows the Z group only for a needle slot). **Two pre-existing failures PROVED not mine:** `test_v75x_plate_mosaic::test_real_24_well_mosaic` (23 vs 24 — the legacy blob detector; re-run with my changes **stashed**, still fails) and the documented `test_v79_cell_targeting_setup_page::test_the_real_saved_profile_reproduces_its_exact_volume` golden-file test over the operator's own re-saved profile. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER:** tick *Mirrored view* and confirm the **slot preview itself** now flips (it never did) → the Andor feed reads un-mirrored → switch objectives 4×→10×→4× and confirm the live view does NOT rotate, then restart and confirm the rotation survived → run per-objective **Calculate µm/px** and confirm its rotation survives a restart (it used to be discarded) → **needle cams: press *Measure Z leg* ON ITS OWN first** (no XY leg; the needle retracts ~200 µm and returns) and confirm it reports roll + µm/px + Z direction while saying the mount direction was left alone → **turn the camera in its mount and confirm the VIEW HOLDS STILL** at its quarter-turn while the reported residual changes (if the view swings to follow the correction, this regressed) → only if the aligner's mount direction needs re-measuring, run XY at ±45° then the Z leg and **check µm/px differs from the XY-alone figure** — that gap IS the foreshortening that used to be silent — with axes ≈ 90° apart → point the XY move down the optical axis and confirm it REFUSES rather than producing a number → ask for a 2000 µm Z leg and confirm the in-frame guard refuses BEFORE any motion → both side views should be un-mirrored and stable at a quarter-turn (NOT levelled by software), with edge-picking still landing on both crosshairs → Z-centring drives the right way with *Invert Z* left alone → pick/place feeds match the microscope's orientation and target rings still land where clicked. |
| `MEBP_v710_CAMERA_MOUNT_SQUARE_UP.md` | v7.10 **Square up a camera mount: a live, no-stage-motion rotation aid + the mirrored-camera rotation measurement fixed** (operator: *“we have a great way to detect camera rotation, but what i want to do is fix the camera rotation to as close to a 0,90,180,270 as possible by physically rotating the actual camera at the mount … monitor the features as i rotate the frame, it tells me to keep turning … rotate the calibrated view to the exact rotation we want, then set its alpha such that when i physically rotate the hardware it will allign with the frame. then we can recalculate the allignment”*). AskUserQuestion (3): **all four slots** · ghost = **photo blend + an edge-outline toggle** · **always store the measured residual** (no snapping to the exact cardinal). The app already MEASURED rotation and corrected it in software; the only feedback for removing it PHYSICALLY was a static slot-card line, pull-refreshed and never live. Squaring the mount is not cosmetic — at an exact multiple of 90° both `_orient_qimage` and `_orient_tile` hit their **no-op fast paths**, so the live feed and every mosaic tile stop being resampled. **🐞🐞 STAGE 0 — THE ROTATION MEASUREMENT IS WRONG FOR A MIRRORED CAMERA, AND THAT IS THIS RIG'S MICROSCOPE.** `plus_column_direction_deg` models the stage→image map as `scale·R(α)` and its OWN DOCSTRING says *“assumes a non-mirrored image”* — nothing ever acted on it. From `pto`'s contract (a plate feature's stage LABEL is invariant under stage motion) the forward model is `d = −(1/u)·F⁻¹·R(−θ)·m`, and **TWO** errors fall out: (1) `diag(−1,−1) == R(180)`, so `R(θ)·F` always reduces to `R(θ+180)·diag(1,−my)` when `mx = −1` — the helper is **180° out for ANY mirrored camera**; (2) conjugating a rotation by a reflection reverses it, so at `det F = −1` it returns **`2c − θ_eff`** — a reflection *about the commanded angle*, not a sign flip, so the reading **changes with whichever direction preset was clicked** and no single reading can look wrong. ⚠ **My first cut fixed only (2) and scored 56/112** on a forward simulation of all four flip combos; with both terms, **112/112 recover θ exactly**. The assigned microscope is `andor:VSC-07863` (`rotation_deg 180`, `mirrored true`) ⇒ `det F = −1`, so pressing the EXISTING **⟳ Rotation…** on it writes **90° instead of 180°** into `CameraManager`, `CameraCalibrationStore` **and every per-objective entry** via `adopt_camera_rotation` — pre-existing, but step 4 of the new tool routes the operator straight into it, so it is fixed first (in `_compute`, NOT in the pure helper whose contract/tests stand). Benign today only because `nearest_square(180) = (180, 0)`. **THE SIGN, derived once:** a scene point at `p` now appears at `R(φ)p` and its stage offset is a property of the plate, so `R(θ)F = R(θ')F·R(φ)` ⇒ `θ' = θ − φ` at `det F = +1` and `θ' = θ + φ` at `det F = −1`, giving **`φ_target = +Δ` unmirrored, `−Δ` net-mirrored**. Only `det F` is read, so `derive_camera_stage_orientation` always parking handedness on `flip_y` is irrelevant (pinned). **⭐ A ROUND-TRIP TEST CANNOT VALIDATE THIS — the ghost and the numeric readout BOTH derive from `φ_target`, so they are consistent BY CONSTRUCTION and lie together if the mirror condition is backwards**; `estimate(ref, make_ghost(ref, φ)) ≈ φ` passes either way. `TestClosedLoopSign` closes the loop through the REAL `pixel_to_stage_offset` instead — build `θ' = θ ∓ φ`, assert 20 random pixels report the same stage offset before and after — and never mentions φ's sign. Likewise the ghost warp has a **second, absolute anchor**: mutation-confirmed that flipping the sign in `make_ghost` AND the estimator together leaves the round-trip perfectly green and fails ONLY the anchor. **WHAT IS DELIBERATELY NOT DERIVED: which way to turn.** Physical-turn → image-rotation direction depends on the optical path (an odd mirror count reverses it), which software cannot know — so the tool says *“start turning either way”* and **latches the direction empirically** after 2° of measured motion, saying **“wrong way — turn back”** when the residual grows. Right on any rig, and a sign error costs two seconds instead of a wrong result; a **Flip ghost direction** button recovers in one click. **🐞 THE 180° UNWRAP WAS DESIGNED, THEN DELETED — IT CANNOT FIRE:** FM's `conf` is the TRANSLATION response measured *after* de-rotating by the estimated angle, so a wrong log-polar branch de-rotates wrongly and it collapses — measured 80° → conf **0.83**, 100° → **0.010**, 180° → 0.015. A wrapped sample can never pass the gate, so the unwrapper would have been dead code with a way to be wrong (and `|Δ| ≤ 45°` by construction anyway). Replaced by fold-to-(−90,90] + gate + *“lost lock”*, pinned by test. **MEASURED CONSTANTS:** `TRACK_SIZE = 256` — 192²/256²/384²/512² cost 5.0/9.6/22.9/43.7 ms for worst errors 0.417/0.255/0.247/0.221°, so 512 costs 4.5× for nothing against a 1° tolerance. **Accuracy pinned by test: EXACTLY 0.000° when the frames coincide** — the reading the operator stops on — and short by ≤0.58° near 2°, the benign direction (the residual reads a touch large, so they turn slightly further). Sampling runs on a daemon worker: FM genuinely releases the GIL (164 ms serial vs 70 ms threaded, **2.36×**), and the views already spend 6–33 ms/frame in `QImage.transformed`. **🐞 THE GHOST CACHE IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT AN OPTIMISATION** — `QImage.transformed(SmoothTransformation)` measures **33 ms at 3664×2748**, so re-orienting the ghost per frame would halve the GUI thread's budget; the dialog also throttles the slot card's own preview (two live views on a 10 Mpx camera stack ~82 ms/frame). **🐞 And a naive `drawImage(0,0)` of the 256² tracking crop would land a small square in the CORNER** — the ghost is built from the full reference frame (≤720 px) and composited in DISPLAY space after the downscale (sub-ms), pinned by sampling the rendered centre AND both corners. The feed shows **flips but NOT rotation** (`rotation_deg=0`): *correct what is not changing, show what is*. **🔴 THE NEEDLE-CAMERA GATE IS NOT THEORETICAL:** `role_nominal_rotations` returns the ±45° DIAGONALS for a needle role, but a needle cam's `rotation_deg` is the sensor ROLL (nominal 0°) with the mount in `column_dir_deg` — so the tool squares against `(0,90,180,−90)` for EVERY role, never `nominal_rotation_delta`. Worse, **three entries in this machine's store still hold the pre-v7.5.x conflated value** (`rotation_deg` 45.0 and two −45.0 on Teslong identities): the v1.0→1.1 migration only converts identities that were ASSIGNED at the time, and these were not. Reading a 45° mount as a roll would command a 45° physical turn, rolling the camera out of level and scaling its column µm/px by `cos 45° = 0.707` — a 41 % error straight into `TwoCameraNeedleAligner`, i.e. the needle driven to the wrong XY. Gate: a needle-role slot must have `column_dir_deg` present, else refuse and point at ⟳ Rotation… (mutation-confirmed). Rolling a PROPERLY calibrated needle cam stays safe — roll leaves the column's XY-plane projection direction unchanged, only scaling its magnitude by `cos ψ`. **STALENESS, the v7.10 bore-gate lesson applied:** the mirror/flip flags live on the card BEHIND the dialog and flipping either inverts `det F` and therefore `φ_target` with no visual cue — so `full_orientation` and θ are re-read every 300 ms tick and the ghost re-rendered (mutation-confirmed); a frozen `frame_count_value()` for >1.5 s reports *“feed stopped”* instead of holding the last value forever (`_grab_frame` calls `stop()` on a read failure); a resolution change is REFUSED rather than hidden by `prepare_frame`'s normalisation; the button gate is refreshed from the SAME two paths as `rot_btn`; and `_on_calibrate_slot_rotation` now **returns whether it committed** (it exits early on five paths) so a cancelled re-measure reports *“Not re-measured”* rather than success. **The tool WRITES NOTHING** — tracking commands no motion and persists nothing; the committed value always comes from step 4 through the host's existing commit path, and the worker is STOPPED before that dialog launches because it moves the stage. **Helper de-duplication:** `wrap_deg`/`fold_parallel_deg`/the nominal search now have exactly ONE implementation each in the Qt-free module, re-exported by `hardware_setup` and `pixel_calibration_dialog` and asserted with `assertIs` so a copy cannot creep back; `MosaicBuilder` is **lazy-imported** inside the estimator (importing it eagerly costs ~483 ms and drags skimage/scipy into the camera path). NEW `SupportClasses/CameraRotationTracker.py` (pure, GUI-free) + NEW `gui/dialogs/camera_rotation_align_dialog.py` (dialog, custom-painted `_ResidualDial`, worker + bridge) + `CameraFeedView.set_alignment_ghost` + a per-slot **⊾ Square up mount…** button + a pointer from `mosaic_calibration_confirm_dialog` when the measurement is >2° off square. **Advisories (documented, deliberately NOT automated — all fail SAFELY):** `ReanchorFeatureStore`/`NeedleFocusTemplateStore` hold raw patches and `matchTemplate` is not rotation-invariant so they stop matching (their confidence gates refuse) — re-save them; saved mosaic composites stay valid (`_orient_tile` bakes tiles into STAGE axes); µm/px is rotation-invariant; ⚠ **a C-mount thread also translates the sensor ~0.10 mm axially over 45°** (a large defocus at 20× — squaring a threaded mount is not free); ⚠ a **non-square sensor landing on ±90° leaves silent mosaic coverage gaps** because `_orient_tile` keeps the tile W×H while the scan steps by the unrotated FOV. Tests: NEW `test_v710_camera_mount_square_up.py` (**64**), **8 mutations confirmed CAUGHT** (swap ±Δ · `XOR`→`OR` · drop the mirror branch · **`make_ghost` + estimator both flipped, which the round-trip cannot see** · ghost cache disabled · Stage 0 correction removed · needle-cam legacy gate removed · `φ_target` snapshotted at construction); regression **985 green across 38 suites, run per-suite** (needle-cam-mount-roll/camera-rotation×2/needle-center-direction/scale-fov/unified-mosaic/calibration-revision/cal-liveview/camera-store/image-correction/hw-controls/async-open/picker-scaling/reanchor/mosaic-orientation×2/memory-perf/mapping-orient/fluor-shift/fluorescence/mosaic-unreachable/simulated-camera/tucsen/andor/suite-hygiene/objective-cal/needle-location/the five v7.10 bore suites/bore-offset/plate-z-autocal×2/hardware-config-bores) plus a `gui.app` import smoke and a real `HardwareSetupPage()` build. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER:** **Stage 0 FIRST and nothing else is trusted until it passes** — on the Andor, ⟳ Rotation… must return ≈180° **from two different direction presets** (preset-dependence IS the bug's signature) → open the tool on the microscope (expect *“already square”*, Δ=0) → loosen and turn a few degrees, confirm the readout tracks and the direction word locks on → align to ≤1°, lock, re-measure, and **confirm the committed θ matches the readout's prediction and NOT its negation** (twice the tilt on the wrong side = flip the ghost and repeat) → cancel a re-measure and confirm it says *“Not re-measured”* → the feed should be visibly sharper → re-save the re-anchor feature and confirm auto re-anchor still finds it. |
| `MEBP_v711_OPTICAL_PLATE_BED_LEVELING.md` | v7.11 **automated optical plate-bed leveling — the microscope measures the plate’s TILT and installs it as a `PlateZPlane`, with the needle retracted throughout** (operator: *“the user will focus on that feature at the bottom of the plate for each objective … the software will then attempt to auto-refocus onto that exact same feature … focus 1mm above and below … in increments that gradient descend towards a focus to build a pointspread … from this we attempt to find a plane of best focus … based on a known algorithm and be verified by the user”*, then *“after a focal plane point is verified by a user for a plate type, we want the software to try to do the focus automatically for the next one … there will be slight plate to plate differences”*). **THIS IS STAGE 7 (“Mode B”) OF `MEBP_v75x_PLATE_Z_PLANE_AUTOCAL.md`, WHICH WAS NEVER IMPLEMENTED** — and the whole consumption path was ALREADY BUILT AND TESTED WITH ZERO PRODUCTION CALLERS (`PlateZPlane.from_focal_readings`, `StageController.set_plate_z_plane`, `PrintManager.well_print_z_zref_mm`). The discarded tilt is not hypothetical: this machine’s own saved calibration records **0.0199 mm/mm ≈ 1.15 mm across a 24-well plate’s row span**, 2–10× a typical print height. AskUserQuestion (4 + 2 follow-ups): anchor = the taught plate bottom (**purely optical run, the needle never touches glass**) · feature **auto-proposed with operator override** · **operator-configured objective ladder** · wizard-style, this tab becomes about the plate BED · **sweep range = a fraction of WORKING DISTANCE** · plate-wide tolerance **better than 50 µm** · **site 1 taught, sites 2..N automatic**. **🐞🐞 SIX BLOCKERS, THREE SILENT. (B1) `get_microscope().state` MISSING ITS `()`** (`needle_bore_wizard.py:1041`) — `state` is a METHOD, so `getattr(bound_method,“has_focus”,False)` was always False, inside a broad `except`: `_microscope_focus_um()` returned **None on every call**, every `NeedleFocusTemplateStore` capture stored `microscope_focus_um=None`, and **there was NO focus↔needle datum anywhere on disk** — the v7.10 optical Z datum has never once been captured. Fixed + a repo-wide guard test. **(B2) THE ACCEPTANCE GATE WAS NOT ENFORCED ON THIS MODE** — `holdout_error_mm` filters `z_zref_mm is not None`, which a focal reading NEVER has, so `from_focal_readings` passed `holdout=None` and `_validate_plate_z_plane` skipped its hold-out branch. The module’s own docstring calls hold-out *“the acceptance gate, not R²”*. **Measured: a 140 µm bad site yields a residual of only 46.7 µm, which PASSES the 50 µm residual gate — so that plane was ACCEPTED.** New `holdout_focal_error_mm`; the before/after is pinned by a test that installs the same plane with `holdout=None` and asserts it used to pass. **(B3) `MountedOptic` threw the numbers away** — NA / working distance / magnification were read and formatted into a `detail` STRING; every sweep bound needs floats and re-parsing that string fails silently into a wrong step or a wrong collision bound. **(B4) the `MicroscopeController` singleton had no exclusivity** and `STALE_OP_S=20 s` DROPS a queued op silently — a dropped `set_objective` means every later sample is taken through the WRONG objective and the result still looks well-formed; added a thread-affinity `try_acquire`/`release` lease. **(B5) `set_plate_bottom_z` had no zero-Z epoch**, so Set Z Zero rejected the *plane* loudly and left the *scalar* stale SILENTLY — and the scalar is what every non-tilt-aware consumer reads. **(B6) CLAUDE.md states “1.0 units/µm” and “travel 0–400000 µm” ~2000 words UPSTREAM of its own correction** to 40 units/µm and 10 mm; annotated in place (the doc is not stale — the correction is there, just buried, and a reader who stops early is 40× wrong). **ALGORITHM, all citable: Berek two-term depth of field** `DOF = λn/NA² + n·(µm/px)/NA` (*Inoué & Spring 2e p.31; Nikon MicroscopyU / Berek 1927*) — the geometric term’s `e/M` **IS** the measured µm/px already in `ObjectiveCalibration`, so it is driven by a measurement not a nameplate, and `e`=1 px (not Nyquist 2) makes it conservative on purpose; verified **4x 57.2 / 10x 10.4 / 20x 4.1 µm**. **Peak = weighted Gaussian fit to log(F−B)** over the half-max set, seeded and cross-checked by the closed-form 3-point log-parabola vertex — LINEAR, so it cannot fail to converge on exactly the pathological curves it must refuse (*Naidu & Fisher BMVC 1991; Yeo et al. IVC 11(10) 1993; Groen et al. Cytometry 6:81 1985; Santos et al. J.Microsc. 188:264 1997; Guo IEEE SPM 28(5) 2011*); measured accuracy **0.3 % of a step**. **FWHM is the operator’s “point spread”** and the shape fault detector: expected 1–3×DOF, hard-refuse outside 0.5–6×DOF because a response narrower than the diffraction-limited DOF is PHYSICALLY IMPOSSIBLE. **⚠ THE COLLISION HAZARD IS THE OBJECTIVE, NOT THE NEEDLE** — the focus drive raises the objective TOWARD the plate, so `half_range = min(requested, 0.25×WD)`: a 20x with ~1 mm WD gets **±250 µm and physically cannot do the ±1 mm that is fine on a 4x**; unknown WD falls back to ±50 µm **and says so** (a silently narrow sweep reads downstream as “no peak found”, an optics diagnosis for a limits problem), and an empty plan **RAISES** rather than returning `()`. The guard lives in the PURE planner and is pinned as a **property over 400 randomised plans, zero violations** — including that the backlash lead-in position is legal too, which is not optional because every rung boundary reverses direction. **12 refusal codes**, two carrying the real weight: `LOW_PROMINENCE` (clean empty glass has almost no contrast — without it the estimator fits sensor noise and reports a confident surface height) and **`MULTIMODAL`** — a #1.5 coverslip’s two faces are ~170 µm apart, and focusing on the outer one offsets the WHOLE plane by the substrate thickness, **a systematic common to every site and therefore invisible to every numeric gate downstream**; only the curve SHAPE can see it (detected at 168 µm in test, measured not assumed). Also `SATURATED` — clipping destroys the gradients the metric measures and clips FIRST at focus, so a saturated stack shows a DIP exactly where the surface is. `MONOTONIC` is checked BEFORE `PEAK_AT_EDGE` or it is unreachable (a monotone curve’s argmax is always at an edge). **TEACH ONCE THEN AUTOMATIC, with the invariant that THE PRIOR ONLY EVER NARROWS A SEARCH AND NEVER CONTRIBUTES TO A FITTED PLANE** — `solve()` has no `prior` parameter and a test inspects its signature to keep it that way, so every run re-measures the tilt and a plate that seats differently is measured rather than assumed; site 1 uses `±(3σ+margin)` from the stored per-plate-type prior, sites 2–3 use `stored_tilt + Δ_run` measured at site 1, sites 4+ use the live fit over this plate’s own sites. Verified self-tightening: four runs of ±20 µm spread take site 1 from **1000 µm → 110 µm**, and no history keeps the full range. **PARFOCALITY + CENTRATION FALL OUT FREE** (the operator’s own observation) — the per-objective focus offset is the median across sites, stamped with each objective’s `MountedOptic.code` so **a physical objective swap invalidates it automatically, because the body tells us**; it is also what makes the ladder fast, turning each escalation from a search into a direct move. **THE TURRET↔CALIBRATION LINK, NARROWED** (CLAUDE.md records it as deliberately deferred): `ObjectiveLadder` resolves µm/px **from the turret position, READ-ONLY, for the duration of one operation**, and never writes `current_objective_name` — that would change the µm/px every other surface reads, mid-run, from a background thread; the turret is restored on exit, and a new gate compares `current_objective_name` against the live turret position, **the first time those two records have ever been checked against each other**. An uncalibrated objective **REFUSES** with no fallback to the live manager’s µm/px — that fallback is exactly how “I literally just calibrated it” happened. **SAFETY: the needle never descends** (`safe_travel_to(target_z_mm=None, apply_insert_floor=False)`, a refused travel STOPS the run), **`set_print_floor_active` is NEVER called** (pinned by `floor_calls == []` — arming a refcount this workflow cannot need risks an unbalanced decrement against a concurrent print), `state().focus_um` is read BACK after every move because `set_focus_um` clamps silently and a clamped sample is a guess, and the **exit guarantee** is asserted on success/failure/cancel: poller resumed exactly once, objective and focus restored, lease released, nothing installed. **Drift closure** re-measures site 1 at the end and blocks Accept above 10 µm (Ti focus drives creep 0.1–1 µm/min — real over a ten-minute survey, and load-bearing now the target is <50 µm). `check_scale`’s band was tightened 2 %→1 % by computing what it costs: the applied scale is exactly ±1, so error `e` leaks `e × Δfocus`, and over a 1.15 mm tilt 2 % spends most of the 25 µm budget. Tests: **180 new across 9 files**, incl. the guard property, the B2 before/after, and cancel-during-the-operator-pause returning in <1 s (a bare `Event.wait()` there is the classic deadlock); **3 mutations confirmed CAUGHT** (restore `holdout=None` → the 140 µm plane is re-accepted · flip `focal_sign` → the tilt inverts · pre-fix `.state` → `None`). Regression **~470 green per suite** across plate-z-plane-math/plate-bottom-plane-controller/print-z-plate-plane/print-z-plate-bottom/print-z-reference-vector/needle-focus-template/nikon-ti/needle-bore-wizard/microscope-bore-sign/bore-focus-roi/print-floor-refcount/bore-gate-live-refresh/optical-needle-datum/objective-calibration/suite-hygiene/z-retract/calibration-revision/last-known-cal/plate-types/plate-z-autocal-per-well-focus + a `gui.app` import smoke and a real offscreen `CalibrationPage` build. **⚠ ONE PLANNED ITEM DELIBERATELY NOT DONE:** relocating the legacy needle-descent auto-cal into the needle-centring wizard. It owns `_zoff_live_view` — the microscope feed the descent watches — and the Custom tab’s button drives it, so the move means relocating a live camera pane between tabs; it is kept as the second sub-tab of the renamed **“Plate Bed Level”** tab and works unchanged. **⚠ OPERATOR CORRECTION — WHERE THE OPTICAL DATUM MAY BE TAKEN:** it pairs a microscope focus reading with a needle Z, so it is valid only where both are at the SAME plane — the plate touch-off, focused on the glass with the tip touching it. It **cannot** come from the needle-centring step: the side cameras are bolted far above the focal plane, so the tip there is nowhere the objective can focus. Verified that path never touches the focus axis (it records the needle-cam Z FIDUCIAL, a needle height). **🐞 But the touch-off captured the focus axis UNCONDITIONALLY** — step 4 asks the operator to focus on the plate bottom and nothing verified it, so a touch-off done with the focus left elsewhere stored a reading describing nothing, paired with a needle Z genuinely at the glass; now gated on an explicit confirmation that records ABSENCE rather than a wrong number (absent is recoverable, wrong is not), with a repo-wide test pinning that exactly ONE production site passes `microscope_focus_um=`. This also corrects the approved plan’s scale/sign protocol: the tip is only focusable near the focal plane, so the sweep **starts at the plate bottom and moves UP** through the bore-survey heights (away from glass, the safe direction) rather than parking “nowhere near glass”. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER:** non-null `microscope_focus_um` from a touch-off with the focus confirmation ticked (B1 — nothing works until this is true) → focus↔needle datum `|scale|` within 1 ± 0.01 → **LOOK AT THE CURVE SHAPE at one site before trusting any number** (two peaks ~170 µm apart = both coverslip faces) → add 10x and check the parfocal offset repeats → 5-site survey with residual+hold-out <50 µm → force the sign inverted and confirm the hold-out rejects it → restart reload → **print a flat pattern at the far corner vs tilt-disabled, the only end-to-end proof** → second plate acquires inside the learned window. **⭐ PART 2 — THE PLATE BOTTOM IS NOW MEASURED WITHOUT TOUCHING THE GLASS** (operator: *"first guess the plate bottom based on the needle zero position and the known plate properties … the user focuses on a point on the plate they think is the plate bottom … then the focus moves up X (default 100) microns. then the needle moves down to be autofocused at the focal plane … the autofocus should tell the user what the best plane of focus has been so far. The user can override this, and if they do, then a picture of the current focus and needle type should be taken and used to train the focus model … the human should confirm that this focus should be used as ground truth"*). AskUserQuestion (4): lives in the **needle-centring (bore) wizard** · automatic mode **sweeps the FOCUS with the needle parked** · **operator-selected ladder of margins, largest first** · **optical default, the contact touch-off retained as fallback**. This retires the one gesture in the whole calibration that can bend a needle: step 4 drove the tip ONTO the glass. **THE CHAIN:** focus on the glass (`f0`) → raise the focus by margin X → the needle makes ONE bounded Z-only descent → the **focus** sweeps through the STATIONARY tip → `h = (f_tip − f0)·focus_up_sign` is the tip's height **MEASURED, not commanded**, so `B = z_needle − zdir·h/1000` does not inherit the error in the guess that positioned the needle (verified over all four `(zdir, focus_up_sign)` combinations and placement errors to 400 µm). **⚠ WHY THE FOCUS SWEEPS AND NOT THE NEEDLE — the literal gesture is unsafe at 4x:** DOF is 57 µm there, so a curve worth fitting spans ~±150 µm, which at a 100 µm margin puts the tip **50 µm THROUGH the glass**; it is fine at 10x (10.4 µm) and 20x (4.1 µm). Sweeping the focus moves only the objective, bounded by 25 % of a 16 mm working distance. **WHY A LADDER:** each margin is an INDEPENDENT estimate of one number and their agreement is the only available verification — a single margin reproduces exactly the weakness of `focus_to_needle_z_mm` (one point fits an offset and must assume the scale). Largest first is a **safety ordering**: only the first descent trusts the guess. **🔴 THE GATE THAT IS NOT OPTIONAL:** the ROI scores **bore 0, the datum**, but a bore protruding further reaches the glass first — a 200 µm bore at a 100 µm margin would be 100 µm *through* the plate while the screen reads a comfortable clearance, so every margin must satisfy `X ≥ max_bore_z_offset_mm·1000 + 50 µm` and it REFUSES rather than clamping. **⚠ THE SCALE GATE IS ONLY MEANINGFUL IF THE DATA CAN SUPPORT IT:** precision is `σ√12/(span√n)` — ~0.33 % at 10x on the default 900 µm ladder but **1.83 % at 4x** (σ = DOF/6 ≈ 9.5 µm), so below the 1 % band it reports **UNVERIFIED** rather than passed; a gate that could not have failed reads on screen as a check that succeeded. **⚠ A WRONG `f0` IS COMMON-MODE AND THE LADDER CANNOT SEE IT** — it shifts every margin equally, so the spread check passes while the answer is wrong by exactly that shift (pinned by a test asserting spread ≈ 0 with the bottom 50 µm out); that is what step A's own on-glass verification is for, and where a coverslip's two faces appear. **🐞🐞 D1 — PART 1's OPTICAL SURVEY COULD NOT GRAB A SINGLE FRAME ON HARDWARE.** `PlateLevelWizard._cam()` read `host.camera_manager` while the page stores `_camera_manager`, AND called `mgr.cameras()` when `cameras` is a **property** — a `TypeError` into a bare `except`. So `_cam()` returned None, the survey worker was built with `cam=None`, and the whole bed-levelling survey was inert. **My own stub host defined `camera_manager`, so no test could see it** — a stub agreeing with the wizard proves nothing; what has to be checked is the wizard agreeing with the REAL class, which a new `test_v711_host_accessor_contract.py` now does by AST over both wizards. **🐞 D2** — `_zoff_estimate_from_needle_cam` pushed `set_plate_bottom_z` with no `source=`, so a guess was indistinguishable from a taught value and the plate-level wizard's *"plate bottom is only estimated"* warning could never fire from the ONLY path that produces an estimate. **🐞 D3 — THE TRAINING BANK WAS WRITE-ONLY IN PRODUCTION:** the sole `NeedleFocusTemplateStore` writer never passed `needle_type=` / `needle_bore_um=` / `focus_score=`, so `reference_focus_score()` returned None for every capture on disk, the needle type survived only as a substring of the key, and `load_patches` / `focus_to_needle_z_mm` have **zero readers in `gui/`**. **🐞 D4** — `focus_sample` was declared, emitted per frame and disconnected in teardown but never connected. **"TRAINING THE FOCUS MODEL", HONESTLY:** not ML — a multi-defocus template bank per needle type (`TM_CCOEFF_NORMED` is invariant to intensity but **not to blur**), a usable reference score, and ONE learned parameter: the median `operator_focus − auto_focus` over confirmed captures, which is measurable only because BOTH numbers are stored. Captured on every adoption (a confirmed-correct automatic pick is an equally valuable positive example), with `ground_truth` set **only** by an explicit operator confirmation — absence recoverable, wrong not — and only those feed the bias. The UI keeps **best sample so far** (a running max over jogged frames, ~half a jog step) separate from the **fitted peak** (sub-step, carries a σ), and an overridden rung stores `focus_sigma_um = 0.0` so it can never be weighted as a fit. NEW pure `SupportClasses/PlateBottomOptical.py` (imports `SCALE_TOLERANCE`/`check_scale` from `PlateFocusDatumStore` rather than copying them) + `PlateBottomRungWorker` inline in the bore wizard (one margin per run, so the lease is never held across an operator pause — overriding means jogging by hand, which the lease would block). **Two bugs my own tests found in this work:** the override path wrote **two** captures for one decision, and the worker used only `plan.rungs[0]` while leaving the focus at the sweep's last position — so a tip that landed off-target was never found (the search was ~3·DOF, far narrower than the guess error it must absorb) and the operator was shown a BLURRED tip when asked to confirm it; it now walks the coarse→fine ladder via `next_rung`, sizes the search from `SEARCH_FRACTION` of the margin, and parks ON the fitted peak. Tests: 4 new files (**93**) + 8 added to the template store → **v711 total 282 green**; **4 mutations confirmed CAUGHT** (drop the longest-bore gate · sweep the needle instead of the focus · revert D1's accessor · strip D3's kwargs). ⚠ **One of my own tests was too weak and a mutation caught it:** the D2 guard asserted `'source="estimated"' in getsource(...)`, which matched **my own explanatory comment**, so removing the keyword from the CODE left it green — rewritten to walk the AST for the keyword on the `set_plate_bottom_z` call (the same trap already recorded for a v7.10 guard that passed on an import line alone). Regression green per-suite across needle-bore-wizard/optical-needle-datum/bore-focus-roi/microscope-bore-sign/bore-gate-live-refresh/needle-focus-template/plate-z-plane-math/plate-bottom-plane-controller/print-z-plate-plane/nikon-ti/plate-z-autocal-tab/per-well-focus/bore-offset/multibore/camera-orientation-audit/calibration-revision/z-retract/bore-safety/suite-hygiene, plus a `gui.app` import smoke and an offscreen build of the real `CalibrationPage` confirming D1 end-to-end. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER:** the step-2 guess reads *estimated* → Set plate-bottom focus with the needle retracted → **look at the curve shape at the largest margin before trusting any number** (two peaks ~170 µm apart = both coverslip faces, common to every margin and invisible to the spread check) → largest margin alone, measured `h` vs commanded X (a gap here IS the guess error this absorbs) → full ladder, spread < 25 µm and scale within 1 % at 10x (expect *unverified* at 4x — that is the correct report, not a failure) → override one margin and confirm the capture carries `needle_type` AND `focus_score` → restart reload → contact fallback unchanged → **the proof: run the contact touch-off on the same well and confirm the two agree**, the only end-to-end check that the optical number is the same physical plane → re-run part 1's survey, which with D1 fixed should acquire frames at all. **⚠ The legacy `_auto_z_*` needle-descent hill-climb is now SUPERSEDED** (this measures the bottom without contact; part 1 measures the tilt without the needle) but is left in place and working — the Custom-tab button still opens it and it owns `_zoff_live_view`; retiring it is its own separately-verified change. |
| `MEBP_v710_NEEDLE_BORE_MICROSCOPE_WIZARD.md` | v7.10 **Needle Location becomes a bore-calibration wizard: microscope-frame XY offsets, a REAL per-bore Z, and a live gate** (operator: *“1) all bores are located in the same z plane. 2) it has a warning to connect the xy stage, but i can confirm the xy stage is connected. 3) bore offsets should be calculated in the xy frame … step 1 needle cam x,y,z zero … step 2 reference z heights … step 3 the needle is lowered into the plate z bottom so we can create the offsets for the needle bore from the center of the camera frame”*). AskUserQuestion (4): the tab becomes a **wizard EVERY needle goes through, single-bore included** (a single-bore needle still needs its XY offset from the microscope centre) whose final step **confirms the plate Z bottom** and captures the **microscope-focus↔needle-tip distance** · bores measured at a **safe height above the glass** · per-bore Z **manual jog + live autofocus score**, not an auto sweep · **all bores visible in one frame**. **🐞 BUG A — the stale XY warning was NOT the obvious culprit:** `is_xy_connected` is a proper `@property` used bare (no bound-method truthiness bug) and `CalibrationPage.controller` is assigned once from the live controller (not stale). The cause is WHEN the gate runs — `_bore_cal_refresh` had four callers (build / `set_hardware_config` / after capture / after clear) and **none fire when the stage connects**; the page is built at startup BEFORE hardware, so the refusal was painted once, every row button `setEnabled(False)`, and nothing re-ran it all session (the only accidental cure was re-saving a hardware config). The freeze was **wholesale, not just the XY clause** — the camera clause reads a live frame and is equally unset at build time, so a fix that only re-checked `is_xy_connected` would have left the tab dead behind a camera refusal. Fixed with a cheap `_bore_cal_gate_key` tuple compared on the existing ~300 ms tick (which only fires for the ACTIVE page): 200 ticks on a static rig rebuild **zero** rows, one transition rebuilds exactly once. **🐞 BUG B — `dz` was always EXACTLY 0.0:** it is the difference of two stage-Z readings, and the whole flow (intro text, both tooltips) tells the operator to jog **XY** onto the side-camera crosshairs, so Z never changes between captures; the one path that could produce a real per-bore Z needs four fresh edge picks AND a checkbox, and `_needle_loc_reset()` wipes the picks after every capture, so it never applies past bore 1. `PickAndPlaceManager._descend_z_mm` was therefore a dormant identity function and coplanarity was **assumed, never verified**. **⚠⚠ THE SIGN — a first draft got it BACKWARDS, and the two existing authorities CONTRADICT EACH OTHER:** the store's own worked example says a bore protruding +320 µm in +X is reached with `S_1 = S_0 − 320`, while `TwoCameraNeedleAligner` returns the needle's displacement `n` and its caller commands `move_xy_relative_um(+n)` to RECENTRE — opposite relative-motion senses. A probe-based “measure it” design was built and then **abandoned as unmeasurable in principle** (needle and microscope sit on the same body in EVERY rig configuration, so the tip never moves in that view and the probe always reads zero) — and that dead end is what exposed the right derivation: **the lab frame never enters**. The only code-verified fact is `pto`'s contract (*a plate feature at pixel P has stage label `c = current_xy + pto(P)`*), so bore k at pixel P_k sits over label `S + pto(P_k)`, giving **`offset_um(k) = pto(P_k) − pto(P_0)`** — **the OPPOSITE argument order to `offset_from_centred_positions` (`datum − bore`), which is correct and must not be “tidied”**: one subtracts positions the stage was DRIVEN to, the other subtracts labels of where the bores ALREADY are. Because the pick workflow builds its target the same way (`T = current_xy + pto(click)`), measurement and consumption are consistent BY CONSTRUCTION whichever body the stage carries. The same clicks also give `needle_camera_offset_um = pto(P_0)` — the raw click, **no** negation — with the invariant `offset(k) == centre_offset(k) − centre_offset(0)` pinned by test. **WIRES UP TWO BUILT-BUT-DEAD CALIBRATIONS:** `NeedleFocusTemplateStore` and `StageController.set_needle_camera_offset_um`/`needle_target_xy_for_feature_um` were complete and tested with **zero production writers or readers**; that store's docstring describes the exact gesture step 4 performs. **🐞 A REAL BUG THE TESTS CAUGHT — Euclidean vs Chebyshev:** the per-bore focus ROIs were sized against the *Euclidean* neighbour distance, but they are **axis-aligned squares**, disjoint only when `max(|dx|,|dy|) >= side`; a triple at (900,500)/(1100,500)/(1000,660) has a 189 px Euclidean gap admitting a 170 px box whose x/y separations are only 100/160 px — overlapping boxes make both bores peak at whichever tip is sharper, so **every bore reports the same focus Z**, reproducing BUG B by another route while looking like a genuine measurement. **🐞 Also caught: the wizard wrote the store but never applied to the live needle**, relying on a host listener that may not exist — now calls `apply_to_needle` itself (idempotent). **PREREQUISITE: `_print_floor_active` REFCOUNTED** — it was a plain bool with three independent callers (`PrintManager`/`SimplePrintManager`/`PickAndPlaceManager`) and this wizard is the fourth, arming it during a manual jog toward glass; with a bool the first disarm drops the floor for everyone = *false* protection, worse than none. Public API unchanged; clamped at zero so an unbalanced disarm cannot poison a later arm. Moving between the two armed wizard steps deliberately does **not** disarm-and-rearm (that window would have the clamp off). **Safety:** the park goes through `safe_travel_to` (never a bare Z then XY), the survey height is `max(typed, MIN_SURVEY_CLEARANCE_MM)` through `print_height_to_zref` (**never a raw literal**, polarity-safe), step 4's approach is **Z only at the current XY** and is planned against `max_bore_z_offset_mm` — the chicken-and-egg resolver, since `dz` is measured first the LONGEST bore stops at the clearance instead of being buried — and a stage drift > 5 µm since the park **refuses** a click (the one-frame method's validity rests on the stage being still). Plausibility bounds **refuse before writing**; a clamped offset is a wrong move that looks right. **The side-camera path is KEPT** as the in-app **cross-validator** (both methods must agree including sign; an exact negation IS the sign bug). NEW `SupportClasses/BoreFocusROI.py` (pure) + NEW `gui/widgets/needle_bore_wizard.py` (Qt-free `BoreMeasurement` inside). Tests: 5 new files — refcount (12) + gate-live-refresh (13) + microscope-sign (16) + optical-datum (16) + focus-ROI (24) + wizard (55) = **136 green**; **3 mutations confirmed CAUGHT** (tick removed → Bug A returns · sign flipped → 640 µm = 2× the spacing · floor back to a bool → nested disarm drops it). Regression per-suite green across bore-offset/bore-safety/multibore/other-workflows-bore-safety/focus-template/needle-location/plate-z-autocal×2/calibration-revision/z-retract/per-bore-cell-targeting/suite-hygiene/quick-print-pick-place/spheroid/cell-labeling/hard-abort/gentle-descent + a `gui.app` import smoke. **Pre-existing failure, PROVED not mine:** `test_v79_cell_targeting_setup_page::test_the_real_saved_profile_reproduces_its_exact_volume` (0.003142 vs 0.012568, exactly 4.0×) is a golden-file test over the operator's own `config/workflows/cell_targeting/__last__.json`, which they re-saved 2026-08-05 changing `push_depth` 0.1→0.400052 and adding `push_volume_nL` — restoring the HEAD copy of that ONE file makes it pass; their file was restored untouched. **Explicitly OUT OF SCOPE:** applying `needle_target_xy_for_feature_um` to the existing click-to-target paths (that store's docstring is clear click-to-CENTRE must NOT apply it while click-to-PICK must — its own change). **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER:** warning clears with no config re-save → park stops ~0.5 mm above glass → click all bores → **drive each bore to the SAME target and check DIRECTION, not just distance** (a flip lands 2× the spacing away on the wrong side) → **cross-validate one bore against the side-camera method** → confirm the focus score peaks at a VISIBLY DIFFERENT Z per bore (~50 µm spread per D7) → step 4 with the longest bore reaching glass first → Ti focus datum → restart restores everything. |
| `MEBP_v79_TUCSEN_LIBRA_CAMERA.md` | v7.9.x **Tucsen Libra 25 — a TUCam SDK camera backend, added as an ALTERNATE MICROSCOPE camera for A/B evaluation against the ANDOR Zyla** (operator: *"I have another camera to install. Its the Libra 25"* + the vendor bundle `Dhyana&FL&Libra16-18-22-25&Aries16_20251120.zip`). AskUserQuestion (2): install = **Both** (vendor driver + optional TUCam app, AND vendor the DLL so the app never depends on the install persisting) · role = **alternate microscope camera under evaluation**. **✅ HARDWARE-VERIFIED on the real camera:** `Libra 25` on `USB\VID_5453&PID_E437` (Status OK, driver `Windows Driver Package - Tucsen (TUUSB3) Image 2.1.6.1` = `oem81.inf`/`tuusb3.inf`, signed by *Microsoft Windows Hardware Compatibility Publisher* — so **none** of the HVCI/Memory-Integrity grief the Nikon Ti driver hit) → `TUCAM_Api_Init` SUCCESS with `uiCamCount=1` → live **mono 16-bit** `2600x2048` frames (`channels=1, elem_bytes=2`) decoded to BGR8 `(2048,2600,3)` → **end-to-end through the production `CameraManager`**: detected as source **"Tucsen: Libra 25"**, identity `tucam:0`, frames in the widget's raw cache, exposure set+readback (15000→15000.9 µs), clean stop. Being mono **vindicates the shared-display-math call** — Libra and Zyla are *both* mono, so one identical conversion means an A/B compares sensors, not our code. **⭐⭐ THE PRE-BENCH PROPERTY MAP WAS WRONG, AND THE `GetAttr` GATE COULD NOT SAVE IT — three findings, each caught by measuring a CONSEQUENCE rather than trusting a readback. (1) 🐞 EXPOSURE WAS POINTED AT THE TEMPERATURE PROPERTY:** the table had `TUIDP_EXPOSURETM=4`, but property 4 is **TEMPERATURE** (declared 500..1000) — "set exposure" would have written a **cooling setpoint** and exposure would never have moved. The gate passed it because property 4 *genuinely exists*: that gate protects against an ID a model does not IMPLEMENT, never against an ID that MEANS SOMETHING ELSE. The enum is sequential from 0 — measured: `0` GLOBALGAIN `0..3` (a gain **mode**, not a %) · **`1` EXPOSURETM `0.0063..5.76e6 ms`** · `3` BLACKLEVEL · `4` TEMPERATURE (**reads 0.375, outside its own declared range**) · `8` GAMMA `1..255/100` · `9` CONTRAST `0..255/128` · `10`/`11` LFT/RGT 14-bit levels; BRIGHTNESS (2) is **absent** and correctly not advertised (no dead slider). Exposure=property 1 in **ms** proven physically: 10 ms → 0.035 s frame interval, 300 ms → 0.228 s. `get_temperature()` now returns **None** unless the value sits inside the SDK's own declared range — "unknown" beats a fabricated °C. **(2) 🐞 AUTO-EXPOSURE WAS POINTED AT AUTO-*LEVELS*:** `TUIDC_ATEXPOSURE` was 8, whose range is `0..3` (the tell for something boolean); enabling capability **3** made exposure self-adjust 20.0→44.5→145.8→200.0 ms while capability 8 left it pinned at 20.001 — capability 8 is auto-LEVELS. Measured capabilities: `0` RESOLUTION `[0=5200x4096(Resolution), 1=2600x2048(Sensitive)]` · `1` PIXELCLOCK · `2` BITOFDEPTH · **`3` ATEXPOSURE** · `4`/`5` mirror/flip · `8` ATLEVELS · `37` binning `[1x1Normal, 2x2Bin_Sum]`. The **empirical** resolution discovery was vindicated twice here: it found capability 0 from that capability's OWN `WxH` value-texts (so the guessed `TUIDC_RESOLUTION` was never relied on) and it did **not** mistake `1x1Normal`/`2x2Bin_Sum` for resolutions — the `\d{2,6}` two-digit minimum is what prevents that, now pinned by a test. **(3) 🐞🐞 A REDUNDANT CAPABILITY WRITE BLACKS OUT THE PREVIEW** — the worst, and findable only on hardware: writing a capability the value it **already holds** is not a no-op on this camera. `set_auto_exposure(False)` while already False **collapsed exposure to the 6.3 µs sensor minimum**; a genuine True→False transition preserves it. **Reachable on a completely ordinary path:** `hardware_setup._apply_hw_controls` restores `auto_exposure` from persisted `hw_controls` on every camera start, and that stored value was itself read back FROM the camera, so it normally EQUALS the current value — the live preview would have gone black **on every startup**, looking like anything but a redundant write. Fixed in `_capa_set` by skipping any write that would not change the value (semantically correct regardless, so it guards **every** capability, not just auto-exposure); verified on hardware incl. the exact `set_auto_exposure(get_auto_exposure())` restore shape. **(4) The model name has to come from the OS:** this SDK build returns **no model text at all** (every string info id empty via both the open handle AND `GetInfoEx`-by-index; only numerics populated — info id 2 = `21587` = the VID, id 3 = `58423` = the PID), while Windows names it exactly "Libra 25". `_read_model` tries the SDK's text ids first, else reads `DeviceDesc` from the registry (stored as the INF reference `@oem81.inf,%…%;Libra 25`, display text after the final `;`) via **`winreg`, NOT a PowerShell spawn** — camera detection runs on the GUI thread and a subprocess there is the freeze class this repo has fixed repeatedly; measured **0.0001 s** vs ~1 s, and cached. A first attempt DID use PowerShell and announced itself instantly: the suite went 2.4 s → **22 s** and fake-SDK tests began inheriting the real camera's name (now pinned, so results never depend on what is plugged in). **Architecture:** NEW `gui/widgets/tucam_backend.py` (`Api_Init → Dev_Open → Buf_Alloc → Cap_Start → [reader thread: Buf_WaitForFrame] → Cap_Stop → Buf_Release → Dev_Close`, the exact call set **Tucsen's own Micro-Manager adapter** uses — read from `mmgr_dal_Tucsen_x64.dll`'s import strings rather than guessed), lazy/guarded like ToupCam+Andor so importing never loads the SDK; **pixel format READ FROM THE FRAME, never assumed** (`ucChannels`/`ucElemBytes`/`ucDepth`/`uiWidthStep`/`usHeader`), which is why the Libra's mono-ness being unknown at authoring time cost nothing; `TUCAM_Api_Init` **refcounted** (process-global — Uninit'ing while another camera streams would kill that stream, the same non-refcounted-global hazard as the poller suspend flag and v7.9's `_print_floor_active`); teardown ordered `Buf_AbortWait → JOIN the reader → Cap_Stop → Buf_Release → Dev_Close` (releasing the buffer under a live reader hands the SDK a freed pointer). **⭐ ONE mono→BGR8 CONVERSION, SHARED:** `_auto_levels`/`_mono_to_bgr8` extracted verbatim to NEW `gui/widgets/mono_display.py`, re-exported from `andor_backend` under the original names (behaviour and every existing reference unchanged, 45 Andor tests green after the move); a test pins all three modules to the SAME function object. **`andor_*` control keys deliberately REUSED** (they are what the settings dialog gates on and `hw_controls` persists, and the Andor suite pins them across ~15 assertions — renaming would churn a hardware-verified suite for a cosmetic win): `get_settings()` emits both correctly-named `mono_*` keys and the `andor_*` aliases, and one new `camera_widget._mono_display_backend()` resolves whichever mono backend is live. Also de-duplicated: the ToupCam and Andor branches of `set_capture_resolution` and `_grab_frame` were **byte-identical**, so a third would have been triplication — both now one branch over `{toupcam,andor,tucam}`. **🐞 `TUCAM_VALUE_INFO/VALUE_TEXT.pText` must be `POINTER(c_char)`, not `c_char_p`** — ABI-identical for a C `char*`, but ctypes coerces a `c_char_p` **field** to immutable `bytes` on read, discarding the pointer and making the string out-parameter unusable/unverifiable. SDK vendored to `DLLs/tucsen dlls/` (27 MB, 10 DLLs chosen by reading `TUCam.dll`'s **PE import table**), mirroring the tracked `DLLs/zyla dlls/`; **only the DRIVER is actually required** (the 360 MB TUCam app is just a reference viewer), so NEW **`tools_install_tucsen_sdk.ps1`** installs the driver by default and the app only with `-WithApp`, refuses politely unless elevated (verified), checks Authenticode (**VALID, `CN=FUZHOU TUCSEN PHOTONICS CO., LTD.`**) + recorded SHA256, and finishes by asking the SDK how many cameras it sees. ⚠ That script is **pure ASCII on purpose**: PowerShell 5.1 reads a BOM-less `.ps1` as ANSI and a UTF-8 em-dash decodes as CP1252 ending in `0x94` = a RIGHT DOUBLE QUOTE, which terminated a string and broke the parse (the Nikon script survives its own non-ASCII only because all of it sits in comments). Tests: NEW `test_v79_tucsen_libra_camera.py` (**74**, driving the PRODUCTION backend through a `FakeTUCamLib` substituted only at the ctypes boundary — open/alloc/Cap_Start/reader thread/`WaitForFrame`/decode/mono→BGR8 all real code — incl. a `TestHardwareConfirmedMapping` class pinning every measured fact and a `TestSourceListVisibility` class pinning the operator-visible "is there a Tucsen option?" chain end-to-end); **8 mutations confirmed CAUGHT**: row pitch ignored (which *shears* the image) · `usHeader` dropped · the ×1000 exposure conversion removed · the `GetAttr` gate removed · the API refcount broken · the Tucsen combo entry deleted · **the exposure ID reverted to 4 (the temperature bug)** · **the redundant-write guard removed (the black-preview bug)**. Regression **437 green** per-suite across simulated-camera/objective-cal/andor×2/async-open/cal-liveview/cal-store/hw-controls/image-correction/rotation×2/scale-fov/mapping-orient/reanchor/picker-scaling/calibration-revision/suite-hygiene + a full `gui.app` import smoke (`test_toupcam_chain.py` reports "NO TESTS RAN" — a hand-run diagnostic script with no `TestCase`, pre-existing and untouched). **Remaining bench work:** calibrate µm/px + orientation for the MICROSCOPE role (a singleton role, so the Libra displaces the Zyla from it) and **confirm a Libra↔Zyla swap loses NEITHER calibration** (they are keyed by device identity, which is what makes the A/B workable); A/B both display-scaling modes; decide whether the **5200x4096** full-res mode is wanted for mosaics (the preview auto-picks 2600x2048, and changing capture resolution invalidates µm/px — the code logs that). **Known gaps:** `tucam:<index>` identity is **positional, not port-stable** (the SDK opens by index, and **no per-unit serial was found** — only VID/PID), fine for one Tucsen camera but two could swap calibrations if enumeration order changed; property ids 5/6/7 + 10/11 are named from the sequential enum but not individually confirmed (`diagnostics()` shows them); `hardware_setup`'s bulk `hw_controls` save omits the display-scale keys (the settings dialog's own path does persist them, and `_apply_hw_controls` restores them) — pre-existing for the Zyla, left alone. **⚠ Process note, disclosed:** updating the plan doc with `open(path,"w")` raised mid-write on a bad unicode escape and left the file **truncated to zero bytes** (`"w"` truncates BEFORE the write); it was untracked so there was no git copy and it had to be rewritten from scratch — build the content first, then write. |
| `MEBP_v79_MULTIBORE_NEEDLES_AND_PER_BORE_CELL_TARGETING.md` | v7.9 **multi-bore needle assemblies (single / backpack / triple) + Cell Targeting & Removal redesign around a per-bore program and a dedicated trypsin bore** (operator: *"We use various needle types 1) Single needle 2) backpack where there are two needles of different sizes bound together 3) tripple needle fused together. I want to be able to do a fluorescent scan of the well in multiple channels then if we are using a tripple needle i want to be able to assign a target per channel. Each channel is connected to a different pump. Also I may assign one pump and channel as the trypsan channel which will be pushed onto the cell right before we aspirate the timeing of this action should be defined by the user."*). **THE NAMING COLLISION, resolved:** "channel" meant five different things in this repo (fluorescence filter cube / needle lumen / pump / microscope turret slot / sketch abstract ink) and the needle-lumen sense existed on disk in BOTH 0-based (`HardwareConfig.needle_channel_pump_map`, live) and 1-based (`NeedleSpec.channel_pump_map`, **zero readers repo-wide**) form — a natural place to reach for that would have pushed trypsin through the wrong bore. New vocabulary, mandatory in new code: **bore** (one lumen; `bore_index` 0-based in code, "Bore 1..N" displayed), **needle form**, **pump**, **imaging channel**, **target type**; `channel_pump_map` is now documented DEPRECATED/WRITE-ONLY. AskUserQuestion (8 decisions, D1–D8): **D1** bore XY offsets are calibrated per-mount, targets visited sequentially · **D5** the trypsin bore is a DIFFERENT bore from the aspirate, with an XY move between push and pull — which **RULES OUT "overlap"** (the operator's own first answer): the two bores are never over the cell simultaneously, so aspirating before the push ends is physically impossible, not merely undesigned · **D2** trypsin timing = lead time + push rate + push volume/depth, no overlap control · **D6** one ROLE + its parameters per bore, run order DERIVED from roles, not authored as a step list · **D3** target types are a user-authored fluorescent-signature RULE per class of object (*"bright in FITC only"*, *"bright in BOTH FITC and mCherry"*) — **rule EVALUATION is explicitly deferred**, nothing detects anything yet · **D4** the multi-channel scan is an EMBEDDED LIVE INSTANCE of the Fluorescence Mosaic page (the v7.8 `embedded=True` pattern) in a second **Well Survey / Viewer** tab, with a **Setup** tab hosting "most of the options and establishment of what and how to do everything" · **D7** bores are ~100–500 µm apart laterally, coplanar within ~50 µm (half the 0.10 mm working clearance — not negligible) · **D8** prep conditions only the bores a run uses, SIMULTANEOUSLY. **`NeedleSpec.bores: list[NeedleBore] | None`** (each bore its own `id_um/od_um/wall_um/length_mm`, optional tip stage, `pump_id`, `offset_um`, `z_offset_mm`); `bores_resolved()` ALWAYS returns ≥1 (synthesizes from the flat fields when unset); `bore(k)` **clamps** out-of-range to bore 0 rather than raising; when `bores` is set it is authoritative and **mirrors bore 0 onto the flat fields**, forcing `bores[0].offset_um=(0,0)` — bore 0 IS the `needle_origin_um` datum. **Byte-identity preserved**: a single-bore needle emits exactly the 7 legacy keys (`needle_form`/`bores` conditional-emit, `bores` only when explicitly multi-bore), verified against all six real `config/hardware/*.json` setups. **The v7.6 fail-safe direction is preserved deliberately**: `cross_section_area_mm2`/`orifice_id_um`/`internal_volume_uL` resolve **BORE 0**, never a sum — a missed call site gets one real bore's number, never a fictional aggregate; a new `assembly_internal_volume_uL` covers the genuine "total fluid in the tool" question. **Per-PUMP flow ceiling (safety-critical):** measured **45×** between a 22G and a 30G bore and **~2000×** between a 22G bore and a 30 µm pulled tip — `SafetyLimits` now derives each pump's ceiling from *its own* bore (`bore_for_pump`) instead of one needle-level value written to all three; adversarial review caught this initially resolving from only ONE of two authorities (`needle.bore_for_pump()` alone, ignoring `HardwareConfig.resolved_bore_pump_map()`), leaving a 30 µm tip's pump **7071× over-permissive** with `clamp_flow_rate` passing 1000 µL/s straight through — fixed to consult both. **NEW `StageController.move_pumps_uL()`** — ONE coordinated Marlin `G0` line (built on the primitive the Xbox jog loop already exercises on real hardware) so D8's "simultaneously" is real motion, not two sequential calls (which would (a) serialize on Marlin's planner order, (b) size the M400 drain timeout for one move not all, (c) fight the **non-refcounted** `PositionPoller._suspended` bool, (d) still serialize on `_serial_lock` anyway). **The physics constraint documented prominently: one coordinated move has ONE vector feedrate — bores cannot run at independent rates in it**, which is why prep (all bores doing the *same* thing) uses it and the slow-push/fast-pull removal sequence stays sequential. Adversarial review caught the vector-feedrate floor being applied to each axis's *ceiling* instead, letting a fine bore run **11.11× over** its limit — fixed. **Executor (`PickAndPlaceManager`):** `BoreRole` (`IDLE/ASPIRATE_TARGET/PUSH_REAGENT/DISPENSE_PLACE`) + `BoreProgram` (bore→pump→role→target-type→params) + `BORE_ROLE_ORDER` derives the run order; `CellRemovalConfig` gains a dedicated trypsin bore (`trypsin_enabled/trypsin_bore/trypsin_bore_index/trypsin_depth_mm/trypsin_volume_uL/trypsin_push_rate_uL_s/trypsin_lead_time_s`) whose push volume is sized from **its own** bore area, not the aspirating bore's; `_execute_cell_removal` now: load trypsin → park the TRYPSIN bore on the target → push → **shift XY so the ASPIRATE bore lands on the same cell** (`_bore_target_xy_um`: `stage = target − offset`, the sign convention pinned end-to-end against the new calibration store) → lead time (`_dwell`, true no-op at 0) → aspirate → travel → dispense; volume balance is now checked **PER BORE** (the pre-v7.9 test summed across all pumps and so passed vacuously on an unbalanced two-bore op). **🐞 GLASS-CRASH BUG FOUND AND FIXED: `_intra_well_move` sent a RAW `move_z_relative(-intra_well_retract_mm)`** while "up" is `z_up_sign × raw` and **both shipped device profiles record `z_up_sign=+1`**, so that "1 mm retract" actually LOWERED the tip 1 mm from ~0.1 mm off the glass; dormant only because picker targets carry `well_name=""`, and v7.9's per-bore intra-well shift is exactly what activates it — fixed to lift in the HEIGHT frame and return via an absolute move (the `_do_wash` pattern). Also fixed: `PickPlaceTarget.from_dict` was an unfiltered `cls(**d)` (a newer build's target dict crashed an older one — the exact hazard `NeedleSpec.from_dict` was hardened against in v7.6); a reagent-load volume-guard asymmetry that dispensed a volume the executor had never aspirated when no reagent well was configured. **NEW `SupportClasses/NeedleBoreCalibrationStore.py`** — per-**mount** (never in `NeedleTypeStore`'s preset library — the `CAMERA_CAL_PERSIST_STORE` lesson, since a fused assembly's rotation in the holder is arbitrary and must be re-measured on every re-seat) bore XY/Z offsets, measured by extending the existing needle-camera centring flow to "centre each bore in turn"; `CalibrationSnapshotStore` fingerprints the bore count so a re-seat invalidates the taught plate-bottom Z (an added key stays ABSENT on old snapshots, not None-vs-value, so no spurious "calibration changed" prompt). **NEW `SupportClasses/TargetTypeStore.py`** — user-defined target types with an authored, persisted, declarative signature rule over imaging channels; explicitly NOT an evaluator (no cv2, no detector) — the image-selection scheme is the operator's stated follow-up work. **Cell Targeting page** becomes a `QTabWidget`: **Setup** (per-bore program table, target types, trypsin timing — no overlap control, a tooltip explains the geometry instead of a disabled checkbox) + **Well Survey / Viewer** (the embedded Fluorescence Mosaic instance). Reused/hardened the v7.8 embedding contract rather than re-deriving it: `_stage_busy()` must consult the embedded page's `is_scanning()` (the poller suspend is not refcounted — whichever finishes first re-enables it under the other); `_travel_to_absolute()` stays a SEPARATE handler from the zero-ref click handler (sharing one adds `zero_position` twice); **🐞 a real defect found in copying the pattern: `hideEvent` called `self._scan_page.hide()`, an EXPLICIT hide on a child widget that STICKS — Qt already delivers the child a hide event from its parent, so the extra call left the survey tab permanently BLANK on the second visit**, fixed here AND in the identical pre-existing bug in `spheroid_pickup_workflow.py`; the two known gaps in the v7.8 pattern (missing `set_settings` forward, a dead `hasattr` branch) were fixed rather than copied. **🐞 Also found: the GUI metered push volume from the flat `cross_section_area_mm2` (bore 0, by the v7.6 fail-safe rule) while the executor resolves the ASPIRATING bore — a measured 4.00× disagreement on a 200/100 µm backpack between the readout and the executed move**, fixed to resolve the same bore both places. **🐞 A saved multi-bore setup lost its bore→pump wiring on LOAD** — the per-bore pump combos were populated before the pump-enable checkboxes were restored, so every saved binding silently dropped to "Unassigned" and both pumps fell back to a single shared flow ceiling; fixed to re-offer + re-apply the saved bindings once the pumps are known, before the legacy-map sync that would otherwise propagate the loss. **D8 (simultaneous prep) was verified NOT actually wired** — `move_pumps_uL` had zero call sites in the executor — so on a two-bore run the trypsin bore went to its reagent well dry; fixed with `prep_bores`/`_prep_bore_plan`/`_prep_pump_move` in BOTH `run_prep` **and** `run_post_clean` (the latter matters specifically because a used trypsin bore also holds residual that must be cleared, not just `prep_bore`), each bore prepped at its OWN internal volume, one coordinated `move_pumps_uL` call per actuation with a sequential fallback on an older controller. **`PrintPlanOfAction`'s bore↔pump validation also read the STORED map**, producing a spurious "N bores but 0 mapped" the moment the Needle card emits a `bores` list — fixed to call `resolved_bore_pump_map()`. **⚠ Incident, disclosed:** while deleting the two now-superseded dead multi-bore stubs (`TrypsinPickupConfig`/`FluorescentTaggingConfig`, unreachable — no GUI ever constructed them, no test covered them, and the trypsin one resolved wells through a well-position dict with zero writers) a bad regex took `PickAndPlaceManager.py` from 2099 to 372 lines, destroying ~76 lines of **uncommitted** v7.6 work in the process. Fully recovered from the `.pyc`, a dangling git blob, and a prior session transcript, verified by per-function bytecode comparison **including line numbers**; 143 tests green afterward. **This branch carries substantial uncommitted work in `PhysicalModels.py` and `PickAndPlaceManager.py` — commit before any `git checkout`/`restore` on those files.** The dead-code deletion itself was deliberately NOT redone as part of this change (their `OperationType` members were restored) — left as its own small, separately-verified follow-up. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER:** measure the bore offsets, then drive each bore to the SAME target and confirm each tip lands on it (a mis-signed offset is a right-distance-wrong-way error — check direction, not just magnitude) → per-bore Z clearance (descend must be planned against the LONGEST bore) → the trypsin push→shift→lead→aspirate sequence with the shift visibly inside the well → simultaneous prep actually moving the used bores together on ONE `G0` → a per-pump ceiling check that a fine bore's pump refuses a flow the coarse bore would allow. Three hand-written conversions collapsed to one `_wait_xy_arrival_um`; both waits now raise **`AbortException`** (not `RuntimeError` — `execute_queue` returns on abort but drives on to the NEXT target after a failure) and an unconfirmed XY leaves the needle **lifted**. 🐞 **`_shift_to_bore` bypassed every ZP-disconnect guard** by calling `_intra_well_move` directly, and neither `wait_for_z_arrival` nor `ensure_retracted_to` can fail closed (both return True when disconnected) — a mid-shift board drop dragged the needle laterally at ~0.1 mm above glass; guard moved into the **primitive** + re-checked immediately before the XY, since the documented failure is a drop *between* the dose and the shift. 🐞 **A NaN offset reached `SafetyLimits.clamp_xy`, whose `max(lo, min(hi, nan))` returns `hi`** — commanding the far envelope corner with the needle down; non-finite now collapses to the datum and `needle_bore_offset_um` is strict, matching the sibling guard on `needle_bore_z_offset_mm` that had no counterpart. Offset magnitude bounded (`MAX_BORE_OFFSET_UM=2000`, `MAX_BORE_Z_OFFSET_MM=1.0`), **refusing rather than clamping** — a clamped offset is a wrong move that looks right. 🐞 **THE CELL GOT TWO DOSES:** with a dosing bore armed the aspirating bore ALSO pushed its own column, out of the orifice about to pull the cell in — cells over-digested, the un-recovered excess digesting neighbours; gated at the **load** not the push (gating the push alone unbalances the pump every target). 🐞 **An unmeasured two-bore assembly** doses every cell then aspirates 100-500 µm away — the whole run's material destroyed, none collected, no error — promoted from advisory to a **Start block** plus an executor backstop placed **BEFORE the dose** (checking at `_shift_to_bore`'s `return False` is too late). 🐞 **Spheroid Pick & Place and Cell Labeling** let the operator pick a **pump** but always positioned **bore 1** and sized volumes from **bore 1's** area — a measured **6.75×** dose error plus 100-500 µm off-region, the identical defect cell targeting had already fixed at 4.00×; both now restricted to the datum bore's pump with a Start backstop (operator decision: restrict now, per-bore later). **D8 finally wired** via `CellRemovalConfig.active_bores()`, with the **byte-identity guarantee inside `_prep_bore_plan`** (a lone `prep_bore` collapses to `[]`, or a single-bore run silently re-routes through the coordinated path and **loses `compensate=None`** on the oil/buffer aspirates) and `_prep_pump_move` now raising on refusal AND on "succeeded but delivered nothing" via a new `delivered` out-param — **seeded with a sentinel**, because a controller accepting the kwarg through `**kwargs` and ignoring it is otherwise indistinguishable from total non-delivery and would false-alarm every prep. **Abort with reagent on a live cell is disclosed, deliberately NOT auto-recovered** (that needs un-refusing the abort guard that keeps Abort responsive during a 180 s drain for *every* workflow, on a path frequently entered *because* of the fault that makes a pump move unsafe;
