gates now conditional, release controls grey out with a reason, and two live controls no longer share the label "Push depth" (**dose** = dosing bore, **push/pull** = the aspirating bore's column). 🐞 **The dose flow was silently clamped** to the bore's own ceiling while the readout kept the typed value: on a 30 µm tip a typed 5 µL/s runs **100× slower** and the lead-time arithmetic is wrong by two orders of magnitude — now surfaced *with its timing consequence*, and the status shows the rate that will actually run. 🐞 Dose volume `0–1000 µL` against a bore holding ~0.1 µL (a typed `10` for `0.10` clamps the plunger and breaks the volume balance) — capped at the bore's own holdup, and the magic `0` reads "auto (from dose depth)". 🐞 **Every `help_text` on the panel was DEAD WEIGHT** — `FormRow` hides its help label at construction and only `MainWindow.register_form_row` reveals it, which the panel never called; now a per-cell narrative built from the SAME config the executor receives, the two numbers that matter on their own line (clearance + cell count), the rendered checklist, and one acknowledgement, **Cancel default, no "don't ask again"**. 🐞 **D10 — a legacy `num_channels: 2` needle gained a FABRICATED 0 µm bore**: the row count read the **resolved** `bore_count` while the geometry loop read the **raw** `bores` field, so row 2 was never filled and saving persisted an invented bore that `validate()` then reported as a blocking error the operator never caused — **this hits the operator's own `Alexs Setup.json`**, fixed with `bores_resolved()` and verified end-to-end against that actual file (neither round-trip test caught it: both glob only `config/hardware/*.json`, where every file is `num_channels: 1`). Also: `CellRemovalConfig.to_dict()` no longer drops all nine v7.9 fields (+`from_dict` with unknown-key filtering); the 🔬 fluorescence toggle and selected tab persist; removal Z minimum 0.005 mm at 3 decimals (0.00 is the needle **on the glass**, and 0.005 was not expressible at 2 decimals); incubation gains a decimal (it silently rounded a typed 90.5 s); `print_workspace.py` stops rendering "2 channel(s)" for a backpack ("channel" = imaging channel; a lumen is a **bore**). **Operator decisions this session (4):** dosing bore **replaces** the aspirate-side push · lead time is **additive** to the incubation (making it *replace* would silently turn the 60 s default into 0 s — a protocol change, not ours) · spheroid/labeling **restricted to bore 1** for now · **build the target-type editor**. Three fixes **mutation-verified** (confirmed to FAIL when reverted): the flipped offset sign, the per-bore-only descend, the double dose. **Still outstanding:** the dead-code deletion (still its own separately-verified change); Quick Print calls `run_prep`/`aspirate_ink`/`run_print_cleanup` **directly** so the plate floor armed by `execute_queue` does not cover it — arming there needs `_print_floor_active` **refcounted** (a plain bool, the same hazard as the poller suspend; a nested arm/disarm would disarm early and give *false* protection); the sequential prep fallback has **no delivery signal at all** (`move_pump_uL` returns `None`); folding Quick Print's own `_render_readiness` onto the shared widget. |
| `MEBP_v75x_NIKON_TI_MICROSCOPE_CONTROL.md` | v7.5.x **Nikon Ti Eclipse body control — filter cubes, focus, objectives** (operator: *"connect this microscope to the GUI … keep this all separated and manual for now, but later we will integrate these motors into all of the workflows and calibrations"*; asked for a jog-panel UI to 1) assign filter cubes to slots + track + switch, 2) raise/lower the focal plane, 3) switch + track the objective). **Manual only by request — no print/workflow/calibration path drives these turrets**; the objective turret is deliberately NOT linked to `ObjectiveCalibrationStore` / `camera_config.current_objective_name` (that link means deciding whether rotating the nosepiece re-points the µm/px calibration, and what happens mid-print — exactly the deferred work). **⚠ NARROWED IN v7.11 — see `MEBP_v711_OPTICAL_PLATE_BED_LEVELING.md`:** the plate-bed-leveling wizard DOES drive the nosepiece, and `SupportClasses/ObjectiveLadder.py` resolves µm/px **from the turret position — read-only, for the duration of that one operation**, restoring the turret on exit. It still **never writes `current_objective_name`** (that would change the µm/px every other surface reads, mid-run, from a background thread), so the deferred decision above stands for prints and every other workflow; only this one calibration reads the link, and an uncalibrated position refuses rather than falling back. The Ti is not an ASCII serial device like the Prior/Marlin, so the driver is a **swappable backend**: NEW GUI-free `SupportClasses/MicroscopeControl.py` with `MicroscopeBackend` + `SimulatedMicroscopeBackend` (always available → the whole UI is exercisable and testable with no scope), `NikonTiSdkBackend` (`comtypes` → `Nikon.LvMic.NikonTi`/`…NikonTi2`) and `MicroManagerBackend` (`pymmcore` → MM's long-proven `NikonTI` adapter, **recommended when installed** since its focus device is µm-native and its state devices report their own labels). ⚠ **THE SDK MAPPING IS UNVERIFIED AGAINST HARDWARE and says so** — rather than assert a guessed COM mapping, every access **probes** several attribute spellings (`FilterBlockCassette1`/`FilterBlockCassette`/…, `Position.Value`/`Position`) and raises a NAMED `MicroscopeError`, plus `diagnostics()` dumps the live COM object model (every device, property and current position) so the mapping is finishable in ONE bench session instead of a guess-and-recompile loop. **`z_units_per_um` (default 100 = a 10 nm Ti Z step) is a per-machine SETTING, not a constant** — it scales every focus move, so it is bench-verifiable (command 100 µm, measure it) and correctable from the UI; likewise **focus direction is a checkbox** (`focus_up_is_positive`), because hard-coded axis polarity has bitten this codebase repeatedly (`ZDIR`, `z_up_sign`, `plate_flip_180`). `MicroscopeController` serializes **every** backend call onto **ONE dedicated worker thread** for two independent reasons: **COM apartment affinity** (an object must be used on its creating thread — same concern as the ToupCam/Andor backends) and **never blocking the Qt event loop** (a turret rotation is multi-hundred-ms; the freeze class already fixed three times by `PUMP_JOG_OFF_GUI_THREAD`/`STAGE_JOG_OFF_GUI_THREAD`/`JOG_TRAVEL_OFF_GUI_THREAD`). The widget **polls an immutable `MicroscopeState` snapshot** rather than registering a cross-thread callback — removing the listener-outlives-the-widget hazard (a callback firing into a deleted Qt object) entirely, at ≤400 ms render latency; two ordering details that would otherwise stick the UI: `busy=True` is published **while holding the queue lock** (else the worker's `busy=False` lands first and the panel shows a move that already finished) and ops older than `STALE_OP_S`=20 s are **dropped, not executed** (a turret move clicked 30 s ago is not what the operator wants now). NEW per-machine `SupportClasses/MicroscopeConfigStore.py` (`config/hardware/microscope.json`, atomic, `$MEBP_MICROSCOPE_CONFIG_DIR`, `get_store()`) owns the slot assignments — **NOT `HardwareConfig`**, which is the swappable print setup: a setup file from another machine must never carry another rig's cube assignments (the `CAMERA_CAL_PERSIST_STORE` lesson). Slots are **1-based everywhere** (matching the numbers engraved on the turret — the turret reports only a POSITION, so "slot 3 is mCherry" is knowledge only the operator has, which is the store's whole purpose); names beyond the slot count are hidden but never destroyed. NEW `gui/widgets/microscope_panel.py` (status + Connect + ⚙ · cube combo · objective combo · focus read-out · ▼/step/▲ · absolute go-to; polling stops while hidden, the shared connection is left alone) mounted as `Card("Microscope")` in `StandardJogContextPanel` (so it rides the **Jog pill everywhere that panel appears**) AND registered as a Custom-panel section — one widget, two surfaces, mirroring the illumination LED. NEW `gui/dialogs/microscope_settings_dialog.py` (driver + per-driver options + slot-assignment grids + focus prefs + **Diagnostics…**) with **nothing persisted until OK** (the rule two other dialogs had to be fixed to obey); `app.py` `closeEvent` releases the body + stops the worker best-effort so a wedged turret can't block close. **Needs real-HW verification on the Ti, IN ORDER** (connect → **Diagnostics… FIRST** to confirm the three devices → read-back follows a hand-rotated turret → switching → **command 100 µm and MEASURE it**, the single most important check on the SDK path, any constant factor IS `z_units_per_um` → ▲ direction → soft limits → switch a turret with a camera live and confirm the UI stays responsive). 🐞 Found in my own test: `QMessageBox.warning` in the dialog's refusal path **blocks forever** under offscreen Qt — modals must be patched in headless tests. **⛔ HARDWARE TEST 2026-07-30 — BLOCKED, NOT A SOFTWARE FAULT:** the body IS physically plugged in and enumerating (`USB\VID_04B0&PID_7832` "HUB-A"; **VID 04B0 = Nikon Corp**) but Windows reports **`CM_PROB_FAILED_INSTALL` — Code 28, "drivers are not installed"**, **zero Nikon COM ProgIDs are registered** (all 5 spellings → `WinError -2147221005` *Invalid class string*), the only Nikon software present is **NIS-Elements *Viewer*** (an ND2 file viewer with no hardware control), and there is no Micro-Manager / `pymmcore` / Ti SDK DLL anywhere. `NikonTiSdkBackend` refused **cleanly with its actionable message — no crash, no hang** (the one real-path behaviour this session did verify). NEW repo-root **`tools_microscope_hw_check.py`** (convention of `tools_render_geometry_panel.py`) runs the whole exercise through the REAL widget against any backend — prints `diagnostics()` **before** commanding anything, uses the operator's real saved config on hardware and never rewrites their slot assignments, and ⚠ **focus moves are OPT-IN (`--focus`)** because the focus drive moves the objective toward the specimen (blind move + loaded dish = objective into glass): read-only by default, else bounded by `--step` (5 µm) with the start position always restored. Verified end-to-end through the real widget+controller on the simulated backend (same code path, only the driver layer substituted): **26/26** tool checks + **35/35** scripted — assignment+restart persistence, per-slot cube switching with read-back, per-position objective switching with read-back, focus ±/absolute/direction-inversion/soft-limit clamp, impossible slot reported without moving the turret, disconnect disabling controls. **🐞 BUG FOUND BY THAT TESTING: the focus jog buttons SILENTLY DROPPED CLICKS** — `_render` disabled ▲/▼ whenever `state.busy`, so a click landing while the previous move was in flight hit a disabled button and vanished (two ▼ after one ▲ netted 0 µm, not −10). Repeated small steps are THE most common microscope interaction and queued focus deltas are additive + step-bounded, so dropping the click is strictly worse than queueing it: ▲/▼ now stay live during a move while go-to + both turret combos stay busy-gated (not additive; re-entrant selection mid-rotation is confusing). **pinned `numpy==2.4.6` confirmed untouched** so the numba/Andor cap holds) + added to `requirements.txt` as optional — and having the real MMCore present bought **2 correctness wins**: (a) **all 11 `CMMCore` methods** the backend calls verified to exist with compatible arity (a typo would otherwise have surfaced mid-session at the bench), with empty/bogus-config paths confirmed raising readable `MicroscopeError`s and disconnected reads returning `None`/`0`/`()`; This resolves statically the exact unknown flagged "unverified" at design time. Install needs **elevation** (quiet install unelevated → **1603**, `HKLM\…\Installer\Rollback\Scripts` access denied; elevation can't be requested non-interactively without a hanging UAC prompt) → NEW repo-root **`tools_install_nikon_ti_sdk.ps1`**: refuses politely unless elevated (verified), downloads/reuses the zip + **checks the recorded SHA256**, quiet-installs accepting 0/3010, `pnputil /add-driver … /install` for micusb.inf, then verifies DLL version + `Nikon.TiScope.NikonTi` registration + the `VID_04B0` device status. ⚠ **Signing disclosed:** the MSI and both DLLs are **unsigned** (user-mode, unenforced) while `micusb.cat` is **Valid, `CN=Nikon Corporation`** but **SHA-1 with a cert valid only 2011-10→2012-10** (counter-timestamped by Symantec, which is why it still verifies) — so the SDK/COM half should install regardless, but **Windows 11 (26200 here) may refuse the 2011 SHA-1 kernel driver**, in which case the SDK registers yet the body stays unreachable and the fallback is Nikon's current Ti Setup Tool; the script says so rather than failing silently. **⭐⭐ SDK INSTALLED — REAL-HARDWARE SESSION 2026-07-30:** elevated install succeeded (MSI exit 0, `NikonTi.dll` v4.4.1.714, `pnputil` **bound** the driver to `USB\VID_04B0&PID_7832` → device now correctly named **"Nikon USB Microscope"**), and **`Nikon.TiScope.NikonTi` connected**, exposing exactly the predicted tree (`Nosepiece`/`FilterBlockCassette1,2`/`ZDrive`/`XDrive`/`YDrive`/`PFS`…) — the ProgID correction is **confirmed on hardware**. **🔴 REMAINING BLOCKER IS HVCI, AND MY SHA-1 THEORY WAS WRONG:** the device moved Code 28 → **Code 39 `CM_PROB_DRIVER_FAILED_LOAD`**, and CodeIntegrity event **3111** names the real cause — *"attempted to load micusb.sys that is **not compatible with hypervisor enforcement**, Failure bitmap 0x2, Status 0xC0000220"* — corroborated by `Win32_DeviceGuard SecurityServicesRunning=2` (**Memory Integrity RUNNING**); a 2012 driver mapping W+X memory cannot load under HVCI. **Fix = Windows Security → Device security → Core isolation → Memory integrity → Off → reboot** (targeted; Secure Boot + signature enforcement stay ON). Downgrading to redist 4.4.1.672 would NOT help (older driver 2.0.2.1 vs 2.0.5.1). **Recorded as a trap: old-driver + Code 39 LOOKS like a signature problem, and the signature fix would not have worked.** **⭐ SDK OBJECT MODEL MAPPED, correcting two more things: NO device property returns a number** — every one returns an **`IMipParameter`** carrying `RawValue` (**the number**), `RangeLowerLimit/HigherLimit/Increment`, `Unit`, `DisplayString` (`IsMounted` → **"Device not available"**), `IsReadOnly`. Reading the *wrapper* is what produced the raw pointer bytes that crashed `int()`; dereferencing `RawValue` makes turret reads **WORK** (filter + nosepiece both `RawValue=1, range 1..6`). **🐞 A 100× FOCUS ERROR AVOIDED: the Z drive declares `Unit='um'` and range `0…400000` — it is µm-NATIVE, so the true scale is 1.0 units/µm, NOT the configured 100**; ⚠⚠ **THIS PARAGRAPH IS SUPERSEDED — DO NOT SIZE ANYTHING FROM IT. `Unit` describes the DISPLAY value, not `RawValue`; the real scale is 40 units/µm and the real travel is 0–10000 µm (10 mm), not 400000. See "TWO OPERATOR-REPORTED BUGS 2026-07-30" later in this same row. Reading only this far and stopping is a 40× focus error and a 40× overestimate of travel.** `focus_units_per_um()` now **READS the SDK's declared unit** (um/nm/mm) and only falls back to the configured number when none is declared (setting relabelled "fallback"), retiring the item flagged as *"the single most important check on the SDK path"* — it no longer needs bench measurement; `focus_limits_um()` likewise returns the declared 0–400000 µm travel. **Operator-readable errors:** a raw `COMError` used to dump a full traceback per failed op; `_com_message()` extracts the SDK's own words so the log reads **`No available instruments. [Nikon.TiScope.Nosepiece.1]`**. The two earlier tests encoding the *guessed* `Position.Value` model were **deleted, not patched** (they asserted an object model that does not exist), replaced by `_TiDevice`/`_MipParam` fakes modelled on the real interface. **✅ HARDWARE VERIFIED 2026-07-30 — 22/22 once Memory Integrity was disabled + rebooted** (`micusb.sys` loads → body live): connect via `Nikon.TiScope.NikonTi`; **all 6 filter slots** and **all 6 objective positions** switched with per-move read-back and start positions restored (objective started at 2, restored to 2); focus read **20045.0 µm** with the scale auto-resolved to **1.0 units/µm** and travel **0–400000 µm** (⚠ both figures SUPERSEDED later in this row — the scale is 40 units/µm and the travel 0–10000 µm). Focus **MOVES** remain the one unverified feature — deliberately opt-in (`--focus`) since the drive moves the objective toward the specimen. **🐞 SAFETY BUG FOUND BY THAT RUN — THE SDK SILENTLY CLAMPS: asked for filter slot 999 it moved the turret to slot 6 and reported SUCCESS** (no error, no exception) — for a *discrete* selector that is the worst failure mode, since a mistyped/stale/off-by-one slot would quietly image through the WRONG cube while the software called the move good; and it is precisely the gap a simulator cannot expose (`SimulatedMicroscopeBackend` had always *raised* for an out-of-range slot, so it looked correct until real hardware disagreed). NEW `_set_turret` validates against the SDK's **own declared** `RangeLowerLimit`/`RangeHigherLimit` and **refuses before writing** (test asserts the device is never written); **deliberately asymmetric — the focus drive CLAMPS to its declared travel instead**, because a continuous axis reaching end-of-travel during a jog is normal operation, not a mistake. **⭐ HARDWARE SETUP → MICROSCOPE TAB + "READ FROM MICROSCOPE"** (operator: *"lets make a hardware configuration tab for the microscope so I can setup the objectives and filter cubes"*). **The body knows its own optics** — probing the live SDK found `FilterBlockCassette1.FilterBlocks` (per slot `Name` = **DAPI/FITC/TxRed/Cy5**, `Code` 0 + name `-----` = empty) and `Nosepiece.Objectives` (per position `Name`/`ProductCode` **MRH20040**, `NumericalAperture`, `WorkingDistance`, and `Magnification` which is an **INDEX into the SDK's own table, not a magnification** — verified against `ObjectiveMagnifications.txt` AND cross-checked on hardware: codes 5/7/9 → **4x/10x/20x**, consistent with the NA/WD those objectives report; an empty position has `Code==0` and every other field RAISES *"No database code is associated with this optical element"*). NEW `MountedOptic` + `mounted_filters()`/`mounted_objectives()` on the backend contract (`()` = driver can't report), implemented for Nikon AND the simulator (page exercisable with no scope), surfaced on `MicroscopeState`, populated on connect + `refresh_mounted()` — deliberately **OFF the ~1 s poll** since optics only change when physically swapped. NEW `gui/pages/hardware/microscope_setup_panel.py` **`MicroscopeSetupPanel`** = the **ONE** setup surface: connection (driver + per-driver options + Connect + live status + Diagnostics), then Filter-cube and Objective tables of **# · what the body reports · your name · Go** with a slot-count spin and **↓ Read from microscope** filling the names from hardware (per-row **Go** rotates the turret so you can SEE what's in a slot; current slot outlined; Go/Read gated on a live connection), plus Focus (live position + declared travel, step, direction, soft limits); **nothing reaches the store until Save**. Registered as Hardware Setup sub-page **"Microscope"** (after Cameras); the Plate tab's icon moves `microscope`→**`grid`** (a plate IS a grid of wells, freeing the microscope icon for the microscope). **ANTI-DIVERGENCE: `MicroscopeSettingsDialog` was rewritten as a thin WRAPPER embedding the same panel** (OK=`commit()`, Cancel=discard) — two independently-written surfaces onto one set of settings is exactly the `UNIFIED_MOSAIC_CALIBRATION` failure. Also an unnamed slot in the jog card now falls back to **the body's own name** before "(empty)" (operator name wins, hardware name next). **Verified on the real Ti-E: one click filled `DAPI/FITC/TxRed/Cy5` + `4x/10x/20x`** (showing `MRH20040 · NA 0.13 · WD 16.4 mm` alongside), empty slots marked, focus `22,564.00 µm (travel 0–400,000 µm)`, Save round-tripped. **🐞 TWO OPERATOR-REPORTED BUGS 2026-07-30, both fixed. (1) FOCUS MOVES WERE 40× TOO SMALL** (*"trying to move up 1000 µm but it only moved 10 microns"*) — my earlier "auto-detect" read `ZDrive.Position.Unit=='um'` and concluded µm-native (factor 1.0), but **`Unit` describes the DISPLAY value, NOT `RawValue`**; the SDK's real model is `display [in Unit] = RawValue × DisplayScale`, and measured `DisplayScale=0.025` + `Unit='um'` ⇒ **0.025 µm (25 nm) per raw unit ⇒ 40 units/µm**. Confirmed 3 independent ways: `RawValue 30844`→`DisplayString '771.100 um'` (30844×0.025=771.100 exact), `ZDrive.Resolution=25` with the ZDrive's *device* `Unit='nm'`, and range `400000`→`'10000.000 um'` = **10 mm** (the Ti's real travel — **400 mm, what the old reading implied, should have been the tell**). `focus_units_per_um()` now computes `1/(DisplayScale × unit_to_um(Unit))` with the configured value used only when no `DisplayScale` exists; `focus_limits_um()` uses the same conversion (travel now 0–10000 µm, not 0–400000). **HW-verified: our reading `771.175 µm` == the SDK's own `DisplayString '771.175 um'` exactly; commanded ±10/±5 µm each moved ±10.000/±5.000 µm; zero net drift.** The two earlier tests asserting the Unit-alone model were **deleted, not patched** (they encoded the bug). **(2) THE BACKGROUND POLL CLOSED AN OPEN DROP-DOWN** (*"every time it reads it cancels the dropdown box I have opened"*) — the ~1 s refresh sets `busy=True`, `_render` then disabled the combo, and a disabled combo snaps its list shut under the cursor (`_rebuild_slot_combos` would have cleared it outright); `_tick()` now skips polling entirely while any popup is open and `_render()` never disables/re-indexes/rebuilds a combo whose popup is showing, while a CLOSED combo still tracks the hardware. Tests: `test_v75x_nikon_ti_microscope.py` (**94**); regression context-panel/illumination/responsive-context/jog-nav (80) + workflow-settings/common-print-settings/jog-pump-fill/axis-speed×2/xz-custom-z (122) green, plus a full `gui.app` import + threaded connect→state→shutdown smoke. **⭐ CONNECT MOVED TO THE `Connect Hardware` CARD 2026-07-31** (operator: *"move the microscope connect button to the connection area for xy stage, zp stage"*) — the body is hardware, so it opens where hardware opens: a **Microscope** row now sits with XY / Z+Pumps / Xbox in `control_panel._build_connect_group` with the same **Connect · Simulate · Disconnect** + status badge. **Connect** uses the driver SAVED on Hardware Setup → Microscope (this row never decides the driver); **Simulate** goes through `connect("simulated")`, a backend override that is **not written to the store**, so a simulator run can never silently rewrite the operator's real driver choice (pinned by test). The badge resolves **asynchronously** — `connect()` queues onto the COM-owning worker thread, so the click only shows "Connecting…" and the outcome lands on a later `on_status_update()` via `_sync_microscope_badge()`, whose in-flight signal is the controller's own `busy && !connected` (not a match on the badge's text). Two placement details follow from the microscope being its **own singleton**, not part of `StageController`: the sync runs **before** the `_controller is None` early-return (so the badge is live with no stage controller injected), and everything is `hasattr('badge_scope')`-guarded because the calibration variant builds this panel `show_connect=False` with no connect card at all. **REMOVED** from `MicroscopeSetupPanel`: its Connect button + `_toggle_connect`, which also **wrote `backend`+`prog_id` to the store on every click** so an unsaved driver choice was used — a hidden write now gone; the panel still REPORTS the link (its Go / ↓ Read need it) and states the consequence: **a driver change takes effect on the next connect after Save**. **NOT removed: the jog card's own Connect** — `StandardJogContextPanel` builds `HardwareControlPanel(show_connect=False)`, so the jog pill has no Connect Hardware card and taking its button away too would leave that surface unable to connect at all. Tests: `TestConnectHardwareRow` (6) → suite **101**; regression context-panel/jog-nav/device-page/pump-plunger/axis-speed + camera/hardware-setup **273** green. |
| `MEBP_v78_SPHEROID_DETECT_MEASURE_AND_PICK.md` | v7.8 **spheroid detection, measurement and curated picking** (operator: *"a toggle mode where each spheroid is detected and a circle is fit around the spheroid to measure its true radius … scan the entire well with a fluorescent mosaic … detect the location and size of all spheroids within a user defined range … can be modified by the user to redraw it for actual diameter … any user selected spheroids should have an image taken and cropped to the bounds … saved in a folder … training data (do not build this training system) … click on a list of spheroids … filtered by size ascending or descending … moves to that spheroid location and the user can click on it … goto, edit, or delete … a needle ID that is at least 1.5x the spheroid diameter"*). AskUserQuestion: **a second tab** with an explicit curated transfer into the pick list · the tab hosts a **live INSTANCE of the Fluorescence Mosaic page** (*"if we make changes to the fluorescent scan workflow it should automatically update this page like an instance"* — `embedded=True` drops only its back-button header, the `FullPrintWorkflowPage`/`PrintingModePage` re-home pattern, so there is exactly ONE single-well scan implementation and it cannot diverge) · per-spheroid volume **opt-in, default ON**, always × the safety factor, whose range widens **1.0–5.0 → 0.01–10** · redraw by **BOTH gestures** (drag the ring, or 3+ rim points) on **both** the mosaic and the live feed · crops from a **fresh live capture**. **⚠ THE DANGEROUS PART, FIXED FIRST: mosaic px → stage µm was not trustworthy.** `MosaicBuilder.canvas_extent_um` **ADDS** `_global_shift_um` to the extent while tile PIXELS stay at raw stage positions (hence `MosaicStore.save`'s `shift_um` + `calibration._ploc_mosaic_world_shift`), but the fluorescence worker called `finalize_global_shift()`, read the now-shifted extent, and saved it through a `save_channel` with **no `shift_um` parameter** — discarding it. `_max_shift_px()` at the default `max_shift_um=0` bounds that shift to **20 % of the FOV width ≈ ±337 µm at 10×, larger than a spheroid**; at `pick_z_offset_mm=0.10` that puts a pulled glass tip on the glass a quarter-millimetre off target. So: `save_channel(shift_um=)` + `get_shift_um` + **`has_shift`** (which distinguishes *recorded as zero* from **never recorded** — the actual motion gate; a legacy mosaic still displays and MEASURES fine because a diameter is translation-invariant, but **Go to and Transfer are disabled** with the error bound stated in µm) + `MEBP_FLUOR_MOSAIC_PATH`/`get_store(path=)`; the worker now captures the shift via `getattr(builder,"_global_shift_um",(0,0))` and `finished_ok` carries it. **A transferred position is a SEARCH HINT** — stamped `PROV_MOSAIC`, drawn **dashed**, listed `~mosaic`, upgraded to `PROV_CONFIRMED` only when a live click re-derives it through `pixel_to_stage_offset`; the operator's own "go there then click it" loop is therefore the safety mechanism, not a convenience. **NEW pure `SupportClasses/SpheroidDetector.py`** — a **SIBLING** of `WellDetector.detect_filled_wells`, never an edit of it: that function's gate is *relative* (`min_area_frac` of the LARGEST blob, floor 50 px², **no maximum**), right for "the N biggest discs on a plate mosaic" and catastrophic here because the biggest bright blob in one well is the **well wall/meniscus**, which both survives AND raises the floor above every real spheroid (a test pins that ours finds them and the legacy gate finds fewer) — and it feeds `MosaicWellRemap.detect_raw_positions`, whose well centres command motion. Reuses its PRIMITIVES via a new public `VisionDetector.fit_circle_robust` delegate. **TWO separately named shape gates**, because `detect_filled_wells`' `circularity_min` is actually a circle-fit **inlier fraction** whose 0.55 default is tuned to accept a *partial well arc*: `fit_fraction_min` 0.60 + genuine isoperimetric `circularity_min` 0.80 (the metric that rejects a doublet, whose min-enclosing-circle inlier fraction looks fine). **`THRESH_TRIANGLE` by default** — Otsu assumes bimodality and a ~95 %-background mosaic is unimodal, so Otsu picks a level INSIDE the background and floods the mask — plus a `bg_mean+3σ` floor, a morphology kernel scaled to the requested minimum (a fixed 7×7 stops meaning anything once the mosaic scale changes), canvas-margin masking (the extent carries half-FOV padding, so edge blobs are clipped arcs), and a default-on well-boundary mask. The gate is on the **FITTED** diameter (that is what becomes the measurement), area only a loose prefilter. `back_project_px` is **the one and only** px→stage-µm implementation (`extent[:2] − shift + px/scale`), pinned against `MosaicWellRemap` so the two cannot drift. **Detection may legitimately return ZERO** — on a nuclear stain a spheroid is a cluster of puncta, not a filled disc, so the realistic outcomes are "0 found" and "hundreds" — hence `DetectionReport`'s per-reason counters (*saw 14 blobs → 3 in band · 5 too small · 4 too large · 2 not round*), an actionable `refuse_reason` (*"an 80 µm spheroid is only 3 px across in this mosaic"*), and **manual add as a FIRST-CLASS path** (click the mosaic, or 3 rim points). Touching spheroids deliberately **not split** in v1 (a doublet exceeds `max_diameter_um` — literally the operator's rule — and watershed over-segments fluorescent spheroids, shipping confidently-wrong diameters). Detection runs on **ONE raw channel**, never `composite_overlay`'s blend (grayscale + saturating `cv2.add` clips overlapping channels and pushes a saturated edge outward → over-reads Ø), and the stored scale is **cross-checked against the image width** (>1 % ⇒ refuse) because `composite_overlay` resizes later channels to channel 0's shape and returns channel 0's extent without saying which; `composite_plate_overlay` (own derived scale) is never back-projected from. **NEW pure `SupportClasses/SpheroidTrainingStore.py`** — crops from the **RAW** `get_current_frame()` array, never a pixmap (the picker paints the crosshair and target rings ONTO its pixmap, so a display crop would bake our own annotation ring into every training image), `pad_frac` 0.25 (a zero-context bbox is untrainable; more pulls in a neighbour), clamped rather than **black-padded** (a synthetic border is a learned artefact), and two refusals that matter more than the happy path: a **clipped circle** (half a spheroid mislabels the Ø) and the **stage not being on the target** (a crop of somewhere else filed under this Ø is worse than no crop). `MEBP_SPHEROID_TRAINING_DIR`, `MAX_SAMPLES` 5000 oldest-first trim with unlink. **PER-SPHEROID VOLUME needed almost no code**: `PickPlaceTarget.size_um` has serialized since v7.3.3 and both overlay renderers already scaled from it, but **nothing ever wrote it**; `PickPlaceOperation.config` is already per-op and `_planned_aspirate_uL` reads only `compute_volume_uL()`, so one `dataclasses.replace` per pair in `_on_start` suffices with **NO executor change** — and an unmeasured pick keeps the **SAME config OBJECT**, which is what makes the queue byte-identical to pre-v7.8 (pinned by test). `size_um` is clamped 1–5000 µm on read now that it moves a volume scaling as **diameter cubed** (200→350 µm is **5.4×**). The **1.5× rule** needed none either: `NeedleSpec.spheroid_pickup_detail`'s `clearance` kwarg (never once overridden) computes `orifice_id_um / d_sph`, so `clearance=1.5` **IS** the rule — now a setting, surfaced **per row** (`⚠ tight 1.25×`), as an aggregate (*orifice 300 µm at 1.50× → up to 200 µm; 2 of 3 picks exceed it: P002, P003*) and at Start, evaluated **per pair** (one shared diameter would ignore the rest) and **never blocking**. Also warns when a fixed `release_volume_uL` meets varying aspirates (the residual then accumulates against the needle's internal volume). **🐞 REAL BUG FOUND EN ROUTE: `_PickerCameraView.to_px` was the plain identity inverse** while the click path applies mirror → flip_y → R(θ), so on any rotated/mirrored camera the marker did **not** land under the click — cosmetic for a point, a correctness bug the moment the operator resizes a circle or clicks a spheroid a Go-to just travelled to; fixed via a new `CameraManager.stage_offset_to_pixel` living **beside** the forward map so they cannot drift, and pinned by a click→stage→overlay→click **closure** test over `(rotation, flip_x, flip_y) ∈ {0,90,180,270,37.5}×{F,T}×{F,T}` — at θ=0 with no flips the buggy and correct forms AGREE, which is exactly why it survived (a companion test asserts the naive inverse is >50 px wrong at 90°). Every back-projection test uses a **non-zero** shift AND origin for the same reason. **Picker rework:** selection captured/restored **by target_id** around the full list rebuild (it died on every add/remove, making a row's own actions unusable), ids in `Qt.UserRole`, deletion **by id never row index**; `[Go to] [Edit…] [Delete]` per list, enabled on exactly one selection, Go-to **emitting** for the host (the picker commands no motion); **multi-select finally enabled** — "Remove selected" always intended plural (its loop walks rows in reverse) but the lists sat on the default single-selection mode; removal radius scaled by `size_um` (the flat 250 µm is narrower than a 400 µm spheroid's own ring). **Live-feed measure mode** (OFF = byte-identical legacy, the non-regression guarantee, so `pick_only`/Cell Labeling is untouched): **ring before centre** hit test in WIDGET px (`_SNAP_RADIUS_WIDGET_PX=12`; a frame-px tolerance is 3 px at 4K and 40 at VGA) so resizing a 40 µm circle is never hijacked into a move, drag clamped to the frame, and the µm↔px **projection FROZEN at press** — the host polls the stage at 5 Hz, so a drag spanning a settle would silently resize the circle. Editable **mosaic** circles reuse the two proven precedents deliberately: rect in ABSOLUTE scene coords with `pos()` left at (0,0) so centre = `rect().center()+pos()` (the `_place_marker`/`_marker_center` convention whose arithmetic already drives the stage), a view-driven radius handle à la `RadiusHandleItem` (**not** `ItemIsMovable`), and label+handle as **CHILDREN** so a drag carries them — `mosaic_well_mapping_dialog` makes them siblings and its labels lag, an existing bug deliberately not retrofitted. **No in-scene numeric widgets** (`plate_designer_canvas` records that proxy spin boxes crashed). `_ZoomImageView` gains opt-in `set_interactive_items` (left button to items, middle-button pan; **default off** so the Fluorescence page is unchanged). **One `_stage_busy()`** replaces `_travel_blocked_by_run` across **all four** motion entry points and now covers the scan worker, which drives `safe_travel_to` and toggles the **NON-refcounted** `suspend_position_poller` (whichever finished first would re-enable it under the other); Start disabled during a scan and vice-versa. **Absolute vs zero-ref µm kept in SEPARATE handlers** (`_travel_to_absolute` vs `_on_workspace_position_clicked`) — sharing one would add `zero_position` twice, a millimetres-off move; a test asserts the same numbers reach DIFFERENT destinations, that `SafeTravelWorker` gets `target_z_mm=None` (retract, wait, travel, never descend), and that no bare `move_xy_absolute*` is ever called. Detection + crop capture are both threaded (a 12 000 px mosaic is seconds of OpenCV; `capture_fresh_frame` is forbidden from a worker by its own docstring, so the `frame_count_value()` pattern is used). Detection params + mode flags persist via `set_extra_state`; the **detected list deliberately does NOT** (a restored coordinate from another plate is a crash, and nothing can prove the mosaic is still the one loaded). **⚠ EXISTING INSTALLS MUST RE-SCAN a well before picking from it** — pre-v7.8 mosaics have no `shift_um` and theirs is unrecoverable. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1, IN ORDER** (1 = go/no-go: scan → Go to → residual within µm/px noise for **three spheroids near the mosaic CORNERS**, since a *scale* error grows with distance and is invisible at one central point; then five-point axis/sign sanity — a mirrored axis reads as "right distance, wrong way"; Ø cross-checked against `MeasurementCameraView`; both redraw gestures agreeing; the advisory firing **while Start still runs**; two visibly different spheroids logging two different aspirate volumes; crops landing **un-annotated**). Tests: `test_v78_spheroid_detection.py` (39) + `test_v78_fluor_mosaic_shift.py` (15) + `test_v78_spheroid_training_store.py` (37) + `test_v78_spheroid_per_target_volume.py` (43) + `test_v78_target_actions.py` (48) + `test_v78_spheroid_survey_tab.py` (49) + `test_v78_spheroid_page_integration.py` (50) = **281 green**; regression fluorescence-mosaic/spheroid×3/cell×2/camera-store/camera-rotation/mosaic-orientation-remap/workflow-settings green, `plate_mosaic` 110 with its two documented pre-existing problems unchanged. ⚠ **Pre-existing environmental flake, proven not mine:** running the camera-probing suites together in ONE process intermittently hangs on real DirectShow enumeration — the identical 9-suite command timed out in a pristine `git worktree` at HEAD as well (exit 124), and completed with exit 0 at both HEAD and this branch on another attempt; every suite passes alone. |
| `MEBP_v75x_UNIFIED_MOSAIC_CALIBRATION.md` | v7.5.x **ONE mosaic/camera calibration surface** (operator: *"we have multiple surfaces for calibrating mosaics, there should only be one and it should be in the camera calibration section … after settings are applied, there should be a final check with the new settings for confirmation … we also need the ability to rotate the entire mosaic to ensure the output is the correct way up and down … Rosette, full plate overview, and fluorescence workflow should all have the exact same behavior. Currently everything is very messed up."*). Investigation found **14 surfaces** writing overlapping camera/mosaic calibration and **6 mosaic-build pipelines**, each resolving its inputs with its own precedence — the features existed, the PLUMBING had diverged. AskUserQuestion: one surface in **Hardware Setup → Cameras** · duplicates **removed entirely** · confirmation = a **small test mosaic** · **commit gated on Accept** · overlap **default 25 %, adjustable** · whole-mosaic rotation **auto + a persisted display-only override** · **needle cams left alone** so `TwoCameraNeedleAligner`'s `column_dir_deg` contract cannot regress. Mapped to the operator's five deviation types: **(1) mirror (2) stage ±X/±Y vs ±pixel axes (3) rotation about +Z** are ONE 2×2 camera→stage matrix, already measured together by `derive_camera_stage_orientation` (`scale_fov_calibration_dialog.py:77`) and decomposed into the canonical `(rotation_deg, flip_x, flip_y)` that `MosaicBuilder._orient_tile` / `CameraFeedView` / `pixel_to_stage_offset` all consume — but **P5 (fluorescence) consumed NONE of it**, applying the retired coarse `mosaic_scan.frame_orient` string instead, so on this machine's camera (stored `rotation 180 + flip_y`) it rotated the tiles but **LOST THE FLIP** while the plate scan applied both: same camera, two orientations. **(4) pixel size**: `CameraCalibrationStore` had **no resolution stamp for `um_per_px`** and `_restore_calibration_for_slot` called `set_um_per_px()` without `resolution=`, so after every restart `effective_um_per_px` degraded to a **passthrough** — and this machine's Andor is calibrated at TWO resolutions (4× 3.227 @1024, 10× 1.320 @2048), a 2× error. **(5) overlap** resolved to 5 % (live) / 25 % (P4) / 0.50 (P6) / a dead `_PLOC_MOSAIC_OVERLAP` depending on path; at 5 % a small µm/px error opens GAPS (spacing = FOV × (1 − overlap)) and starves registration of texture, so (4) and (5) compounded. Two hidden top-precedence overrides made it worse: `mosaic_scan.fov_um` (an Advanced-submenu spin beating every measured value, forever) and the learned `MosaicAlignmentStore` "Store FOV/spacing" value **shadowing the objective calibration just measured** ("I literally just calibrated it"). **NEW pure `SupportClasses/MosaicCalibration.py`** — frozen `MosaicCalibration` + `resolve()` (ONE precedence: objective calibration → live manager → refuse; camera store → manager → neutral for orientation) + `build_mosaic_builder()` (the single place builder kwargs are chosen — what makes the pipelines identical *by construction*; they previously disagreed on `target_mosaic_px` 3000/1800/2500/2000, `registration_method`, and whether `retain_for_reorient` was set at all, and **without it the caller's `optimize_registration` silently did nothing**, so the operator tuned against a mosaic stitched more weakly than the scan he then ran) + `refuse_reason()` + `warnings_for()` + per-field **provenance**. Store **v1.1→1.2** adds `um_per_px_resolution` with a backfill from a uniquely-matching objective entry, else `hw_controls.resolution`, else **left absent — absent means UNKNOWN, never assumed** (verified on a copy of the real `camera_calibrations.json`: Andor recovered `3.227061 @ 1024×1024`, ToupCam likewise, six Teslongs from `hw_controls`, one correctly unstamped, re-load byte-identical). NEW `CameraManager.restore_calibration_from_store()` called from `start`/`start_async` so orientation is live **regardless of page-visit order** (previously only the Hardware Setup page pushed it — why P3/P4 built un-oriented). **The one surface**: the card becomes **"Mosaic & Camera Calibration"** with one button; NEW `gui/dialogs/mosaic_calibration_confirm_dialog.py` states the measurement in operator language (`_axis_phrase`: *"Moving the stage +X moves the image left; +Y moves it down"*), hosts overlap (**priced in tiles**) + output rotation, and offers **Build test mosaic…** through the shared path — with **nothing persisted until Accept**, which required fixing two dialogs that wrote `set_mirrored` on the checkbox's **first click, even on Cancel**. **Whole-mosaic output rotation is DISPLAY-ONLY** (composed into `JogWorkspaceView`'s existing render cache + `MosaicRegistrationView`); baking it into the composite/extent would break the `stage = (extent − shift) + px/scale` back-projection every consumer uses to derive well centres for MOTION — the class of bug that once drove the stage millimetres off target — and a test pins that it never reaches `MosaicBuilder`. **Removed:** `frame_orient` + `fov_um`/`spacing_um` controls, the `fov_um` spin and its "Apply to full mosaic" write-back, `ObjectiveCalibrationDialog` (verified zero instantiations), the legacy per-well double-click scan (hardcoded 3.34 µm/px / 0.50 overlap / desyncing `build_mosaic`), `_PLOC_MOSAIC_OVERLAP`. **Live views:** `auto_orient=True` on 8 microscope feeds incl. the rosette one (**highest priority — its clicks become sub-well centres**) + `MeasurementCameraView` now forwards the kwarg; needle feeds stay raw. **3 bugs found beyond the plan:** `live_target_picker` pushed the manager's own µm/px back with `resolution=None`, **clearing a correct stamp**; `set_calibrated_um_per_px` shared one `try` across three pushes so a µm/px failure also skipped `column_dir_deg` (**the value the needle aligner refuses to run without**); and `test_frame_orient_transforms_tile` had been **failing unnoticed** behind a pre-existing `TestManualAlignPage` hang while contradicting its own sibling. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (run the one calibration → measured mirror/rotation match the bench; test mosaic stitches with no gaps and the output rotation puts it the right way up; Cancel leaves the previous calibration untouched; switch 4×→10× → FOV changes ~2× automatically **and survives a restart** — the regression that used to break silently; all three mosaics oriented+scaled **identically** (fluorescence previously lost the flip_y); map wells and drive to two — the needle must land on the centres, proving the display-only rotation didn't disturb back-projection). Tests: `test_v75x_unified_mosaic_calibration.py` (**37**) + regression 380 green + `plate_mosaic` 104 (2 pre-existing problems unchanged: the documented `test_real_24_well_mosaic` CV failure and the `TestManualAlignPage` hang — **that hang makes a whole-suite run never terminate and hides real failures**). Remaining by choice: the shared-worker extraction (behaviour already unified), the Custom-tab Camera Setup legacy panel, and the `micron_per_pixel_override` UI. |
| `MEBP_v75x_STARTUP_WELL_MAP_MOSAIC_OFFSET.md` | v7.5.x **fix — "every time I start the software, the wells are not mapped correctly to the mosaic for the full plate; I have to press Map wells each time"** (operator, 2026-07-29). Root cause, verified against the operator's own settings.json: the saved `plate_warp` stores (predicted→measured) pairs and `_load_calibration` re-applies the warp to a RECOMPUTED predicted grid — a contract that silently requires the fit-time and load-time prediction frames to match. They didn't: `set_hardware_config` re-seeded `_predicted_positions` with the geometry-only **envelope-centred** grid on EVERY config push (even with a live calibration), so the Map-wells warp was fitted against the envelope frame, while the restart rebuilt an **A1-anchored** frame (`_compute_predicted_positions` from the measured `taught_a1`) and evaluated the warp there → every well shifted by the A1 prediction error — on this machine warp src A1 (104993.5, 66167.35) = exactly the envelope grid vs taught_a1 (105617.6, 65890.4) ⇒ **~(+624, −277) µm ≈ 0.68 mm off the mosaic at every launch**; re-mapping re-fitted against the envelope frame again, so the cycle never converged. **Fixes (both in `gui/pages/calibration.py`):** (1) `set_hardware_config` gates the envelope seed on "nothing calibrated" (the `recenter_default_plate` gate) and otherwise rebuilds predictions FROM the taught A1 — one frame everywhere (also stabilises `_ploc_apply_global_translation`'s reload contract); calibrated-without-A1 leaves the grid untouched. (2) `_save_calibration` persists the explicit `calibrated_positions` **also when a warp exists** (previously only the no-warp re-derive wrote it) and `_load_calibration` restores them as the authoritative positions while STILL restoring the warp object (re-anchor folds corrections into it), reconstructing positions from the warp only for legacy saves — the restart is byte-identical to what the operator saw at save time. Deliberately NOT auto-applying the mosaic-stored `wells_um` at startup (would clobber a newer manual teach; no timestamps to arbitrate). **Existing installs: run Map wells… once after updating** (the on-disk warp is envelope-framed with no explicit dict); every restart after that reproduces the mapping. Tests: `tests/test_v75x_startup_well_map_persistence.py` (6, incl. the operator's exact frame-shift reproduced: legacy reconstruction >100 µm off while the restored calibration lands on the measured mapping); orientation-remap/freeform-warp/last-known-cal/predict-well/plate-centering/plate-types/orientation/reanchor/plate-mosaic/rosette-reanchor/single-well-reregister regression green. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (Map wells once → restart ×2 → rings sit on the mosaic wells with no re-map; re-anchor then restart keeps the shifted map). |
| `MEBP_v75x_XZ_CUSTOM_Z_LOCATIONS.md` | v7.5.x **custom Z locations on the Jog XZ side view** (operator: drag on the Z-axis view → live mm readout tag → release sets a custom location; clicking the current-Z line tags the needle's current height). Opt-in at the widget level — `XZSideView.set_custom_z_enabled(True)` is called ONLY by the Jog page, so the other four hosts (calibration Needle-Offset/Plate-Location tabs, spheroid/cell workflows) keep byte-identical mouse behaviour. Values stored in the RAW zero-ref mm frame (same as `_z_refs` + the `go_to_z_requested` emit → move contract/soft limits hold on both Z polarities); all display through the existing `_disp()` sign. Gesture: press inside the plot = pending; > ~4 px movement = live drag (dashed yellow ghost line + `Z +x.xxx mm — release to tag` pill, inverse mapping `_px_to_z_disp` through the ZOOMED bounds so dragging while zoomed is exact); plain click adds the current Z only within ~6 px of the current-Z line (stray plot clicks do nothing). Custom locations render in the same pass as the Z-ref badges (shared collision-nudge/leader) as `★ +x.xx mm` pills (click → `go_to_z_requested`, right-click removes) + an ✕ circle (removes). API: `set/custom_z_locations` (programmatic set does NOT emit — no save loop), `add/remove_custom_z` (dedup/match tol 0.005 mm), signal `custom_z_changed(list)`. Jog page persists per machine in settings.json `jog.custom_z_mm` (restore in `set_settings`, save on every change). **Needs GUI verification on ME3B V1.** Tests: `tests/test_v75x_xz_custom_z_locations.py` (25: drag/readout/sign-frame/zoom, click-line vs click-elsewhere, badges/✕/right-click, dedup/no-emit-restore, off-by-default, Jog enable+persist+restore); XZ-side-view/jog regression 66 green. |
| `MEBP_v75x_ZYLA_DISPLAY_AUTOSCALE_CONTROL.md` | v7.5.x **Zyla "camera keeps adjusting itself" → controllable display scaling** (operator: wants an auto-exposure/gain-style option for the Zyla + manual control). Root cause: the Zyla has NO ISP auto-gain — `AndorBackend._mono_to_bgr8` normalized EVERY frame to its own 1–99 percentile (mono16→BGR8 display conversion), so display brightness chased the scene. Now an option: backend display-scale state (`get/set_display_auto_scale`, `put_display_black/white` in raw counts 0..65535, `hi>lo` clamped; toggling auto OFF **freezes the last auto frame's levels** so the image keeps its current look) picked up by the reader loop; camera settings dialog gains **"Auto display scaling (per-frame)"** checkbox + **Black/White level** sliders (visible only on an Andor slot, sliders disabled while auto on; Defaults restores auto); deliberately NOT named "auto-exposure" (the real hardware exposure row stays independent — reusing the auto-exposure checkbox would wrongly disable it). Persisted per identity in `hw_controls` (`andor_auto_scale/_scale_lo/_scale_hi`), restored on camera start (auto flag FIRST, then levels, so stored levels beat the freeze-seed). Caps advertise `andor_*` controls; manager/widget `set_hw_andor_*` delegates return False on other backends. **Needs real-HW verification on the Zyla rig.** Tests: `tests/test_v75x_andor_display_scaling.py` (21) + `FakeAndorCam` mirrored; andor/camera-controls/store/correction/liveview/rotation/async suites 176 green. |
| `MEBP_v75x_NEEDLE_CAM_SYMMETRIC_45_AND_VIEW_ROLL.md` | v7.5.x **needle side cameras physically re-mounted symmetric about stage +X at +45°/−45° — mount direction split from display roll + manual needle center** (operator: cameras now aligned to +X at ±45; the ±45° stored as `rotation_deg` was tilting the live view; "Set current as location" didn't wire into the needle center). ONE conflated per-camera `rotation_deg` previously served BOTH the aligner's column→stage mount direction AND the display orientation. Split: NEW per-identity **`column_dir_deg`** (mount direction, consumed ONLY by `TwoCameraNeedleAligner` via `_needle_loc_camera_info`; `CameraCalibrationStore.get/set_column_dir` + `set_calibration(column_dir_deg=)`, `CameraManager.get/set_column_dir_deg`) while **`rotation_deg` becomes strictly the DISPLAY roll** — for needle cams the *deviation of the measured stage-motion vector from parallel*, computed by NEW `pixel_calibration_dialog.fold_parallel_deg`/`view_roll_from_displacement` → `result_view_roll_deg` (sign PINNED by a test composing it with the extracted pure `camera_feed_view.view_transform_coeffs` — the measured vector must display level for every angle × mirror × flip-Y). Commit paths split by role (needle card passes `rotation_deg=roll, column_dir_deg=mount`; `_on_calibrate_slot_rotation` needle branch + NEW `_apply_slot_column_dir`; restore pushes both; needle slot readout `mount −44.6° (Δ+0.4°) · roll +1.2°`). **The aligner REFUSES when either needle cam's direction is unmeasured** (operator: cams are interchangeable "Needle cam 1/2", which-is-which comes from the µm/px calibration) — the legacy orthogonal 90°/0° fallback would drive the stage the wrong way on this rig; roles relabelled "Needle cam 1/2" (`needle_role_label`; enum values `needle_x/needle_y` frozen — serialized). **Store migration v1.0→1.1**: a needle-assigned legacy `rotation_deg` MOVES to `column_dir_deg` (that WAS its semantic) so old machines' views render level immediately; fresh stores are born at 1.1 (else a first-write roll would be wrongly migrated — caught by test). **Manual needle center**: "Set current as needle center" now records the FULL center via factored `_needle_loc_record_origin_here()` (shared with Center & Save): `needle_origin_um` + quick-move XY + needle-cam Z fiducial, behind a confirm dialog. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (calibrate both needle cams → views level, mount ≈ +45/−45 measured; Center & Save lands the needle on both crosshairs; manual button records origin+XY+cam-Z; restart restores both fields). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_needle_cam_mount_and_roll.py` (31) + updated quick-move/store/rotation-monitor suites; ~420 regression green. |
| `MEBP_v77_QUICKPRINT_UX_AND_REPORTING.md` | v7.7 **Quick Print usability, live information, and the first post-print report** (operator: *"make it the best most straightforward as well as keep the user as informed as possible with all of the data we can give them. we may want to include post print reports to help the user understand the print output."*). AskUserQuestion: in-app Report view **+** self-contained HTML/CSV export reusing the four existing `print_results.py` QPainter widgets (**matplotlib stays out of the GUI**) · motion mode **auto-selects** per machine · bioprinting checks **advisory, hard-block only the physically impossible** · **three zones Setup/Run/Report**. **Why:** the v7.6 HW run proved the engine but showed the UI hid what the software knew — the DEFAULT `open_loop` mode made every prediction/overlay/time-estimate dark (`_kick_prediction` returns early unless `velocity`), the two knobs the code itself calls *"THE TWO PARAMETERS everything else derives from"* sat in a popout behind four inline controls, the simulator's 7 µm vs the machine's 27 µm (**3.75×**) had no feedback loop, and there was **no post-print report at all** — the JSONL exec log had **no reader anywhere in the repo**. **(0) 14 DEFECTS FIXED:** doubled progress counter (`[14/57] [14/57]` — `PrintManager._report_progress` already prefixes); terminal states now report WHY (the specific error / ZP-disconnect text was overwritten by a generic "Error — see log"); an abort/error states the fluidic consequences that lived **only in the log file** (cleanup did NOT run so the needle still holds ink; dispensed volume INDETERMINATE); **`Abort` live during ANY positioning** (was gated on an active executor → DEAD through a plain-print preposition while the stage was travelling); `Pause`/`Resume` exposed (existed, unreachable); `Open log`; dead `_update_top_speed_cap` finally called; the summary's silent `except: pass` now logs+falls back; post-clean refusal named a non-existent control; navigator tooltip said "fast-travel" where a click only selects; the nine silently-editing popout fields fixed **structurally** via `notify_on_field_change` (debounced, so no FUTURE field can go stale either); inline help falls back to each widget's tooltip + registers with the Help toggle (`help=` was passed on **zero** of ~25 fields); multi-ink mapping persists via a new generic `set_extra_state`; the prediction stays visible mid-print (`dev 38 µm (pred 7)`). **(1) NEW pure `SupportClasses/PrintReadiness.py`** (`Check`/`Readiness`/`ReadinessContext`/`evaluate`, zero Qt — 19 checks / 8 groups) replaces a single run-on wrapping label of up to ten `"   "`-joined clauses AND the four ANDed booleans behind Print, so a disabled Print NAMES every missing precondition. Two invariants pinned by tests: **unknown data never blocks** (a `None` getter must not disable Print forever) and **`can_print()` blocks on exactly the pre-existing gates** (XY+ZP, object, plate, well) **plus the one impossible case** (particles ≥ bore) — it cannot start refusing prints that used to run. Newly surfaced: which of the three limits binds **and the headroom when none does** (silent before unless exceeded), calibration staleness (`CalibrationStatusStore`, never read here), `StageCharacteristics.missing()`+`measured_at`, dead-time provenance (`measured`/`phase_lag`/`unmeasured` — whose docstring says it exists "so the UI can say WHICH number is capping the speed"), resolved ABSOLUTE print/travel Z **including when a travel height was synthesised**, taught-vs-geometric well centre, **object-fits-the-well** (a check that did not exist), bead width, path length + strokes, pickup breakdown, syringe fill. Advisories that never ran for a print: `InkSpec.flow_compatibility_detail` clog risk, `FlowPhysics.wall_shear_stress` vs the 5 Pa cell-viability limit, and the enforced ceiling disclosed as a **reference-fluid** figure. **(2) THREE ZONES** — `QStackedWidget` + pill strip; the two parameters **promoted onto Setup** yet still saved with the profile via new `WorkflowSettingsDialog.register_external` (a widget has one parent, so it cannot also live in a section); well selector moved to Setup; Run is the watch surface; Print→Run and terminal→Report auto-advance. **The attribute-name contract is pinned by a test** — the partial-page suites never build the UI, so a rename breaks them SILENTLY. **(3) LIVE NUMBERS:** `vel_sample` gains `v_meas_mm_s` (already computed and thrown away) + `deposited_uL`, plus `PrintManager.on_vel_sample` — all on the **existing every-5th-tick branch**, so the ~25 Hz control loop gains NO per-tick work; the Qt slot only STORES and rendering runs on the existing 10 Hz timer, so a slow paint can't back-pressure the loop. Shows progress+ETA (finally calling the public `progress_fraction()`), **live deviation vs the operator's OWN element** with a verdict + running max/p95 (both numbers were in hand, never compared), dispensed-vs-planned µL, section n/N, measured-vs-commanded speed. **(4) THE REPORT:** NEW pure `SupportClasses/PrintLogReader.py` — the **first reader** for `logs/prints/*.jsonl` (streaming, tolerates a truncated final line and a log still being written, counts `n_bad_lines`, never raises) + `to_recorder_samples` producing the exact `list[dict]` the four widgets consume **in mm** (verified: `_error_to_color` and `StatisticsPanel` both ×1000) + `section_stats`/`restart_decomposition`/`volume_reconciliation`/`problems`/`comm_health`; NEW `gui/pages/workflows/quick_print_report.py` — verdict badge, **predicted vs actual with the ratio**, the four reused widgets (path coloured by error, playback scrubber, error-vs-time, statistics), per-section table annotated with the plan's own `reason` AND the p95 measured inside it, the restart-vs-mid-section decomposition, fluidics reconciliation, machine health, problems + traceback; self-contained HTML (base64 PNGs via `QWidget.grab()`, no external URLs) + CSV. `PrintExecutionLogger.path_points()` now stores the (decimated, endpoint-preserving) PRINT_PATH points so a SAVED log can be scored — `manifest_for_job` recorded only `n_points`; older logs open with no deviation scoring and SAY so. `planned_x/y` = the ideal point at the achieved arc length, NOT the pursuit carrot (which sits a lookahead ahead, so error against it would measure the lookahead); `sample.lag_um` is deliberately never presented as tracking error (in velocity mode nothing updates its target inside the loop, so it degrades to "distance from the path start"). **⭐ VALIDATED AGAINST THE REAL v7.6 HARDWARE LOG** (`print_20260729_121416_*.jsonl`): 0 bad lines; section offsets reconstructed 0→25.78 mm; `restart_decomposition` returns **near-boundary p95 31.5 µm vs mid-section 19.8 µm**, independently reproducing the 31.7/17.8 computed BY HAND in the v7.6 session; the report scores it **p95 27.5 / rms 11.7 / max 35.9 µm PASS** with a predicted/actual ratio of **3.76** against the 3.75 computed by hand; planned volume 3.8373 µL = the harness's own figure; ZP 477/477 acked; time estimate 21.2 s vs 21.7 s (+2 %). **🐞 THREE BUGS FOUND WHILE VERIFYING, none in new code:** (a) `_reserve_warning` read `self._hw_config` with no getattr guard, breaking the `__new__`-partial pattern the whole suite relies on; (b) **`_needle_cross_section_mm2` was a class-level ALIAS of `_needle_orifice_area_mm2`** — the same function object, so overriding one name (as every partial-page test does) left the other pointing at the real implementation and **the flow-ceiling warning, which read the other name, had quietly stopped firing**; now a delegating method, and `_append_limit_warnings` uses the same accessor as its three siblings so the warning threshold and the commanded flow can never disagree about which area the bead uses; (c) **my own reader indexed the full-path arc length with a SECTION-LOCAL `s_mm`** (a feed-plan `vel_sample`'s `tot_mm` is only its section's length), which would have mis-projected every section after the first — fixed by reconstructing global arc length from each `plan_section`'s `base_s_mm` (works on logs already on disk); the same mistake was caught twice more in the live panel (progress % and planned volume from `tot_mm`) and corrected. **(5) CLOSED-LOOP DEFAULT + NEVER PAUSE ON CORNERS** (operator, superseding the auto-select design: *"the closed loop velocity mode should now be default. We should never pause on corners, just slow down. We always want the pump moving unless there is a break in the section that is planned."*): **closed-loop velocity is now the default motion mode** (open-loop reads NO position during the path, so every v7.7 information surface was inert in the old default); NEW **`XYFeedPlan.build_continuous_plan`** = ONE section with **arc-length profiles** for lookahead and speed instead of per-section scalars, wired through new optional `lookahead_at=` on `VelocityControl.pursuit_step` and `lookahead_at=`/`speed_limit_at=` on `XYPathSimulator.follow_path` (**both default None → the shared follower is byte-identical when unused**; 126 follower/plan tests unchanged); `PrintSettings.feed_plan_corner_policy` with **`"slow"` the default** and `"stop"` retaining the hardware-validated v7.6 sectioned plan, logged in `path_start` as `corner_policy`+`continuous`. The pump therefore never pauses mid-path (no split ⇒ no dwell, deposition tracks arc length), only at a genuinely planned pen-up — pinned by a monotone-deposition test. **🐞 TWO MODELLING BUGS THE SIMULATION CAUGHT — the first attempt was far WORSE, not better:** (a) **the arc formula does not describe a corner** — sizing the carrot with `la=0.8·√(2·R·δ)` at a sharp vertex asked 0.092 mm at 90° and simulated **40 µm** against an 18.75 µm budget, because on a polyline vertex the cut is `≈(la/2)·sin(θ/2)`, **LINEAR** in lookahead not quadratic; new `corner_budget_lookahead_mm(θ,δ)=2δ/sin(θ/2)` asks 0.053 mm and the plan takes the **min of both models** per vertex → Square **58.6→7.6 µm**, Star 54.9→7.5, Zigzag 62.6→7.7, Comb 58.0→7.6; (b) **gating knots on turn angle ignored smooth curvature** — a 5 mm circle sampled at 0.5 mm turns only ~11°/vertex, under any sane threshold, so circles ran at full lookahead and simulated **48.7 µm**; knots are now created wherever EITHER model binds, with no angle gate → Circle 5 mm **48.7→5.3 µm**, Circle 10 mm 25.4→3.1. Plus **lead compensation** (`LEAD_LAG_MARGIN`): the slowdown is commanded `v·(dead+τ+loop/2)·1.25` BEFORE the corner, approach-side only, for the same reason the v7.6 end taper had to be lag-aware. **⚠ ONE THING "never pause" CANNOT DO: a near-180° reversal still stops** (`REVERSAL_STOP_DEG=150°`) — a cusp cannot be traversed by pure pursuit at any speed, since a carrot placed forward in ARC LENGTH sits BEHIND the stage in SPACE; simulated without a stop, Line-Reversal ran to the wall cap (855 s) with no progress. Geometry, not policy, and the plan says so in its notes. **MEASURED TRADE (simulation, ME3B V1, 3 mm/s, 30 µm element): never-stopping holds the element just as well — 10/10 shapes pass, within ~1 µm of the stopping plan (Square 6.9→7.6, Star 5 8.1→7.5, Circle 10 3.1→3.1, Comb 8.0→7.6) — but costs ~1.5–1.9× the WALL TIME on corner-heavy geometry** (Star 10 mm 18.3→34.0 s, Zigzag 19.9→36.4, Comb 27.6→42.0), because crawling a corner at ~0.3 mm/s over a ~1 mm ramp costs more than a 1.3 s stop; on smooth shapes it is slightly faster. **The reason to prefer it is print quality — deposition is never interrupted — not speed.** Stage 5 is **simulation-verified only**; `feed_plan_corner_policy="stop"` restores v7.6 behaviour with no code change if the corner slowdown misbehaves on hardware. Still outstanding from v7.6: `PREDICTION_HEADROOM` is 1.6 in `XYFeedPlan` and 2.0 in `sketch_printability_dialog` and remains unreconciled — the report now measures the ratio every run so that call can be evidence-based. Tests: `test_v77_print_readiness.py` (39) + `test_v77_print_log_reader.py` (23, incl. truncated-file tolerance, `tracking_error_xy×1000 == cross_um`, the section-offset projection, and integration against the real log) + `test_v77_quick_print_zones.py` (54, incl. the attribute-name contract, the doubled-counter regression, HTML self-containment, the live panel); regression **496 green**. Two legitimate test updates: the new `predicted_p95_um` kwarg, and `test_v75x_print_setup_routine` still driving the `speed_pct` knob v7.6 retired. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (live panel through a velocity print with the camera running; the Report zone opening on the terminal state with its ratio matching a hand check; HTML/CSV opening outside the app). |
| `MEBP_v76_PULLED_GLASS_CAPILLARY_NEEDLE.md` | v7.6 **pulled glass capillary needles — two-stage needle geometry** (operator: *"on the needle hardware setup page we need the option of pulled glass capillary needles … an inner diameter and length for the bulk needle then a tip diameter and length where we pull the tip to a certain diameter"*, used differently by **1)** max flow rate, **2)** print line bead diameter, **3)** spheroid size we can pick up, **4)** needle volume for ink pickup). `NeedleSpec` modelled ONE straight cylinder (gauge from `needles.json` + a length combo) and ~30 call sites read that assumption; a capillary is a bulk barrel (D1,L1) feeding a pulled tip (D2,L2) with D2 typically 10–100 µm against a ~1 mm barrel, so all four consumers were wrong for it. **AskUserQuestion:** tip shape = **both profiles selectable** (`tip_profile` ∈ cylinder/cone, cylinder default) · needle volume = **total for prep multiples, tip-only for the Quick Print ink reserve** · spheroid too big for the tip = **warn, never block** · presets = **free-form spins + a savable preset library**. Convention: total = barrel + tip, additive; `length_inches` keeps meaning the BARREL (a capillary stores mm/25.4) so `length_mm` and every barrel-length consumer are untouched; `total_length_mm = length_mm + tip_length_mm`. **THE CORE DECISION — `cross_section_area_mm2` is REDEFINED to the ORIFICE area** (+ a new explicit `barrel_area_mm2`): every external reader of it — the F-1 bead model in `FlowPhysics`/`GeometryEngine`/`SketchTrajectory`/Quick Print, the bore-column volumes in `PickAndPlaceManager`, the four workflow `_bore_area_mm2()` helpers — means "the bore the material passes through", which is the tip, so redefining is the FAIL-SAFE direction: a call site missed in the sweep gets the RIGHT number, whereas keeping barrel meaning would make any missed site silently wrong by ~1000× on a capillary. Barrel dims keep their legacy names (`id_um`/`od_um`/`length_mm` = what the operator types and every legacy readout prints); `orifice_*` aliases exist for readable new code; duck-typing-tolerant free functions (`needle_orifice_area_mm2`/`needle_flow_segments`/`needle_bore_profile`/`needle_orifice_od_mm`/`needle_particle_ratio`) are used at every consumer because several sites pass needle-LIKE stubs exposing only `gauge`/`id_m`/`length_mm`. **(1) MAX FLOW — the safety-critical one:** Hagen–Poiseuille on one d and one L reported ~49000 µL/s for a 580 µm blank whose 30 µm tip actually ceilings at **0.80 µL/s** (the ceiling hard-clamps every pump move, so an over-pressure shatters a glass tip). NEW `FlowSegment` (frozen; `volume_uL`, `equivalent_area_mm2`, `resistance_factor` K with R = 128µ/π·ΣK — cylinder `K=L/d⁴`, cone `K=(L/3)(d1²+d1d2+d2²)/(d1³d2³)`, the exact conical-Poiseuille result which **reduces to the cylinder form at d1==d2**, test-locked) + `NeedleSpec.flow_segments()` as **the ONLY place the taper model is expressed**; `needle_resistance_factor`/`needle_hydraulic_resistance`/`needle_pressure_drop_Pa`/`limiting_flow_segment`; `max_safe_flow_rate_uL_s` → `Q_max = ΔP/R_total` and `calculate_flow_safety` sums segments. Reynolds, **wall shear** (under-stated by (D1/D2)³ before → the cell-viability check silently passed) and the **clogging ratio** (a 1 mm barrel with a 30 µm tip was classified "free flow" for a 200 µm spheroid; now JAMMING) all switch to the ORIFICE. Cylinder is the default profile: it matches how a pull is specified, it is CONSERVATIVE on pressure (higher resistance for the same D2/L2 → ceiling errs low, the safe direction for glass), and it makes lift↔volume piecewise-LINEAR. **(2) BEAD:** deposited volume-per-mm + line spacing + closure overlap + the sketch bead band + the needle markers all follow the tip (a 1 mm barrel OD would give a 1 mm raster pitch for a 30 µm bead — fills as disconnected stripes). **(3) SPHEROID:** NEW frozen `BoreProfile(barrel_area, tip_area, tip_length)` with the **piecewise** `lift(V) = V/A2` for `V ≤ A2·L2` else `L2 + (V−A2·L2)/A1` (collapsing to the single-area form with no tip = byte-identical legacy); executor gains `bore_profile` + `_bore()`/`_lift_for_uL`/`_uL_for_lift` replacing two bare divisions in `_planned_aspirate_uL` and the disengage recompute — `_bore()` returns **None** (not a zero-area profile) when unusable, which is what preserves the "no bore ⇒ carrier volume, no wait" contract; a tapered tip uses its **equal-VOLUME cylinder area** (exact in volume, monotone, closed-form invertible — the exact frustum inverse is a cubic; the single documented approximation in the sink model). NEW `spheroid_pickup_detail` (too_large / tight / **past_tip**) — there was **NO fit check anywhere before**, and per the operator every severity is ADVISORY. The sink store's docstring premise ("the curve is a property of spheroid+fluid, a needle change only warns") is **FALSE for a capillary** — wall drag differs by orders of magnitude between a 250 µm tip and a 1 mm barrel and the curve becomes piecewise at L2 — corrected, with `tip_id_um`/`tip_length_mm`/`tip_area_mm2`/`tip_profile`/`needle_type` now stored beside the curve (written only when supplied → a straight-needle save is the identical JSON entry). **(4) VOLUME:** `internal_volume_uL` = barrel + tip (unchanged for a straight needle); NEW `ink_reserve_volume_uL`. ⚠ **The operator's tip-only reserve needed a guard**: a 30 µm × 5 mm tip holds ~3.5 nL, far below one pump step, so a literal tip-only reserve would put the buffer plug at the orifice the instant a print starts — the reserve is tip volume **when a tip exists, else the full bore volume** (so every straight needle keeps today's ~3.4 µL) and Quick Print warns via `_reserve_warning` below `_MIN_MEANINGFUL_RESERVE_UL` so the operator raises the ink padding instead. **UI:** Needle page gains a **needle-type combo** (Hypodermic (gauge) / Pulled glass capillary) switching between the untouched legacy gauge+length row and a new capillary card (barrel ID/OD/length, tip ID/OD/length, tip-profile combo; ranges deliberately WIDER than the common band because a clipping spin corrupts data silently while `validate()` can warn); channels clamp to 1 (single-bore); two-stage info label with per-stage µL; NEW **`SupportClasses/NeedleTypeStore.py`** (mirrors `WellTypeStore` — builtin/user dirs, user shadows builtin, atomic write, GUI-free, AST-locked) + 3 bundled pull recipes, with **STAMP-don't-reference** provenance (`needle_type_id` is never resolved at load, and the picker flips to "(custom)" the moment geometry diverges, so editing/deleting a preset can never mutate a saved setup or invalidate a calibration). **Persistence:** `to_dict` emits exactly the 7 legacy keys in their original order and appends a new key only when non-default; `from_dict` now FILTERS unknown keys instead of `cls(**data)` — the old form raised TypeError on any unrecognised key and, since `settings["hardware_config"]` is a full `to_dict()` mirror restored inside a broad `except` in `gui/app.py`, an older build reading a capillary settings.json would have silently lost the ENTIRE hardware config. `max_safe_flow_rate_uL_s` keeps a **single-cylinder fast path evaluating the ORIGINAL expression in the ORIGINAL operand order** — not an optimisation but the byte-identity guarantee (the two algebraic forms differ in the last ULP and the ceiling is stored/compared downstream); the test asserts `assertEqual`, not almost-equal. **Validation** branches (capillary checks tip ≤ barrel, positive lengths, OD > ID, single channel) while a gauge-less HYPODERMIC still reports the exact legacy `"No needle gauge selected"`; `__repr__` stops printing `NoneG`; `CalibrationSnapshotStore` gains `needle_type`/`needle_bore_um`/`needle_tip_length_mm` (gauge alone would fingerprint every capillary identically → a stale calibration silently trusted; and a pulled needle is barrel+tip LONG so a tip-length change invalidates the plate-bottom Z touch-off) **plus a required guard skipping dimensions absent from the saved fingerprint**, else every existing user gets a bogus "needle bore: None → 210" on first launch. **Deliberately NOT switched to the tip:** the optical µm/px calibration (`calibration.py:~14589`) MEASURES the needle silhouette — a 30 µm tip is ~20–40 px near the detector's floor and it will lock onto the in-focus barrel shoulder anyway, so it stays on the barrel OD (no behaviour change); a 30× error there would corrupt every spatial calibration invisibly. **Real numeric change by design:** the cell-removal/labeling bore columns now use the tip, so 0.1 mm through a 30 µm tip is ~0.07 nL (below one microstep). **THREE LATENT BUGS FIXED in the blast radius**, each reading `outer_diameter_um`/`inner_diameter_um` — names `NeedleSpec` has NEVER had: `jog_control.py:524` inside a swallowing `except`, so the jog workspace + XZ side view have **never** drawn a real needle diameter; `standard_jog_context.py` + `context_sections.py` silently dropped OD/ID so the needle readout on Jog, Calibration and all four pick&place workflows only ever showed the gauge (both now route through a shared `NeedleSpec.summary_line()`). Fixed here because they are 2-line changes on the identical lines this work touches, and shipping capillary support while that panel stays blind means jogging a 30 µm tip near glass with no tip geometry shown. **Also fixed:** `calibration.py` built text for `ctx_lbl_needle_info`, a widget created **nowhere in the repo** (its `hasattr` guard was always False) → dead block deleted; `wizard.py:386` assigned the catalog's OWN `NeedleSpec` instance into `config.needle` so a later edit mutated the shared catalog → now copies; `should_show_onboarding` gains a `hardware_config.needle` gate so a capillary-first operator (who never writes `workspace.needle_gauge`) isn't shown the wizard every launch; and `_apply_config_to_ui` no longer silently coerces a `length_inches` outside {1.0,1.5,2.0} to 1.0" on the next save. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (in order: 27G unchanged → capillary ceiling drops orders of magnitude + Quick Print speed auto-reduces → bead matches the tip → sink staircase linear through the L2 knee → tip-length edit fires the calibration-changed prompt). Tests: `tests/test_v76_pulled_capillary_needle.py` (**69, no skips** — byte-identity incl. every on-disk needle block, cone→cylinder reduction, bit-exact legacy ceiling, monotone-in-tip-Ø, cylinder≤cone conservatism, BoreProfile inverses/continuity/flattening, executor contracts, advisory-only feasibility, validation, fingerprint added-key guard, preset shadow/resurface/atomicity/GUI-free, GUI round-trips + provenance kept-vs-dropped, and all three readout bugfix regressions); existing bead/extrusion/max-flow/axis-speed/sink-timing/sketch/two-param/last-known-cal/context-panel/workflow-settings/cell/spheroid suites green. Two pre-existing failures on this branch confirmed NOT from this change (reproduced with it stashed): `test_v75x_pump_relief_and_bead_model` ×2 (suck-back, from uncommitted `PrintManager.py` WIP) and `test_v75x_print_setup_routine` ×2 (`_speed_pct_spin`, replaced by the v7.6 two-param rework). |
| `MEBP_v76_QUICKPRINT_TWO_PARAM_FEEDPLAN.md` | v7.6 **Quick Print driven by TWO parameters (resolution µm + top XY speed mm/s), the feed plan driving the REAL print, path overlays, never-stalling live position, and a hard abort** (operator: *"the user should set two parameters: the resolution they want, and the top speed of the XY stage … everything should calculate based on these parameters"*, + flow-limit/unattainable-resolution warnings, optional speed/flow/error/time overlays, real-time position that doesn't stall on axis motion or camera work, and *"abort print needs to knock out the motion of anything instantly and the needle should retract to a safe position"*). AskUserQuestion: the feed plan **drives the real velocity-mode print** (not just the preview) + the speed parameter is an **absolute mm/s** (old `speed_pct` profiles migrated). **(B) HARD ABORT — before v7.6 abort stopped NOTHING:** no XY stop, no pump stop, and a fire-and-forget `move_z_absolute` that was **not raise-only** (it DESCENDED when the needle was already above travel Z) issued **on the GUI thread**, able to freeze the UI 10–180 s behind an in-flight M400; `flush_moves`/`_wait_pump_move_complete`/`wait_for_z_arrival` were all abort-unaware. Now: NEW `ZPStage.quickstop()` (Marlin **M410**; lock acquired → `M410\n`, **contended → raw `\nM410\n` without the lock** — safe because the holder sits in its *read* loop, the leading newline terminates any partial line, and the OS queues each write whole; **self-healing**: with EMERGENCY_PARSER the M410 stops motion → the M400 completes → the blocked wait releases the lock, so the abort un-sticks the wait that was blocking it) + `resync_position()` (double M114 — M410 loses position accuracy and leaves a stray ok) + **EMERGENCY_PARSER parsed from M115 at connect** and logged by every abort (without it M410 queues → degrades to "no early stop", never worse than before);
