The residual 19 % is Z's 0.02 mm increment quantisation + the apex reversal, not sequencing. Figures `logs/hwtest/v76_simultaneous_axes.png` + `v76_star_hw_run.png`. ⚠ **Honest caveat:** the feed plan deliberately STOPS XY at each sharp corner (9 here) and, since Z/pump advance ∝ XY progress, all axes pause together there — that is the mechanism buying ≤30 µm. A truly never-stopping print is the opposite end of the same trade (set `corner_split_deg` > 180 to disable splitting) and restores the ~220 µm corner cut; flagged for an operator decision, not silently chosen. **⭐ END-TO-END QUICK PRINT THROUGH THE PRODUCTION EXECUTOR — 2026-07-29** (operator: *"this should be testing the print with the simulated trajectory that we are making via quick print … make a quick print and oversee the process so that we can test the code on real hardware and compare what we think is going to happen with actual performance … instead of using the pump axis we should use the z axis … at the end, i want a pannel of real vs simulated vs ideal"*) — closes the "drove `run_plan` directly" gap: the whole production chain ran, nothing re-implemented — `QuickPrintWorkflowPage._build_settings` (real stamping; `__new__` partial page + stub spins so the GUI isn't booted against live HW) → `build_well_plate_job` (7 cmds, `return_home=False`) → `PrintManager.start` → confirmed descent → prime → `_execute_print_path_feed_plan` → `_run_plan_section` ×10 → suckback → retract, with `build_plan`+`simulate_plan` captured **before** the run so the comparison is a prediction not a postdiction. **PUMPS NOT USED via ONE choke point** (not per-call-site patches): `ctrl.move_pump_uL` → accumulate signed volume + NON-BLOCKING `move_z_absolute` hard-clamped to [39,44] (so Z advances *while* XY moves, the pump's contract) and `ctrl.move_pump_relative` → refused+logged (never reached). A plunger advances monotonically, so Z descends 44.0→39.0 across the path and the job's own `TRAVEL_UP` restores 44.0; **print Z set EQUAL to travel/safe Z (44.0)** so the needle never descends toward the plate. Two NEW pre-motion guards beyond the four above: a `_feed_plan_char` pre-check (**an incomplete stamp silently falls back to the legacy follower — that would not be a feed-plan test at all**) and an XY-envelope fit check that refuses rather than trace a clamped edge. A **dry run on the simulators first** caught two harness faults free (sim's persisted Z can't reach print height → G92 re-datum; my JSONL parser keyed `event` when the field is `ev`, which made the run look like it logged nothing). **Result:** COMPLETED; `path_start feed_plan=true, n_sections=10, n_stops=9`; **10 `plan_section` events** with global `base_s_mm` 0→25.78; `path_end status=arrived, s_mm=28.644 == tot_mm`; **0 fallback**; wall **21.7 s vs 21.2 s estimated (+2 %)** — the corrected `STOP_COST_S` holds on a second independent path; 156 Z increments = 3.9377 µL ≙ 5 mm, 0 clamps; **pump drift 0.000**; ended at 44.000. **simulated p95 7 / rms 2 / max 9 µm vs REAL p95 27 / rms 12 / max 36 µm, both completion 1.000, both PASS vs 30 µm**; an independent 20 Hz **cached**-position sampler agreed at p95 26 µm (back-fill again keeping the GUI live through a real PRINT_PATH). Panel `logs/hwtest/v76_quickprint_panel.png`, data `quickprint_hw_20260729_121442.json`. **🔎 The comparison's real lesson: the error is section RESTARTS, not corner cutting** — decomposed ±0.5 mm about the splits, real p95 **31.7 µm near a section end vs 17.8 µm mid-section** (sim 7.7 / 2.4); the vertex itself is *hit* (what the stop buys), each peak is the next section spinning up and it decays before the following corner, and the real stage carries a ~6 µm mid-section floor the FOPDT model has no term for. **⚠ FINDING NEEDING A DECISION — `PREDICTION_HEADROOM = 2.0` is not an upper bound:** real/sim p95 = **3.75×** here, and re-deriving from both stored HW panels (rather than trusting the earlier summary) the tuning-only panel medians **1.55×** (4/17 over 2.0) while on the planned panel the **curved** shapes are consistently optimistic — Circle 10 mm **4.28×**, Star 10 mm 3.26×, Star 5 mm 3.21×, Circle 5 mm 3.18×, Circle 2 mm 3.16× (5/18 over 2.0) — and the polygons pessimistic (Square/Zigzag/Comb 0.13–0.25×). So 3.75× is **consistent with prior HW data, not an anomaly**, and 2.0 is a middling figure used as a safety factor: a shape predicted at 16 µm is called PASS for a 30 µm element yet could measure ~60 µm. Raising it (≈4.0 for curved geometry) would demand predicted ≤ 7.5 µm — safer but it will flag designs that would print. **Deliberately NOT changed unilaterally** (trades false-pass against false-fail). **Still outstanding:** Prior `I` mid-VS + a deliberate abort drill (M410 stop latency, raw-write-during-M400) — not triggered during clean runs; corner blobs with real ink; the `PREDICTION_HEADROOM` decision; and a run with the **real pumps** (the executor + bookkeeping are now proven; the plunger, its compliance and corner ooze are not). One pre-existing failure confirmed NOT from this change: `test_v75x_zp_auto_reconnect_and_fast_z::test_no_z_max_keeps_defaults` (200 vs 500 from `get_max_z_feedrate_mm_min`; present in HEAD, zero hits in this diff, already documented below). |
| `MEBP_v75x_XY_CHALLENGE_UPGRADE.md` | v7.5.x **XY Printing Challenge upgrade — the bench that tunes the closed-loop velocity follower was producing wrong answers.** Operator: *"the tuning sweep is just bringing the velocity down … we want to keep the velocity of the stage and only do the corner slowdowns"* + *"every time I autotune I get different answers"*. **Root causes, all verified numerically against this machine's stored calibration + the operator's own sweep:** (1) **`lookahead` IS the speed knob** — `speed_cap = lookahead/((loop+phase)·safety)`, so the tuner's lookahead grid sweeps speed 0.314 → 2.038 mm/s and minimising path error minimises *speed*; the sweep's RMS 8 → 104 µm tracks that exactly, and 3 of 4 descent-tuned params sit on a grid endpoint. (2) **Corner slowdown is provably INERT** — at `print_speed=5` the corner limits span 1.00–4.25 mm/s, all above the 0.314 cap, so `min(cap, corner_limit)` is always the cap (hence the operator's RMS 8 µm flat across the whole `corner_speed_factor` grid); "slow only at corners" was unreachable until the cap was fixed. (3) The cap is driven by **one bad sample**: `get_phase_lag_s()` averages `by_phase.intercept_s` = 0.2872 s, 90 % of the dead-time budget, whose dominant 0.5766 s term is a **single-point fit of one run** and whose set includes two physically impossible negatives — capping prints at **0.31 mm/s on a stage measured at 5.9 mm/s** (`slope_s_per_seg` is NOT the fix: seconds *per segment*, a unit error). (4) **Runs that never finished won** — every best-scoring run logged `wall-time cap hit`, was truncated, and was scored anyway. (5) **The metric is blind**: one-sided/unsigned/untimed over visited samples only, so the logged deadlock (12.4 mm travelled for 1.0 mm net over 37 s) and an exact retrace both score **~0 µm**. **Landed (Stages 0–2):** `tests/support/store_fixture.py` fixing two singleton-store leaks (one restored `TCS._STORE`, a name that does not exist); **golden legacy-identity tests** pinning `pursuit_step`/`resolve_control`/`plan_speed_limits` to 1e-12 before any edit, plus this machine's 0.3136 mm/s cap and the inert-corner proof; the dialog's **seven parallel param structures collapsed into one declarative `PARAM_SPEC`** (needed a `grid_modes` field or the shared corner-angle spec leaked its grid into the velocity sweep). `resolve_control` gains `dead_time_s`/`lead_time_frac`/`min_lookahead_frac`/`max_speed_frac`/`hold_speed` (all 0 = legacy) and now returns the **resolved** lookahead + `cap_reason` naming the binding term; new **`SupportClasses/XYDeadTime.py`** measures the real command→motion dead time by step response (±X/±Y/±diag, medians+MAD, FOPDT cruise fit giving an independent top-speed cross-check; always stops the stage, returns to start, never touches Z, poller suspended, envelope-clamped); store gains `get/set_velocity_dead_time_s` + `effective_dead_time_s()` and ~25 velocity keys all defaulting to 0; `PrintSettings.vel_tuning` (**one dict** instead of ~25 scalars) + `xy_jerk_pct` + `velocity_tuning()`, consumed by `_execute_print_path_velocity` (resolved lookahead, jerk, `cap_reason`/`dead_time_s`/`tuning` logged in `path_start`) and stamped by Quick Print; the bench's `_resolve` makes the **same call with the same arguments** so tuning still transfers. New **speed-budget readout** + **"Measure dead time"** button: on the operator's numbers it reads *"commanded 0.31 mm/s — limited by dead_time … ⚠ Corner tuning is INERT here"*, and after storing a measured 45 ms it reads 1.30 mm/s (4.1×), or 5.00 mm/s with `hold_speed`. **Honest scoring** in `XYChallenge.py`: `Sample` (tuple subclass → zero consumer churn), an **exact** `_SegGrid` spatial index (proved equal to the retained brute force), `signed_cross_track`/`two_sided_deviation`/`coverage`/`completion_fraction`/`path_length_ratio` (the dither detector)/`reversal_count`/`corner_overshoot`/`along_track_lag`/`throughput`/`dwell_events` → `path_report` (keeping `legacy` for comparability) → `composite_score` with **graded** hard failures (`1000 + penalty`, not `inf`, so descent keeps a gradient out of the failing region it currently ships in) + `verdict_for` requiring both RMS and max; new shapes **Line-Reversal** / **Comb** / **Dwell-Stitch** + `SHAPE_PARAMS`/`shape_meta`. Caught a real bug while validating: `completion_fraction` used a global projection, so on a **closed** shape the closing point projected to `s=0` and a *perfect* run read 98.8 % complete → every perfect run would have hard-failed; fixed with a forward-windowed monotone cursor. **Verified:** deadlock/retrace/half-path all FAIL with the right reasons while `path_error` reports ~0; 25 µm passes, 150 µm fails, a 10× slower run scores worse. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (verify `VS` direction on a low safe path FIRST → measure dead time → confirm corner tuning finally *does* something with `hold_speed` → bench-vs-print `cross_um` overlay). **PID tuner fix** (operator: *"the PID tuner is not doing a good job"* — `pid_kp` walked 4.938 → 6.216 → 6.763 → 7.052 across consecutive presses): the cross-track plant is a **pure integrator with transport delay** (a perpendicular velocity command integrates directly into cross-track position), so `Ku = π/(2L)` and `Tu = 4L` are **analytic** — the relay was measuring something computable. NEW `VelocityControl.plant_ultimate_gain` / `pid_gains_from_dead_time` (exactly repeatable, no stage motion) / `relay_sanity` (a measured `Ku` above `π/(2L)` is physically impossible → gate + fallback), a new **"PID from dead time"** button, and three relay defects fixed in `_worker_zn`: **no hysteresis** on the sign switch (so it chattered on µm quantisation near the line, manufacturing spurious tiny half-cycles), the amplitude was the **mean** over half-cycles (which those cycles dragged down — and `Ku ∝ 1/a`, so an under-measured amplitude *inflates* the gain), and no repeat/sanity check → now a hysteresis band scaled off the resolution element, the **median of per-cycle peaks** with transients dropped, ≥6 flips, and the physical gate. `kd`/`ki` now deliberately 0 with the reasoning recorded (unfiltered D on a µm-quantised 25 Hz encoder adds noise; the plant is already an integrator so P alone has zero steady-state error and a second integrator is a classic limit-cycle source — `ki` was previously discarded by accident). **`pid_kp` descent grid widened `[0,0.5,1,2,4]` → `[0,1,2,3.5,5,7,9]`: the correct gain for this machine is ≈5.24, ABOVE the old grid's maximum, so the descent was structurally unable to find it.** Model validated by the operator's own relay run — it measured `Tu = 0.391 s` vs the predicted 0.396 s (~1 %) while its `Ku` was 1.19× the physical maximum, which is exactly the signature of the amplitude bug. **Also confirmed on real HW: "Measure dead time" returned 67 ms** against the 287 ms `by_phase` was claiming — a 4.3× correction that raises the speed cap AND moves the correct `kp` from 1.8 to 5.24. **⭐ ROOT CAUSE OF THE DEADLOCK FOUND + FIXED — the projection window.** Surfaced while bench-simulating the one-click calibrator: derived settings failed verification at 2 mm/s but PASSED at 3 mm/s, and slower-should-be-easier means bug, not tuning. `pursuit_step` bounds the projection search to `max_ds = max(0.15, speed·dt·6)` and `project_on_polyline` broke its scan as soon as the next segment's far end exceeded that window — so **when the window is smaller than the local segment only the CURRENT segment is ever examined**, `s` pins at that segment's end, `seg_i` never advances, the carrot stops, and the stage sits there while the cross-track term thrashes it. The operator's numbers fit exactly: 0.996 mm/s @ 25 Hz ⇒ window **0.239 mm** against a `seg_mm_mean` of **0.4859 mm**; the log froze at `s = 0.509 mm` ≈ one segment, with 12.4 mm travelled for 1.0 mm net. Fix = `MIN_SEGMENTS_EXAMINED = 2` (always examine the segment AFTER the cursor); two segments is nothing like a spiral revolution so loop-snap protection is untouched, and the per-tick COMMITTED advance is still bounded by `max_ds` so pump deposition + the snap guard are unchanged (both asserted; the existing spiral no-snap tests still pass). **⚠ a deliberate default-path behaviour change** — the old behaviour is a deadlock. In simulation every shape × speed now completes (`completion 1.000`, dither 0.9–1.0) where it previously froze at ~0.1 completion with dither 40–75; residual error is now pure-pursuit **corner cutting** scaling with the lookahead (p95 117 µm @ 0.34 mm → 587 µm @ 1.68 mm), which is exactly what the withheld cross-track gain removes. **ALL-IN-ONE ONE-CLICK CALIBRATION** (operator: *"does everything in the XY timing calibration in one shot … applied for all prints … a systematic one-click process"*): the insight is that almost nothing needs SEARCHING — three measurements (loop period, dead time, top speed) plus closed-form physics fix everything else, so the same machine measured twice yields the same settings. NEW pure **`SupportClasses/XYAutoCalibration.py`** (`MeasuredMachine`/`CalibrationPolicy`/`derive_settings` where every value carries a `Note` with its reasoning/`validate`/dependency-ordered `CALIBRATION_STEPS`), deriving `lookahead = target·L·safety·headroom` (**inverted**, so the lookahead is sized FROM the desired speed instead of dictating it), `pid_kp` from `Ku = π/(2L)`, `decel ≥ coast + ramp`, `d_filter_hz`, `settle_tol` and the guard values; `lookahead_margin`=1.5 because solving for the target puts the operating point exactly ON the stability boundary (a bench sweep showed that dithers). Wired to a **⚙ Calibrate XY (one click)** button → centre → comms → dead time → derive → apply → verify on Square+Star with the completion/dither-aware score. **Operator requirement — never reach the travel extents:** every probe moves RELATIVE to its start and a clamped probe fails silently (it measures the clamp), so `envelope_center_um`/`usable_test_radius_um`/`check_fits`/`fit_shape_size_mm`/`center_stage` centre the stage in the reachable envelope first, shrink any excursion that will not fit with a 2 mm margin, and refuse when the envelope is unknown; `XYDeadTime` centres by default and shape sizes auto-shrink; centring goes through `safe_travel_to(..., target_z_mm=None)` so Z retracts first and never descends. **"Applied to ALL prints" was FALSE — gap closed:** only Quick Print stamped the tuning, **Full Print stamped none of it**, so a bench session had no effect there; one shared `stamp_print_settings` is now called by both `quick_print_workflow._build_settings` and `print_setup_legacy._get_settings`. **Gain safety gate:** `VC.SUPPORTS_BOUNDED_CROSS_TRACK=False` makes `derive_settings` emit `pid_kp = 0` (pure pursuit) while the law still adds the PD to a full-magnitude vector, reporting the analytic gain it *would* use and why — an automated calibration must not hand the machine a gain the law mishandles; flip with Stage 3.1. Also corrected a derivation error caught in simulation: `pace_correction` was top-speed ÷ the dead-time probe's cruise, but that probe raises SMS to ~1.5× its own step so its cruise reads back the PROBE speed (it read 1.98 purely because the probe ran at 3 mm/s against a 5.9 mm/s stage) — now left to the dedicated sweep. Derived for ME3B V1 @ 5 mm/s (L = 99 ms): lookahead 0.2→**1.49**, control_hz 25→**31.5**, decel 0.3→**0.565** (0.3 was SHORTER than the 495 µm coast ⇒ endpoint overshoot), hold_speed off→**on**, max_speed_frac→**0.9**. **Remaining (Stages 3–7):** bounded cross-track authority + re-acquire + stall/dither guards (**the accuracy step — corner cutting is now the dominant error**), the derivative filter (`d_filter_hz` stored but unconsumed; required before `kd` can be non-zero), lead predictor, curvature-aware speed profile, shared `build_follower`/`follower_tick`, bench JSONL + run history, the repeatable tuner, and the robustness panel (**it still persists nothing — its results exist only in the in-memory log pane**). Tests: `test_v75x_xy_challenge_metrics.py` (42) + `test_v75x_xy_dead_time.py` (22) + `test_v75x_pid_from_dead_time.py` (20) + `test_v75x_xy_auto_calibration.py` (45) + challenge suites **192**; regression 125 + 180 → **497 green**. **REAL-HW SESSION (2026-07-28, ME3B V1, XY-only on COM6 — COM4/Marlin never opened):** `VS` direction VERIFIED correct on all four axes (the long-standing caveat, settled); measured loop 32.0 ms / dead time 67.1 ms ± 0.34 (apparent, through the read path; transport ~40 ms) / τ 27.1 ms / cruise 2996 vs 3000 commanded — all persisted to the store; verification runs Circle p95 84 µm & Star p95 364 µm both completion 1.000 at 3 mm/s with the derived lookahead 0.892. Hazard caught: a fresh `StageController`'s DEFAULT ±130000 limits put the "centre" at the real envelope's CORNER — load real `safety_limits` before any envelope-relative probe; `center_stage` now VERIFIES arrival. **🐞 NEW BUG diagnosed (NOT fixed): Prior ack corruption** — `send_command` never drains the Prior's `R` ack (it answers `R` to everything incl. `SMS`/`SAS`), the stale `R` is consumed by the next `P` read (`Expected 3 values, got 1: R`, present in the operator's own app.log) → `wait_for_xy_arrival` polls garbage and times out though the stage arrived, undermining confirm-mode + point-to-point; a naive write+drain fix made reads come back EMPTY on real HW and was REVERTED (investigation note in `XYStage._send_protocol_command`; `send_command(drain_ack=)` left in, default off) — needs a raw-serial bench session; the comms-rate checker is suspect for the same reason. **STAGE MODEL + SIMULATOR + GEOMETRY PANEL + PRINTABILITY (2026-07-28 second HW session):** the measured dynamics are now a SAVED artefact — new pure `SupportClasses/XYStageModel.py` (`StageCharacteristics.from_store/to_dict` + a FOPDT `XYStageModel`: dead-time command PIPELINE (not a resettable timer — an earlier ad-hoc sim got that wrong and froze), first-order τ lag, top-speed clamp, quantised readout) + `SupportClasses/XYPathSimulator.py` with **ONE `follow_path` loop over injected `FollowIO` bindings** — an exact transcription of the bench `_drive_velocity` tick — bound to the model with a virtual clock (`model_io`, deterministic) or to the real controller (`controller_io`), so sim ≡ hw in loop logic by construction; `simulate_follow`→`SimResult` (report/score/verdict/signed cross profile/`fail_regions` in world coords), `tuning_from_store`, `simulate_panel`, `printing_subpaths_from_trajectory` (Nx7 → printing sub-paths, `_travel_mask` rule, weld/Z-lift safe) + `check_toolpath/check_trajectory`. GUI: `gui/dialogs/xy_geometry_panel_dialog.py` (shape×size matrix, ideal grey vs simulated dashed vs hardware solid coloured by verdict; Simulate = instant/no motion, Run-on-stage = centred + poller/watchdog suspended + poll-verified gotos + VS 0,0 finally; persists/reloads `logs/challenge/geometry_panel_*.json`; launched from the challenge dialog's **📐 Geometry panel…**) and `gui/dialogs/sketch_printability_dialog.py` + **🎯 Check printability** on Print Builder → Sketch (splits the compiled trajectory, simulates each printing sub-path with saved characteristics + current tuning, rings the predicted fail/warn regions, per-path "stalls at 38%" detail, speed spin re-simulates instantly; verdicts use `PREDICTION_HEADROOM`=2.0). **VALIDATED on an 18-cell real panel (6 shapes × 2/5/10 mm) driven through the SAME shipped `follow_path`: 16/18 verdict agreement, ALL 4 real stalls predicted (wall-times to ~0.1 s), median actual/predicted p95 = 1.56 → the 2.0 headroom; headline: Star 2 mm predicted deadlock (p95 1111, wall-cap) → actual deadlock (p95 1154, dither 22.8)** — a hardware failure called before the stage moved. Two operational findings (sim AND hw): **180° reversals stall the velocity follower** (all Line-Reversal cells; the sketch back-trace/retrace features produce exactly this geometry → velocity-mode retraced prints will stall until Stage 3.3 lands; the checker flags it at design time), and **feature size ≲ 2× lookahead risks a pursuit orbit at low speed** (Star 2 mm @ 0.892 lookahead deadlocked). Tests: `test_v75x_xy_stage_model.py` (14) + `test_v75x_xy_path_simulator.py` (24, incl. the hardware-verified operating point pinned in a loose band) + `test_v75x_geometry_panel_and_printability.py` (11); challenge/calibration regression 195 + sketch-page 21 green. **⭐ FEED PLANNER — 18/18 SHAPES ≤ 30 µm ON REAL HARDWARE (2026-07-28 second panel; operator: "we know the print path a priori … get all 18 shapes under 30 microns"):** derived-optimal TUNING alone still failed 16/18 in sim (corner cut ≈ 0.4·lookahead and reversal stalls are structural), so NEW pure **`SupportClasses/XYFeedPlan.py`** builds a deterministic, feature-aware plan FROM the path: sharp corners (>55°) + 180° reversals become section SPLITS (decelerate, stop ON the vertex at 10 µm arrive + settle dwell, continue as a fresh section → zero corner cut / zero reversal stall by construction); curved sections get a curvature-sized lookahead `la=0.8·√(2·R·δ)` (pursuit standoff la²/2R inverted for budget δ=element/1.6) whose dead-time stability rule sets the section speed `v=la/(L·safety)` (2 mm circle → 0.79 mm/s; straights → full speed); `build_plan` (per-section reasoning strings) / `run_plan` (sections = consecutive `follow_path` runs over the same injected I/O — sim ≡ hw) / `simulate_plan` (scored vs the FULL path) / `plan_rows` (time-matched X/Y/Z/pump rows, volume ∝ arc length, stop dwells — the deterministic all-axis trajectory). Enabling fix: **lag-aware end taper** in `follow_path` (opt-in `arrive_mm`/`end_lag_s`/`end_floor_frac`, defaults = legacy) — the naive `remaining/decel` taper commanded speeds for where the stage WAS a dead-time ago → crossed section ends at ~1.5 mm/s and overshot a measured **153 µm**; the taper now subtracts MEASURED velocity (successive reads; commanded under-predicts during braking) × lag, floor ~80 µm/s → coast ≈ 8 µm. **Planned HW panel (`geometry_panel_planned_20260728_172210.json`): 18/18 ≤ 30 µm, actual p95 1–27 µm — Star 2 mm 1154 µm DEADLOCK → 7 µm, Square 2 mm 348 → 1 µm, Line-Reversal STALL → 1 µm; sim predicted 18/18 first (p95 0–16); stops cost ~10–20 % wall time.** Figures: `logs/challenge/geometry_panel_planned_*.png` + `geometry_panel_before_after.png`; renderer `tools_render_geometry_panel.py` (repo root; newest panel default; `MEBP_CHALLENGE_LOG_DIR` redirects panel records in tests). **Quick Print:** `PrintTrajectoryMonitorView.set_predicted_path(segments, note)` (dashed peach simulated stage path, in the auto-fit bbox) + live **progress readout** (`print 42% · 12.3/29.0 mm · dev 38 µm` — forward-only `project_on_polyline` cursor over the plan while recording); `QuickPrintWorkflowPage._kick_prediction` simulates on a background thread after every plan refresh (velocity mode + measured machine only; generation-tagged) via new `_PrintBridge.predicted`; motion-mode combo refreshes it. Wiring the FeedPlan into the print EXECUTOR is the tracked follow-up (pump semantics need their own pass). Tests: `test_v75x_xy_feed_plan.py` (17) + `test_v75x_quick_print_predicted_overlay.py` (8). |
| `MEBP_v75x_STAGE_JOG_OFF_GUI_THREAD.md` | v7.5.x **fix — "the camera freezes whenever I use any other stages in jog mode."** Follow-on to `MEBP_v75x_PUMP_JOG_OFF_GUI_THREAD.md` (pump was already off-thread): the **XY/Z** incremental-jog handlers `HardwareControlPanel._on_jog_xy`/`_on_jog_z` (the jog-button-array, used on Jog / Calibration / jog workflows) still ran on the GUI thread — the move (`move_xy_relative_um`/`move_z_user_relative`, a serial round-trip; Z is the Marlin `ok`-handshaked write) **plus** `_force_refresh_positions()` doing **two** blocking `cached=False` serial reads (Prior `P` + Marlin `M114`, contended with the poller) — freezing the Qt event loop so no `QTimer` camera feed could tick. The button jog was the worst case (move + two fresh reads). **Fix:** new `_run_stage_jog(busy_attr, move_fn, label)` runs the speed-set + move + the fresh `get_xy_position`/`get_zp_position(cached=False)` reads on a daemon thread (serial access is `_serial_lock`-protected), then hands the results to the GUI thread via a new queued `_stage_jog_done(object, object)` signal → `_on_stage_jog_done` paints the readout with **no serial I/O on the GUI thread**; per-axis busy guards (`_xy_jog_busy`/`_z_jog_busy`) drop overlapping clicks (independent, so XY isn't blocked by an in-flight Z). Jog behaviour + signal contract unchanged. Keyboard jog (`jog_control._key_jog_*`, move-only, no fresh reads) left on the GUI thread (much cheaper; key auto-repeat wants tight coupling) — documented follow-up. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1/V3** (jog XY/Z while watching a live camera feed — no freeze; readout still updates). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_stage_jog_off_gui_thread.py` (3: XY off-thread + overlapping-click dropped + fresh reads off-thread; Z off-thread; independent guards) + updated `test_v75x_axis_max_speed_inputs.py` (wait for the backgrounded `set_velocity`); jog/speed/context regression 136 green. |
| `MEBP_v75x_QUICK_PRINT_CONFIRMED_SEGMENTS.md` | v7.5.x **fix — Quick Print ink laydown XY trajectory "very wrong" (smeared) while pickup/wash were fine → CLOSED-LOOP velocity following** (operator, 2026-07-27). Root-caused from the operator's own run `logs/prints/print_20260727_171512_..._B6.jsonl`: PRINT_PATH streams each XY segment OPEN-LOOP (`move_xy_absolute` fire-and-forget, paced only by `time.sleep(seg_len/print_speed)`, nothing waits for arrival), but the Prior stage runs **~2.5× slower** than commanded (`SMS 11%`, measured `max=22282 µm/s`; optimistic SMS%↔speed cal + short ~0.48 mm accel-dominated segments), so the physical stage falls PROGRESSIVELY behind the stream — sampled `lag_um` grew **0 → 6144 (median) → 10736 (max) µm** across a ~10 mm print, end-of-path drain **timed out 30 s still 2.09 mm short** — while the pump extrudes on the fast software schedule → smeared pattern. Pickup/wash are point-to-point `safe_travel_to` (WAIT for arrival → slow≠wrong); PRINT_PATH is the only open-loop path (slow→wrong). **Two executor modes added to `_execute_print_path`, both gated by new `PrintSettings` flags (default False = legacy open-loop, so Full Print / Print Setup unchanged):** **(1) `velocity_follow` — CLOSED-LOOP velocity following, the Quick Print default (operator asked for continuous velocity correction, NOT stop-and-go):** NEW `_execute_print_path_velocity` runs a ~25 Hz (`_VEL_CONTROL_HZ`) loop that each tick polls the ACTUAL position (`get_xy_position(cached=False)`; poller already suspended for PRINT_PATH), projects it onto the toolpath → real **arc-length** progress `s` (`project_on_polyline`), deposits pump volume ∝ REAL `Δs` (`vol_per_mm=flow/print_speed`; non-blocking, no M400 in-loop), and commands a **velocity vector** (`send_velocity_xy`→Prior `VS,vx,vy` µm/s) toward a **carrot** placed `_VEL_LOOKAHEAD_MM` ahead of `s` (pure pursuit), ramping down over the last `_VEL_DECEL_MM`. Carrot tied to REAL progress ⇒ it can never run ahead of a slow stage (the open-loop failure mode) + position feedback re-centres each tick + pump tracks real distance ⇒ bead correct at any speed. SMS set to max + brisk accel so `VS` reaches the commanded velocity; velocity clamped to `safety_limits.max_xy_speed`. **Safety:** always stops the stage (`VS 0,0`) on every exit (abort/pause/ZP-drop/stale/runaway/completion/exception via `finally`); a sustained cross-track error (`>_VEL_MAX_CROSS_TRACK_MM` for `_VEL_RUNAWAY_TICKS` — e.g. a `VS` sign inversion) trips a **runaway guard** → `RuntimeError` → outer loop ERROR + retract to safe Z. Falls back to the discrete path on a stage without a continuous-velocity cmd (Ludl HLC). NEW pure helpers `polyline_arclength`/`point_at_arclength`/`project_on_polyline` (unit-tested). **(2) `confirm_each_segment` — stop-and-go fallback (landed first, NOT the Quick Print default):** waits for physical arrival (`_wait_for_xy_settle`, ±`_SEGMENT_SETTLE_TOL_UM`=40 µm) + drains the pump (M400) after EACH segment; correct but stop-start (slower), so superseded by velocity_follow for Quick Print, retained as a general executor fallback. **Also fixed:** the Timing-Calibration **"Run sweep" button was always greyed** — `_update_button_state` gates on live XY+ZP connection but was only called on construction / calibration-data pushes, never on `showEvent`/`on_status_update`, so a connect made after opening the page left it stuck; now refreshed on show + each tick. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (pattern correct/no smear + continuous motion; JSONL `path_start.mode:"velocity"` + `vel_sample.cross_um` stays small; **VERIFY `VS` DIRECTION FIRST on a low safe path — if the stage flies off, the runaway guard aborts+retracts → flip the `vx/vy` sign; tune `_VEL_LOOKAHEAD_MM`/`_VEL_CONTROL_HZ` to real latency**; sweep button enables once XY+ZP connected). Design: reused the existing `send_velocity_xy`→`move_stage_at_velocity`(`VS`, already used by Xbox jog) + poller-suspended PRINT_PATH; chose arc-length pure-pursuit over the existing time-based `VelocityExecutor`/`MotionController` (a time-parametrized target still runs ahead of a slow stage). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_quick_print_velocity_follow.py` (13, incl. integrating fake stage that follows `VS`/deposits-per-distance/runaway + spiral snap) + `tests/test_v75x_quick_print_confirmed_segments.py` (7); barrier regression (10); quick-print/timing/seam/pick-place/multi-ink/exec-log suites green. **Addendum (2026-07-27) — spiral projection-snap runaway FIXED:** first HW run tripped the runaway guard (`print_20260727_184045_..._C5.jsonl`) — NOT a sign error (tracking was ~100 µm for 6.5 mm, position responded in the commanded `VS` direction), but on the **spiral** toolpath `s` jumped **+4.8 mm while position moved 0.2 mm** then cross-track blew to 3.57 mm. Root cause: the projection's forward search window (`lookahead+4=4.6 mm` arc length) EXCEEDED an inner spiral loop's circumference, so the projection SNAPPED to the next loop (passes near the same x,y) → carrot teleported → runaway (+ a one-tick pump over-deposit). Fix: bound the projection search AND per-tick `s` advance to the physically-plausible `print_speed × real_dt × _VEL_SNAP_GUARD` (ceiling `_VEL_MAX_SNAP_MM`=1.5) — far below a loop's circumference (loops are a full revolution apart in arc length), well above the true per-tick advance, so `s` can't jump a loop and `Δs` (pump) is bounded. Locked by `test_bounded_window_prevents_loop_snap` + `test_completes_multiloop_spiral_without_snap`. Tuning note: on the innermost loops the fixed `_VEL_LOOKAHEAD_MM`=0.6 is a large fraction of the circumference → some corner rounding; reduce/adapt lookahead if inner loops print too round. **Addendum 2 (2026-07-27, "the old version worked on my other setup; why not here?"):** investigation (git + this machine's configs/logs) — the open-loop path is correct WHEN the stage tracks commanded speed; **this machine delivers only ~40% of commanded print speed and it's NOT a recent regression** — `print_timing_calibration.json` (2026-06-18) already recorded `5.0→1.85 mm/s` (correction 2.71), and today's open-loop log agrees (2.49→~1.0). Accel is fine (`SAS,80`, range [1,100]); Quick Print anchors speed% to the measured CRUISE top speed (22282 µm/s) the stage never reaches on a print's short segments → commanded ~2.5× > delivered → open-loop runs ahead → smear. The other machine tracks its rated speed / lacks the stored value. Decision (operator "do both, A/B on hardware"): Quick Print settings popout gains a **Motion mode** selector (`_motion_mode_combo`→`_motion_mode()`→`_build_settings` maps to the flags): **`open_loop` (DEFAULT — the original streamed path that worked on the other setup)**, `velocity`, `confirm` — so the same print runs each way on this machine for direct comparison; persisted with the profile. Tests updated (velocity 13→14; confirmed selector). Fastest fix for the old path on this machine: re-run Timing-Calibration "Measure top speed" (button works now) or lower the speed % until commanded ≈ real throughput. **Addendum 3 (2026-07-27, "the camera isn't a good tool for this — set up the calibrator a different way; my hunch is this stage is slower"):** the timing calibrator only used the camera as a still-vs-moving detector; the stage's OWN reported position is a better one (rest ≈0-1 µm/poll, motion ≈hundreds of µm/poll; immune to focus/texture/light). NEW `_EncoderMotion` (drop-in for `_FrameMotion` — same available/reset/metric; `metric()`=|Δposition| µm via `get_xy_position(cached=False)`, poller suspended during a run) + a **Detector selector** ("Stage position (no camera)" DEFAULT / "Microscope camera") → `_make_tracker()`; `_preflight` only requires the camera in camera mode. Both "Measure top speed" AND the settle sweep now run camera-free into the same store (`set_xy_max_speed_um_s`/`correction_for`) + `_apply_measured_xy_top_speed` fan-out — directly testing the "stage is slow" hunch (if measured top speed ≪ stored 22282 µm/s, storing it makes the mm/s↔SMS conversion real so a commanded print speed is achievable and the open-loop path stops running ahead). Follow-up lever (not wired; tried+reverted once — revisit with the camera-free measurement): pace open-loop `_execute_print_path` by the stored per-speed `correction_factor`. **Follow-up (2026-07-27) — settle sweep timed out on the encoder** (top speed worked; sweep's short/slow/streamed segments didn't): it reused the camera's scene-relative `_calibrate_threshold` — on a short segment the "peak" caught one poll spanning the whole ~260 µm move → threshold set high (~78 µm), but each sweep poll only advances ~40 µm (below threshold) → motion never detected → `_watch_until_still` timeout → no data. Fix: NEW `_encoder_threshold` (used by both `_run`+`_run_top_speed` when the tracker is `_EncoderMotion`) measures ONLY at-rest jitter (no move — the move-peak was the fragile part) and sets still-threshold `max(STILL_UM, floor×3, floor+3)`, far below real per-poll motion. Tests: `tests/test_v75x_timing_encoder_detector.py` (13) + timing suite (33 green). **Addendum 4 (2026-07-28) — comms-frequency checker + open-loop redefined as feed-forward velocity streaming.** (A) NEW `StageController.measure_control_loop_rate` (interleaved `send_velocity_xy`+`get_xy_position(cached=False)` cadence measured WHILE the stage oscillates ±amplitude, net ~0; poller suspended; always `VS 0,0`+return-to-start; speed clamped to `max_xy_speed`) = the real closed-loop `loop_period` that bounds the velocity follower's max stable speed — distinct from the poll-only `test_command_rate`. Persisted via `PrintTimingCalibrationStore.get/set_control_loop_ms` + `stable_velocity_speed_mm_s(lookahead,safety)` (`v≈lookahead/(loop·safety)`); surfaced as a **"Check comms rate"** button on the Timing-Calibration run row (XY-connected gate; retracts to Safe Z; no camera) logging loop ms/Hz/read/cmd + derived max stable speed. (B) Velocity-follower "back-and-forth over the same line" root-caused = **dead-time limit cycle**: the loop free-runs at serial-latency rate (~5–10 Hz, not 25), `VS` persists the whole tick, so when `speed×loop_period>lookahead` the stage overshoots the carrot → reverses → dithers, worst at the endpoint/reversals (15% decel floor > 40 µm arrive tol). Not a sign bug (that's a runaway). Dead-time-aware speed clamp is the proposed next fix; the checker measures the bound. (C) **Operator: open-loop should "just update the velocity vector needed, not move to prescribed points — that's confirm."** So `open_loop` → NEW `_execute_print_path_open_velocity`: feed-forward `VS` along the path **tangent**, target advancing by wall-clock time at commanded speed, NO position reads / NO point-to-point (continuous velocity for even laydown; `vol_per_mm=flow/print_speed` so `pace_correction` stretches time not volume; decel ramp + a final positioning move lands exactly on the endpoint; wall-cap + always-stop; no runaway guard — verify VS direction; falls back to the discrete point-stream with no `send_velocity_xy`). NEW helper `tangent_at_arclength`; `PrintSettings.velocity_open_loop`; Quick Print maps `open_loop`→`velocity_open_loop` (combo relabelled "Open-loop velocity (streamed vectors)"/"Confirmed per-segment (point-to-point)"); XY Challenge `_drive_open_loop` rewritten to the same streamer. `confirm`=point-to-point+wait, `velocity`=closed-loop pursuit (both unchanged). **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (comms Hz plausible; open-loop velocity lays a continuous bead — VERIFY VS direction on a low safe path first). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_comms_frequency_check.py` (12) + `tests/test_v75x_open_loop_velocity.py` (12); regression open-loop-velocity/comms/velocity-follow/confirmed/challenge/timing/encoder/barrier 104 + quick-print pick-place/multi-ink 61 green. **Addendum 5 (2026-07-28) — corner-aware closed-loop + shared control law + XY-Challenge rework** (operator: velocity closed-loop is the good mode; make it consume measured calibration, cut corner overshoot, rework the challenge bench, and fix confirm to stop only at corners). AskUserQuestion: scheduling **+ cross-track PID**; **both** ZN-relay + coordinate-descent auto-tune; configurable-small robustness matrix (PASS/FAIL vs a 30 µm resolution element). **NEW `SupportClasses/VelocityControl.py`** — the shared PURE law both the XY-Challenge bench AND `PrintManager._execute_print_path_velocity` run (tuning transfers): arc-length helpers MOVED here (PrintManager re-exports), `corner_flags`, `plan_speed_limits` (per-corner speed limit from turn angle, reachable ramp over `decel_mm`), `PursuitState`/`pursuit_step` (carrot pursuit + perpendicular **cross-track PID** kp/kd, clamped to `min(speed_cap, corner-limit)`), `resolve_control` (grounds control_hz in `control_loop_ms`, SMS/clamp in `xy_max_speed_um_s`, + a **dead-time speed cap** `v≤lookahead/((loop+phase)·safety)` that kills the pure-pursuit "back-and-forth" limit-cycle), `relay_ultimate_gain` (Åström Ku=4d/πa). Follower rewired onto it (measured-cal + corner slowdown + PID, all default-off = legacy; end-decel now ramps from the CAPPED speed → clean stop). **Confirm branch**: `corner_flags(points, confirm_corner_angle_deg)` → wait/drain ONLY at corners + last point, straight edges STREAM (barrier runs on streamed nodes) — so the star stops at its tips, not every node. New `PrintSettings` fields (default 0=legacy): `xy_max_speed_um_s`/`control_loop_ms`/`phase_lag_s`/`xy_accel_pct`/`vel_corner_angle_deg`/`vel_corner_speed_factor`/`vel_pid_kp`/`vel_pid_kd`/`confirm_corner_angle_deg`. Store: velocity/confirm defaults gain corner+PID keys; `get/set_resolution_element_um`(30), `get/set_robustness_matrix`, `get_phase_lag_s`. **`gui/dialogs/xy_challenge_dialog.py` reworked**: overlay recolour by role (ideal grey / newest **red** / best **green** / others low-alpha **blue**); per-mode param visibility (mode combo → `_refresh_param_visibility`); drivers call the shared law (bench==print); auto-tune = **coordinate descent** over each mode's ordered param list + a **Auto-tune PID (ZN)** relay button (velocity) → `compute_zn_pid_gains`; new **Robustness panel** (shape×size×speed matrix, PASS/FAIL vs the resolution element). Quick Print `_build_settings` stamps all new tuned + measured fields. Reused `MotionController.compute_zn_pid_gains` + `TrajectoryPlanner.detect_corners` logic. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (verify VS direction first; tighter corners + no back-and-forth; ZN/descent converge; confirm stops at star tips only; robustness green ≤ element). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_velocity_control.py` (17) + `tests/test_v75x_xy_challenge_panel.py` (8) + updated confirmed-segments (corner-only) + xy_challenge (new defaults); regression 125 + 124 + 32 green. |
| `MEBP_v75x_MOSAIC_SCALE_FOV_AND_REGISTRATION.md` | v7.5.x **mosaic/camera calibration batch** (operator, 2026-07-24: "the mosaic calibration is not working … it autocalculates the camera FOV but assumes a resolution — pull it from the camera hardware; the camera setup should auto-calibrate µm/px from stage motion; calculate the camera extents in microns by tracking edge features across a move; mirror the camera output in that view; a better registration algorithm — the current feature detection doesn't work when moving the stage; and the video everywhere should match the camera's aspect, not a fixed box"). AskUserQuestion: **stage-motion edge-feature auto-cal** (items 1-3) + **full pairwise + global-optimize** registration (item 5). Read-only agents root-caused each first. **(item 1 — "FOV assumes a resolution")** the mosaic FOV = `frame_w × _ploc_microscope_um_per_px(fw, um0)` = `objective_meas × cal_w` — pinned to the objective calibration's STORED resolution `cal_w`, which `ObjectiveCalibrationCard._on_calibrate_clicked` stamped from `config.camera_config.active_resolution` (stale/assumed). Fix: new `_true_capture_resolution(cam_idx)` reads the LIVE `frame.shape` (authoritative) → HW settings → config, used by BOTH the auto-cal and the existing objective cal, so µm/px is always stamped with the REAL captured resolution. **(items 2-3 — stage-motion scale + FOV auto-cal)** NEW `VisionDetector.select_trackable_patch` (most-textured central patch) + existing `find_template` (TM_CCOEFF_NORMED) track a feature across a LARGE baseline (where whole-frame phase corr loses lock); NEW `gui/dialogs/scale_fov_calibration_dialog.py::ScaleFovCalibrationDialog` (live feed + mirror control; auto-sizes the baseline to ~40% of the frame; QTimer state machine moves +X then +Y, tracks the feature each, derives `µm/px = move/|disp|` per axis, `FOV = frame_px × µm/px` extent, rotation via `plus_column_direction_deg`) — all anchored to the captured `frame.shape`, exposed as `result_um_per_px/_rotation_deg/_resolution/_fov_um`, >15% X-vs-Y disagreement warns; **"Auto-calibrate scale + FOV…"** button on `ObjectiveCalibrationCard` persists per-objective (`ObjectiveCalibrationStore.set_calibration`, true resolution) + live to the manager + rotation per identity — the SAME sinks the mosaic reads, so it flows into the mosaic FOV. **(item 4 — mirror in the calibration view)** `PixelCalibrationDialog` (and the new dialog) seed the feed with the camera's `(mirror, rotation)` via `set_view_orientation` + a **"Camera shows a mirrored image"** checkbox → flips the DISPLAY live, pushes `set_mirrored`, persists per identity; µm/px is measured on RAW frames so it can't corrupt the result. **(item 5 — better registration)** tiles were placed OPEN-LOOP by stage position with only ONE global median phase-corr shift on the display extent (weak confidence, no illumination/rotation handling); the richer batch path was gated off. NEW `MosaicBuilder._register_overlap_cv2` (CLAHE-normalise + Hanning + `cv2.phaseCorrelate` real cross-power peak as confidence; sign LOCKED by test) + `optimize_registration()` (registers EVERY overlapping pair over the retained canvas-res tiles → weighted global least-squares `_global_optimize_positions`, tile 0 anchored → `_reblend_at_positions` at the optimized positions; each correction BOUNDED to `_max_shift_px` so a bad match can't fling a tile; degenerate solve = no-op keeping the open-loop composite; bounded memory via the small canvas-res tiles, so the batch >200-tile gate is irrelevant). Wired in `calibration.py::_MosaicScanWorker`: full-plate scan builder now `retain_for_reorient=True`; after the tile loop → `optimize_registration()` → `finalize_global_shift` (extent offset, complementary) → detection on the improved composite → `free_reorient()` (frees tiles + ~190 MB accumulators, keeps the display cache). **(item 6 — camera aspect)** content was never distorted (`KeepAspectRatio` on the true `frame.shape`) — the operator saw LETTERBOX bars because the widget box didn't track the camera W:H; `CameraFeedView` gains `heightForWidth` (driven by `_last_image_size`, swapped for a ~90° view rotation) + a `setHeightForWidth(True)` size policy + `_maybe_update_geometry` (fires only on aspect change), so box/splitter hosts fill camera-shaped; grid hosts keep the undistorted fit (no regression). One base class → every live feed inherits it. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (auto-cal → plausible µm/px+FOV+rotation at the true resolution; scanned mosaic tiles line up better — flip the one-line `_register_overlap_cv2` sign if registration goes the wrong way; video fills its box with the camera aspect; mirror checkbox flips the feed + persists; FOV sizes from the real resolution). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_camera_scale_fov_and_registration.py` (11, incl. the +7 px injected-error optimize/sign-lock); mosaic (58) + objective/rotation/mapping (77) + camera-store/correction (49) + cal-liveview/hw-controls (32) suites green. **Addendum (2026-07-24, operator follow-ups):** **(A)** the mosaic "still had the wrong values" because a stale **"Store FOV/spacing" learned value in `MosaicAlignmentStore` SHADOWED** the fresh objective/scale calibration (precedence: learned→else objective, and un-rescaled) — fixed: a fresh calibration now CLEARS the learned value (`ObjectiveCalibrationCard._clear_stale_mosaic_fov` + `_mosaic_align_key` matching `_ploc_camera_objective_key`) and the mosaic rescales BOTH the learned value (by its stored resolution) and the objective fallback (via `CameraManager.effective_um_per_px`) to the scan frame width. **(B)** new **"🔍 Verify with a quick mosaic"** button on `ScaleFovCalibrationDialog` builds a 3×3 self-check mosaic at the current position with the measured µm/px (reuses `_MosaicScanWorker`+`MosaicBuilder`+global-optimize, applies the camera orientation, displays the composite) — **safe: only moves XY at the current height, never descends** (retract Z=current, `target_z=None`). **(C)** new opt-in **`CameraFeedView(auto_orient=True)`** re-reads the camera's saved mirror/rotation from `CameraManager.view_orientation` each frame (display-only + click-inverting; edge-pick-suppressed), enabled in both calibration dialogs so the calibration view stays consistent with the live/mosaic views (scoped to the dialogs — `CameraManager` has no role getter, and a blanket default would wrongly rotate the ~45° needle feeds; enable per-view on any workflow microscope feed still found inconsistent). Also fixed a pre-existing gap: `test_v75x_plate_mosaic._FakeCtrl.safe_travel_to` lacked the `apply_insert_floor` kwarg the earlier rosette-Z work added. Tests: suite now 19; regression 94 + 90 green. **Addendum 2 (2026-07-24, 2nd follow-up):** **(1)** the "Microscope Camera Setup" block's resolution went stale when the camera **settings gear** changed the device resolution (only the block's own combo drove the device, not the reverse) — fixed with a signal chain `CameraSettingsDialog.resolution_applied(cam_idx,w,h)` → `CameraFeedView.resolution_changed` → `HardwareSetupPage._on_slot_resolution_changed` (updates `camera_config.active_resolution` + the block combo for the microscope slot). **(2)** the mosaic's x,y-vs-camera mapping was flipped (camera-axis-vs-stage orientation wrong) — corrected via the operator flip controls in (3), which persist per identity + propagate. **(3)** the Scale/FOV "Verify" is now **"🔍 Verify & correct (mosaic)…"** which opens the full `MosaicCalibrationDialog` (reused) seeded with the measured µm/px + orientation + current pos + no-descent safe Z + the objective keys — giving the X/Y spacing sliders + rotate + Store-FOV + **relabelled flip buttons "⇄ Flip X axis" / "⇅ Flip Y axis"**; new `MosaicCalibrationDialog._propagate_um_per_px` (from "Store FOV/spacing") pushes the corrected µm/px to the live manager (click mapping) AND the objective store (mosaic fallback + restore) so a correction is GROUND TRUTH everywhere; the card clears the stale learned FOV BEFORE the dialog (not after) so a fresh correction survives; Scale/FOV re-syncs its result from the manager after the correction so Accept can't clobber it. Tests: suite now 26; regression 78 + 112 + 52 green. **Addendum 3 (2026-07-24, 3rd follow-up):** **(1)** the full-plate scan "didn't apply the calibrated rotations/scales" — wiring existed (`_ploc_microscope_frame_orientation` + `eff_um_per_px`), so hardened: orientation now falls back to the persisted `CameraCalibrationStore` value when the live manager has none, and the scan logs `Mosaic scan applying: resolution … µm/px … FOV … rotation … mirror … registration …`. **(2)** "registration is very bad — explore better methods (Fourier-Mellin) + a manual backup": NEW `MosaicBuilder._register_overlap_fourier_mellin` (log-polar of the FFT magnitude → rotation+scale via phase corr, then de-rotate/scale + phase-corr for translation; CLAHE; recovers −9,−4 / −8°/×0.909 on synthetic data), used by DEFAULT in `optimize_registration(method=…)` with phase-corr fallback + median rotation/scale diagnostic; **manual backup** = a `registration_method` param + a "Registration method" combo (Fourier-Mellin / Phase / **Off — manual/stage only**), where Off makes optimize a no-op and the operator aligns by hand. **(3)** camera resolution now has ONE source of truth — `camera_config.active_resolution` ALWAYS drives the microscope on start (`_on_camera_started_hw` no longer gates on hw_controls; `_apply_hw_controls` skips resolution for the microscope; `_maybe_apply_resolution_to_device` reads active_resolution) so the camera reads the setup block as ground truth. Tests: suite now 34; updated `test_active_resolution_is_single_source_for_microscope` (active_resolution wins over hw_controls — intended contract change) + settings round-trip for `reg_method`; regression 63 + 115 green. **Addendum 4 (2026-07-24, 4th follow-up — "I literally just calibrated it"):** **(A)** the mosaic's objective-confirm warned "⚠ No stored per-objective calibration" although the µm/px in use WAS the calibrated value — KEY MISMATCH: `_ploc_confirm_objective` looked up the objective store via `_get_camera_model_for_idx()` (extracted model TOKEN) while the card WRITES + the FOV read (`_ploc_microscope_um_per_px`) use the full `camera_spec.name`; fixed to use `camera_spec.name` (the calibration was stored + used; only the note was wrong). **(B)** "full mosaic not using the correct flip/rotation": `_ploc_microscope_frame_orientation` read the LIVE manager, which can come up UN-SYNCED after navigating from Hardware Setup → saw 0°/unmirrored; fixed to per-field precedence preferring the PERSISTED `CameraCalibrationStore` ground truth (manager fills absent fields) + a diagnostic log of the resolved rotation/mirror + source. Tests: suite 36; regression 83 + 64 green. **Addendum 5 (2026-07-24, "the mosaic is not flipping the x axis"):** the correction dialog's flip/rotate (`MosaicCalibrationDialog._orient_set`) pushed orientation LIVE to the manager but persisted to the store ONLY on "Apply to camera setup" — so a flip made without Apply never reached the store, and the full scan (which reads the store as ground truth per Addendum 4) saw no mirror. Fix: `_orient_set` now PERSISTS IMMEDIATELY (`_persist_orientation` → `CameraCalibrationStore.set_rotation`/`set_mirrored` per identity) on every flip/rotate, so the flip is ground truth at once and the full mosaic applies it via `_orient_tile` at tile placement. Tests: suite 38 (flip persists mirror, rotate persists rotation); regression 65 green. If the WRONG axis flips, it's an `_orient_tile` sign issue, not persistence. **Addendum 6 (2026-07-24, per-camera Flip X/Y + rotation):** (A) fixed the objective-confirm KEY MISMATCH (`_ploc_confirm_objective` used `_get_camera_model_for_idx` model-token vs the card's full `camera_spec.name`) → the false "No stored calibration" warning. (B) operator: "on each camera a checkbox to flip axis X, Y + custom rotation" → AskUserQuestion: ONE unified system affecting live view + mosaic + click-mapping, per-slot on Hardware Setup → Cameras. Added an INDEPENDENT **flip Y** to the existing (mirror=flip X, rotation) so the transform is `R(θ)·diag(sx,sy)` in `MosaicBuilder._orient_tile`, `CameraFeedView._orient_qimage`, and `pixel_to_stage_offset` (negates dy); `CameraManager.get/set_flip_y`+`full_orientation`; `CameraCalibrationStore.get/set_flip_y`; `_ploc_microscope_frame_orientation→(rotation,flip_x,flip_y)` (store=ground truth) threaded into both scan builders + feed; per-slot **⇄ Flip X axis**/**⇅ Flip Y axis** checkboxes + **Rotation °** spin (push-all-consumers + persist per identity + restore); the calibrate dialog's flips toggle flip_x/flip_y independently. Tests: scale-fov suite 44 + rotation-monitor 42 (per-slot push/persist/restore); regression 90 + 91 + 48 green. **Addendum 7 (2026-07-27, "the placement of the images is not on the correct side … stage +X ≠ camera +X, same for Y"):** operator had to manually flip X+Y to make the objective mosaic stitch, got it **upside down**, and the full mosaic placed tiles **on the wrong side**. Root cause: `_orient_tile` was fed the operator's **eyeball live-view flips**, but a **mirror is not a rotation** and `plus_column_direction_deg` **explicitly assumes non-mirrored**, so the scale/FOV auto-cal derived rotation ONLY and **discarded the handedness** — even though the +X and +Y move displacements (`_disp_x`/`_disp_y`) encode the mirror in their **cross-product sign**. Combined with the plate view's `plate_flip_180`=True (default from `None`) 180° display flip, it double-counted. **Fix (measure, don't guess):** new `scale_fov_calibration_dialog.derive_camera_stage_orientation(dxX,dyX,dxY,dyY)` decomposes the FULL camera→stage 2×2 (`M=-(d/µmpx)·P⁻¹`) into the canonical `(rotation_deg,flip_x,flip_y)` that `_orient_tile`/`pixel_to_stage_offset` (`R(θ)·diag(mx,my)`) consume (`sign(det)=mx·my` → handedness on flip_y; θ from M's col0), **sign-locked** by `test_derive_camera_stage_orientation` (identity / mirror-X→(180°,flipY) / mirror-Y / **180° mount→(180°,NO flip)** / 90° / 90°+mirror) and provably == `plus_column_direction_deg` for any non-mirrored camera (no regression). `_compute` stores `result_flip_x`/`result_flip_y`; the caller (`ObjectiveCalibrationCard._on_autocal_scale_fov_clicked`) pushes them to the manager (`set_mirrored`/`set_flip_y`) + persists per identity alongside rotation → the live view, mosaic `_orient_tile`, and clicks all use the ONE **measured** orientation. Verify-step re-sync reads `full_orientation`. **Also WYSIWYG:** `MosaicRegistrationView.set_flip_180` (whole-view 180° rotation) + `MosaicCalibrationDialog(plate_flip_180=None)` auto-resolves from `controller.plate_flip_180()` → the calibration view renders in the SAME plate frame as the full mosaic (no more "upside down here, wrong side there"). Manual flips remain as override; if the GLOBAL sign is inverted on a given camera build (documented `phaseCorrelate`/`plus_column_direction_deg` caveat) one manual flip corrects + persists. Tests: scale-fov +10 (`TestDeriveCameraStageOrientation`×8 + `TestScaleFovComputeDerivesFlips`×2) + mosaic-adjust +5; regression 145 green. **Addendum 7b (2026-07-27, "calibration builds correctly the first try, but the full-plate mosaic scan still isn't correct"):** DECISIVE root cause of the full-plate divergence = a **double orientation**. The full-plate `_MosaicScanWorker` applied a **legacy coarse per-tile `_orient_frame`** (`none/rot180/fliph/flipv` from `mosaic_scan.frame_orient`) to each raw frame, THEN the builder applied the **calibrated `_orient_tile`** (rotation+flipX+flipY from the store) on top → orientation TWICE. The operator's `settings.json` had a stale `frame_orient="rot180"` (a pre-calibration compensator), so the whole-plate scan was rotated an extra 180°. The **calibration dialog worked** only because it's built with `settings={}` → `frame_orient` defaults `"none"` → single orientation (`_orient_tile`) — exactly "calibration right, full plate wrong." **Fix:** RETIRE the coarse `frame_orient` in `_MosaicScanWorker` (`self._frame_orient` forced `"none"`; `_orient_frame` now an identity no-op; the `frame = self._orient_frame(frame)` call removed; the scan setup no longer reads/passes it) → tile orientation is applied ONCE by the calibrated `_orient_tile` on both the full-plate scan and the calibration-dialog worker (same class). `settings.json`'s stale `rot180` left as-is — the SEPARATE fluorescence worker (`_SingleWellMosaicWorker`, unoriented builder) still reads that shared key for its own coarse orientation (documented follow-up); the plate mosaic now ignores it. Test: `test_worker_does_not_double_orient`; plate-mosaic/fluorescence/mapping/scale-fov/mosaic-adjust regression 121 green. Combined with Addendum 7 (measured handedness), the full mosaic now assembles the same as the calibration. |
| `MEBP_v75x_SKETCH_OVERLAP_AND_START_END_MARKERS.md` | v7.5.x **Print Builder → Sketch: closure OVERLAP + draggable start/end markers** (operator: "some shapes don't close due to the printing method; add a start↔end overlap — a **distance** overlap or a **needle-diameter** overlap that continues along the shape's own path past the start; also control start + end by **dragging markers along the path**"). AskUserQuestion: closed-shape end marker = **overlap handle** (slides past the seam, synced with the numeric control); open shapes = start+end **trim** to a sub-segment. **Model (one source of truth):** RETIRES the boolean `SketchShape.overlap_closure` → `overlap_mode` ("none"/"needle"/"distance") + `overlap_distance_mm`, and adds `end_point` (OPEN-shape trim end). `overlap_amount_mm(od,bead)`: needle = **one full needle OUTER Ø** (operator said "diameter"; the retired bool used the RADIUS — fallback one bead) / distance = typed mm / none = 0. Conditional-emit → **legacy sketches byte-identical**; `from_dict` MIGRATES legacy `overlap_closure:true` → `overlap_mode:"needle"`. **Compiler/geometry:** new arc-length helpers `_cumlen`/`_project_arclen`/`_point_at_arclen`/`_subpath_arclen` + **`trim_open_path(path,start,end)`** (projects both anchors onto the polyline, returns the sub-path; end unset → far extreme → reduces EXACTLY to the legacy flip, byte-identical). `_shape_paths`: closed unfilled → `reorder_path_to_start` (seam, unchanged); open unfilled with a start/end anchor → `trim_open_path`. Over-closure overshoot is now **per-shape** `shape.overlap_amount_mm(_od,bead)` fed to the existing `extend_closed_path` (closed unfilled only), in both `compile_to_trajectory` and `plan_print_sections`. `_resolve_start_on_shape` for open shapes now returns the nearest point ALONG the path (not just an endpoint) so markers trim anywhere. **Canvas:** new **red ■ end marker** (mirrors the green ▸ start flag) — OPEN shapes always (trim end, default = far endpoint); CLOSED loops only once `overlap_mode != none` (the overlap handle at seam + overlap arclen on the rolled ring via `_closed_ring`). Drag (`_apply_resize` `"end"`): open → `end_point`; closed → project cursor onto the ring → `overlap_mode="distance"` + `overlap_distance_mm = arclen-from-seam`. `set_needle_od` (page feeds the outer Ø so a needle-mode marker matches the compiler); `clear_end_point`; `_shape_supports_end`/`_effective_end_world`/`_end_marker_screen`/`_end_handle_at`. (Fixed: canvas had no `numpy` import — `_closed_ring` uses the arrays `_shape_paths` already returns.) **Page:** closed loops get a **Closure overlap** combo (None / Needle Ø / Custom distance) + distance spin (Custom seeds a visible default so the handle appears off the seam); open shapes get a **Print end** custom/default row + "Reset end point" (mirrors the start row). **Needs GUI verification on ME3B V1.** Tests: `tests/test_v75x_sketch_overlap_start_end_markers.py` (25) + migrated `..._overlap_sequence_needle_mode` (33); all 19 sketch suites green (327) + print-library/quick-print-multi-ink/travel-split/sketch-edit/extrusion-volume (77). |
| `MEBP_v75x_SKETCH_PARAMETRIC_CONSTRAINTS.md` | v7.5.x **Print Builder → Sketch gains parametric-modeling constraints** (operator: "tangent, join, coincident, parallel, horizontal, vertical, concentric"; chose via AskUserQuestion the **full set** — + perpendicular, equal length/radius, driven **distance/radius dimensions**, fix/lock — with **live solve while dragging** and **snap→constraint auto-capture**). **Architecture mirrors `PlateSketchSolver`** (scipy `least_squares` LM/TRF, residual rows per kind, transient weight-1000 `drag_ghost`, DOF via Jacobian rank) but operates **directly on SketchShape DOFs** (line/polygon = vertices; circle = cx,cy,**r**; ellipse/rect = center+size; travel = cx,cy; `region` excluded) — no entity-graph restructure. **Only shapes referenced by ≥1 constraint enter the variable vector** (pay-for-play; unconstrained sketches never touch the solver). **Stable ids:** new `SketchShape.id` (0=unassigned, emitted only when ≠0) + `Sketch.constraints`/`SketchConstraint{id,kind,refs=[[shape_id,anchor]…],value,mode,weight}` (emitted only when non-empty) → **legacy sketches serialize byte-identically** (regression-locked); ids assigned lazily by `ensure_shape_ids()` on first constraint use. Index refs impossible — `optimize_print_order` REORDERS the list (verified it never mutates vertex order, so `p{k}` anchors stay valid); `backtrace_shape` copies clear `id` (dup-id corruption); `prune_constraints()` drops refs to deleted shapes/stale vertices (canvas delete cascades). Anchors: `center`/`p{k}`/rect corners `c0..c3`/`shape` + virtual drag anchors `mid`(line)/`centroid`(polygon). Tangent = line↔circle (cross²/len²−r²) or circle↔circle with `mode` external/internal **chosen from the drawn geometry** (`tangent_mode_for`); `equal_radius` is a plain residual (radius IS a variable here, unlike the plate solver); `fix` excludes the shape's DOFs (a locked shape's drag ghost is structurally filtered → dragging it moves NOTHING). Ghost held **solver-side** (never on the model → an undo snapshot mid-drag can't serialize it); structural `_constraint_valid` filter makes predicted rows EXACT (conflict attribution + LM/TRF choice). **NEW `SupportClasses/SketchConstraintSolver.py`** (reuses `SolveReport`/`DOFStatus`); write-back clamps sizes ≥0.05 mm. **Canvas:** constrained move/endpoint drags → ghost pin + 33 ms-throttled `QTimer` solves (`_begin_cdrag_ghost`/`_queue_cdrag`/`_flush_cdrag`/`_end_cdrag`); size-handle drags → "plain" throttled re-solve; group ops + typed edits → `solve_after_edit()`; `_snap` now records provenance (`_snap_hit`; `_snap_vertices` yields `(x,y,idx,anchor)`) so **drawing/dragging onto a vertex auto-captures coincident, onto a line/circle edge → point_on** (`_maybe_add_snap_constraint`, deduped, toggleable), wired into line/circle/rect-corner/polygon commits + endpoint-drag release; `add_constraint_for_selection(kind)` = selection-based creation (Join auto-resolves the NEAREST anchor pair; point-on resolves curve-vs-point with a line+circle convention: circle=curve; fix toggles); constraint **glyph layer** (H/V/∥/⊥/=/T/🔒 pills, coincident rings, dashed distance leaders + `↔ value` labels, conflicts render RED from the last report); `constraints_changed` signal; undo/redo round-trips constraints (they live in the sketch dict). **Page:** persistent **"Constraints" card** (DOF/status line: fully-constrained / N DOF free / ⚠ conflicting; "Capture joins while drawing" checkbox; 13 per-selection-gated buttons; constraint rows = click-to-select-shapes label + editable value spin for dimensions + ✕ delete, rebuilt on the debounced preview tick with a focus guard so typing in a value spin isn't yanked). Constraints are **design-time only** — compiler/welds/optimizer semantics untouched. **Needs GUI verification on ME3B V1.** Tests: `tests/test_v75x_sketch_constraints.py` (43); all 18 sketch suites green (300) + print-library/quick-print-multi-ink/travel-split (44). |
| `MEBP_v75x_MOSAIC_ORIENTATION_ADJUST_TOOL.md` | v7.5.x **interactive "Fix orientation" tool on the plate mosaic → saves the camera's mirror + rotation** (operator, 2026-07-24: "for any mosaic we build … buttons that flip/rotate the individual images to match the correct view … these settings then applied to the camera setup … continuously change and set the actuals"). AskUserQuestion: correct **feed + mosaic**; **flip H/V + 90° + fine angle**; **instant re-blend from retained tiles**. **Reworks the two prior same-day mirror cuts** (source-flip; rotation downstream) into a clean **raw-frames + apply-per-consumer** model so the mosaic can re-blend against a *changing* orientation: (1) **display** — `CameraFeedView.set_view_orientation(mirrored, rotation_deg)` transforms the SHOWN pixmap (flip+rotate, matching `_orient_tile`) so the feed reads upright/un-mirrored, and **inverts clicks back to raw frame coords** via `QImage.trueMatrix().inverted()` (so `pixel_to_stage_offset` is unaffected); overlays mapped through the same xform; opt-in per view, no-op at (False,0°), suppressed in `set_edge_pick_mode` (needle row=Z). (2) **click→stage** — `pixel_to_stage_offset` re-applies mirror (`dx→−dx`) then `R(θ)` on the raw click. (3) **mosaic** — `MosaicBuilder._orient_tile` mirror+`R(θ)` per tile, **retains the small canvas-res tiles** (`retain_for_reorient`) + `set_frame_orientation`/`reblend_reoriented` (zero accumulators + re-blend every retained tile; `_accumulate_oriented_tile` factored out; `free_accumulators` a no-op while retaining, `free_reorient` releases). **camera_widget** source-flip reverted → `_mirrored` is a stored FLAG only, frames raw (`_grab_frame`/`capture_fresh_frame` return raw); `camera_manager.get_rotation_deg`/`get_mirrored`/`_widget` getattr-safe (clears the pre-existing `test_v75x_spheroid_picker_scaling` `_rotation_deg` errors) + `view_orientation` convenience. **calibration.py:** `_ploc_microscope_frame_orientation()`→`(rot,mir)` fed into the full-plate + well-scan builders (so the mosaic renders oriented); live feed corrected via `_ploc_apply_feed_orientation` (`_ploc_ensure_live_camera` + `_refresh_ploc_view`). **[RELOCATED per operator: the flip/rotate tool lives in the single-well "Calibrate mosaic scan" dialog, NOT the full-plate Plate Location mosaic — it's fast + already has the manual slide/image-gaps registration.]** `MosaicBuilder.tile_images_px` now applies `_orient_tile` per tile (no-op default) so the dialog's per-image `MosaicRegistrationView` reflects the orientation. **`gui/dialogs/mosaic_calibration_dialog.py`** seeds `(mir,rot)` from `CameraManager.view_orientation`, builds with it, keeps the builder alive (retained frames), and adds a **"Camera orientation (flip / rotate images)"** group (`⇄ Flip H`/`⇅ Flip V`/`⟲ 90°`/`⟳ 90°`/Rotation-° spin/**Apply to camera setup**) — each control re-orients the shown tiles (`set_frame_orientation`+`_show_tiles`) + pushes the camera live (feed+clicks+future scans), Apply persists to `CameraCalibrationStore` (flip-H=`rot→−rot`+toggle-mirror, flip-V=`rot→180−rot`+toggle, rotate=`±90`, normalised). `retain_for_reorient`/`reblend_reoriented`/`free_reorient` remain a general (unused-in-prod) builder capability. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (scan → flip/rotate until correct + feed upright → Apply → re-scan correct; clicks land right; restart restores; needle edge-pick + rotation cal still work; flip an `_orient_tile`/`_orient_qimage` sign if a control goes the wrong way). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_mosaic_orientation_adjust.py` (12: builder retain/reblend/identity/free + feed-view transform/click-inverse/90°-dims/edge-pick-suppress) + updated `camera_rotation_cal_and_monitor` (mirror back in mapping; widget flag-only; `view_orientation`) + `mapping_camera_orient_and_rosette_z` (builder mirror back); camera store/liveview/correction/hw-controls/picker-scaling + mosaic/reanchor/fluorescence/plate-location-page suites green (236). |
| `MEBP_v75x_DARK_PALETTE_WHITE_BACKGROUND_FIX.md` | v7.5.x **fix — "multiple pages have a white background now"** (operator, 2026-07-24). Root cause (investigation): the dark theme is a **QSS only** (`styles.py::build_theme` via `MainWindow.setStyleSheet`), but **no dark `QPalette` is set anywhere** (`main.py` does `app.setStyle("Fusion")` but never `app.setPalette`; repo-wide grep = zero `setPalette`), and the QSS has **no `QScrollArea`/`QGraphicsView` rules** + no base-`QWidget` background. Fusion follows the **OS/system palette** when none is set, so the palette `Base` role is white on a light-mode OS (or after a Qt dark-mode-detection shift) — and **every widget the QSS doesn't explicitly paint renders white**: QScrollArea viewports (most page bodies), QGraphicsView viewports (5 view classes — `_CalibrationPlateView`/`_CalibrationYZView`/`WellPlateView`/`ProjectionPane`/`ProjectionView` — set **no** background brush, unlike 5 siblings that do `setBackgroundBrush(COLORS["base"])`), and the fully-transparent left context panel (`#contextScrollArea QWidget { transparent }`) that bottoms out at `Base`. **Not from the recent camera/mapping work** — `styles.py`/`main.py`/calibration styling all unchanged (git); an external OS/Qt palette shift exposed the latent fragility. **Fix:** new `main.py::_apply_dark_palette(app)` (after `setStyle(fusion)`) pins a Catppuccin-Mocha `QPalette` (`Base #181825` = the viewport bg, `Window #1e1e2e`, `Text #cdd6f4`, `Button #313244`, `Highlight #cba6f7`, disabled `#6c7086`, PlaceholderText guarded for Qt-version) so the dark theme is decoupled from the OS theme — standard practice for a Fusion+dark-QSS app. The QSS still overrides per-widget wherever it sets an explicit bg (styled widgets unchanged); only the previously-white uncovered viewports/panels now default to dark, across **all** pages incl. needle calibration. Verified headlessly (`Base==#181825`, `Window==#1e1e2e` after apply); tests make their own `QApplication` (no palette) so unaffected. **Needs operator visual confirmation on the running app.** Optional follow-up: add `setBackgroundBrush` to the 5 brush-less QGraphicsViews for palette-independent consistency (the palette fix already darkens them). |
| `MEBP_v75x_MAPPING_CAMERA_ORIENTATION_AND_ROSETTE_Z_CLAMP.md` | v7.5.x two operator reports (2026-07-24, follow-up to the per-camera rotation/mirror work), each root-caused with a read-only investigation agent first. **(1) Camera µm/px + mirror + rotation must be applied when mapping the plate/rosette.** Finding: the live-feed click paths (manual click-rim, re-anchor, click-to-move) already route through `CameraManager.pixel_to_stage_offset` (µm/px + rotation + mirror) — but the **mosaic BUILD** placed raw frames (`MosaicBuilder._blend_tile_to_composite` did only a `cv2.resize`, no per-frame rotation/mirror — only a coarse manual none/rot180/flip dropdown that ignored the calibration store), and every mosaic back-projection (`MosaicWellMappingDialog._on_confirm`, `_ploc_fit_from_mosaic_detections`, `MosaicWellRemap`, rosette sub-well offsets) is a raw `stage = extent + px/scale` map (µm/px only). So tile CENTERS were right but within-tile offsets back-projected the wrong way for a rotated/mirrored camera (mirror flips handedness; error grows to ~½ FOV). **Fix at BUILD time** (the only place per-tile orientation about each tile's own centre is available): `MosaicBuilder` gains `frame_rotation_deg` + `_orient_tile(tile)` (`R(θ)` about the tile centre, matching `pixel_to_stage_offset`; **no-op fast path** at 0° = byte-identical legacy placement; applied after the resize in `_blend_tile_to_composite`, so incremental + rebuild + all scan paths inherit it). Then the composite is stage-aligned and the existing raw back-projection is correct for free (all four consumers unchanged). `CalibrationPage._ploc_microscope_frame_orientation()` reads the microscope slot's `get_rotation_deg` and feeds the full/rosette scan + `_well_scan_builder`. Large non-axis rotations clip tile corners (mapping stays correct, only peripheral coverage lost — overlap covers it). **Rotation sign needs real-HW verification** (mis-signed θ rotates the mosaic the wrong way — same caveat as the rotation calibration). **MIRROR is NOT handled in the builder** — a mirrored camera is un-mirrored at the frame source (`CameraWidget`, see `MEBP_v75x_CAMERA_ROTATION_CAL_AND_MONITOR_CAMERA.md`), so the builder already receives an un-mirrored image; the initial cut passed `frame_mirrored` here + flipped in `_orient_tile`, removed when the mirror moved to the source. Fluorescence mosaic + mosaic-calibration-dialog builders left unoriented (documented follow-up). **(2) Rosette mapping clamped Z instead of going to the assigned safe Z.** Root cause: the rosette scan/map retracts pass `_safe_z` to `safe_travel_to`/`ensure_retracted_to`, but those apply the **insert-clearance floor** `_min_travel_z_mm` (raise to clear the tallest tube), armed **only when `plate.max_rim_height_mm > 0`** = rosette tube inserts (exactly why plate mapping was unaffected). Two facets: (a) the floor overrode the assigned safe Z during imaging-height mapping travel where it isn't needed (needle never descends there, `target_z_mm=None`), and (b) `app.py::_update_insert_clearance` computed `top_z + max_rim + margin` **without the `z_up_sign` factor** — the same polarity bug already fixed in `PrintTrajectoryPlanner._well_print_z`. Fix: new `apply_insert_floor: bool = True` on `safe_travel_to`/`ensure_retracted_to` (False skips the floor → exact assigned safe Z), passed False by the 3 calibration mapping/scan retracts (pre-scan `ensure_retracted_to`, `_MosaicScanWorker` first-tile, `_AutoReanchorWorker`); **print/pick-place travel keep the floor (default True)**; + `_update_insert_clearance` now uses `top_z + z_up_sign * (max_rim + margin)` (no-op on the current `z_up_sign=+1` ME3B V1 profile, correct on -1). **Safety:** the floor is bypassed ONLY for imaging-height mapping travel (needle stays at the assigned safe Z = imaging height, inherently above the tubes, never descends), so it cannot drag through a tube; well-descending travel keeps the floor. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (mapped well/sub-well centres land correctly with a calibrated rotated/mirrored camera; rosette map retracts to the assigned safe Z with no clamp; printing over a rosette still clears the tubes). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_mapping_camera_orient_and_rosette_z.py` (13) + mosaic (`test_v731_mosaic`/`_mosaic_memory_and_overlay_perf`/`_mosaic_orientation_remap`, 44) + z-retract/jog-travel/plate-location-z (36) green; no-op default preserves all existing mosaic + floor behavior. |
| `MEBP_v75x_CAMERA_ROTATION_CAL_AND_MONITOR_CAMERA.md` | v7.5.x three operator requests (2026-07-24). **(1) Per-camera rotation-vs-stage calibration on Hardware Setup → Cameras:** every slot mini-card (Camera Detection & Assignment) gains a row-3 strip — a role-aware readout `Rotation vs stage: θ° (Δ from nominal) — <mount hint>` + a **"⟳ Rotation…"** button (enabled while that camera runs) that launches the existing stage-motion `PixelCalibrationDialog` and commits **ROTATION ONLY** (`result_rotation_deg` → `CameraManager.set_rotation_deg` live + `CameraCalibrationStore.set_rotation` per device identity, µm/px siblings preserved — the needle/objective µm/px calibrations keep their own flows). Nominal mounts per role: **microscope** views along Z → rotation is in the stage **XY plane** (axis-aligned nominals 0/±90/180°); **needle X/Y** side cams mounted **~45° to the stage X/Y axes** (±45/±135°); monitor axis-aligned. New pure helpers `role_nominal_rotations`/`nominal_rotation_delta`/`role_rotation_hint` (module-level in `hardware_setup.py`; Δ is a sanity hint only — motion always uses θ itself via the existing `pixel_to_stage_offset` path). When the calibrated slot holds the MICROSCOPE role the fresh rotation also syncs into every per-objective calibration via new public `ObjectiveCalibrationCard.adopt_camera_rotation` (extracted from `_on_calibrate_orientation_clicked`, which now reuses it) — the stale-per-objective-rotation guard from `MEBP_v75x_REANCHOR_MOSAIC_AND_CAMERA_ORIENTATION.md`. Readouts restore on slot assignment through the existing `_restore_calibration_for_slot` rotation restore; refresh hooks in `_refresh_camera_preview_state` + `_refresh_role_derived_displays`. **(2) New "Monitor" camera** — rests on the stage, overviews the entire operation: `CameraRole.MONITOR` (singleton, "Monitor (overview)" in the role combo, info badge) + **`MAX_LIVE_CAMERAS` 3 → 4** (4th slot card; `CameraManager` default + `app.py` now source the constant instead of a hardcoded 3; `from_dict` pads legacy 3-entry `camera_roles` → migration-free). **(3) Per-camera "Mirrored view" flag** (follow-up): a **"⇄ Mirrored view"** checkbox (row 4 of each slot card, enabled once the slot has a source) that says the camera shows a left↔right flipped view. A mirror reverses image **handedness** — which a proper rotation can NEVER express — so it's a separate flag. **The feed is flipped at the FRAME SOURCE** — `CameraWidget._maybe_mirror_frame` (shared by `_grab_frame` *before the raw cache* and the direct `capture_fresh_frame` mosaic path) `cv2.flip(frame, 1)` when set — so **what the operator SEES is un-mirrored** AND the cached raw frame used by detection / mosaic / click-mapping is the same corrected image; nothing downstream re-mirrors. New `CameraCalibrationStore.get/set_mirrored` (a `mirrored` bool sibling of `rotation_deg`; stored only when True, False pops the key → legacy entries byte-identical, µm/px/rotation/image-correction siblings preserved) + `CameraWidget._mirrored`/`set_mirrored`/`mirrored` + `CameraManager.get/set_mirrored` **delegate to the widget** (like image correction; local `_mirrored[]` cache for no-widget slots) + `_on_toggle_mirror`/`_apply_slot_mirror` (push live + persist per identity) + restore in `_restore_calibration_for_slot` + "· mirrored view" appended to the rotation readout. Geometric flip (before the raw cache), unlike brightness/gamma (display-only, after). Mirror lives ONLY in the identity store (not the objective store — survives objective swaps since the objective push never touches `set_mirrored`). **[UPDATED 2026-07-24 — operator: "if i select mirror camera it needs to mirror the output of the camera feed so what i see is not mirrored."
